IAE-1-SRC-0001 The area above the zygomatic arch is called:
['Scalp.', 'Face.', 'Preauricular region.', 'Temporal fossa.', 'Infratemporal fossa.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the temporal fossa is the shallow depression on the side of the skull lying above the zygomatic arch, occupied mainly by the temporalis muscle. The infratemporal fossa (e) is the space below the zygomatic arch, so it is the mirror-image answer. The scalp (a) covers the vault of the skull, while the face (b) and preauricular region (c) lie anterior and inferior to this area.

IAE-1-SRC-0002 The muscle that divides the neck into anterior & posterior triangles is:
['Sternohyoid.', 'Sternothyroid.', 'Sternomastoid.', 'Omohyoid.', 'Digastric.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the sternomastoid (sternocleidomastoid) runs obliquely across the neck from the manubrium and clavicle to the mastoid process, dividing each side of the neck into an anterior and a posterior triangle. Sternohyoid and sternothyroid (a, b) are infrahyoid strap muscles that lie within the muscular triangle. Omohyoid (d) and digastric (e) subdivide those triangles further but do not create the primary anterior/posterior division.

IAE-1-SRC-0003 All the following arteries share in the blood supply of the head & neck EXCEPT:
['Common carotid artery.', 'Internal carotid artery.', 'External carotid artery.', 'Subclavian artery.', 'Axillary artery.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the axillary artery is the continuation of the subclavian artery beyond the outer border of the first rib and supplies the upper limb, not the head and neck. The common carotid (a) and its internal (b) and external (c) divisions are the principal suppliers of the head and neck. The subclavian artery (d) contributes through its vertebral, thyrocervical and costocervical branches.

IAE-1-SRC-0004 Right common carotid artery:
['Is a branch of arch of aorta.', 'Ends at the level of the lower border of the cricoid cartilage.', 'Ends opposite the disc between C3 & C4.', 'Shows carotid sinus & body at its beginning.', 'Divides into external carotid & subclavian artery.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the right common carotid artery ends at the upper border of the thyroid cartilage, which lies opposite the disc between C3 and C4. Option (a) is wrong because the right common carotid is a branch of the brachiocephalic artery, not the arch of the aorta. Option (b) is wrong as it ends at the upper border of thyroid cartilage, (d) is wrong because the carotid sinus and body lie at its termination, and (e) is wrong because it divides into external and internal carotid arteries.

IAE-1-SRC-0005 The artery that ends inside the parotid gland is the:
['Common carotid artery.', 'External carotid artery.', 'Internal carotid artery.', 'Subclavian artery.', 'Brachiocephalic artery.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the external carotid artery enters the substance of the parotid gland and ends within it at the level of the neck of the mandible by dividing into the superficial temporal and maxillary arteries. The internal carotid (c) does not enter the parotid; it ascends in the carotid sheath and enters the skull. The common carotid (a) ends in the neck, while the subclavian (d) and brachiocephalic (e) arteries never reach the parotid region.

IAE-1-SRC-0006 Match each artery in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
The common carotid ends at the upper border of the thyroid cartilage by dividing into internal and external carotids. The external carotid terminates within the parotid gland at the neck of the mandible, whereas the internal carotid gives no branch in the neck and ends by supplying the brain. The vertebral artery is the first branch of the subclavian artery, and the left common carotid arises directly from the arch of the aorta.

IAE-1-SRC-0007 The external carotid artery gives all the following branches EXCEPT:
['Facial artery.', 'Vertebral artery.', 'Occipital artery.', 'Superficial temporal artery.', 'Maxillary artery.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the vertebral artery is the first branch of the subclavian artery, not of the external carotid. The facial (a) and occipital (c) arteries are true branches of the external carotid, and the superficial temporal (d) and maxillary (e) arteries are its two terminal branches within the parotid gland.

IAE-1-SRC-0008 Costocervical trunk is a branch of:
['Brachiocephalic artery.', 'Left common carotid artery.', 'Right common carotid artery.', 'Subclavian artery.', 'Internal carotid artery.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the costocervical trunk arises from the second part of the subclavian artery and divides into the superior intercostal and deep cervical arteries. The common carotid arteries (b, c) give no branches in the neck before their terminal division, and the internal carotid (e) similarly gives no cervical branches. The brachiocephalic artery (a) simply divides into the right common carotid and right subclavian arteries.

IAE-1-SRC-0009 The vein that ends in subclavian vein is the:
['External jugular vein.', 'Internal jugular vein.', 'Superior vena cava.', 'Right brachiocephalic vein.', 'Left brachiocephalic vein.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the external jugular vein descends superficially across the sternomastoid and pierces the investing layer of deep fascia to drain into the subclavian vein. The internal jugular vein (b) joins the subclavian vein to form the brachiocephalic vein rather than ending in it. The brachiocephalic veins (d, e) unite to form the superior vena cava (c), which drains into the right atrium.

IAE-1-SRC-0010 Superior vena cava is formed by the union of:
['External & internal jugular veins.', 'External jugular & subclavian veins.', 'Internal jugular & subclavian veins.', 'Right brachiocephalic & subclavian veins.', 'Right brachiocephalic & left brachiocephalic veins.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the superior vena cava is formed behind the first right costal cartilage by the union of the right and left brachiocephalic veins. Option (c) describes the formation of a brachiocephalic vein, not the superior vena cava. The external jugular vein (a, b) drains into the subclavian vein and takes no direct part in forming the superior vena cava.

IAE-1-SRC-0011 As regards the parotid gland, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It lies below external auditory meatus.', 'Its duct crosses over buccinator.', 'Its duct pierces masseter.', 'It contains internal carotid artery.', 'Its duct opens in floor of mouth.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the parotid gland occupies the space below and in front of the external auditory meatus, between the ramus of the mandible and the mastoid process. Option (b) is wrong because the duct pierces the buccinator rather than crossing over it, and (c) is wrong because the duct crosses over the masseter instead of piercing it. Option (d) is wrong because the gland contains the external carotid artery, and (e) is wrong because the parotid duct opens into the vestibule of the mouth opposite the second upper molar.

IAE-1-SRC-0012 As regards the submandibular gland, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is the largest salivary gland.', 'It is formed of a superficial part & a deep part.', 'Its duct opens in the vestibule of the mouth.', 'It lies beneath the mucous membrane of the floor of the mouth.', 'It contains the facial nerve inside it.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the submandibular gland is J-shaped and folded around the posterior border of the mylohyoid, giving it a large superficial part and a smaller deep part. Option (a) is wrong because the parotid is the largest salivary gland, and (c) is wrong because its duct opens in the floor of the mouth beside the frenulum of the tongue. Option (d) describes the sublingual gland, and (e) is wrong because the facial nerve lies within the parotid gland.

IAE-1-SRC-0013 Match each structure in the neck in the left column with its related statement in the right column:
 KEY
The superficial fascia of the neck encloses the platysma, a muscle of facial expression supplied by the facial nerve. The investing layer of deep cervical fascia completely encircles the neck like a collar, splitting to enclose sternomastoid and trapezius. The pretracheal fascia forms a sheath for the thyroid gland, the prevertebral fascia covers the prevertebral muscles in front of the vertebral column, and the carotid sheath encloses the carotid arteries, the internal jugular vein and the vagus nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0014 As regards the anterior triangle in the neck, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It lies behind the sternomastoid muscle.', 'It is bounded anteriorly by sternomastoid.', 'It is bounded posteriorly by midline of neck.', 'It is bounded inferiorly by base of mandible.', 'It is divided into 4 triangles.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the anterior triangle is subdivided by the digastric and omohyoid muscles into four smaller triangles: the digastric (submandibular), carotid, muscular and submental triangles. Option (a) is wrong because the anterior triangle lies in front of sternomastoid, and (b) and (c) are wrong because it is bounded posteriorly by sternomastoid and anteriorly by the midline of the neck. Option (d) is wrong because the base of the mandible forms its superior, not inferior, boundary.

IAE-1-SRC-0015 Match each structure in the left column with its enclosing triangle in the right column:
 KEY
The muscular triangle contains the infrahyoid strap muscles together with the thyroid gland, larynx and trachea. The digastric (submandibular) triangle is occupied mainly by the submandibular salivary gland. The carotid triangle contains the carotid system and its related structures, including the common carotid artery, the facial vein as it descends to join the internal jugular vein, and the hypoglossal nerve as it crosses the carotid arteries.

IAE-1-SRC-0016 All the following are contents of orbit EXCEPT:
['Oculomotor nerve.', 'Ophthalmic nerve.', 'Lacrimal gland.', 'Ophthalmic artery.', 'Maxillary nerve.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the maxillary nerve leaves the middle cranial fossa through the foramen rotundum to enter the pterygopalatine fossa, not the orbit proper. The oculomotor nerve (a) and ophthalmic nerve (b) enter through the superior orbital fissure, and the ophthalmic artery (d) enters via the optic canal. The lacrimal gland (c) occupies the lacrimal fossa in the upper lateral part of the orbit.

IAE-1-SRC-0017 As regards the bony orbit, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is pyramidal in shape.', 'Its apex is the opening on face.', 'It shows optic canal at its apex.', 'It has 2 fissures.', 'It contains the lacrimal apparatus.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the apex of the orbit is directed posteriorly, while the opening on the face is the orbital base. The orbit is indeed pyramidal (a), with the optic canal at its posteriorly directed apex (c). It has superior and inferior orbital fissures (d) and contains the lacrimal gland, sac and duct (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0018 Match each muscle in the left column with its nerve supply in the right column:
 KEY
The oculomotor nerve supplies all the extraocular muscles except two, so levator palpebrae superioris, superior rectus and medial rectus are all oculomotor. The two exceptions follow the classic rule LR6 SO4: lateral rectus is supplied by the abducent (6th) nerve and superior oblique by the trochlear (4th) nerve. The ophthalmic and optic nerves are purely sensory and supply no eye muscles.

IAE-1-SRC-0019 Sensory supply of lacrimal gland is:
['Oculomotor nerve.', 'Trochlear nerve.', 'Abducent nerve.', 'Ophthalmic nerve.', 'Optic nerve.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the lacrimal nerve, a branch of the ophthalmic division of the trigeminal nerve, carries the sensory supply to the lacrimal gland. The oculomotor (a), trochlear (b) and abducent (c) nerves are purely motor to the extraocular muscles. The optic nerve (e) is a special sensory nerve concerned only with vision.

IAE-1-SRC-0020 All the following are contents of cranial cavity EXCEPT:
['Middle meningeal vessels.', 'Vertebral artery.', 'Pituitary gland.', 'External carotid artery.', 'Internal carotid artery.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the external carotid artery remains entirely outside the skull, ending within the parotid gland. The middle meningeal vessels (a) run in the extradural space of the cranial cavity, and the vertebral (b) and internal carotid (e) arteries both enter the cranial cavity to supply the brain. The pituitary gland (c) sits in the sella turcica within the middle cranial fossa.

IAE-1-SRC-0024 Match each artery in the left column with its artery of origin in the right column:
 KEY
The anterior spinal artery arises from the intracranial part of the vertebral artery, and the two vertebral arteries then unite to form the basilar artery, which ends by dividing into the two posterior cerebral arteries. The internal carotid artery gives the ophthalmic artery within the cranial cavity and ends by dividing into the anterior and middle cerebral arteries. The middle meningeal artery is a branch of the first part of the maxillary artery and enters the skull through the foramen spinosum.

IAE-1-SRC-0026 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['Scalp extends from hair margin to lower border of mandible.', 'The aponeurosis of scalp contains blood vessels & muscles of scalp.', 'Orbicularis oculi muscle closes the eye.', 'Orbicularis oris muscle is the muscle of the cheecks.', 'Buccinator is pierced by submandibular duct.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because orbicularis oculi is the sphincter muscle of the eyelids and closes the eye, being supplied by the facial nerve. Option (a) is wrong because the scalp covers the vault of the skull, whereas the face extends from the hair margin to the lower border of the mandible. Option (b) is wrong because the vessels and muscles of the scalp lie in the connective tissue layer, (d) is wrong because orbicularis oris is the muscle of the lips, and (e) is wrong because buccinator is pierced by the parotid duct.

IAE-1-SRC-0027 Match each structure in the left column with its nerve supply in the right column:
 KEY
The general rule is that the trigeminal nerve is sensory to the skin of the face and to the scalp in front of the auricle, while the facial nerve is motor to all muscles of facial expression, including orbicularis oris and occipitofrontalis. The important exception is the skin over the angle of the mandible, which is supplied by the great auricular nerve from the cervical plexus (C2, C3) rather than by the trigeminal nerve. This exception is clinically useful in distinguishing trigeminal from cervical sensory loss.

IAE-1-SRC-0028 Blood supply of face comes from:
['Internal carotid artery.', 'External carotid artery.', 'Common carotid artery.', 'Internal & external carotid arteries.', 'Ophthalmic artery.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the face receives blood from both carotid systems: the external carotid supplies most of it through the facial, superficial temporal and maxillary arteries, while the internal carotid contributes through the ophthalmic artery, whose supratrochlear, supraorbital and dorsal nasal branches supply the upper face. Options (a) and (b) are each only half the answer, and (e) is only one branch of the internal carotid contribution. The common carotid (c) supplies the face only indirectly through its terminal divisions.

IAE-1-SRC-0029 Which of the following IS NOT a content of Infratemporal fossa?
['Medial pterygoid muscle.', 'Temporalis.', 'Maxillary artery.', 'Mandibular nerve.', 'Pterygoid plexus of veins.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because temporalis is a content of the temporal fossa above the zygomatic arch, although its tendon descends deep to the arch to reach the coronoid process. The medial pterygoid (a), the maxillary artery (c), the mandibular nerve (d) and the pterygoid venous plexus (e) are all true contents of the infratemporal fossa.

IAE-1-SRC-0030 Lateral pterygoid muscle is surrounded by:
['Ramus of mandible.', 'Masseter.', 'Branches of mandibular nerve.', 'Branches of maxillary artery.', 'Pterygoid plexus of veins.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the pterygoid venous plexus surrounds the lateral pterygoid muscle, lying around and between its two heads within the infratemporal fossa. The ramus of the mandible (a) and masseter (b) lie lateral to the muscle rather than surrounding it. Branches of the mandibular nerve (c) and maxillary artery (d) are related to the muscle but do not envelop it in the way the venous plexus does.

IAE-1-SRC-0031 Suprahyoid muscles include all the following EXCEPT:
['Digastric.', 'Stylohyoid.', 'Sternomastoid.', 'Mylohyoid.', 'Geniohyoid.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because sternomastoid is a large muscle running from the sternum and clavicle to the mastoid process and is not attached to the hyoid bone at all. The suprahyoid group consists of the digastric (a), stylohyoid (b), mylohyoid (d) and geniohyoid (e), all of which lie above the hyoid bone and elevate it during swallowing.

IAE-1-SRC-0032 Which one of the following muscles has 2 bellies?
['Digastric.', 'Stylohyoid.', 'Sternomastoid.', 'Mylohyoid.', 'Geniohyoid.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the digastric muscle, as its name indicates, has an anterior and a posterior belly joined by an intermediate tendon that is held to the hyoid bone by a fibrous sling. The two bellies also have different nerve supplies, the anterior from the mandibular nerve and the posterior from the facial nerve. Stylohyoid (b), mylohyoid (d) and geniohyoid (e) are single-bellied, and sternomastoid (c) has two heads of origin but not two bellies.

IAE-1-SRC-0033 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['Submandibular salivary gland is the largest salivary gland.', 'Sublingual salivary gland is wedge-shaped.', 'Submandibular gland lies below mucosa of floor of mouth.', 'Submandibular duct opens in vestibule of mouth.', 'Sublingual salivary gland ducts open in floor of mouth.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the sublingual gland drains by numerous small ducts that open directly onto the floor of the mouth along the sublingual fold. Option (a) is wrong because the parotid is the largest salivary gland, and (c) is wrong because it is the sublingual gland that lies beneath the mucosa of the floor of the mouth. Option (d) is wrong because the submandibular duct opens in the floor of the mouth beside the frenulum, and it is the parotid duct that opens into the vestibule.

IAE-1-SRC-0034 Match each part of the nose in the left column with its related statement in the right column:
 KEY
The roof of the nasal cavity is narrow and formed largely by the cribriform plate of the ethmoid, lying above the cavity and separating it from the anterior cranial fossa. The floor is formed by the hard palate, which also serves as the roof of the mouth. The medial wall is the nasal septum shared by the two cavities, while the lateral wall is characterised by the superior, middle and inferior conchae, beneath which the paranasal sinuses drain.

IAE-1-SRC-0035 Which of the following lies between orbit & nose?
['Anterior ethmoidal air sinuses.', 'Frontal air sinus.', 'Sphenoidal air sinus.', 'Maxillary air sinus.', 'Cavernous sinus.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the ethmoidal air cells occupy the ethmoidal labyrinth, which forms the thin medial wall of the orbit and the upper lateral wall of the nose, placing them directly between the two cavities. The frontal sinus (b) lies above the orbit and the maxillary sinus (d) below it. The sphenoidal sinus (c) lies behind the nasal cavity, and the cavernous sinus (e) is a dural venous sinus inside the cranial cavity.

IAE-1-SRC-0036 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Vestibule of mouth lies outside the teeth.', 'Oral cavity proper ends by palatoglossal arch.', 'Mylohyoid muscle forms floor of oral cavity proper.', 'Oral cavity proper is roofed by tongue.', 'Vestibule of mouth receives opening of parotid duct.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the oral cavity proper is roofed by the hard and soft palates, while the tongue occupies its floor. The vestibule is correctly described as the slit outside the teeth (a) that receives the parotid duct opposite the second upper molar (e). The oral cavity proper does end posteriorly at the palatoglossal arch (b), and the mylohyoid forms the muscular floor (c).

IAE-1-SRC-0037 As regards the pharynx, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It begins at base of skull.', 'Ends at level of 4th cervical vertebra.', 'It has no posterior wall.', 'Laryngopharynx contains palatine tonsil.', 'Esophagus contains pharyngeal tonsil.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the pharynx is a fibromuscular tube that begins at the base of the skull and descends to become continuous with the oesophagus. Option (b) is wrong because it ends at the level of the 6th cervical vertebra, and (c) is wrong because the pharynx has a complete posterior wall but an incomplete anterior wall where it opens into the nose, mouth and larynx. Option (d) is wrong because the palatine tonsil lies in the oropharynx, and (e) is wrong because the pharyngeal tonsil lies in the nasopharynx.

IAE-1-SRC-0038 As regards the larynx, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It ends at level of C6.', 'Its thyroid cartilage is paired.', 'It has 2 upper vestibular folds & 2 lower vocal cords.', 'Its vestibule lies between its inlet & vestibular folds.', 'Its sinus lies between its vestibular & vocal folds.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the thyroid cartilage is a single unpaired cartilage, as are the cricoid and epiglottis, while the arytenoid, corniculate and cuneiform cartilages are the paired ones. The remaining statements are true: the larynx ends at C6 where it continues as the trachea (a), it contains two vestibular folds above and two vocal folds below (c), the vestibule lies between the inlet and the vestibular folds (d), and the laryngeal sinus lies between the vestibular and vocal folds (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0039 As regards the ear, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Middle ear lies within the temporal bone.', 'External ear shows the auricle.', 'Stapes is one of the muscles that lie within the middle ear.', 'Malleus is a bony ossicle.', 'Tensor tympani is a middle ear muscle.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because this statement is incorrect: the stapes is the third and smallest auditory ossicle, a bone rather than a muscle. The two muscles of the middle ear are the stapedius and the tensor tympani (e). The remaining statements are true, as the middle ear cavity lies within the petrous temporal bone (a), the auricle is part of the external ear (b), and the malleus is the first auditory ossicle (d).

IAE-1-SRC-0040 All of the following are branches of external carotid artery except:
['Lingual artery.', 'Post auricular artery.', 'Supra orbital artery.', 'Sup thyroid artery.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the supraorbital artery is a branch of the ophthalmic artery, which arises from the internal carotid, and so belongs to the internal carotid system. The lingual, posterior auricular and superior thyroid arteries are all named branches of the external carotid artery.

IAE-1-SRC-0041 All of the following are branches of subclavian artery except:
['Internal thoracic artery.', 'Occipital artery.', 'Vertebral artery.', 'Thyro cervical trunk.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the occipital artery is a posterior branch of the external carotid artery, not of the subclavian. The internal thoracic artery, the vertebral artery and the thyrocervical trunk all arise from the first part of the subclavian artery.

IAE-1-SRC-0042 Scalenus anterior divides which of the following arteries?
['Internal carotid artery.', 'Vertebral artery.', 'External carotid artery.', 'Sub clavian artery.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because scalenus anterior crosses in front of the subclavian artery and divides it into three parts: the first medial to the muscle, the second behind it and the third lateral to it. The carotid and vertebral arteries are not divided in this way.

IAE-1-SRC-0043 All of the following are branches of vertebral artery except:
['Anterior spinal artery.', 'Posterior spinal artery.', 'Posterior inferior cerebellar artery.', 'Posterior cerebral artery.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the posterior cerebral artery is a terminal branch of the basilar artery, not of the vertebral artery. The anterior and posterior spinal arteries and the posterior inferior cerebellar artery all arise from the vertebral artery in the cranial cavity.

IAE-1-SRC-0044 The occipitofrontalis muscle occupies which layer of the scalp:
['1st layer.', '3rd layer.', '2nd layer.', '4th layer.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because occipitofrontalis lies in the third layer of the scalp, the musculo-aponeurotic layer formed by the muscle and the intervening epicranial aponeurosis. The first layer is skin, the second the dense superficial fascia carrying the vessels and nerves, and the fourth the loose areolar layer.

IAE-1-SRC-0045 Skin covering angle of mandible is supplied by:
['V.', 'VII.', 'Great auricular n.', 'Cervical branch of VII.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the skin over the angle of the mandible is supplied by the great auricular nerve, a branch of the cervical plexus, and not by the trigeminal nerve like the rest of the face. This is why sensation there is spared in trigeminal neuralgia, and the facial nerve (b, d) is motor rather than cutaneous here.

IAE-1-SRC-0046 Ext jugular vein ends in which of the following?
['Retro mandibular vein.', 'Brachio cephalic vein.', 'Sub clavian vein.', 'Int. jagular vein.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the external jugular vein descends over sternomastoid, pierces the deep fascia above the clavicle and drains into the subclavian vein. It is formed in part by the posterior division of the retromandibular vein, so the retromandibular vein is its source rather than its termination.

IAE-1-SRC-0047 All of the following muscles supplied by occulo motor nerve except:
['Inf oblique.', 'Superior rectus.', 'Inf rectus.', 'Sup oblique.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the superior oblique is supplied by the trochlear nerve, the fourth cranial nerve. The oculomotor nerve supplies the superior, inferior and medial recti, the inferior oblique and levator palpebrae superioris, while lateral rectus is supplied by the abducent nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0048 Which of the following muscles don't belong to infra hyoid muscles?
['Sterno hyoid.', 'Post. Belly of digastrics.', 'Sterno thyroid.', 'Thyro hyoid.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the posterior belly of digastric is a suprahyoid muscle, lying above the hyoid bone and supplied by the facial nerve. The four infrahyoid or strap muscles are sternohyoid, sternothyroid, thyrohyoid and omohyoid.

IAE-1-SRC-0049 Nerves and vessels of scalp located in which layer?
['2nd layer.', '3rd layer.', '4th layers.', '5th layer.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the vessels and nerves of the scalp run in the second layer, the dense superficial fascia, where the fibrous septa hold cut vessels open and cause profuse bleeding from scalp wounds. The fourth layer, the loose areolar tissue, is instead the dangerous area for the spread of infection.

IAE-1-SRC-0052 Para nasal sinus drain in the which part of the nose?
['Roof.', 'Lateral wall.', 'Medial wall.', 'Floor.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because all the paranasal sinuses open onto the lateral wall of the nasal cavity, on or around the three conchae and in the hiatus semilunaris. The roof carries the olfactory mucosa, the medial wall is the septum and the floor is formed by the palate.

IAE-1-SRC-0053 Auditory tube opens into:
['Lat wall of the nose.', 'Laryngopharynx.', 'Oropharynx.', 'Nasopharynx.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the pharyngeal opening of the auditory (Eustachian) tube lies on the lateral wall of the nasopharynx, behind the inferior concha and above the level of the soft palate. This is why nasopharyngeal infection readily spreads to the middle ear, especially in children.

IAE-1-SRC-0054 Pretrachial fascia enclose which of the following?
['Sternocledomastoid.', 'Thyroid cartilage.', 'Carotid vessels.', 'Thyroid gland.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the pretracheal fascia splits to enclose the thyroid gland, forming its false capsule and binding it to the larynx and trachea so that it moves on swallowing. Sternomastoid is enclosed by the investing layer and the carotid vessels by the carotid sheath.

IAE-1-SRC-0055 Which of the following doesn't carry taste?
['Vagus nerve.', 'Facial nerve.', 'Hypoglossal nerve.', 'Glossopharyngeal nerve.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the hypoglossal nerve is purely motor to the muscles of the tongue and carries no taste fibers. Taste is carried by the facial nerve from the anterior two thirds of the tongue, by the glossopharyngeal from the posterior third, and by the vagus from the epiglottic region.

IAE-1-SRC-0056 Nerves that supply muscles of the tongue:
['Hypoglossal.', 'Vagus.', 'Facial.', 'Oculomotor.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the hypoglossal nerve supplies all the muscles of the tongue, both intrinsic and extrinsic, with the single exception of palatoglossus, which is supplied by the pharyngeal plexus. The vagus, facial and oculomotor nerves have no motor role in the tongue.

IAE-1-SRC-0057 Muscles of the face is supplied by which nerves?
['III.', 'VIII.', 'V.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the muscles of facial expression are supplied by the facial nerve, the seventh cranial nerve, which is not among the options offered. The trigeminal nerve (V) is sensory to the face and motor only to the muscles of mastication, while III moves the eye and VIII serves hearing and balance.

IAE-1-SRC-0058 All of the following nerves carry parasympathetic except:
['Oculomotor.', 'Hypoglossal.', 'Facial.', 'Glossopharyngeal.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the hypoglossal nerve carries no parasympathetic fibers, being purely motor to the tongue muscles. The four cranial nerves carrying parasympathetic fibers are the oculomotor, facial, glossopharyngeal and vagus.

IAE-1-SRC-0059 All of the following nerves are pure sensory except:
['Olfactory.', 'Vestibule chochlear.', 'Trochlear.', 'Optic.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the trochlear nerve is purely motor, supplying the superior oblique muscle. The three purely sensory cranial nerves are the olfactory, the optic and the vestibulocochlear.

IAE-1-SRC-0060 All of the following at the level of C6 except:
['End of larynx.', 'End of pharynx.', 'End of trachea.', 'Begining Of esophagus.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the trachea ends much lower, at the level of the sternal angle opposite the T4/T5 disc, where it bifurcates. The sixth cervical vertebra marks the end of the larynx and pharynx and the beginning of the trachea and oesophagus.

IAE-1-SRC-0061 All the following cartilages are paired except:
['Epiglottis.', 'Arytenoid.', 'Cuniform.', 'Corniculate.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the epiglottis is a single, unpaired cartilage, like the thyroid and cricoid cartilages. The arytenoid, corniculate and cuneiform cartilages all occur in pairs.

IAE-1-SRC-0062 Posterior boundary of post. Triangle of the neck is:
['Sternocledo mastoid.', 'Trapezius.', 'Sterno hyoid.', 'Clavicle.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the anterior border of trapezius forms the posterior boundary of the posterior triangle of the neck. Sternomastoid forms its anterior boundary and the middle third of the clavicle its base.

IAE-1-SRC-0063 Ant cerebral artery is branch from:
['Ext. carotid artery.', 'Int. carotid artery.', 'Ophthalmic artery.', 'Vertebral artery.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the anterior cerebral artery is one of the two terminal branches of the internal carotid artery, the other being the middle cerebral artery. The external carotid supplies structures outside the cranial cavity, and the vertebral artery contributes to the posterior part of the circle of Willis.

IAE-1-SRC-0064 Which of the following muscles is responsible for pushing the food from vestibule of mouth?
['Masseter.', 'Orbicularis oris.', 'Mylohyoid.', 'Buccinator.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because buccinator forms the muscular wall of the cheek and, on contracting, presses the cheek against the teeth to push food from the vestibule back onto the occlusal surfaces. Masseter elevates the mandible, orbicularis oris closes the lips and mylohyoid forms the floor of the mouth.

IAE-1-SRC-0065 Arterio-venous anastomosis is frequently present in:
['Cornea.', 'Cartilage.', 'Nails.', 'Epidermis.', 'Skin of nose.'] KEY 4
Arteriovenous anastomoses are abundant in nasal skin and aid thermoregulation.

IAE-1-SRC-0066 What is the joint that contains a cartilaginous disc?
['Superior radioulnar joint.', 'Ankle joint.', 'Metacarpophalangeal joints.', 'Temporo-mandibular joint.', 'Carpometacarpal joint of thumb.'] KEY 3
The temporomandibular joint contains an articular disc.

IAE-1-SRC-0067 Which of the following is a uniaxial joint?
['Metacarpophalangeal joints.', 'Intervertebral disc.', 'Atlanto-axial joint.', 'Hip joint.', 'Wrist joint.'] KEY 2
The median atlanto-axial joint is a uniaxial pivot joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0068 What is the type of joint that connects the bones of the skull?
['Gomphosis.', 'Sutures.', 'Cartilaginous.', 'Synovial.'] KEY 1
Most skull bones are connected by fibrous sutures.

IAE-1-SRC-0069 Which one of the following muscles has spiralized fibers?
['Flexor pollicis longus.', 'Subscapularis.', '2nd dorsal interosseous.', '3rd palmar interosseous.', 'Trapezius.'] KEY 4
The source identifies trapezius as an example of spiralized fiber arrangement.

IAE-1-SRC-0070 The maxilla is considered:
['Long bone', 'Irregular bone', 'Flat bone', 'Short bone', 'Pneumatic bone'] KEY 4
The maxilla contains an air sinus and is therefore a pneumatic bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0071 An example of the pivot joints is:
['Sagittal suture of the skull', 'Intervertebral joints.', 'Atlanto-axial joint.', 'Knee joint.', 'Elbow joint.'] KEY 2
The atlanto-axial joint is a pivot joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0072 The joint that connects the root of a tooth with its socket?
['Gomphosis', 'Suture', 'Syndesmosis', 'Cartilaginous', 'Synovial'] KEY 0
A gomphosis is the fibrous peg-and-socket joint between a tooth root and its alveolus.

IAE-1-SRC-0073 Regarding the mouth cavity proper all the following boundaries are true, except:
['In front and sides it is bounded by gums and teeth', 'Roof is formed palate.', 'Floor is formed tongue.', 'Posteriorly, it communicates with the larynx.'] KEY 3
The oral cavity proper communicates posteriorly with the oropharynx, not directly with the larynx.

IAE-1-SRC-0074 Regarding the tongue, all the following statements are true except:
['Its tip is the anterior free end.', 'General sensation of the Anterior 2/3 is supplied by lingual nerve.', 'General sensation of the posterior 1/3 is supplied by glossopharyngeal nerve.', 'Taste sensation of the posterior 1/3 is supplied by glossopharyngeal nerve.', 'Taste sensation of the Anterior 2/3 is supplied by vagus nerve.'] KEY 4
Taste from the anterior two-thirds of the tongue is mainly carried by chorda tympani, not the vagus nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0075 Regarding the pharynx, all the following statements are true except:
['The nasopharynx lies behind the nasal cavity.', 'The nasopharynx contains on its posterior wall the two opening of the auditory tubes.', 'The oropharynx lies behind the oral cavity.', 'The laryngopharynx lies behind the larynx.', 'The oropharynx contains in its lateral walls the Palatine tonsils.'] KEY 1
The auditory tube openings are in the lateral walls of the nasopharynx, not its posterior wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0076 Regarding the salivary glands one of the following statements is true;
['The parotid glands lie below the angle of the mandible.', 'The submandibular glands have numerous small ducts.', 'The parotid glands duct opens in the vestibule of the mouth opposite the upper canine tooth.', 'The submandibular glands duct opens in the floor of the mouth below the tongue at sublingual papillae.', 'The Sublingual glands lie under the mucous membrane of the pharynx.'] KEY 3
The submandibular duct opens at the sublingual papilla in the floor of the mouth.

IAE-1-SRC-0077 In the oral cavity proper, ___ forms the roof.
 KEY
The palate forms the roof of the oral cavity proper.

IAE-1-SRC-0078 Palate is the partition between the ___ and ___ cavities.
 KEY
The palate separates the oral cavity from the nasal cavity.

IAE-1-SRC-0079 The main arterial supply of the tongue is ___.
 KEY
The lingual artery is the main arterial supply of the tongue.

IAE-1-SRC-0080 The ___ nerve is the motor nerve supply of the tongue.
 KEY
The hypoglossal nerve provides motor supply to most tongue muscles.

IAE-1-SRC-0081 The ___ nerve supplies general sensation to the anterior part of the tongue.
 KEY
The lingual nerve supplies general sensation to the anterior two-thirds of the tongue.

IAE-1-SRC-0082 Each individual has two sets of teeth ___ and ___.
 KEY
Humans develop temporary teeth first, followed by permanent teeth.

IAE-1-SRC-0083 Palatine tonsils are present in the lateral wall of ___.
 KEY
The palatine tonsils lie in the lateral walls of the oropharynx.

IAE-1-SRC-0084 Adenoids are present in the posterior wall of ___.
 KEY
Adenoids or the pharyngeal tonsil lie in the nasopharynx.

IAE-1-SRC-0101 As regards Trigeminal nerve, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is the 3rd cranial nerve.', 'It is a purely motor nerve.', 'It supplies muscles of mastication.', 'It is responsible for equilibrium.', 'It secretory to 2 salivary glands.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the mandibular division of the trigeminal nerve is motor to the four muscles of mastication as well as to mylohyoid, anterior belly of digastric, tensor tympani and tensor palati. Option (a) is wrong because it is the 5th cranial nerve, and (b) is wrong because it is a mixed nerve, being mainly sensory to the face. Option (d) is wrong because equilibrium is served by the 8th cranial nerve, and (e) is wrong because the trigeminal itself carries no secretomotor fibers to salivary glands.

IAE-1-SRC-0102 As regards Facial nerve, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is a mixed nerve.', 'It is the 5th cranial nerve.', 'It is motor to muscles of pharynx.', 'It is secretory to parotid gland.', 'It is sensory to face.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the facial nerve is a mixed nerve, carrying motor fibers to the muscles of facial expression, special sensory taste fibers from the anterior two-thirds of the tongue, and secretomotor fibers to the submandibular, sublingual and lacrimal glands. Option (b) is wrong because it is the 7th cranial nerve, and (c) is wrong because the vagus is motor to the pharyngeal muscles. Option (d) is wrong because the glossopharyngeal nerve is secretory to the parotid, and (e) is wrong because the trigeminal is sensory to the face.

IAE-1-SRC-0103 As regards glossopharyngeal nerve, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is the 10th cranial nerve.', 'It is a purely motor nerve.', 'It supplies one muscle of pharynx.', 'It is secretory to 2 salivary glands.', 'It is motor to muscles of pharynx.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the glossopharyngeal nerve supplies only one muscle, the stylopharyngeus. Option (a) is wrong because it is the 9th cranial nerve, and (b) is wrong because it is a mixed nerve. Option (d) is wrong because it is secretory to the parotid gland only, and (e) is wrong because the remaining pharyngeal muscles are supplied by the vagus through the pharyngeal plexus.

IAE-1-SRC-0104 As regards Vagus nerve, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It supplies muscles of mastication.', 'It carries taste sensation.', 'It supplies muscles of neck.', 'It is secretory to parotid gland.', 'It supplies muscles of tongue.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the vagus nerve carries taste sensation from the epiglottis and the most posterior part of the tongue through its internal laryngeal branch. Option (a) is wrong because the trigeminal supplies the muscles of mastication, and (c) is wrong because the accessory nerve supplies muscles of the neck. Option (d) is wrong because the glossopharyngeal nerve is secretory to the parotid, and (e) is wrong because the hypoglossal nerve supplies the muscles of the tongue.

IAE-1-SRC-0105 Match each nerve in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
The first two cranial nerves are purely special sensory, the olfactory carrying smell and the optic carrying vision. The oculomotor nerve is motor to most of the extraocular muscles, and the trigeminal nerve, through its mandibular division, supplies the muscles of mastication. The facial nerve carries taste from the anterior two-thirds of the tongue through its chorda tympani branch.

IAE-1-SRC-0106 Match each nerve in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
The facial nerve is secretomotor to the submandibular and sublingual salivary glands through the chorda tympani and submandibular ganglion. The glossopharyngeal nerve supplies only stylopharyngeus, while the vagus carries parasympathetic fibers to the heart as well as to the rest of the thoracic and abdominal viscera. The spinal part of the accessory nerve supplies sternomastoid and trapezius in the neck, and the hypoglossal nerve is motor to the muscles of the tongue.

IAE-1-SRC-0114 Which of the following cranial nerves is purely motor?
['Olfactory.', 'Vestibulocochlear.', 'Trigeminal.', 'Trochlear.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the trochlear nerve is purely motor, supplying only the superior oblique muscle. The olfactory and vestibulocochlear nerves are purely sensory and the trigeminal nerve is mixed, carrying sensation from the face and motor fibers to the muscles of mastication.

IAE-1-SRC-0021 Match each structure in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
The falx cerebri is a sickle-shaped vertical fold of dura that descends between the two cerebral hemispheres, which is the origin of its name. The tentorium cerebelli is a horizontal tent-like fold separating the occipital lobes of the cerebrum above from the cerebellum below. The falx cerebelli is a small vertical fold between the two cerebellar hemispheres, while the diaphragma sellae roofs the hypophyseal fossa and covers the pituitary gland.

IAE-1-SRC-0022 All the following are paired venous sinuses EXCEPT:
['Straight sinus.', 'Sigmoid sinus.', 'Transverse sinus.', 'Cavernous sinus.', 'Superior petrosal sinus.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the straight sinus is a single midline sinus lying at the junction of the falx cerebri with the tentorium cerebelli, draining into the confluence of sinuses. The sigmoid (b), transverse (c), cavernous (d) and superior petrosal (e) sinuses all exist as right and left pairs.

IAE-1-SRC-0023 Which of the following sinuses lies on either side of pituitary gland?
['Straight sinus.', 'Sigmoid sinus.', 'Transverse sinus.', 'Cavernous sinus.', 'Superior petrosal sinus.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the cavernous sinuses lie one on each side of the body of the sphenoid, flanking the pituitary gland in the sella turcica. This close relationship explains why pituitary tumours can compress structures in the cavernous sinus. The straight sinus (a) is midline and posterior, while the transverse (c) and sigmoid (b) sinuses lie in the posterior cranial fossa and the superior petrosal sinus (e) runs along the upper border of the petrous temporal bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0025 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Middle meningeal artery runs an extradural course.', 'Anterior cerebral artery is a branch of internal carotid artery.', 'Posterior inferior cerebellar artery is a branch of vertebral artery.', 'The 2 vertebral arteries unite to form posterior cerebral artery.', 'Basilar artery supplies pons.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the two vertebral arteries unite at the lower border of the pons to form the basilar artery, not the posterior cerebral artery. The posterior cerebral arteries are instead the terminal branches of the basilar artery. The other statements are true: the middle meningeal artery runs extradurally (a), the anterior cerebral artery is a terminal branch of the internal carotid (b), the posterior inferior cerebellar artery branches from the vertebral artery (c), and the basilar artery gives pontine branches (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0050 Which of the following dural folds covering pituitary gland?
['Falx cerebri.', 'Diaphragm sellae.', 'Falx cerebelli.', 'Tentorium cerebelli.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the diaphragma sellae is the small circular fold of dura that roofs the hypophyseal fossa over the pituitary gland, pierced centrally by the infundibulum. The falx cerebri lies between the cerebral hemispheres, the falx cerebelli between the cerebellar hemispheres, and the tentorium cerebelli between the cerebrum and cerebellum.

IAE-1-SRC-0051 Superior sagittal sinus related to which fold?
['Falx cerebri.', 'Diaphragm sellae.', 'Falx cerebelli.', 'Tentorium cerebelli.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the superior sagittal sinus runs in the attached upper (convex) margin of the falx cerebri, while the inferior sagittal sinus runs in its free lower margin. The diaphragma sellae carries no sagittal sinus, and the tentorium cerebelli is related to the transverse and superior petrosal sinuses.

IAE-1-SRC-0085 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Grey matter is formed of nerve cell bodies.', 'White matter is formed of nerve fibers.', 'In spinal cord, the grey matter surrounds the white matter.', 'In brain stem, the grey matter collects into nuclei.', 'Cerebellum is covered from outside by cerebellar cortex.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because this statement is reversed: in the spinal cord the white matter lies peripherally and surrounds the centrally placed H-shaped grey matter. The arrangement is the opposite of that in the cerebrum and cerebellum, where grey matter forms an outer cortex. The other statements are true, as grey matter consists of cell bodies (a), white matter of fibers (b), brain stem grey matter is broken up into nuclei (d), and the cerebellum has an outer cerebellar cortex (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0086 Match each structure in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
These four terms follow a simple two-by-two rule based on cell bodies versus fibers and inside versus outside the central nervous system. Collections of cell bodies are called nuclei inside the CNS and ganglia outside it. Bundles of nerve fibers are called tracts inside the CNS and nerves outside it.

IAE-1-SRC-0087 Match each structure in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
A commissure is a white-matter connection linking the two sides of the central nervous system, the corpus callosum being the largest example. A decussation is the point where a descending tract crosses the midline, a synapse is the site of neuronal contact, a pathway is a chain of functionally related tracts, and a lemniscus is a collection of ascending fibers in the brain stem.

IAE-1-SRC-0088 Brain stem is formed of:
['Cerebrum & cerebellum.', 'Cerebral hemispheres & diencephalon.', 'Midbrain, pons & diencephalon.', 'Pons, medulla & cerebellum.', 'Midbrain, pons & medulla oblongata.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the brain stem consists of three parts from above downwards: the midbrain, the pons and the medulla oblongata. The cerebellum (a, d) lies behind the brain stem and is connected to it by peduncles but is not part of it. The diencephalon (b, c), containing the thalamus and hypothalamus, belongs to the forebrain rather than the brain stem.

IAE-1-SRC-0089 As regards cerebral hemispheres, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its inferior surface is formed of orbital & medial parts.', 'It has 3 poles; frontal, parietal & occipital.', 'Insula is a part of cerebral cortex deep into lateral sulcus.', 'The most important sulci are the central & medial sulci.', 'Its parietal lobe is mainly motor in its function.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the insula is a buried area of cerebral cortex lying deep within the lateral sulcus, hidden by the overlying opercula. Option (a) is wrong because the inferior surface is formed of orbital and tentorial parts, and (b) is wrong because the three poles are frontal, temporal and occipital. Option (d) is wrong because the most important sulci are the central and lateral sulci, and (e) is wrong because the parietal lobe is mainly sensory in function.

IAE-1-SRC-0090 Match each lobe in the left column with its appropriate function in the right column:
 KEY
The frontal lobe contains the primary motor cortex in its precentral gyrus, while the parietal lobe contains the primary sensory cortex in its postcentral gyrus, the two being separated by the central sulcus. The temporal lobe houses the auditory cortex and the occipital lobe the visual cortex around the calcarine sulcus. The limbic lobe, on the medial surface, is concerned with emotion and memory through structures such as the cingulate gyrus and hippocampus.

IAE-1-SRC-0091 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['Corpus callosum is formed of grey matter.', 'Internal capsule connects the 2 hemispheres.', 'Corpus callosum has anterior limb, genu & posterior limb.', 'Caudate nucleus is one of the basal nuclei.', 'The lateral ventricle is the cavity of the diencephalon.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the caudate nucleus is one of the basal nuclei, together with the putamen, globus pallidus, amygdala and claustrum. Option (a) is wrong because the corpus callosum is formed of white matter, and (b) is wrong because it is the corpus callosum, not the internal capsule, that connects the two hemispheres. Option (c) is wrong because the anterior limb, genu and posterior limb belong to the internal capsule, and (e) is wrong because the lateral ventricle is the cavity of the cerebral hemisphere.

IAE-1-SRC-0092 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Thalamus is an oval mass of grey matter.', 'Thalamus is a relay station of all sensations except smell.', 'Hypothalamus is inhibitory to many endocrine glands.', 'Hypothalamus controls autonomic functions.', 'Hypothalamus controls hunger & thirst.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because this statement is incorrect: it is the epithalamus, through the pineal body and its melatonin secretion, that is inhibitory to endocrine glands, whereas the hypothalamus is largely stimulatory to the pituitary through its releasing hormones. The remaining statements are true, since the thalamus is an oval mass of grey matter (a) that relays all sensations except olfaction (b), and the hypothalamus controls autonomic function (d) as well as hunger and thirst (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0093 As regards the brain stem, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Midbrain shows 2 anterior elevations which are pyramid & olive.', 'Midbrain has 2 cerebral peduncles.', 'Pons gives attachment to middle 4 cranial nerves.', 'Midbrain shows posterior 4 colliculi.', 'Fourth cranial nerve is attached to midbrain.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because this statement is incorrect: the pyramid and the olive are surface elevations of the medulla oblongata, not of the midbrain. The midbrain instead shows the two cerebral peduncles anteriorly (b) and the four colliculi of the tectum posteriorly (d). The pons gives attachment to the middle four cranial nerves, the 5th to 8th (c), and the trochlear nerve emerges from the dorsal aspect of the midbrain (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0094 What are the cranial nerves attached to the midbrain?
['1st-4th cranial nerves.', '2nd-4th cranial nerves.', '3rd-4th cranial nerves.', '3rd-6th cranial nerves.', 'Middle 4 cranial nerves.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because only the oculomotor (3rd) and trochlear (4th) nerves are attached to the midbrain, the oculomotor emerging from the interpeduncular fossa anteriorly and the trochlear from the dorsal surface below the inferior colliculus. The 1st and 2nd cranial nerves (a, b) are not attached to the brain stem at all, arising from the cerebrum. The middle four cranial nerves (e) are attached to the pons.

IAE-1-SRC-0095 As regards the cerebellum, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is formed of 2 hemispheres joined by a vermis.', 'Its surface is covered by a layer of white matter called cerebellar cortex.', 'Archicerebellum is responsible for equilibrium.', 'Its middle peduncles connect it with pons.', 'Paleocerebellum controls posture.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the cerebellar cortex covering the surface is formed of grey matter, not white matter, with the white matter lying deep to it. The remaining statements are true, as the cerebellum consists of two hemispheres joined by the midline vermis (a), the archicerebellum (flocculonodular lobe) maintains equilibrium (c), the middle cerebellar peduncles connect it to the pons (d), and the paleocerebellum controls posture and muscle tone (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0096 As regards the cerebellum, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Neocerebellum helps in planning of movements.', 'Has 6 peduncles.', 'Has cerebellar nuclei embedded in white matter.', 'Functionally, it has 3 lobes.', 'Forms floor of 4th ventricle.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because this statement is incorrect: the cerebellum forms the roof of the fourth ventricle, while the floor is formed by the posterior surface of the pons and medulla, the rhomboid fossa. The remaining statements are true, since the neocerebellum plans and coordinates movement (a), there are three pairs of peduncles making six in total (b), the cerebellar nuclei lie embedded in the white matter (c), and functionally it is divided into archi-, paleo- and neocerebellum (d).

IAE-1-SRC-0097 Which one of the following is the closest to brain tissue?
['Epidural space.', 'Dura mater.', 'Arachnoid mater.', 'Pia mater.', 'Subarachnoid space.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the pia mater is the innermost meningeal layer and adheres intimately to the surface of the brain, dipping into its sulci. From outside inwards the order is epidural space, dura mater, arachnoid mater, subarachnoid space and finally pia mater. The subarachnoid space (e) lies just superficial to the pia and contains cerebrospinal fluid.

IAE-1-SRC-0098 Subarachnoid space lies:
['Between dura & arachnoid mater.', 'Between arachnoid & pia mater.', 'Outside arachnoid mater.', 'Deep to pia mater.', 'Between dura & pia mater.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the subarachnoid space lies deep to the arachnoid mater and superficial to the pia mater, and it is filled with cerebrospinal fluid. The space between the dura and arachnoid (a) is the potential subdural space, and nothing lies deep to the pia (d) since it is adherent to brain tissue. Option (e) is too broad because it would include the arachnoid membrane itself.

IAE-1-SRC-0099 As regards the 3rd ventricle, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It lies between the 2 thalami.', 'It is connected with the lateral ventricle through interventricular foramen of Monro.', 'It is a part of the ventricular system.', 'It has 3 foramina that open in subarachnoid space.', 'It is connected to 4th ventricle through cerebral acquiduct of Sylvius.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: it is the fourth ventricle, not the third, that has three foramina opening into the subarachnoid space, namely the median foramen of Magendie and the two lateral foramina of Luschka. The remaining statements are true, since the third ventricle is the midline cleft between the two thalami (a), communicates with each lateral ventricle through the foramen of Monro (b), belongs to the ventricular system (c), and drains to the fourth ventricle through the cerebral aqueduct (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0100 Match each structure in the left column with its appropriate statement in the right column:
 KEY
Each part of the ventricular system is the cavity of the region of the neural tube from which it develops. The lateral ventricles are the cavities of the cerebral hemispheres and the third ventricle is the midline cavity of the diencephalon between the two thalami. The cerebral aqueduct is the narrow canal of the midbrain, the fourth ventricle is the tent-shaped cavity between the brain stem in front and the cerebellum behind, and the central canal is the cavity of the spinal cord.

IAE-1-SRC-0107 The only cranial nerve that emerges from back of brain stem is:
['Abducent.', 'Trochlear.', 'Accessory.', 'Oculomotor.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the trochlear nerve is the only cranial nerve to emerge from the dorsal aspect of the brain stem, appearing below the inferior colliculi before winding round the midbrain. The abducent and oculomotor nerves emerge ventrally, and the cranial root of the accessory nerve emerges from the side of the medulla.

IAE-1-SRC-0108 Cavity of Diencephalon is:
['Lat. Ventricle.', '3rd ventricle.', '4th ventricle.', 'Aquiduct of sylvius.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the third ventricle is the midline cavity of the diencephalon, bounded laterally by the thalamus and hypothalamus. The lateral ventricles belong to the cerebral hemispheres, the aqueduct to the midbrain and the fourth ventricle to the pons and medulla with the cerebellum.

IAE-1-SRC-0109 CSF is drained to:
['Inf. Sagittal sinus.', 'Transverse sinus.', 'Cavernous sinus.', 'Sup Sagittal sinus.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because cerebrospinal fluid is absorbed into the venous blood through the arachnoid villi that project into the superior sagittal sinus and its lateral lacunae. The other sinuses listed receive cerebral venous blood but are not the site of CSF absorption.

IAE-1-SRC-0110 Motor cortex is in which lobe of cerebrum?
['Frontal.', 'Occipital.', 'Temporal.', 'Parietal.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the primary motor cortex occupies the precentral gyrus of the frontal lobe. The occipital lobe carries the visual cortex, the temporal lobe the auditory cortex and the parietal lobe the general sensory cortex in the postcentral gyrus.

IAE-1-SRC-0111 Auditory cortex is in which lobe of cerebrum?
['Frontal.', 'Occipital.', 'Temporal.', 'Parietal.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the auditory cortex lies in the superior temporal gyrus of the temporal lobe, in and around the transverse temporal gyri. The frontal lobe carries the motor cortex, the parietal lobe the sensory cortex and the occipital lobe the visual cortex.

IAE-1-SRC-0112 All are true about Diencephalon except:
['Epithalamus is responsible for delay of puberty.', 'Hypothalamus can control spleen and autonomic system.', 'Its cavity is 3rd ventricle.', 'All special sensations must relay in thalamus.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because olfaction is the one special sensation that reaches the cortex without relaying in the thalamus, so not all special sensations relay there. The remaining statements are true, since the epithalamus with its pineal gland restrains the onset of puberty, the hypothalamus governs the autonomic system and influences the spleen, and the cavity of the diencephalon is the third ventricle.

IAE-1-SRC-0113 All are true about cerebellum except:
['The 2 cerebellar hemispheres are connected by vermis.', 'The paleo cerebellum controls posture.', 'The outer gray matter contains nuclei.', 'The Neo cerebellum is for motor planes.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the outer grey matter of the cerebellum is its cortex, while the cerebellar nuclei are islands of grey matter buried deep within the white matter. The remaining statements are true, since the vermis joins the two hemispheres, the paleocerebellum is concerned with posture and muscle tone and the neocerebellum with the planning and coordination of voluntary movement.

IAE-1-SRC-0115 Which of the following brain fibers is considered as best example of commissural fibers?
['Descending tracts.', 'Corpus callosum.', 'Internal capsules.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the corpus callosum is the largest commissure of the brain, connecting corresponding areas of the two cerebral hemispheres. Descending tracts and the internal capsule are projection fibers, carrying impulses between the cortex and lower centres.

IAE-1-SRC-0116 The artery that runs in front of pons is:
['Vertebral artery.', 'Posterior cerebral artery.', 'Basilar artery.', 'Ant. Spinal artery.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the basilar artery, formed by the union of the two vertebral arteries at the lower border of the pons, ascends in the midline on the ventral surface of the pons. The vertebral arteries lie on the medulla and the posterior cerebral arteries are its terminal branches around the midbrain.

IAE-1-SRC-0117 The nerve that emerges between olive and ICP of medulla oblongata is:
['Hypoglossal.', 'Facial.', 'Glossopharyngeal.', 'Trigeminal.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the glossopharyngeal nerve emerges from the postolivary sulcus, the groove between the olive and the inferior cerebellar peduncle, along with the vagus and the cranial root of the accessory nerve. The hypoglossal nerve emerges in front of the olive, and the facial and trigeminal nerves emerge at the pons.

IAE-1-SRC-0118 Which one of the following structures has no lymphatics?
['Pleura.', 'Brain.', 'Intestine.', 'Skin.', 'Liver.'] KEY 1
The classical teaching is that brain parenchyma lacks conventional lymphatic vessels.

IAE-1-SRC-0119 Thalamus is a part of:
['Cerebellum.', 'Cerebral hemisphere.', 'Pons.', 'Diencephalon.', 'Midbrain.'] KEY 3
The thalamus is a major component of the diencephalon.

IAE-1-SRC-0120 Brain stem is formed of:
['Pons, medulla and cerebellum.', 'Pons, medulla and thalamus.', 'Pons, midbrain and hypothalamus.', 'Midbrain, medulla and cerebellum.', 'Midbrain, pons and medulla.'] KEY 4
The brainstem consists of midbrain, pons and medulla oblongata.

IAE-1-SRC-0121 The following meningeal space contains CSF:
['Epidural space.', 'Subdural space.', 'Subarachnoid space.', 'All of the above.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 2
Cerebrospinal fluid is found in the subarachnoid space.

IAE-1-SRC-0122 Regarding the brain stem, it consists of:
['Midbrain, pons, medulla oblongata and cerebellum.', 'Midbrain, pons and diencephalon.', 'Midbrain, pons and medulla oblongata.', 'Midbrain, pons and thalamus.', 'Midbrain, pons, medulla oblongata and spinal cord.'] KEY 2
The brainstem is made up of the midbrain, pons and medulla oblongata.

IAE-1-SRC-0123 Regarding the ventricles of the brain, one is correct:
['The lateral ventricles lie in thalamus.', 'The third ventricle lies in cerebral hemisphere.', 'The third ventricle is connected to the fourth ventricle by aqueduct.', 'The fourth ventricle lies posterior to midbrain.', 'The fourth ventricle is connected to the third ventricle by interventricular foramen.'] KEY 2
The cerebral aqueduct connects the third ventricle to the fourth ventricle.

IAE-1-SRC-0124 Regarding the meninges the middle layer is:
['The dura matter.', 'The pia matter.', 'The arachnoid matter.', 'The falx cerebri', 'The diaphragm sella.'] KEY 2
The arachnoid mater lies between the dura mater and pia mater.

IAE-1-SRC-0125 All of the following are contents of sup mediastinum except:
['Arch of Aorta.', 'Left brachio cephalic vein.', 'Ascending Aorta.', 'Vagi.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the ascending aorta lies in the middle mediastinum within the pericardium, not in the superior mediastinum. The arch of the aorta, the left brachiocephalic vein and both vagus nerves are all classical contents of the superior mediastinum.

IAE-1-SRC-0126 Which of the following structures doesn't pass through the Aortic opening of Diaphragm?
['Thoracic duct.', 'Descending thoracic Aorta.', 'Azygous vein.', 'Right phrenic nerve.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the right phrenic nerve pierces the diaphragm through the vena caval opening at T8, alongside the inferior vena cava. The aortic opening at T12 transmits the descending thoracic aorta, the thoracic duct and the azygos vein.

IAE-1-SRC-0127 Azygous vein ends in which of the following vein?
['I.V.C.', 'S.V.C.', 'Brachiocephalic vein.', 'Subclavian vein.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the azygos vein arches over the root of the right lung and drains into the superior vena cava. It does not join the inferior vena cava, a brachiocephalic vein or the subclavian vein, although it does form an important collateral channel between the caval systems.

IAE-1-SRC-0128 Apex of the heart is formed by:
['Right Atrium.', 'Left ventricle.', 'Left atrium.', 'Right Ventricle.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the apex of the heart is formed by the left ventricle and lies in the left fifth intercostal space just medial to the midclavicular line. The right ventricle forms most of the sternocostal surface, while the atria lie at the base of the heart.

IAE-1-SRC-0129 Upper border of the heart is formed by:
['Right Atrium.', 'Right Ventricle.', 'Left Atrium.', 'Left ventricle.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the upper border of the heart is formed by the two atria, mainly the left atrium and its auricle, which lie most superiorly and posteriorly. The ventricles form the inferior and sternocostal parts of the heart rather than its upper border.

IAE-1-SRC-0130 Sternocostal surface of the heart is formed mainly by:
['Right Atrium.', 'Right Ventricle.', 'Left atrium.', 'Left ventricle.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the right ventricle forms the greater part of the sternocostal (anterior) surface of the heart, which is why it is the chamber most often injured in penetrating anterior chest wounds. The right atrium contributes to the right of this surface and the left ventricle only a narrow strip on the left.

IAE-1-SRC-0131 All of the following structures seen on the base of the heart except:
['Left Atrium.', 'Pulmonary veins.', 'Left ventricle.', 'Right Atrium.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the left ventricle does not appear on the base of the heart, which is formed mainly by the left atrium receiving the four pulmonary veins, with a small contribution from the right atrium. The base faces posteriorly towards the vertebral column.

IAE-1-SRC-0132 Thoracic duct receives lymph from all the following except:
['Left lower limb.', 'Right Upper limb.', 'Pelvis.', 'Left upper limb.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because lymph from the right upper limb drains through the right lymphatic duct, not the thoracic duct. The thoracic duct drains both lower limbs, the pelvis and abdomen, the left half of the thorax, the left upper limb and the left side of the head and neck.

IAE-1-SRC-0133 Thoracic duct end in which of the following?
['Right Brachiocephalic vein.', 'S.V.C.', 'Left Brachiocephalic vein.', 'I.V.C.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the thoracic duct ends by opening into the beginning of the left brachiocephalic vein, at the junction of the left subclavian and left internal jugular veins. It has no connection with the right brachiocephalic vein, the superior vena cava or the inferior vena cava.

IAE-1-SRC-0134 The arteries that supplying the heart muscle are branches of:
['Left common carotid artery.', 'Right Subclavian artery.', 'Ascending Aorta.', 'Arch of Aorta.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the right and left coronary arteries arise from the ascending aorta, just above the aortic valve in the right and left aortic sinuses. The arch of the aorta gives the three great vessels of the head, neck and upper limbs instead.

IAE-1-SRC-0135 All are true about sterna angle except:
['End of trachea.', 'Opposite to 2nd costal cartilage.', 'Beginning of Ascending Aorta.', 'End of Ascending Aorta.', 'Opposite to the disc between T4, T5.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the ascending aorta begins lower down, at the aortic valve behind the third costal cartilage, and not at the sternal angle. The sternal angle lies opposite the second costal cartilages and the T4/T5 disc, and marks the bifurcation of the trachea and the end of the ascending aorta where the arch begins.

IAE-1-SRC-0136 The coronary sinus (vein of the heart) ends in:
['S.V.C.', 'Right Brachiocephalic vein.', 'I.V.C.', 'Right Atrium.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the coronary sinus, which collects most of the venous blood of the heart, opens into the right atrium between the opening of the inferior vena cava and the atrioventricular orifice. It does not drain into the caval veins or the brachiocephalic vein.

IAE-1-SRC-0137 All are true about esophageal opening of diaphragm except:
['Transmits phrenic nerve.', 'Opposite to level of 10th thoracic vertebra.', 'Located 1 inch lt to median plane.', 'Transmits vagi nerve.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the phrenic nerve does not use the oesophageal opening: the right phrenic passes through the vena caval opening and the left phrenic pierces the muscular dome directly. The oesophageal opening lies at T10, about an inch to the left of the median plane, and transmits the oesophagus with both vagi and the oesophageal branches of the left gastric vessels.

IAE-1-SRC-0138 Neuro vascular plane of thorax lies between:
['Ext. intercostals, Int intercostals.', 'Int. intercostal, innermost intercostals.', 'Ext. intercostal, innermost intercostals.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the intercostal nerve and vessels run in the plane between the internal intercostal and the innermost intercostal muscles, sheltered in the subcostal groove. This is the plane entered deliberately during intercostal nerve block and avoided during chest drain insertion.

IAE-1-SRC-0139 The intercostal nerves are:
['Ventral roots of (T1 - T11).', 'Ventral roots of (T1 - T12).', 'Ventral rami of (T1 - T12).', 'Ventral rami of (T1 - T11).'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the intercostal nerves are the ventral rami of the first eleven thoracic spinal nerves; the ventral ramus of T12 lies below the twelfth rib and is named the subcostal nerve. Options (a) and (b) are wrong because a ventral root is a component of the spinal nerve itself, not a named peripheral nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0140 All are the contents of middle mediastinum except:
['Pulmonary trunk.', 'Ascending aorta.', 'Pulmonary veins.', 'Right vagus.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the right vagus nerve descends through the superior and then the posterior mediastinum and is not a content of the middle mediastinum. The middle mediastinum contains the heart and pericardium, the ascending aorta, the pulmonary trunk, the pulmonary veins, the lower half of the superior vena cava and the phrenic nerves.

IAE-1-SRC-0141 As regards the thoracic cage, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is formed of sternum anteriorly.', 'It is formed posteriorly by thoracic vertebrae.', 'The junction between body and xiphoid process is called sternal angle.', 'Sternum consists of 3 parts.', 'The ribs form the lateral part of the thoracic cage.'] KEY 2
The sternal angle is the junction between the manubrium and the body of the sternum; the junction with the xiphoid process is the xiphisternal joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0142 Sternal angle lies opposite the disc between:
['T2 & T3', 'T3 & T4', 'T4 & T5', 'T5 & T6', 'T6 & T7'] KEY 2
The correct answer is T4 & T5. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0143 False ribs are:
['Upper 7 ribs', '8th, 9th & 10th ribs', '6th, 7th & 8th ribs', 'Last 2 ribs', 'Last 7 ribs'] KEY 1
The correct answer is 8th, 9th & 10th ribs. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0144 The ribs that have no anterior attachment are:
['1st rib', '1st & 2nd ribs', 'Lower 5 ribs', 'Lower 3 ribs', 'Lower 2 ribs'] KEY 4
The correct answer is Lower 2 ribs. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0145 Each vertebra has all the following EXCEPT:
['2 pedicles', '2 laminae', '2 spines', '2 transverse processes', '2 superior articular processes'] KEY 2
The correct answer is 2 spines. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0146 Diaphragm takes origin from all the following EXCEPT:
['Sternum', 'Lower 8 ribs', 'Sides of bodies of vertebrae', 'Medial arcuate ligament', 'Lateral arcuate ligament'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Lower 8 ribs. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0147 Crura of diaphragm are attached to:
['Sternum', 'Lower 3 ribs', 'Lower 2 costal cartilages', 'Bodies of thoracic vertebrae', 'Bodies of lumbar vertebrae'] KEY 4
The correct answer is Bodies of lumbar vertebrae. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0148 Caval opening of diaphragm lies at the level of:
['T8', 'T9', 'T10', 'T11', 'T12'] KEY 0
The correct answer is T8. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0149 Vagi pass in diaphragm through:
['Caval opening', 'Esophageal opening', 'Aortic opening', 'Crura of diaphragm', 'Arcuate ligaments'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Esophageal opening. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0150 Esophageal opening of diaphragm transmits:
['Aorta & inferior vena cava', 'Esophagus & inferior vena cava', 'Esophagus & aorta', 'Esophagus & vagi', 'Esophagus, inferior vena cava & vagi'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Esophagus & vagi. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0151 As regards the diaphragm, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It takes origin from lower 6 ribs.', 'It is inserted into arcuate ligaments.', 'It has 2 copulae curved downwards.', 'Its aortic opening transmits the vagi.', 'Its esophageal opening lies at level of T8.'] KEY 0
The diaphragm arises from the xiphoid process, the inner surfaces of the lower six costal cartilages and adjacent ribs, and the lumbar crura, so origin from the lower six ribs is the correct statement here.

IAE-1-SRC-0152 As regards intercostal muscles, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Fibers of external intercostal muscle are directed upwards & forwards.', 'Internal intercostal muscle forms the intermediate layer.', 'Fibers of internal intercostal muscle are directed downwards & forwards.', 'Innermost intercostal muscle forms a complete layer.', 'Innermost intercostal muscle is localized to intercostal space.'] KEY 1
The intercostal muscles are arranged in three layers: external, internal, and innermost. The internal intercostal muscle is therefore the intermediate layer.

IAE-1-SRC-0153 Each intercostal space contains:
['One large posterior intercostal artery', 'One large anterior intercostal artery', 'Two small posterior intercostal arteries', 'Two large posterior intercostal arteries', 'Posterior ramus of a thoracic spinal nerve'] KEY 0
The correct answer is One large posterior intercostal artery. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0154 Each intercostal space contains all the following EXCEPT:
['One large posterior intercostal artery', 'Two small anterior intercostal arteries', 'Posterior ramus of a thoracic spinal nerve', 'Two small anterior intercostal veins', 'One large posterior intercostal vein'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Posterior ramus of a thoracic spinal nerve. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0155 Boundaries of mediastinum include all the following EXCEPT:
['Sternum', 'Vertebral column', 'Superior thoracic opening', 'Diaphragm', 'Ribs'] KEY 4
The correct answer is Ribs. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0156 Match each structure with its relation to the mediastinum:
 KEY
Each structure is matched to its standard anatomical relation or compartment.

IAE-1-SRC-0157 As regards the middle mediastinum, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It lies above an imaginary line at level of sternal angle.', 'It contains the heart.', 'It lies below a line at level of T6 vertebra.', 'It lies between heart & sternum.', 'It lies between heart & vertebral column.'] KEY 1
The middle mediastinum contains the pericardium and heart, together with the roots of the great vessels. The other options describe boundaries or neighboring compartments rather than its main content.

IAE-1-SRC-0158 Posterior mediastinum is bounded by all the following structures EXCEPT:
['Sternum', 'Vertebral column', 'Heart', 'Diaphragm', 'Imaginary line at level of sternal angle'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Sternum. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0159 Which of the following DOES NOT share in the walls of the anterior mediastinum?
['Sternum', 'Heart', 'Diaphragm', 'Imaginary line at level of T4', 'Vertebral column'] KEY 4
The correct answer is Vertebral column. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0160 Superior mediastinum is bounded by all the following EXCEPT:
['Sternum', 'Superior thoracic opening', 'Imaginary line between sternal angle & T4', 'Heart', 'Vertebral column'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Heart. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0161 The structure that lies in anterior & superior mediastinum is:
['Heart', 'Ascending aorta', 'Trachea', 'Esophagus', 'Thymus'] KEY 4
The correct answer is Thymus. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0162 Match each structure with its mediastinal location:
 KEY
The ascending aorta lies within the pericardium in the middle mediastinum. The arch of the aorta lies in the superior mediastinum, the descending thoracic aorta lies in the posterior mediastinum, the thymus occupies the superior mediastinum and may extend into the anterior mediastinum, and the esophagus passes through the superior and posterior mediastina.

IAE-1-SRC-0163 As regards superior vena cava, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is formed in superior mediastinum.', 'It is the continuation of the left brachiocephalic vein.', 'It enters the left atrium.', 'Right brachiocephalic vein ends in its middle.', 'It lies in posterior mediastinum.'] KEY 0
The superior vena cava is formed by the union of the right and left brachiocephalic veins in the superior mediastinum, then descends to open into the right atrium.

IAE-1-SRC-0164 As regards azygos vein, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is formed by union of right & left brachiocephalic veins.', 'It ends in inferior vena cava.', 'It ascends in anterior mediastinum.', 'It enters thorax through caval opening of diaphragm.', 'It opens in back of superior vena cava.'] KEY 4
The azygos vein arches over the root of the right lung and opens into the posterior aspect of the superior vena cava. It does not end in the inferior vena cava or run in the anterior mediastinum.
