IAE-1-SRC-0165 As regards ascending aorta, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It lies in superior mediastinum.', 'It lies within the pericardium.', 'It arises from right ventricle.', 'It continues as descending aorta.', 'It gives 3 branches.'] KEY 1
The ascending aorta begins at the left ventricle and lies within the fibrous pericardium before continuing as the arch of the aorta.

IAE-1-SRC-0166 As regards arch of aorta, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is the continuation of ascending aorta.', 'It lies in middle mediastinum.', 'It gives brachiocephalic artery.', 'It gives left common carotid & left subclavian artery.', 'Its branches leave thorax through superior aperture.'] KEY 1
The arch of the aorta belongs to the superior mediastinum, not the middle mediastinum. It continues from the ascending aorta and gives the brachiocephalic, left common carotid, and left subclavian arteries.

IAE-1-SRC-0167 As regards descending aorta, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It runs in superior & posterior mediastinum.', 'It is continuation of ascending aorta.', 'It gives brachiocephalic artery.', 'It passes through diaphragm to enter the abdomen.', 'It lies within the pericardium.'] KEY 3
The descending thoracic aorta passes through the aortic hiatus of the diaphragm to become the abdominal aorta. It is not within the pericardium and does not give the brachiocephalic artery.

IAE-1-SRC-0168 As regards the trachea, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is 10-20 cm long.', 'It is a completely muscular tube.', 'It passes through superior thoracic aperture.', 'It descends in posterior mediastinum.', 'It begins at level of sternal angle.'] KEY 2
The trachea begins at the level of C6, enters the thorax through the superior thoracic aperture, and descends to bifurcate near the sternal angle.

IAE-1-SRC-0169 In superior mediastinum, the structures have the following arrangement from anterior to posterior:
['Arch of aorta, trachea & esophagus', 'Trachea, arch of aorta & esophagus', 'Trachea, esophagus & arch of aorta', 'Arch of aorta, esophagus & trachea', 'Esophagus, trachea & arch of aorta'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Arch of aorta, trachea & esophagus. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0170 As regards esophagus, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is 25 cm long.', 'It runs in middle mediastinum.', 'It descends behind trachea.', 'It ends in stomach.', 'It passes in diaphragm at level of T10.'] KEY 1
The esophagus runs mainly in the posterior mediastinum after beginning in the neck, so describing it as a middle-mediastinal structure is the incorrect statement.

IAE-1-SRC-0171 As regards the thoracic duct, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It ascends through esophageal opening of diaphragm.', 'It ascends through middle & superior mediastinum.', 'It ascends behind esophagus.', 'It ends in thorax.', 'It ends into right brachiocephalic vein.'] KEY 2
The thoracic duct ascends in the posterior mediastinum behind the esophagus before entering the root of the neck. It does not end inside the thorax or drain into the right brachiocephalic vein.

IAE-1-SRC-0172 As regards the thoracic duct, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It ascends through aortic opening of diaphragm.', 'It ascends through posterior & superior mediastinum.', 'It ascends through superior thoracic aperture.', 'It ends in neck.', 'It ends into right brachiocephalic vein.'] KEY 4
The thoracic duct usually ends at the left venous angle in the root of the neck, so drainage into the right brachiocephalic vein is the incorrect statement.

IAE-1-SRC-0173 As regards the vagi, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['They descend from neck to thorax.', 'They give parasympathetic supply to bronchi & heart.', 'They pass through aortic opening of diaphragm.', 'The left vagus gives left recurrent laryngeal nerve.', 'They end in abdomen.'] KEY 2
The vagus nerves pass through the diaphragm with the esophagus via the esophageal hiatus, not through the aortic hiatus.

IAE-1-SRC-0174 The mediastinum contains all the following structures EXCEPT:
['Right vagus', 'Left vagus', 'Left recurrent laryngeal nerve', 'Right recurrent laryngeal nerve', 'Right phrenic nerve'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Right recurrent laryngeal nerve. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0175 As regards the phrenic nerves, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['They begin in neck.', 'They pass through superior thoracic aperture.', 'They descend in superior & posterior mediastinum.', 'They supply diaphragm.', 'They descend on sides of mediastinum.'] KEY 2
The phrenic nerves descend through the neck and superior mediastinum on either side of the pericardium; they are not posterior-mediastinal nerves.

IAE-1-SRC-0176 Match each structure with its containing mediastinum:
 KEY
Each structure is matched to its standard anatomical relation or compartment.

IAE-1-SRC-0177 Match each structure with its mediastinal course:
 KEY
Each structure is matched to its standard anatomical relation or compartment.

IAE-1-SRC-0178 As regards the pericardium, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is a fibroserous sac.', 'It encloses roots of great vessels.', 'It has one fibrous layer.', 'Its serous part is formed of 2 layers.', 'It has a cavity filled with fluid & it lies between fibrous & visceral pericardium.'] KEY 4
The pericardial cavity contains a thin film of serous fluid and lies between the parietal and visceral layers of the serous pericardium; it is not between the fibrous and visceral pericardium.

IAE-1-SRC-0179 As regards the heart, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its apex is directed to the left.', 'Its base is anterior.', 'Its sternocostal surface is posterior.', 'Its diaphragmatic surface is to the right.', 'Its base is formed mainly by right atrium.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Its apex is directed to the left. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0180 As regards the heart, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Its apex is formed by left ventricle.', 'Its base is formed by 2 atria.', 'Its sternocostal surface is formed mainly by left ventricle.', 'Its diaphragmatic surface is inferior.', 'Atria in sternocostal surface are hidden behind ascending aorta & pulmonary artery.'] KEY 2
The sternocostal surface of the heart is formed mainly by the right ventricle. The left ventricle contributes much of the left border and apex instead.

IAE-1-SRC-0181 Match each cardiac border with the chamber(s) forming it:
 KEY
Each structure is matched to its standard anatomical relation or compartment.

IAE-1-SRC-0182 As regards the coronary circulation, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Heart is supplied by 2 coronary arteries.', 'Coronary arteries arise from aortic arch.', 'Right coronary artery gives marginal & anterior interventricular arteries.', 'Left coronary artery gives circumflex & posterior interventricular arteries.', 'Coronary sinus opens in left atrium.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Heart is supplied by 2 coronary arteries. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0183 Posterior interventricular artery is a branch of:
['Left coronary artery', 'Right coronary artery', 'Ascending aorta', 'Aortic arch', 'Circumflex artery'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Right coronary artery. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0184 As regards lung & pleura, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Pleural space lies between visceral & parietal pleura.', 'Pleura is a fibrous sac.', 'Pleura is invaginated by lung from medial surface.', 'Each lung has the shape of half a cone.', 'Each lung has an apex & a base.'] KEY 1
The pleura is a serous sac with parietal and visceral layers, separated by a potential pleural cavity. It is not a fibrous sac.

IAE-1-SRC-0185 Left lung:
['Is shorter than right lung', 'Has 2 fissures', 'Has 3 lobes', 'Has a cardiac notch', 'Is broader than right lung'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Has a cardiac notch. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0186 As regards right lung, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is shorter than left lung.', 'It has a lingula.', 'It has 2 fissures.', 'It is formed of 3 lobes.', 'It is broader than left lung.'] KEY 1
The right lung has three lobes and two fissures, but it does not have a lingula. The lingula is a feature of the left upper lobe.

IAE-1-SRC-0187 The structure that supplies the lung tissue with oxygenated blood is:
['Bronchi', 'Pulmonary artery', 'Pulmonary vein', 'Bronchial veins', 'Bronchial arteries'] KEY 4
The correct answer is Bronchial arteries. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0188 Pulmonary circulation includes the flow of blood from:
['Left ventricle through aorta to body.', 'Right ventricle through pulmonary trunk to lungs.', 'Left atrium through left ventricle to aorta.', 'Left ventricle through aorta to lungs.', 'Right atrium through right ventricle to aorta.'] KEY 1
Pulmonary circulation begins at the right ventricle and travels through the pulmonary trunk to the lungs.

IAE-1-SRC-0189 An example of a functional end artery:
['Coronary artery.', 'Mesenteric artery.', 'Cerebral artery.', 'Central retinal artery.', 'Renal artery.'] KEY 0
Coronary arteries have anastomoses that are usually insufficient during sudden occlusion.

IAE-1-SRC-0190 The joints between the parts of the sternum are:
['Synovial plane.', 'Synovial hinge.', 'Primary cartilaginous.', 'Secondary cartilaginous.', 'Fibrous.'] KEY 3
The manubriosternal joint is a secondary cartilaginous symphysis.

IAE-1-SRC-0191 Which part of the thoracic spinal nerve contains preganglionic sympathetic fibers?
['Dorsal root.', 'Dorsal root ganglion.', 'Ventral root.', 'Dorsal ramus.', 'Grey ramus communicans.'] KEY 2
Preganglionic sympathetic fibers leave the spinal cord through ventral roots.

IAE-1-SRC-0192 Axial skeleton includes:
['Femur.', 'Tibia.', 'Sternum.', 'Clavicle.', 'Hip bone.'] KEY 2
The sternum belongs to the axial skeleton.

IAE-1-SRC-0193 An example of axial bone is:
['Hip bone.', 'Humerus.', 'Femur.', 'Metacarpal bone.', 'Sternum.'] KEY 4
The sternum belongs to the axial skeleton.

IAE-1-SRC-0194 Pleurae are serous membranes of the:
['Abdominal cavity', 'Lungs', 'Heart', 'Diaphragm', 'Brain'] KEY 1
Pleurae are the serous membranes associated with the lungs.

IAE-1-SRC-0195 Regarding the Base of the heart, one is correct:
['Forms the anterior surface of the heart.', 'Formed mainly by the left atrium and a small part by the right atrium.', 'Formed by the left ventricle.', 'Directed downwards, forwards and to the left.', 'Divided by the coronary groove into atrial portion above, and a ventricular portion below.'] KEY 1
The base of the heart is formed mainly by the left atrium, with a small contribution from the right atrium.

IAE-1-SRC-0196 Regarding the apex of the heart, one is correct:
['Forms the anterior surface of the heart.', 'Formed mainly by the left atrium.', 'Formed by the right ventricle.', 'Directed downwards, forwards and to the right.', 'Lies in the left 5th intercostal space, 3.5 inches from the median plane.'] KEY 4
The apex beat is found in the left 5th intercostal space about 3.5 inches from the median plane.

IAE-1-SRC-0197 Regarding the sternocostal surface of the heart, one is correct:
['Divided by the coronary groove into atrial portion above, and a ventricular portion below.', 'Formed mainly by the left atrium.', 'Directed posteriorly.', 'It is named diaphragmatic surface.', 'It is divided equally into right and left halves.'] KEY 0
The coronary groove separates the atrial portion above from the ventricular portion below on the sternocostal surface.

IAE-1-SRC-0198 Regarding the borders of the heart, one is correct:
['There are five borders.', 'Inferior border formed by left ventricle (mainly).', 'Left border formed by right ventricle (mainly).', 'Superior border formed by right atrium (mainly).', 'Right border formed by the right atrium.'] KEY 4
The right border of the heart is formed mainly by the right atrium.

IAE-1-SRC-0199 Regarding the chambers of the heart, one is correct:
['The Right Atrium receives oxygenated blood by the way of the superior and inferior vena cava.', 'From the Right Atrium, the blood passes through the right atrioventricular orifice into the Right Ventricle.', 'Contraction of Right Ventricle forces the blood to pass through the aorta to the lungs.', 'The right atrioventricular orifice is guarded by mitral valve.', 'The orifice of pulmonary artery is guarded by tricuspid valve.'] KEY 1
Blood flows from the right atrium through the right atrioventricular orifice into the right ventricle.

IAE-1-SRC-0200 Which of the following arteries is a branch from the arch of the aorta?
['The right common carotid artery.', 'The right subclavian artery.', 'The Left Bronchial artery.', 'The Posterior intercostal artery.', 'The left subclavian artery.'] KEY 4
The left subclavian artery is a direct branch of the arch of the aorta.

IAE-1-SRC-0201 Which of the following arteries is a branch from the ascending aorta?
['The right common carotid artery.', 'The right subclavian artery.', 'The Left Bronchial artery.', 'The left coronary artery.', 'The Posterior intercostal artery.'] KEY 3
The coronary arteries arise from the ascending aorta at the aortic sinuses.

IAE-1-SRC-0202 Which of the following veins ends in the right atrium?
['The Superior vena cava.', 'The Four pulmonary veins.', 'The brachiocephalic veins.', 'The Internal jugular vein', 'The subclavian vein.'] KEY 0
The superior vena cava opens directly into the right atrium.

IAE-1-SRC-0203 Normally, the esophagus shows constrictions at the following sites, except:
['At its beginning.', 'Where it is crossed by the arch of aorta.', 'Where it is crossed by the left bronchus.', 'Where it is crossed by the thoracic duct.', 'As it pierces the diaphragm.'] KEY 3
The thoracic duct does not form one of the normal oesophageal constrictions.

IAE-1-SRC-0204 Regarding the trachea one of the following statements is true:
['It starts as a continuation of larynx at the level of C6.', 'It is separated from the vertebral Column it by the pharynx.', 'It is related anteriorly to the oesophagus.', 'It is composed of four incomplete C shaped rings of hyaline cartilage.', 'It is related laterally, in its upper part to the lungs.'] KEY 0
The trachea begins as a continuation of the larynx at the level of C6.

IAE-1-SRC-0205 Regarding the bronchi one of the following statements is true:
['Right bronchus is longer than Left bronchus.', 'Left bronchus is wider than Right bronchus.', 'Right bronchus divides into 3 bronchi', 'Left bronchus is more vertical nearly in line with trachea.', 'Left bronchus divides into 3 bronchi.'] KEY 2
The right main bronchus divides into three lobar bronchi for the three lobes of the right lung.

IAE-1-SRC-0206 Thoracic cage is formed of ___ anterior and ___ in each side.
 KEY
The sternum forms the anterior part of the thoracic cage and ribs lie on each side.

IAE-1-SRC-0207 The true ribs are ___ in number while the floating ribs are ___ in number.
 KEY
There are 7 pairs of true ribs and 2 pairs of floating ribs.

IAE-1-SRC-0208 The esophagus is about ___ cm in length and show ___ constrictions.
 KEY
The oesophagus is about 25 cm long and has four normal constrictions.

IAE-1-SRC-0209 Trachea starts at the level of ___ and ends at the level of ___.
 KEY
The trachea begins at C6 and bifurcates around the T4 level.

IAE-1-SRC-0210 The ___ bronchus is shorter than the ___ bronchus.
 KEY
The right main bronchus is shorter, wider, and more vertical than the left.

IAE-1-SRC-0211 The right lung divides into ___ lobes while the left lung divides into ___ lobes.
 KEY
The right lung has three lobes and the left lung has two.

IAE-1-SRC-0233 As regards the diaphragm, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It takes origin from lower 8 costal cartilages.', 'Its origin includes 5 crura & 2 arcuate ligaments.', 'Phrenic nerve supplies its upper surface.', 'During inspiration, it descends down.', 'It takes origin from superior thoracic aperture.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because contraction of the diaphragm flattens and lowers its domes, so it descends during inspiration and thereby increases the vertical diameter of the thorax. Option (a) is wrong because it arises from the lower 6 costal cartilages, and (b) is wrong because its origin includes 2 crura and 5 arcuate ligaments. Option (c) is wrong because the phrenic nerve supplies its inferior surface, and (e) is wrong because it arises from the circumference of the inferior thoracic aperture.

IAE-1-SRC-0234 Esophageal opening of diaphragm:
['Lies at level of T10.', 'Lies within left crus of diaphragm.', 'Its diameter is not affected by contraction of diaphragm.', 'It lies 1 inch to the right of midline.', 'Lies within central tendon.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the oesophageal opening lies at the level of the 10th thoracic vertebra, giving the useful mnemonic that the oesophagus passes at T10. Option (b) is wrong because it lies within the fibers of the right crus, and (c) is wrong because those fibers act as a physiological sphincter that narrows the opening on contraction. Option (d) is wrong because it lies about an inch to the left of the midline, and (e) is wrong because the caval opening, not the oesophageal, lies in the central tendon.

IAE-1-SRC-0235 Aortic opening of diaphragm lies:
['At level of T12, one inch to left of midline.', 'At level of T10, one inch to left of midline.', 'At level of T12 in midline.', 'At level of T8 in midline.', 'At level of T12, one inch to right of midline.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the aortic opening lies in the midline at the level of the 12th thoracic vertebra, behind the median arcuate ligament. It is the oesophageal opening at T10 that is displaced to the left of the midline (b), and the caval opening lies at T8 (d) to the right. Options (a) and (e) give the correct vertebral level but wrongly displace the opening from the midline.

IAE-1-SRC-0236 Aortic opening of the diaphragm:
['Dilates during inspiration to increase venous return.', 'Lies behind median arcuate ligament.', 'Lies at level of T10.', 'Is constricted during diaphragm contraction.', 'Prevents gastric reflux during inspiration.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the aortic opening is an osseoaponeurotic hiatus lying behind the median arcuate ligament, which arches between the two crura. Because the aorta passes behind rather than through muscle fibers, its diameter is unaffected by diaphragmatic contraction, which makes (d) wrong. Option (a) describes the caval opening, (c) gives the level of the oesophageal opening, and (e) describes the sphincteric action at the oesophageal opening.

IAE-1-SRC-0409 As regards the breast, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is a modified skin gland.', 'It extends between 3rd rib to 6th rib.', 'It drains its lymph into axillary lymph nodes.', 'Cancer of one side can cross to opposite side.', 'The areola surrounds the nipple.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the breast extends vertically from the second to the sixth rib. The remaining statements are true, since the breast is a modified sweat gland of the skin (a), drains mainly to the axillary nodes (c), can spread across the midline through cutaneous lymphatics (d), and has a pigmented areola surrounding the nipple (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0458 All are true about breast except:
['Both males and females have breast.', 'The dark pigmented area is named areola.', 'The tail is considered as content of axilla.', 'The lower boundary (Extent) of breast is 9th rib.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the breast extends vertically from the 2nd to the 6th rib, not to the 9th. The other statements are true: the breast is present in both sexes though rudimentary in the male, the pigmented skin around the nipple is the areola, and the axillary tail of Spence passes through the deep fascia to lie in the axilla.

IAE-1-SRC-0212 The deepest layer in the anterior abdominal wall is:
['Internal oblique muscle.', 'Transversus abdominis muscle.', 'Fascia transversalis.', 'Peritoneum.', 'Extraperitoneal fat.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the parietal peritoneum is the innermost, and therefore deepest, layer of the anterior abdominal wall, lining the abdominal cavity itself. Working inwards the order is skin, Camper's fascia, Scarpa's fascia, the three muscle layers, fascia transversalis, extraperitoneal fat and finally peritoneum. Extraperitoneal fat (e) and fascia transversalis (c) are therefore both superficial to the peritoneum.

IAE-1-SRC-0213 The anterior abdominal wall has the following arrangement from superficial to deep:
["External oblique, Scarpa's fascia & Camper's fascia.", 'Transversus abdominis, Fascia transversalis & parietal peritoneum.', 'Fascia transversalis, transversus abdominis & internal oblique.', "Scarpa's fascia, transversus abdominis & internal oblique.", 'Parietal peritoneum, fascia transversalis & transversus abdominis.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because transversus abdominis is the deepest of the three flat muscles, and it is followed inwards by fascia transversalis and then the parietal peritoneum. Option (a) is reversed because the fascial layers lie superficial to external oblique, and (c) and (e) list the layers from deep to superficial. Option (d) is wrong because internal oblique lies superficial to transversus abdominis, not deep to it.

IAE-1-SRC-0214 The structure that lies deep to transversus abdominis muscle is:
['Parietal peritoneum.', 'Extraperitoneal fat.', 'Fascia transversalis.', "Scarpa's fascia.", "Camper's fascia."] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because fascia transversalis is the layer immediately deep to transversus abdominis. Extraperitoneal fat (b) and parietal peritoneum (a) lie deeper still, so they are not the structure immediately deep to the muscle. Camper's and Scarpa's fasciae (d, e) are components of the superficial fascia and lie far superficial to the muscles.

IAE-1-SRC-0215 As regards the external oblique muscle, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its fibers are replaced laterally by an aponeurosis.', 'Its upper border forms the inguinal ligament.', 'Its fibers are directed downwards & backwards.', 'It is attached to linea alba.', 'It is attached to costal cartilages.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the aponeurosis of external oblique reaches and is attached to the linea alba in the midline. Option (a) is wrong because it is the medial part of the muscle that becomes aponeurotic, and (b) is wrong because it is the lower border that rolls on itself to form the inguinal ligament. Option (c) is wrong because its fibers are directed downwards and forwards, and (e) is wrong because it is attached to ribs rather than to costal cartilages.

IAE-1-SRC-0216 As regards the internal oblique muscle, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It lies deep to external oblique.', 'Its fibers are attached to iliac crest.', 'Its fibers are directed downwards & forwards.', 'It is replaced medially by an aponeurosis.', 'It is attached to linea alba.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because this statement is incorrect: the fibers of internal oblique run upwards and forwards, in the opposite direction to those of external oblique, so that the two muscles cross at right angles. The remaining statements are true, since internal oblique is the middle of the three flat muscles (a), arises in part from the iliac crest (b), becomes aponeurotic medially (d) and reaches the linea alba (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0217 As regards the internal oblique muscle, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its fibers are directed upwards & laterally.', 'Its fibers in a right angle to fibers of external oblique.', 'Its aponeurosis splits to enclose transversus abdominis muscle.', 'Its upper border forms an arch above spermatic cord.', 'Medially, it is formed of fleshy fibers.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the fibers of internal oblique cross those of external oblique at a right angle, which is the basis of the strength of the abdominal wall. Option (a) is wrong because the fibers are directed upwards and medially, and (c) is wrong because the aponeurosis splits to enclose rectus abdominis, not transversus abdominis. Option (d) is wrong because it is the lower border that arches above the spermatic cord, and (e) is wrong because medially the muscle is aponeurotic rather than fleshy.

IAE-1-SRC-0218 As regards the transversus abdominis muscle, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its lower border forms an arch above round ligament of uterus.', 'Its fibers run horizontally & laterally.', 'Its fibers & fibers of external oblique form conjoint tendon.', 'It lies deep to external oblique.', 'It is not attached to linea alba.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the lower border of transversus abdominis arches over the contents of the inguinal canal, which is the round ligament of the uterus in the female and the spermatic cord in the male. Option (b) is wrong because its fibers run horizontally and forwards, and (c) is wrong because the conjoint tendon is formed by the fibers of internal oblique and transversus abdominis. Option (d) is wrong because it lies deep to internal oblique, and (e) is wrong because it does reach the linea alba.

IAE-1-SRC-0219 As regards rectus abdominis muscle, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It lies on either side of linea alba.', 'It is attached above to symphysis pubis.', 'Its upper end is narrow.', 'Its medial border forms linea semilunaris.', 'It is divided by 2 longitudinal intersections.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the two rectus abdominis muscles are strap muscles lying one on each side of the midline linea alba. Option (b) is wrong because it is attached below to the symphysis pubis, and (c) is wrong because its upper end is wide while its lower end is narrow. Option (d) is wrong because the lateral border forms the linea semilunaris, and (e) is wrong because it is divided by three transverse intersections.

IAE-1-SRC-0220 As regards rectus abdominis muscle, choose INCORRECT statement:
['It is enclosed within an aponeurosis.', 'It lies inside the rectus sheath.', 'It is narrow below.', 'Above, it is attached to ribs & sternum.', 'Its lateral border forms linea alba.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because this statement is incorrect: the lateral border of rectus abdominis forms the linea semilunaris, whereas the linea alba is the midline raphe between the two muscles. The remaining statements are true, since the muscle is enclosed in the aponeurotic rectus sheath (a, b), is narrow at its lower end (c), and is attached above to the 5th to 7th costal cartilages and the xiphoid process (d).

IAE-1-SRC-0221 As regards muscles of anterior abdominal wall, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Transversus abdominis is enclosed within rectus sheath.', 'Pyramidalis may be absent.', 'External oblique is attached to lower 4 ribs.', 'Fibers of internal oblique are directed downwards & medially.', 'Rectus abdominis muscle lies on each side of linea semilunaris.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because pyramidalis is a small, inconstant triangular muscle in front of the lower rectus abdominis and is frequently absent. Option (a) is wrong because it is rectus abdominis that lies within the rectus sheath, and (c) is wrong because external oblique arises from the lower 8 ribs. Option (d) is wrong because internal oblique fibers run upwards and medially, and (e) is wrong because rectus abdominis lies on each side of the linea alba.

IAE-1-SRC-0222 Which one of the following muscles has its fibers directed upwards & forwards?
['External oblique.', 'Internal oblique.', 'Transversus abdominis.', 'Rectus abdominis.', 'Pyramidalis.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because internal oblique is the only flat muscle of the abdominal wall whose fibers are directed upwards and forwards. External oblique fibers (a) run in the opposite direction, downwards and forwards, and transversus abdominis fibers (c) run horizontally. Rectus abdominis (d) and pyramidalis (e) are vertical muscles.

IAE-1-SRC-0223 Match each muscle in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
Each muscle of the anterior abdominal wall has a signature feature. The rolled lower border of external oblique forms the inguinal ligament, internal oblique fibers run upwards and medially, and transversus abdominis fibers run horizontally and forwards. Rectus abdominis is crossed by three transverse tendinous intersections, and pyramidalis is the small triangular muscle in front of its lower part.

IAE-1-SRC-0224 As regards rectus sheath, choose INCORRECT statement:
['It encloses rectus abdominis muscle.', 'It is derived from aponeurosis of external oblique, internal oblique & transversus abdominis.', 'Its anterior wall is made by aponeurosis of external oblique & anterior lamella of internal oblique.', 'Its posterior wall is made by aponeurosis of external oblique & posterior lamella of internal oblique.', 'It contains epigastric arteries.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the posterior wall of the rectus sheath is formed by the aponeurosis of transversus abdominis together with the posterior lamella of the internal oblique aponeurosis, not by external oblique. The external oblique aponeurosis contributes only to the anterior wall (c). The remaining statements are true, since the sheath encloses rectus abdominis (a), is formed from all three flat muscle aponeuroses (b) and contains the superior and inferior epigastric arteries (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0225 Rectus sheath encloses all the following EXCEPT:
['Rectus abdominis.', 'Transversus abdominis.', 'Superior epigastric artery.', 'Inferior epigastric artery.', 'Lower intercostal nerves.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because transversus abdominis is one of the muscles whose aponeurosis helps to form the rectus sheath, so it lies outside the sheath rather than within it. The contents of the sheath are rectus abdominis and pyramidalis (a), the superior and inferior epigastric vessels (c, d) and the terminal parts of the lower intercostal nerves (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0226 As regards the inguinal canal, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It lies below the inguinal ligament.', 'Its anterior wall is formed by fascia transversalis.', 'It gives passage to round ligament of uterus.', 'It is 10 cm long.', 'Its roof is formed by inguinal ligament.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the inguinal canal transmits the round ligament of the uterus in the female and the spermatic cord in the male. Option (a) is wrong because the canal lies above the inguinal ligament, and (b) is wrong because fascia transversalis lies in the posterior wall while external oblique forms the anterior wall. Option (d) is wrong because the canal is about 4 cm long, and (e) is wrong because the inguinal ligament forms its floor.

IAE-1-SRC-0227 As regards the inguinal canal, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It gives passage to spermatic cord.', 'It extends between superficial & deep inguinal rings.', 'Its floor is formed by conjoint tendon.', 'Its anterior wall is formed by external oblique.', 'Its posterior wall is formed by fascia transversalis.'] KEY 2
The floor of the inguinal canal is formed by the inguinal ligament. The roof is formed by the arching fibers of internal oblique and transversus abdominis; the conjoint tendon reinforces the medial posterior wall rather than forming the roof.

IAE-1-SRC-0228 Match each wall of inguinal canal in the left column with its forming structure in the right column:
 KEY
The anterior wall of the inguinal canal is formed mainly by the aponeurosis of external oblique, the posterior wall by fascia transversalis, the roof by the arching fibers of internal oblique and transversus abdominis, and the floor by the inguinal ligament.

IAE-1-SRC-0229 Match each description in the left column with its name in the right column:
 KEY
The costal margin is formed by the cartilages of the lower ribs, and the xiphoid process is the small lowest part of the sternum. The linea alba is the vertical midline band formed by the interlacing aponeuroses of the flat abdominal muscles, running from the xiphoid process to the symphysis pubis. The umbilicus is a scar in the linea alba marking the site of the fetal umbilical cord.

IAE-1-SRC-0230 Match each description in the left column with its name in the right column:
 KEY
The linea semilunaris is the curved lateral border of rectus abdominis, and it crosses the costal margin at the tip of the 9th costal cartilage. The iliac crest is the upper border of the ilium running between the anterior and posterior superior iliac spines, while the rolled lower border of external oblique forms the inguinal ligament. The symphysis pubis is the secondary cartilaginous joint uniting the two pubic bones in the midline.

IAE-1-SRC-0231 Match each plane in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
The intercristal plane joins the highest points of the two iliac crests and lies at the level of L4, which is why it is used as a landmark for lumbar puncture. The transpyloric plane passes through the tips of the 9th costal cartilages, and the intertubercular plane joins the tubercles of the iliac crests at the level of L5. The subcostal plane connects the lowest points of the costal margin, at the 10th costal cartilages.

IAE-1-SRC-0232 Transpyloric plane passes through all the following structures EXCEPT:
['Hilum of kidney.', 'Tip of 7th costal cartilage.', 'Duodenojejunal junction.', 'Pylorus of stomach.', 'Neck of pancreas.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the transpyloric plane passes through the tips of the 9th costal cartilages, not the 7th. All the other structures listed do lie in this plane, including the hila of the kidneys (a), the duodenojejunal junction (c), the pylorus of the stomach (d) and the neck of the pancreas (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0237 The muscle attached to all lumbar vertebrae is:
['Psoas major.', 'Psoas minor.', 'Quadratus lumborum.', 'Transversus abdominis.', 'Iliacus.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because psoas major arises from the bodies, transverse processes and intervertebral discs of all five lumbar vertebrae before descending to the lesser trochanter of the femur. Quadratus lumborum (c) is attached to the iliac crest and the last rib, with only limited vertebral attachment. Iliacus (e) arises from the iliac fossa and transversus abdominis (d) from the lumbar fascia.

IAE-1-SRC-0238 Match each muscle in the left column with its appropriate attachment in the right column:
 KEY
Quadratus lumborum is a quadrilateral muscle stretching between the iliac crest below and the last rib above. Transversus abdominis arises posteriorly from the lumbar fascia and is inserted anteriorly into the linea alba. Psoas major runs from all the lumbar vertebrae to the femur, while iliacus arises from the iliac fossa and joins the psoas tendon to reach the femur, the two together forming iliopsoas.

IAE-1-SRC-0239 All the following nerves emerge from lateral side of psoas major EXCEPT:
['Iliohypogastric nerve.', 'Ilioinguinal nerve.', 'Genitofemoral nerve.', 'Lateral cutaneous nerve of thigh.', 'Femoral nerve.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the genitofemoral nerve is the one branch of the lumbar plexus that pierces psoas major and emerges on its anterior surface. The iliohypogastric, ilioinguinal, lateral cutaneous nerve of the thigh and femoral nerve all appear at the lateral border of the muscle. The lumbosacral trunk and obturator nerve, by contrast, emerge from its medial border.

IAE-1-SRC-0240 All the following are branches of lumbar plexus EXCEPT:
['Obturator nerve.', 'Femoral nerve.', 'Genitofemoral nerve.', 'Lateral cutaneous nerve of thigh.', 'Subcostal nerve.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the subcostal nerve is the ventral ramus of the 12th thoracic nerve and therefore belongs to the thoracic nerves, not to the lumbar plexus. The obturator, femoral, genitofemoral and lateral cutaneous nerve of the thigh are all true branches of the lumbar plexus, which is formed from the ventral rami of L1 to L4. The plexus lies within the substance of psoas major.

IAE-1-SRC-0241 Which one of the following emerges from front of psoas major?
['Iliohypogastric nerve.', 'Ilioinguinal nerve.', 'Lateral cutaneous nerve of thigh.', 'Genitofemoral nerve.', 'Femoral nerve.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the genitofemoral nerve pierces the anterior surface of psoas major and descends on it before dividing into its genital and femoral branches. All the other nerves listed emerge at the lateral border of the muscle. This anterior course explains why the nerve is at risk in operations on the posterior abdominal wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0242 Which one of the following emerges from medial side of psoas major?
['Lumbosacral trunk.', 'Ilioinguinal nerve.', 'Lateral cutaneous nerve of thigh.', 'Genitofemoral nerve.', 'Femoral nerve.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the lumbosacral trunk emerges from the medial border of psoas major to descend into the pelvis and join the sacral plexus. The ilioinguinal nerve, lateral cutaneous nerve of the thigh and femoral nerve all emerge from the lateral border. The genitofemoral nerve is exceptional in emerging from the anterior surface of the muscle.

IAE-1-SRC-0243 Nerves of posterior abdominal wall include all the following EXCEPT:
['Subcostal nerve.', 'Lower intercostal nerves.', 'Femoral nerve.', 'Sympathetic chain.', 'Lateral cutaneous nerve of thigh.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the lower intercostal nerves run in the neurovascular plane of the anterior abdominal wall rather than on the posterior abdominal wall. The subcostal nerve (a), the branches of the lumbar plexus such as the femoral and lateral cutaneous nerve of the thigh (c, e), and the lumbar part of the sympathetic chain (d) all lie on the posterior abdominal wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0244 As regards abdominal aorta, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It ends by giving 2 common iliac arteries.', 'It ends at level of L5.', 'It gives a pair of median sacral arteries.', 'It gives a single suprarenal artery.', 'It gives inferior mesenteric artery at level of L4.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the abdominal aorta terminates by dividing into the right and left common iliac arteries. Option (b) is wrong because this bifurcation occurs at the level of L4, and (c) is wrong because there is only one median sacral artery. Option (d) is wrong because the suprarenal arteries are paired, and (e) is wrong because the inferior mesenteric artery arises at the level of L3.

IAE-1-SRC-0245 All the following are single branches of abdominal aorta EXCEPT:
['Coeliac artery.', 'Phrenic artery.', 'Superior mesenteric artery.', 'Inferior mesenteric artery.', 'Median sacral artery.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the phrenic arteries are paired, one passing to each half of the diaphragm. The three ventral visceral branches, namely the coeliac, superior mesenteric and inferior mesenteric arteries, are all single midline vessels, as is the median sacral artery. Paired branches supply the paired structures of the posterior abdominal wall and the retroperitoneum.

IAE-1-SRC-0246 All the following are paired branches of abdominal aorta EXCEPT:
['Common iliac artery.', 'Suprarenal artery.', 'Phrenic artery.', 'Coeliac artery.', 'Renal artery.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the coeliac artery is a single midline ventral branch that arises at the level of L1 and supplies the foregut. The common iliac, suprarenal, phrenic and renal arteries are all paired, arising as right and left vessels. The distinction between single and paired branches follows the midline versus bilateral arrangement of the structures they supply.

IAE-1-SRC-0247 Match each artery in the left column with its level of origin in the right column:
 KEY
The coeliac trunk arises at about L1, the renal arteries at about L2, the inferior mesenteric artery at about L3, and the aorta bifurcates into the common iliac arteries at L4. The gonadal arteries usually arise from the abdominal aorta around L2, typically just inferior to the renal arteries.

IAE-1-SRC-0248 As regards inferior vena cava, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It begins at level of L4.', 'It ascends on left side of aorta.', 'It receives 2 renal veins.', 'It receives 2 gonadal veins.', 'Ascends through esophageal opening of diaphragm.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the inferior vena cava receives both the right and the left renal veins directly, the left being the longer as it crosses in front of the aorta. Option (a) is wrong because the cava begins at the level of L5 by the union of the two common iliac veins, and (b) is wrong because it ascends on the right side of the aorta. Option (d) is wrong because only the right gonadal vein drains into it, the left draining into the left renal vein, and (e) is wrong because it traverses the caval opening in the central tendon.

IAE-1-SRC-0249 Inferior vena cava receives all the following veins EXCEPT:
['Right & left renal veins.', 'Right & left hepatic veins.', 'Right & left suprarenal veins.', 'Right gonadal vein.', 'Right & left common iliac veins.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the inferior vena cava receives the right suprarenal vein only, while the left suprarenal vein drains into the left renal vein. The renal veins (a), the hepatic veins (b), the right gonadal vein (d) and the common iliac veins (e) all drain directly into the cava. The left suprarenal and left gonadal veins share this same indirect route through the left renal vein.

IAE-1-SRC-0250 Left gonadal vein ends in:
['Left suprarenal vein.', 'Left renal vein.', 'Left common iliac vein.', 'Left hepatic vein.', 'Inferior vena cava.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the left gonadal vein ascends to drain at a right angle into the left renal vein, whereas the right gonadal vein drains directly into the inferior vena cava. This asymmetry produces a longer and more obstructed venous route on the left. It is the accepted anatomical explanation for the greater frequency of left-sided varicocele.

IAE-1-SRC-0251 Which of the following is retroperitoneal?
['Liver.', 'Stomach.', 'Duodenum.', 'Omenta.', 'Small intestine.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the duodenum, apart from its very first part, lies behind the peritoneum on the posterior abdominal wall and is therefore retroperitoneal. The liver, stomach, omenta and small intestine are all intraperitoneal, being invested by peritoneum and suspended by peritoneal folds. Retroperitoneal organs are covered by peritoneum on their anterior aspect only.

IAE-1-SRC-0252 Which of the following is intraperitoneal?
['Pancreas.', 'Ascending colon.', 'Kidney.', 'Suprarenal gland.', 'Stomach.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the stomach is completely invested by peritoneum and is suspended by the greater and lesser omenta, which makes it intraperitoneal and mobile. The pancreas, ascending colon, kidney and suprarenal gland are all retroperitoneal and therefore relatively fixed. The peritoneal folds attached to intraperitoneal organs also carry their blood supply.

IAE-1-SRC-0253 All the following are retroperitoneal structures EXCEPT:
['Blood vessels.', 'Ureters.', 'Descending colon.', 'Mesentry.', 'Duodenum.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the mesentery is itself a double-layered peritoneal fold, the structure that suspends the small intestine, and so it cannot be a retroperitoneal structure. The great vessels, the ureters, the descending colon and the duodenum all lie behind the peritoneum on the posterior abdominal wall. Peritoneal folds such as the mesentery belong to the intraperitoneal arrangement.

IAE-1-SRC-0254 All the following are intraperitoneal structures EXCEPT:
['Omenta.', 'Intestine.', 'Suprarenal gland.', 'Liver.', 'Stomach.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the suprarenal gland lies on the upper pole of the kidney in the posterior retroperitoneal layer, behind the peritoneal sac. The omenta, intestine, liver and stomach are all intraperitoneal, being enclosed by peritoneum and connected to the wall by peritoneal folds. This retroperitoneal position is why the suprarenal gland is approached posteriorly in surgery.

IAE-1-SRC-0255 As regards the retroperitoneal organs, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['They lie behind peritoneal sac.', 'They are surrounded all around by peritoneum.', 'They include kidneys which lie in posterior retroperitoneal layer.', 'They include duodenum which lies in anterior retroperitoneal layer.', 'They lie on posterior abdominal wall.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: retroperitoneal organs are covered by peritoneum on their anterior aspect only, and it is intraperitoneal organs that are surrounded on all sides. The remaining statements are true, since these organs lie behind the peritoneal sac (a) on the posterior abdominal wall (e), with the kidneys in the posterior layer (c) and the duodenum in the anterior layer (d).

IAE-1-SRC-0256 Posterior retroperitoneal layer includes all the following EXCEPT:
['Pancreas.', 'Kidneys.', 'Ureters.', 'Aorta.', 'Inferior vena cava.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the pancreas belongs to the anterior retroperitoneal layer, together with the duodenum and the ascending and descending colon. The posterior retroperitoneal layer contains the kidneys, ureters, suprarenal glands and the great vessels lying in front of the vertebral column. The distinction reflects the order in which these structures are encountered from front to back.

IAE-1-SRC-0257 Anterior retroperitoneal layer includes all the following EXCEPT:
['Pancreas.', 'Ascending colon.', 'Duodenum.', 'Descending colon.', 'Ureters.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the ureters descend with the kidneys in the posterior retroperitoneal layer, on the front of psoas major. The anterior retroperitoneal layer consists of the parts of the gut that have become secondarily fixed, namely the duodenum, pancreas, ascending colon and descending colon. Recognising these two layers helps predict what is encountered at successive depths on the posterior abdominal wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0258 As regards the posterior retroperitoneal layer, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It includes the suprarenal glands on the sides.', 'It includes some organs of GIT.', 'It includes abdominal aorta infront of vertebral column.', 'It includes tributaries of inferior vena cava.', 'They form the most posterior layer of organs in abdomen.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the retroperitoneal parts of the gastrointestinal tract, namely the duodenum, pancreas and ascending and descending colon, all lie in the anterior retroperitoneal layer. The posterior layer is made up of urinary and endocrine organs and the great vessels, including the suprarenal glands (a), the aorta in front of the vertebral column (c) and the tributaries of the inferior vena cava (d). It is therefore the most posterior layer of abdominal organs (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0259 As regards the intraperitoneal organs, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its organs invade the peritoneal cavity from the front.', 'They are covered by peritoneum from front only.', 'Its visceral layer lines the abdominal wall.', 'Its parietal layer covers the organs.', 'Visceral & parietal layers are connected by peritoneal folds.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the visceral peritoneum covering the organs becomes continuous with the parietal peritoneum lining the wall through double-layered peritoneal folds such as the mesentery and omenta. Option (a) is wrong because the organs invade the peritoneal cavity from behind, and (b) is wrong because intraperitoneal organs are completely covered. Options (c) and (d) reverse the two layers, since the visceral layer covers the organs and the parietal layer lines the abdominal wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0260 As regards the intraperitoneal organs, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Peritoneal folds are double-layered.', 'Peritoneal folds enclose the blood supply of the organ.', 'Peritoneal folds suspend the stomach.', 'Peritoneal folds surround the ascending colon.', 'Visceral peritoneum covers small intestine.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the ascending colon is a retroperitoneal organ, covered by peritoneum on its anterior aspect only, and is not surrounded by a peritoneal fold. The remaining statements are true, since peritoneal folds are double-layered (a), carry the vessels and nerves of the organ between their layers (b), suspend the stomach as the omenta (c), and the small intestine is invested by visceral peritoneum (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0261 Match each organ in the left column with its related peritoneal fold in the right column:
 KEY
Peritoneal folds are named according to the organ they suspend. The stomach is suspended by the greater and lesser omenta, the small intestine by the mesentery, and the mobile parts of the large intestine by their mesocolons. The spleen and liver are attached by peritoneal ligaments, such as the gastrosplenic and lienorenal ligaments for the spleen and the falciform and coronary ligaments for the liver.

IAE-1-SRC-0262 Right lobe of liver is suspended by:
['Falciform ligament.', 'Left triangular ligament.', 'Coronary ligament.', 'Lesser omentum.', 'Greater omentum.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the coronary ligament attaches the right lobe of the liver to the diaphragm, encircling the bare area. The falciform ligament (a) lies between the right and left lobes, and the left triangular ligament (b) anchors the left lobe. The lesser omentum (d) connects the liver to the stomach, while the greater omentum (e) has no attachment to the liver.

IAE-1-SRC-0263 The peritoneal ligament that lies between right & left lobes of liver is:
['Falciform ligament.', 'Right triangular ligament.', 'Left triangular ligament.', 'Coronary ligament.', 'Lesser omentum.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the falciform ligament is the sickle-shaped double fold of peritoneum that runs on the diaphragmatic surface of the liver and marks the division between the right and left lobes. The triangular ligaments (b, c) anchor the extremities of the lobes to the diaphragm, and the coronary ligament (d) surrounds the bare area. The lesser omentum (e) passes from the liver to the lesser curvature of the stomach.

IAE-1-SRC-0264 The lesser omentum:
['Is attached to greater curvature of stomach.', 'Is attached between liver & stomach.', 'Has 2 anterior layers & 2 posterior layers.', 'Is folded upon itself.', 'Is apron-shaped.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the lesser omentum is the two-layered peritoneal fold that passes from the porta hepatis and fissure for the ligamentum venosum of the liver to the lesser curvature of the stomach. Option (a) is wrong because it attaches to the lesser and not the greater curvature. Options (c), (d) and (e) all describe the greater omentum, which is apron-shaped, folded upon itself and therefore composed of four layers.

IAE-1-SRC-0265 As regards the peritoneal folds of the stomach, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its lesser omentum has 2 anterior layers & 2 posterior layers.', 'Its lesser omentum is attached to its greater curvature.', 'Its lesser omentum is folded upon itself.', 'Its lesser omentum connects stomach with posterior abdominal wall.', 'Its greater omentum is formed of 4 layers.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the greater omentum descends from the greater curvature as a two-layered apron and then folds back on itself, so that it is composed of four layers. Options (a), (c) and (d) wrongly attribute the characters of the greater omentum to the lesser omentum, which is a simple two-layered fold. Option (b) is wrong because the lesser omentum is attached to the lesser curvature.

IAE-1-SRC-0266 As regards the greater omentum, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It connects greater curvature of stomach with post. abdominal wall.', 'Spleen is attached to its right convexity.', 'Lienorenal ligament is continuous with its posterior 2 layers.', 'Gastrosplenic ligament is continuous with its anterior 2 layers.', 'Spleen divides it into gastrosplenic & lienorenal ligaments.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the spleen is attached to the left convexity of the greater omentum, in keeping with its position in the left hypochondrium. The remaining statements are true, since the greater omentum runs from the greater curvature to the posterior abdominal wall (a), and the spleen splits its left extremity into the gastrosplenic ligament from the anterior two layers and the lienorenal ligament from the posterior two layers (c, d, e).

IAE-1-SRC-0267 As regards the peritoneal cavity, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Stomach & omenta divide it into greater & lesser sacs.', 'Its greater sac lies infront of stomach.', 'Its lesser sac lies behind stomach.', 'Both sacs are continuous through epiploic foramen.', 'Epiploic foramen lies behind free border of greater omentum.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because this statement is incorrect: the epiploic foramen of Winslow lies behind the free right border of the lesser omentum, which contains the portal triad. The remaining statements are true, since the stomach and omenta partition the cavity into a greater and a lesser sac (a), the greater sac lies in front of the stomach (b), the lesser sac behind it (c), and the two communicate through the epiploic foramen (d).

IAE-1-SRC-0268 As regards the transverse colon, choose INCORRECT statement:
['It is seen on reflecting greater omentum upwards.', 'Its peritoneal fold is adherent to anterior 2 layers of greater omentum.', 'It lies at level of pancreas.', 'Its mesocolon suspends it to posterior abdominal wall.', 'Its mesocolon is separatable from greater omentum.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the transverse mesocolon is adherent to the posterior two layers of the greater omentum, not the anterior two. The remaining statements are true, since the transverse colon is exposed by reflecting the greater omentum upwards (a), lies at the level of the pancreas (c), is suspended by the transverse mesocolon from the posterior abdominal wall (d), and that mesocolon can be separated surgically from the omentum (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0269 Which one of the following peritoneal folds is located in the left side of the abdomen?
['Mesentry.', 'Mesoappendix.', 'Sigmoid mesocolon.', 'Transverse mesocolon.', 'Falciform ligament.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the sigmoid mesocolon suspends the sigmoid or pelvic colon, which lies in the left iliac fossa and pelvis. The mesoappendix (b) is on the right side with the caecum, and the mesentery (a) and transverse mesocolon (d) are largely midline structures. The falciform ligament (e) lies in the median plane of the upper abdomen.

IAE-1-SRC-0270 Which one of the following organs lie below the subcostal plane?
['Spleen.', 'Stomach.', 'Duodenum.', 'Liver.', 'Small intestine.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the coils of the small intestine occupy the central and lower parts of the abdominal cavity, below the subcostal plane. The spleen, stomach, duodenum and liver all lie above this plane in the upper abdomen, largely protected by the lower thoracic cage. The subcostal plane is therefore a useful divider between the upper and lower abdominal viscera.

IAE-1-SRC-0271 As regards liver, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It lies mainly in left hypochondrium.', 'It is hidden by thoracic cage.', 'It is triangular in shape.', 'Its apex is directed to the right.', 'It lies below subcostal plane.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the liver lies high under the diaphragm and is almost entirely concealed behind the lower ribs and costal cartilages, which is why it is not normally palpable in health. Option (a) is wrong because it lies mainly in the right hypochondrium, and (c) is wrong because it is wedge-shaped. Option (d) is wrong because its apex points to the left, and (e) is wrong because it lies above the subcostal plane.

IAE-1-SRC-0272 The peritoneal fold that is attached between right & left lobes of liver:
['Is called ligamentum venosum.', 'Is attached to visceral surface.', 'Is attached besides porta hepatis.', 'Is sickle-shaped.', 'Its lies beside bare area.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the fold between the right and left lobes is the falciform ligament, whose name means sickle-shaped. Option (a) is wrong because the ligamentum venosum is the fibrous remnant of the ductus venosus and not a peritoneal fold, and (b) and (c) are wrong because the falciform ligament is attached to the diaphragmatic and not the visceral surface. Option (e) is wrong because the bare area is encircled by the coronary ligament.

IAE-1-SRC-0273 The visceral aspect of the liver shows all the following EXCEPT:
['Groove for inferior vena cava.', 'Fissure for ligamentum teres.', 'Attachment of falciform ligament.', 'Groove for gall bladder.', 'Fissure for ligamentum venosum.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the falciform ligament is attached to the diaphragmatic aspect of the liver, not to its visceral surface. The visceral surface bears the H-shaped set of grooves formed by the groove for the inferior vena cava (a), the fissures for the ligamentum teres and ligamentum venosum (b, e) and the fossa for the gall bladder (d). The horizontal bar of this H is the porta hepatis.

IAE-1-SRC-0274 The area which serves as entrance to & exit from liver is called:
['Caudate lobe.', 'Fissure for ligamentum venosum.', 'Fissure for ligamentum teres.', 'Porta hepatis.', 'Quadrate lobe.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the porta hepatis is the transverse fissure on the visceral surface through which the portal vein and hepatic artery enter and the hepatic ducts leave the liver. The caudate and quadrate lobes (a, e) are areas of liver substance bounded by these grooves, not gateways. The fissures for the ligamentum teres and ligamentum venosum (b, c) contain fibrous remnants of fetal vessels.

IAE-1-SRC-0275 The horizontal link in the H-shaped groove on visceral aspect of liver is formed by:
['Caudate lobe.', 'Fissure for ligamentum venosum.', 'Fissure for ligamentum teres.', 'Porta hepatis.', 'Quadrate lobe.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the porta hepatis forms the transverse limb that links the two vertical limbs of the H-shaped groove. The right vertical limb is formed by the groove for the inferior vena cava and the fossa for the gall bladder, and the left by the fissures for the ligamentum venosum and ligamentum teres. The caudate and quadrate lobes are the areas of liver enclosed within this H.

IAE-1-SRC-0276 The bare area is a part of:
['Right lobe.', 'Left lobe.', 'Porta hepatis.', 'Caudate lobe.', 'Quadrate lobe.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the bare area is the triangular part of the posterior surface of the right lobe that is devoid of peritoneum and rests directly against the diaphragm. It is bounded by the two layers of the coronary ligament, and the inferior vena cava lies in a groove at its left limit. The caudate and quadrate lobes (d, e) are on the visceral surface and are peritonealised.

IAE-1-SRC-0277 As regards the stomach ends, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its cardiac end has a strong anatomical sphincter.', 'It cardiac end lies between stomach & esophagus.', 'Its cardiac end lies within the fibers of left crus of diaphragm.', 'Its pyloric end has a thin sphincter.', 'Its pyloric end lies between stomach & jejunum.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the cardiac end is the junction between the oesophagus and the stomach, situated behind the 7th left costal cartilage. Option (a) is wrong because the cardiac sphincter is physiological rather than anatomical, and (c) is wrong because it lies within the fibers of the right crus of the diaphragm. Option (d) is wrong because the pyloric sphincter is a strong anatomical sphincter, and (e) is wrong because the pyloric end opens into the duodenum.

IAE-1-SRC-0278 Gastrosplenic ligament is attached to:
['Cardiac end of stomach.', 'Pyloric end of stomach.', 'Lesser curvature of stomach.', 'Greater curvature of stomach.', 'Right crus of diaphragm.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the gastrosplenic ligament passes from the greater curvature of the stomach to the hilum of the spleen, being derived from the anterior two layers of the greater omentum. The lesser curvature (c) gives attachment instead to the lesser omentum. The ligament transmits the short gastric and left gastroepiploic vessels.

IAE-1-SRC-0279 As regards the stomach, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its fundus lies below the level of the cardiac end.', 'Its pyloric antrum is funnel-shaped.', 'Its pyloric canal lies between fundus & body.', 'Its body lies after its imaginary line between notch on lesser curvature & bulge of greater curvature opposite it.', 'Its body usually contains an air bubble.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the pyloric antrum is the funnel-shaped part of the pyloric portion of the stomach, leading from the body to the narrow pyloric canal. Option (a) is wrong because the fundus rises above the level of the cardiac orifice, and (c) is wrong because the pyloric canal lies distal to the body. Option (d) is wrong because the body lies before that imaginary line, and (e) is wrong because it is the fundus that usually contains the gastric air bubble.

IAE-1-SRC-0280 Large intestine includes all the following parts EXCEPT:
['Caecum.', 'Splenic flexure.', 'Appendix.', 'Rectum.', 'Duodenum.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the duodenum is the first part of the small intestine, not of the large intestine. The large intestine extends from the caecum with its appendix, through the ascending colon, hepatic flexure, transverse colon, splenic flexure, descending colon and sigmoid colon, to the rectum and anal canal. Its distinguishing features are the taeniae coli, sacculations and appendices epiploicae.

IAE-1-SRC-0281 Which order of arrangement of large intestine is CORRECT?
['Hepatic flexure - transverse colon - ascending colon - descending colon.', 'Caecum - ascending colon - descending colon - transverse colon.', 'Caecum - hepatic flexure - sigmoid colon - rectum.', 'Splenic flexure - transverse colon - ascending colon - sigmoid colon.', 'Sigmoid colon - splenic flexure - hepatic flexure - caecum.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the proper sequence is caecum, ascending colon, hepatic flexure, transverse colon, splenic flexure, descending colon, sigmoid colon, rectum and anal canal, and option (c) lists four of these in their true relative order. Options (a), (b) and (d) place the ascending or transverse colon out of sequence, and (e) reverses the whole course. Tracing the gut in order from the ileocaecal junction distally is the safest way to answer such questions.

IAE-1-SRC-0282 Which of the following is a mobile organ?
['Ascending colon.', 'Descending colon.', 'Hepatic flexure.', 'Splenic flexure.', 'Pelvic colon.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the pelvic or sigmoid colon is suspended by the sigmoid mesocolon and is therefore intraperitoneal and mobile. The ascending and descending colon and the two colic flexures are secondarily retroperitoneal and thus fixed to the posterior abdominal wall. This mobility explains why the sigmoid colon is the commonest site of colonic volvulus.

IAE-1-SRC-0283 As regards large intestine, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Its first part is caecum.', 'It is 1 1/2 meters long.', 'It surrounds coils of small intestine.', 'Its walls has 2 long bands of taenia coli.', 'Its walls show appendices epiploicae.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the wall of the large intestine carries three longitudinal bands of taeniae coli, formed by the condensed outer longitudinal muscle. The remaining statements are true, since it begins as the caecum (a), measures about one and a half metres (b), frames the coils of small intestine (c) and bears fatty appendices epiploicae (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0284 As regards the duodenum, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is formed of 3 parts.', 'It is an intraperitoneal organ.', 'Its 2nd part is 2 inches long.', 'It surrounds head of pancreas.', 'It is 10 cm long.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the duodenum is a C-shaped tube whose concavity clasps the head of the pancreas. Option (a) is wrong because it is formed of four parts, and (b) is wrong because it is retroperitoneal apart from its first part. Option (c) is wrong because its second part is about 3 inches long, and (e) is wrong because the whole duodenum measures about 25 cm, that is 10 inches.

IAE-1-SRC-0285 As regards the pancreas, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It extends from concavity of duodenum to hilum of spleen.', 'It is an intraperitoneal organ.', 'It lies between L2 & L4.', 'Its neck shows an uncinate process.', 'Its parts include: head, body & tail.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the pancreas stretches obliquely across the posterior abdominal wall from the concavity of the duodenum on the right to the hilum of the spleen on the left. Option (b) is wrong because it is a retroperitoneal organ, and (c) is wrong because it lies between L1 and L3. Option (d) is wrong because the uncinate process is a part of the head, and (e) is wrong because its parts are head, neck, body and tail.

IAE-1-SRC-0286 As regards the kidney, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It rests on posterior abdominal wall.', 'Its upper pole lies at level of T12.', 'Its lower pole lies at level of L3.', 'Its hilum lies at level of L2.', 'It is a retroperitoneal organ.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the renal hilum lies at the level of L1, which is also the level of the transpyloric plane. The remaining statements are true, since the kidney rests on the posterior abdominal wall (a), extends from about T12 above to L3 below (b, c) and lies behind the peritoneum (e). The transpyloric plane is therefore a reliable surface marker for the renal hila.

IAE-1-SRC-0287 Regarding to abdominal organs all the following organs are retroperitonal except:
['Dudenum.', 'Ascending colon.', 'Spleen.', 'Pancrease.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the spleen is completely intraperitoneal, suspended by the gastrosplenic and splenorenal ligaments. The duodenum apart from its first inch, the ascending colon and the pancreas apart from its tail are all retroperitoneal structures on the posterior abdominal wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0288 Subcostal plane opposite to all the following levels except:
['10th costal cartilage.', '3rd lumber vertebrae.', 'Costal margin.', '5th lumbar vertebrae.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the subcostal plane lies at the level of the third lumbar vertebra, not the fifth. It passes through the lowest point of the costal margin, formed by the tenth costal cartilage on each side, which is exactly what options (a), (b) and (c) describe.

IAE-1-SRC-0289 Neuro vascular plane of the ant abdominal wall located between which of the following pairs?
['Ext. oblique, Int oblique.', 'Int oblique, rectus abdominis muscles.', 'Int oblique, transversus abdominis.', 'Transversus abdominis, fascia transversalis.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the lower intercostal and subcostal nerves and vessels run forwards in the plane between the internal oblique and the transversus abdominis, which is the abdominal continuation of the thoracic neurovascular plane. This is the plane used for a transversus abdominis plane block.

IAE-1-SRC-0290 All are true about duodenum except:
['It is a retro peritoneal in position.', 'Its concavity related to pancrease.', 'Its vertical part measures 2 inches.', 'The 3rd part opposite to 3rd lumbar vertebrae.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the vertical (second, descending) part of the duodenum measures three inches, not two; the classical lengths are 2, 3, 4 and 1 inches for the four parts. The remaining statements are true, since the duodenum apart from its first inch is retroperitoneal, its concavity embraces the head of the pancreas, and the third part crosses in front of the third lumbar vertebra.

IAE-1-SRC-0291 Liver occupies all the following areas except:
['Right hypochondrium.', 'Hypogastrium.', 'Left hypochondrium.', 'Epigastrium.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the liver never reaches the hypogastrium, the lowest median region of the abdomen. It occupies the right hypochondrium and epigastrium and extends into the left hypochondrium as far as the left lateral line.

IAE-1-SRC-0292 Bare area of liver located on which surface?
['Right surface.', 'Ant surface.', 'Inf surface.', 'Post Surface.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the bare area lies on the posterior surface of the right lobe, where the liver is in direct contact with the diaphragm without any intervening peritoneum, bounded by the reflections of the coronary ligament. The anterior, right and inferior surfaces are all covered by peritoneum apart from the fossae for the gallbladder and porta hepatis.

IAE-1-SRC-0293 All the following structures are completely intra peritoneal except:
['Appendix.', 'Jejunum.', 'Descending colon.', 'Spleen.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the descending colon is retroperitoneal, lying on the posterior abdominal wall with peritoneum covering only its anterior and lateral surfaces. The appendix has a mesoappendix, the jejunum a mesentery, and the spleen its own peritoneal ligaments, so all three are completely intraperitoneal.

IAE-1-SRC-0294 Which of the following peritoneal folds are attached to lesser curvature of the stomach?
['Lieno renal ligament.', 'Lesser omentum.', 'Gastro splenic lig.', 'Greater omentum.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the lesser omentum passes from the lesser curvature of the stomach and the first inch of the duodenum to the porta hepatis and the fissure for the ligamentum venosum. The greater omentum and the gastrosplenic ligament are attached to the greater curvature, while the splenorenal ligament runs from the spleen to the posterior abdominal wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0295 Abdominal part of I.V.C begins at which level?
['L5.', 'L4.', 'T10.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the inferior vena cava is formed at the level of the fifth lumbar vertebra by the union of the two common iliac veins, a little below and to the right of the aortic bifurcation. It then ascends to pierce the diaphragm at T8.

IAE-1-SRC-0296 All of the following muscles shares in the post abdominal wall except:
['Psoas Major.', 'Quadratus lamborum.', 'Internal oblique.', 'Iliacus.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the internal oblique is one of the three flat muscles of the anterolateral abdominal wall, not of the posterior wall. The posterior abdominal wall is formed by psoas major, quadratus lumborum and iliacus, together with the diaphragm above.

IAE-1-SRC-0297 All of the following branches from abdominal aorta at level of (L1) except:
['S.M.A.', 'Gonadal artery.', 'Coeliac trunk.', 'Suprarenal artery.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the gonadal (testicular or ovarian) artery arises lower down, at the level of the second lumbar vertebra. The coeliac trunk arises at the T12/L1 level and the superior mesenteric and middle suprarenal arteries at L1.

IAE-1-SRC-0298 Which of the following nerves related to medial border of psoas major?
['Ilio inguinal nerve.', 'Obterator nerve.', 'Genitofemoral nerve.', 'Lat. Cutenous N of thigh.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the obturator nerve emerges from the medial border of psoas major to run along the lateral pelvic wall towards the obturator canal. The genitofemoral nerve pierces the anterior surface of the muscle, while the iliohypogastric, ilioinguinal and lateral cutaneous nerve of the thigh appear at its lateral border.

IAE-1-SRC-0299 All are true about inguinal canal except:
['Its superficial ring in ext. oblique aponeurosis.', 'The roof is by fascia transversalis.', 'The floor is formed by inguinal ligament.', 'The ant wall is by ext. oblique.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the roof of the inguinal canal is formed by the arching lowest fibers of internal oblique and transversus abdominis; the fascia transversalis forms the posterior wall, not the roof. The remaining statements are true, since the superficial ring is a defect in the external oblique aponeurosis, the floor is the grooved upper surface of the inguinal ligament, and the anterior wall is the external oblique aponeurosis throughout.

IAE-1-SRC-0300 The neurovascular plane of the anterior abdominal wall lies between:
['Superficial fascia and external oblique muscle', 'External oblique and internal oblique muscles', 'Internal oblique and transversus abdominis muscles', 'Transversus abdominis muscle and fascia transversalis'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Internal oblique and transversus abdominis muscles. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0301 The inguinal ligament is the lower border of which muscle?
['External oblique', 'Internal oblique', 'Transversus abdominis', 'Rectus abdominis'] KEY 0
The correct answer is External oblique. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0302 The conjoint tendon is formed by the lower borders of which muscles?
['External oblique and internal oblique', 'Internal oblique and transversus abdominis', 'Transversus abdominis and pyramidalis', 'None of the above'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Internal oblique and transversus abdominis. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0303 A cut wound reaching the rectus sheath will not affect:
['Superior epigastric vessels', 'Inferior epigastric vessels', 'Upper 6 intercostal nerves', 'Lower 6 intercostal nerves'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Upper 6 intercostal nerves. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0304 The deep inguinal ring is an opening in:
['Superficial fascia', 'External oblique muscle', 'Transversus abdominis muscle', 'Fascia transversalis'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Fascia transversalis. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0305 A stab wound at the transpyloric plane will NOT affect the:
['Pylorus of the stomach', 'Beginning of the jejunum', 'Right colic flexure', 'Neck of pancreas'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Right colic flexure. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0306 The highest points on the iliac crests lie at the level of which vertebra?
['L1', 'L3', 'L4', 'L5'] KEY 2
The correct answer is L4. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0307 A muscle which may be absent is:
['Iliacus', 'Psoas major', 'Psoas minor', 'Quadratus lumborum'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Psoas minor. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0308 All the following nerves lie lateral to the psoas major muscle EXCEPT:
['Iliohypogastric', 'Ilioinguinal', 'Obturator', 'Femoral'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Obturator. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0309 The abdominal aorta ends at the lower border of which vertebra?
['L2', 'L4', 'S2', 'S4'] KEY 1
The correct answer is L4. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0310 The inferior vena cava receives all the following tributaries EXCEPT the:
['Right renal vein', 'Left renal vein', 'Right gonadal vein', 'Left gonadal vein'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Left gonadal vein. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0311 Which of the following organs is retroperitoneal?
['Stomach', 'Pancreas', 'Liver', 'Small intestine'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Pancreas. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0312 The peritoneal fold of the small intestine is called:
['Omentum', 'Mesentery', 'Ligament', 'Mesocolon'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Mesentery. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0313 The structure that suspends the lesser curvature of the stomach to the liver is the:
['Lesser omentum', 'Greater omentum', 'Falciform ligament', 'Coronary ligament'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Lesser omentum. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0314 The gall bladder is attached to which surface of the liver?
['Anterior', 'Posterior', 'Superior', 'Inferior'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Inferior. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0315 All the following are true about the stomach EXCEPT:
['It has fundus, body and pylorus', 'Both cardiac end and pyloric end have thick muscular sphincter', 'Lesser curvature gives attachment to lesser omentum', 'Greater curvature gives attachment to greater omentum'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Both cardiac end and pyloric end have thick muscular sphincter. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0316 The vermiform appendix is attached to:
['Cecum', 'Ascending colon', 'Descending colon', 'Sigmoid colon'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Cecum. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0317 The kidney extends between which vertebral levels?
['T9–T12', 'T10–L1', 'T12–L3', 'L1–L4'] KEY 2
The correct answer is T12–L3. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0318 The circulation that begins from capillaries and ends into capillaries:
['Coronary.', 'Cerebral.', 'Portal.', 'Systemic.', 'Pulmonary.'] KEY 2
A portal system connects two capillary beds in series.

IAE-1-SRC-0319 Which of the following circulations connects two sets of blood capillaries?
['Portal.', 'Systemic.', 'Pulmonary.', 'Sinusoids.', 'Lymphatic.'] KEY 0
Portal circulation carries blood from one capillary bed to another capillary bed.

IAE-1-SRC-0320 The following are parts of alimentary tract, except:
['The mouth', 'The esophagus.', 'The pharynx.', 'The liver.', 'The large intestine.'] KEY 3
The liver is an accessory digestive organ, not a segment of the alimentary tract.

IAE-1-SRC-0321 All The following structures are related to the stomach bed, except:
['Transverse colon.', 'Liver.', 'Pancreas.', 'Spleen', 'Left kidney.'] KEY 1
The liver is mainly an anterior relation of the stomach, not a component of the stomach bed.

IAE-1-SRC-0322 Regarding the duodenum one of the following statements is true;
['It is the longest part of small intestine.', 'It extends from the pylorus to the iliocaecal junction.', 'It is the middle part of small intestine.', 'Its length is about three meters.', 'Its second part receives the opening of the common bile duct.'] KEY 4
