The common bile duct opens into the second part of the duodenum at the major duodenal papilla, usually together with the main pancreatic duct.

IAE-1-SRC-0323 The following are parts of the large intestine, except:
['The Caecum.', 'The esophagus.', 'The Ascending colon.', 'The Rectum.', 'The Anal canal.'] KEY 1
The oesophagus is not part of the large intestine.

IAE-1-SRC-0324 The commonest position of the human appendix is:
['Retrocaecal', 'Subcaecal', 'Pre-ileal', 'Retro-ileal', 'Pelvic.'] KEY 0
The retrocaecal position is the commonest position of the appendix.

IAE-1-SRC-0325 The porta hepatis contains all the following structures, except:
['Portal vein.', 'Hepatic artery.', 'Hepatic ducts.', 'Coeliac artery.', 'Lymph vessels.'] KEY 3
The coeliac artery itself does not pass through the porta hepatis.

IAE-1-SRC-0326 Regarding the common bile duct one of the following statements is true;
['Its Dimensions are 3.5 cm long and 20 mm in diameter.', 'Its Beginning is formed by the union of cystic duct and right hepatic duct.', 'It descends behind the fourth part of duodenum then behind the head of pancreas.', 'It unites with the pancreatic duct to form the hepato-pancreatic duct (ampulla of vater).', 'The ampulla opens at the middle of the jejunum.'] KEY 3
The common bile duct joins the main pancreatic duct to form the hepatopancreatic ampulla.

IAE-1-SRC-0327 ___ and ___ are parts of alimentary tract.
 KEY
Both the pharynx and oesophagus are parts of the alimentary tract.

IAE-1-SRC-0328 The stomach has two orifices ___ and ___.
 KEY
The stomach has a cardiac inlet and a pyloric outlet.

IAE-1-SRC-0329 Liver is related to ___ surface of the stomach while the pancreas is related to ___ surface.
 KEY
The liver is an anterior relation of the stomach, while the pancreas is a posterior relation.

IAE-1-SRC-0330 Parts of the stomach are ___, ___ and ___.
 KEY
The stomach is described here as fundus, body, and pyloric part.

IAE-1-SRC-0331 Branches of the ___ artery form the arterial supply of the stomach.
 KEY
The stomach is supplied by branches of the coeliac trunk.

IAE-1-SRC-0332 The small intestine is formed of ___, ___ and ileum.
 KEY
The small intestine consists of duodenum, jejunum, and ileum.

IAE-1-SRC-0333 The Caecum communicates superiorly with ___ and medially with ___.
 KEY
The caecum continues superiorly as ascending colon and receives the ileum medially.

IAE-1-SRC-0334 The commonest position of the appendix is ___.
 KEY
The retrocaecal position is the commonest position of the appendix.

IAE-1-SRC-0335 Cystic duct joins ___ duct to form ___ duct.
 KEY
The cystic duct joins the common hepatic duct to form the common bile duct.

IAE-1-SRC-0336 The common bile duct terminates in ___ part of the duodenum.
 KEY
The common bile duct opens into the second part of the duodenum.

IAE-1-SRC-0337 The bones sharing in the formation of the hip bone meet at:
['Ilium.', 'Pubis.', 'Ischium.', 'Acetabulum.', 'Iliac fossa.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the three bones that fuse to form the hip bone, namely the ilium, ischium and pubis, all meet at the acetabulum. Options (a), (b) and (c) simply name the three constituent bones rather than their meeting point. The iliac fossa (e) is a smooth concavity on the inner surface of the ilium alone.

IAE-1-SRC-0338 As regards the hip bone, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Lower border of ilium is called iliac crest.', 'Pubis is formed of a body & one ramus.', 'Ischial spine separates greater & lesser sciatic notches.', 'Pubic arch is formed of ischial tuberosity & inferior pubic ramus.', 'Obturator foramen is enclosed by the ischial rami.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the ischial spine projects from the posterior border of the ischium and separates the greater sciatic notch above from the lesser sciatic notch below. Option (a) is wrong because the iliac crest is the upper border of the ilium, and (b) is wrong because the pubis has a body and two rami. Option (d) is wrong because the pubic arch is formed by the ischial and inferior pubic rami, and (e) is wrong because the obturator foramen is enclosed by the pubic rami.

IAE-1-SRC-0339 As regards the sacrum, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is formed of 4 fused vertebrae.', 'It shows 5 anterior sacral foramina.', 'Its body lies between the alae.', 'Its promontory articulates with the coccyx.', 'Its upper end forms the apex.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the body of the first sacral vertebra lies in the midline between the two expanded alae. Option (a) is wrong because the sacrum is formed of 5 fused vertebrae, and (b) is wrong because it shows 4 anterior sacral foramina on each side. Option (d) is wrong because it is the apex that articulates with the coccyx, and (e) is wrong because the apex is the lower end and the base is the upper end.

IAE-1-SRC-0340 As regards the female pelvis, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is wide.', 'It is long.', 'Its acetabulum is narrow.', 'Its subpubic angle is wide.', 'It is formed of 2 hip bones & sacrum.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the female pelvis is short as well as wide, an adaptation for childbirth. The remaining statements are true, since the female pelvis is broad (a), has a relatively narrow acetabulum (c), a wide subpubic angle (d), and like every pelvis is formed by the two hip bones together with the sacrum and coccyx (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0341 Sacrum shares in the formation of all the following joints EXCEPT:
['Right sacroiliac joint.', 'Left sacroiliac joint.', 'Symphysis pubis.', 'Sacrococcygeal joint.', 'Lumbosacral joint.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the symphysis pubis is the midline joint between the right and left hip bones and does not involve the sacrum at all. The sacrum does take part in the two sacroiliac joints (a, b), in the sacrococcygeal joint below (d) and in the lumbosacral joint above (e). Recognising which bones form each pelvic joint is the key to this question.

IAE-1-SRC-0342 Which of the following is (are) 2ry cartilaginous joints?
['Symphysis pubis & sacroiliac joints.', 'Sacrococcygeal & sacroiliac joints.', 'Sacroiliac joints.', 'Lumbosacral joints.', 'Lumbosacral & sacroiliac joints.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the lumbosacral joint is an intervertebral disc joint, and all intervertebral discs are secondary cartilaginous joints (symphyses). Every option that includes the sacroiliac joint is wrong, because the sacroiliac joint is a plane synovial joint, which excludes options (a), (b), (c) and (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0343 Which of the following shares in the formation of the pelvic inlet?
['Tip of coccyx.', 'Ischial tuberosity.', 'Arcuate line.', 'Inferior pubic ramus.', 'Obturator foramen.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the arcuate line of the ilium forms part of the pelvic brim, which is the boundary of the pelvic inlet, together with the sacral promontory, the ala of the sacrum and the pubic crest. The tip of the coccyx (a), the ischial tuberosity (b) and the inferior pubic ramus (d) are all boundaries of the pelvic outlet instead. The obturator foramen (e) lies in the lateral wall of the pelvis.

IAE-1-SRC-0344 True pelvis lies:
['Above pelvic brim.', 'Between pelvic floor & outlet.', 'Below pelvic outlet.', 'Between pelvic brim & pelvic outlet.', 'Between pelvic brim & pelvic diaphragm.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the true or lesser pelvis is the cavity between the pelvic brim above and the pelvic diaphragm, that is the pelvic floor, below. Option (a) describes the false or greater pelvis, and (b) and (c) describe the perineum below the pelvic floor. Option (d) is wrong because the muscular pelvic floor, not the bony outlet, is the lower limit of the true pelvic cavity.

IAE-1-SRC-0345 As regards the obturator internus, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It lies in the lateral wall of the pelvic cavity.', 'It arises from the hip bone.', 'It leaves the pelvis through the greater sciatic foramen.', 'It is covered by a fascia.', 'Its fascia gives origin to levator ani muscle.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because this statement is incorrect: obturator internus leaves the pelvis through the lesser sciatic notch, not the greater sciatic foramen, and it is piriformis that uses the greater sciatic foramen. The remaining statements are true, since obturator internus lines the lateral pelvic wall (a), arises from the hip bone around the obturator foramen (b), is covered by obturator fascia (d), and that fascia gives origin to levator ani along its thickened tendinous arch (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0346 As regards the pelvic muscles, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Piriformis muscle leaves pelvis through the greater sciatic foramen.', 'Piriformis muscle takes origin from hip bone.', 'The pelvic floor is formed by the levator ani & piriformis.', 'The retropubic space lies posterior.', 'The levator ani is formed of iliococcygeus anteriorly & pubococcygeus posteriorly.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because piriformis passes out of the pelvis through the greater sciatic foramen, which it almost fills, to reach the greater trochanter. Option (b) is wrong because piriformis takes origin from the front of the sacrum, and (c) is wrong because the pelvic floor is formed by levator ani and coccygeus. Option (d) is wrong because the retropubic space lies anteriorly, and (e) reverses the parts of levator ani, since pubococcygeus is anterior and iliococcygeus posterior.

IAE-1-SRC-0347 Which one of the following is a parietal branch?
['Vaginal artery.', 'Inferior vesical artery.', 'Middle rectal artery.', 'Internal pudendal artery.', 'Uterine artery.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the internal pudendal artery supplies the perineum and the pelvic walls rather than a pelvic viscus, and is therefore classed as a parietal branch of the internal iliac artery. The vaginal, inferior vesical, middle rectal and uterine arteries all supply pelvic organs and are visceral branches. Sorting the branches into parietal and visceral groups is the simplest way to handle these questions.

IAE-1-SRC-0348 Which one of the following is a branch of posterior division of internal iliac artery?
['Vaginal artery.', 'Iliolumbar artery.', 'Middle rectal artery.', 'Internal pudendal artery.', 'Uterine artery.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the iliolumbar artery is one of the three branches of the posterior division of the internal iliac artery, together with the lateral sacral and superior gluteal arteries. All the other arteries listed arise from the anterior division. The posterior division gives only parietal branches, which is a useful check when answering.

IAE-1-SRC-0349 Which one of the following is a visceral branch?
['Superior gluteal artery.', 'Lateral sacral artery.', 'Middle rectal artery.', 'Internal pudendal artery.', 'Obturator artery.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the middle rectal artery supplies the rectum, a pelvic viscus, and is therefore a visceral branch of the internal iliac artery. The superior gluteal, lateral sacral, internal pudendal and obturator arteries are all parietal branches supplying the pelvic walls, gluteal region and perineum. Visceral branches are named after the organs they supply.

IAE-1-SRC-0350 Which one of the following is a branch of the lumbar plexus & runs on ala of sacrum?
['Obturator nerve.', 'Sympathetic trunk.', 'Sciatic nerve.', 'Pudendal nerve.', 'Lumbosacral trunk.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the lumbosacral trunk is the descending contribution of the lumbar plexus to the sacral plexus, and it crosses the ala of the sacrum to enter the pelvis. Option (a) is wrong because the obturator nerve runs on the side wall of the pelvis, and (b) is wrong because the sympathetic trunk is not a branch of the lumbar plexus. The sciatic and pudendal nerves (c, d) are branches of the sacral plexus.

IAE-1-SRC-0351 Which one of the following carries ganglia?
['Obturator nerve.', 'Sympathetic trunk.', 'Sciatic nerve.', 'Pudendal nerve.', 'Lumbosacral trunk.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the sympathetic trunk is a chain of paravertebral ganglia linked by fibers, and in the pelvis it descends medial to the anterior sacral foramina. The obturator, sciatic and pudendal nerves and the lumbosacral trunk are all somatic nerves without ganglia along their course. Ganglia are collections of nerve cell bodies characteristic of the autonomic chain.

IAE-1-SRC-0352 Which one of the following pairs pass through greater sciatic foramen?
['Obturator nerve & lumbosacral trunk.', 'Sympathetic trunk & sciatic nerve.', 'Sciatic nerve & pudendal nerve.', 'Pudendal nerve & obturator nerve.', 'Lumbosacral trunk & pudendal nerve.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because both the sciatic nerve and the pudendal nerve leave the pelvis through the greater sciatic foramen below piriformis, the pudendal nerve then re-entering the perineum through the lesser sciatic foramen. The obturator nerve leaves through the obturator canal, and the lumbosacral trunk and sympathetic trunk remain within the pelvis or descend over its wall. Only option (c) pairs two structures that both use this foramen.

IAE-1-SRC-0353 As regards the sacral plexus, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is formed infront of levator ani.', 'It gives the obturator nerve.', 'It is formed medial to anterior sacral foramina.', 'It is formed by ventral rami of S1,2,3,4 & lumbosacral trunk.', 'The plexus of both sides unite infront of coccyx.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the sacral plexus is formed by the ventral rami of the upper four sacral nerves together with the lumbosacral trunk descending from the lumbar plexus. Option (a) is wrong because the plexus lies in front of piriformis, and (b) is wrong because the obturator nerve is a branch of the lumbar plexus. Option (c) is wrong because it is the sympathetic trunk that lies medial to the anterior sacral foramina, and (e) is wrong because it is the sympathetic trunks that unite in front of the coccyx.

IAE-1-SRC-0354 Which of the following organs runs along the concavity of the sacrum?
['Sigmoid colon.', 'Rectum.', 'Urinary bladder.', 'Anal canal.', 'Prostate.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the rectum follows the anterior concavity of the sacrum and coccyx, which gives it its characteristic sacral flexure. The sigmoid colon (a) lies above it in the left iliac fossa and pelvis, and the anal canal (d) passes downwards and backwards below the pelvic floor. The bladder (c) and prostate (e) lie anteriorly, behind the pubis.

IAE-1-SRC-0355 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['Sigmoid colon ends at S5.', 'Rectum has its Mesocolon.', 'Anal canal begins 1 inch beyond tip of coccyx.', 'Anal canal runs downwards & forwards.', 'Rectum ends at anal orifice.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the rectum ends and the anal canal begins about one inch beyond the tip of the coccyx, where the gut turns sharply backwards to pierce the pelvic floor. Option (a) is wrong because the sigmoid colon ends at S3, and (b) is wrong because it is the sigmoid colon and not the rectum that has a mesocolon. Option (d) is wrong because the anal canal runs downwards and backwards, and (e) is wrong because it is the anal canal that ends at the anal orifice.

IAE-1-SRC-0356 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Urinary bladder is pyramidal in shape.', 'Urinary bladder apex gives attachment to median umbilical ligament.', 'Trigone of urinary bladder is a part of its neck.', 'Ureters open in superolateral angles of urinary bladder.', 'Prostatic urethra emerges from neck of urinary bladder.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because this statement is incorrect: the trigone is the smooth triangular area of the base, or posterior surface, of the bladder, bounded by the two ureteric orifices and the internal urethral orifice. The remaining statements are true, since the empty bladder is pyramidal (a), its apex is continuous with the median umbilical ligament (b), the ureters open at the superolateral angles of the base (d), and the prostatic urethra begins at the bladder neck (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0357 Match each structure in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
The male urethra is described in three parts along its course. The prostate sits immediately below the bladder neck and is traversed by the prostatic urethra, which begins at that neck; the membranous urethra then pierces the deep perineal pouch, and the penile urethra runs in the bulb and corpus spongiosum within the superficial perineal pouch. The seminal vesicles lie behind the base of the bladder, lateral to the ampullae of the vasa deferentia.

IAE-1-SRC-0358 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['Seminal vesicles lie medial to ampullae of vasa deferentia.', 'Ampullae of vasa deferentia are thin dilated parts.', 'The necks of the 2 seminal vesicles unite to form the ejaculatory duct.', 'Ejaculatory ducts pierce apex of prostate.', 'Ejaculatory ducts open on the front of the prostatic urethra.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the ampulla is the dilated terminal part of the vas deferens, lying behind the base of the bladder. Option (a) is wrong because the seminal vesicles lie lateral to these ampullae, and (c) is wrong because the ejaculatory duct is formed by the union of the duct of the seminal vesicle with the vas deferens of the same side. Option (d) is wrong because the ejaculatory ducts pierce the base of the prostate, and (e) is wrong because they open on the back of the prostatic urethra.

IAE-1-SRC-0359 As regards the pelvic peritoneum in male, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Above the sacrum, the peritoneum forms the sigmoid mesocolon.', 'The upper 1/3 of rectum is covered by peritoneum on front & sides.', 'The middle 1/3 of rectum is covered by peritoneum on front only.', 'Pararectal fossae lie on each side of rectum.', 'Rectovesical pouch is formed at junction between upper 2/3 & lower 1/3 of rectum.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because this statement is incorrect: the sigmoid mesocolon is formed above the middle piece of the sacrum, at the level of S3, not above the sacrum as a whole, since the mesocolon ends where the rectum begins. The remaining statements are true, since the upper third of the rectum is covered in front and at the sides (b), the middle third in front only (c), pararectal fossae flank the rectum (d), and the rectovesical pouch is formed where the peritoneum leaves the rectum at the junction of its upper two thirds with the lower third (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0360 Match each structure in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
The pelvic peritoneum sweeps over the pelvic organs and leaves characteristic fossae and pouches. The sigmoid colon retains a mesocolon, while the rectum progressively loses its peritoneal covering, being clothed in front and at the sides in its upper third and in front only in its middle third. The peritoneum then reflects forwards onto the bladder to form the rectovesical pouch, and paravesical fossae lie on either side of the bladder.

IAE-1-SRC-0361 In male pelvis, peritoneum covers all the following EXCEPT:
['Middle 1/3 of rectum.', 'Lower 1/3 of rectum.', 'Uppermost part of base of urinary bladder.', 'Upper part of seminal vesicle.', 'Upper surface of urinary bladder.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the lower third of the rectum lies below the level of the rectovesical pouch and has no peritoneal covering at all. The middle third is covered anteriorly (a), and the peritoneum of the rectovesical pouch also covers the uppermost part of the base of the bladder and the upper parts of the seminal vesicles (c, d), as well as the superior surface of the bladder (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0362 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['Uterine tubes are attached to the lateral angles of the uterus.', 'Medial end of uterine tube opens in peritoneal cavity.', 'Fimbria surround medial end of uterine tube.', 'The cervix is the uppermost part of the uterus.', 'Cervix of uterus is closed by hymen.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because each uterine tube is attached to the lateral angle, or cornu, of the uterus and runs laterally in the upper border of the broad ligament. Option (b) is wrong because it is the lateral end that opens into the peritoneal cavity, and (c) is wrong because the fimbriae surround that lateral end. Option (d) is wrong because the fundus is the uppermost part of the uterus, and (e) is wrong because the hymen covers the lower end of the vagina.

IAE-1-SRC-0363 Match each structure in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
The lateral end of the uterine tube is the infundibulum, whose margin bears the fimbriae. The cervix projects into the upper anterior wall of the vagina, and the angle between the axis of the cervix and that of the vagina is the angle of anteversion. The ovaries lie against the lateral pelvic wall, and the cavity of the body of the uterus is triangular in shape.

IAE-1-SRC-0364 In female pelvis, peritoneum covers all the following EXCEPT:
['Upper part of posterior wall of vagina.', 'Superior surface of uterus.', 'Fundus of uterus.', 'Upper surface of urinary bladder.', 'Front of cervix.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the anterior surface of the cervix is not covered by peritoneum, the peritoneum leaving the uterus at this level to reflect onto the bladder and form the uterovesical pouch. The posterior surface of the cervix and the upper part of the posterior vaginal wall are covered as part of the rectouterine pouch (a), as are the superior surface and fundus of the uterus (b, c) and the upper surface of the bladder (d).

IAE-1-SRC-0365 Uterine tubes lie in:
['Uterovesical pouch.', 'Rectovaginal pouch.', 'Paravesical fossae.', 'Broad ligament of uterus.', 'Pararectal fossae.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because each uterine tube runs laterally in the free upper border of the broad ligament, the double fold of peritoneum passing from the uterus to the lateral pelvic wall. The uterovesical and rectovaginal pouches (a, b) are peritoneal recesses in front of and behind the uterus, and the paravesical and pararectal fossae (c, e) flank the bladder and rectum. The broad ligament also contains the ovarian ligament, vessels and the round ligament.

IAE-1-SRC-0366 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Perineum lies below pelvic diaphragm.', 'Perineum is bounded anteriorly by pubic arch.', 'Perineum is divided into anal triangle anteriorly & urogenital triangle posteriorly.', 'Tip of coccyx forms the posterior boundary of the perineum.', 'Posterolateral boundary of perineum is formed by sacrotuberous lig.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because this statement is incorrect: the perineum is divided by a line between the two ischial tuberosities into the urogenital triangle anteriorly and the anal triangle posteriorly, which is the reverse of what is stated. The remaining statements are true, since the perineum lies below the pelvic diaphragm (a) and is bounded by the pubic arch in front (b), the tip of the coccyx behind (d) and the sacrotuberous ligaments posterolaterally (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0367 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['Urogenital triangle lies above pelvic floor.', 'Urogenital triangle is divided by perineal membrane into 2 pouches.', 'Superficial perineal pouch contains sphincter urethrae.', 'Deep perineal pouch contains root of external genitalia.', 'Anal triangle contains anal canal & one ischiorectal fossa.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the perineal membrane divides the urogenital triangle into a superficial and a deep perineal pouch. Option (a) is wrong because the urogenital triangle lies below the pelvic floor, between it and the membranous superficial fascia. Option (c) is wrong because the sphincter urethrae lies in the deep pouch and (d) is wrong because the root of the external genitalia lies in the superficial pouch, and (e) is wrong because the anal triangle contains two ischiorectal fossae.

IAE-1-SRC-0368 Female external genitalia include all the following EXCEPT:
['Labia majora.', 'Labia minora.', 'Vestibule of vagina.', 'Clitoris.', 'Glans penis.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the glans penis is part of the male external genitalia. The female external genitalia, collectively the vulva, comprise the labia majora and minora, the vestibule of the vagina and the clitoris. The clitoris is the female homologue of the penis, but the glans penis itself is a male structure.

IAE-1-SRC-0369 In male, perineal pouches contain all the following EXCEPT:
['Bulb of vestibule.', 'Crura of penis.', 'Bulb of penis.', 'Bulbourethral glands.', 'Sphincter urethrae.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the bulb of the vestibule is a female structure lying in the superficial perineal pouch of the female, and it has no place in the male perineum. The male superficial pouch contains the crura and bulb of the penis (b, c), while the deep pouch contains the bulbourethral glands and the sphincter urethrae (d, e). The bulb of the vestibule is the female homologue of the bulb of the penis.

IAE-1-SRC-0370 Lat wall of pelvis is formed by:
['Piriformis.', 'Obterator internus.', 'Ilio coccygeus.', 'Coccygeus.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the lateral wall of the pelvis is formed by the hip bone below the pelvic brim, covered by obturator internus and its fascia. Piriformis and coccygeus clothe the posterior wall, while iliococcygeus is a part of levator ani forming the pelvic floor.

IAE-1-SRC-0371 Sigmoid colon end at level of:
['Tip of coccyx.', 'S2 vertebrae.', 'Sacro iliac joint.', 'S3 vertebrae.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the sigmoid colon begins at the pelvic brim opposite the left sacroiliac joint and ends in front of the third piece of the sacrum, where it becomes the rectum. The rectum then runs from S3 to the tip of the coccyx, where it pierces the pelvic diaphragm as the anal canal.

IAE-1-SRC-0372 The Ejaculatory duct opens in:
['Post surface of membranous urethra.', 'Ant surface of penile urethra.', 'Post surface of penile urethra.', 'Post surface of Prostatic urethra.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because each ejaculatory duct, formed by the union of the vas deferens and the duct of the seminal vesicle, opens on the posterior wall of the prostatic urethra on the margin of the prostatic sinus beside the colliculus seminalis. The membranous and penile parts of the urethra receive no genital ducts of this kind.

IAE-1-SRC-0373 All are true about Anal triangle of perineum except:
['Bounded laterally by sacro tuberous ligament.', 'Traversed by anal canal.', 'Contains ischiorectal (ischio anal) fossa on sides of anal canal.', 'Traversed by perineal membrane.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the perineal membrane belongs to the urogenital triangle, where it separates the superficial from the deep perineal pouch; the anal triangle has no perineal membrane. The remaining statements are true, since the anal triangle is bounded laterally by the sacrotuberous ligament and the ischial tuberosities, is traversed by the anal canal and contains an ischioanal fossa on either side of it.

IAE-1-SRC-0374 Which is not true about urinary bladder?
['The ureter opens in the post sup angle.', 'The base is related to seminal vesicle in male.', 'Sup surface related to utero vesical pouch in female.', 'The median umbilical ligament attached to the Neck.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the median umbilical ligament, the remnant of the urachus, is attached to the apex of the bladder and ascends to the umbilicus, not to the neck. The remaining statements are true, since the ureters open at the posterosuperior angles, the base is related to the seminal vesicles and vasa in the male, and the superior surface is covered by peritoneum forming the uterovesical pouch in the female.

IAE-1-SRC-0375 All are true about female pelvis except:
['The peritoneum has 2 pouches.', 'The sub pubic angle is wider than males.', 'The vertical axis is longer than males.', 'The horizontal axis is wider than males.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the female pelvic cavity is shallower than the male, so its vertical axis is shorter, not longer. The remaining statements are true, since the female peritoneum forms both a uterovesical and a rectouterine pouch, and the subpubic angle and the transverse dimensions are all wider than in the male to favour childbirth.

IAE-1-SRC-0376 Which of the following is Retro peritoneal?
['Sigmoid colon.', 'Uterus.', 'Rectum.', 'Fallopian tube.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the rectum is retroperitoneal: its upper third is covered by peritoneum in front and at the sides, the middle third in front only, and the lower third not at all. The sigmoid colon hangs on a mesocolon, while the uterus and the uterine tubes are enclosed within the broad ligament and are essentially intraperitoneal.

IAE-1-SRC-0377 The sacro-iliac joint is:
['Plane synovial', 'Primary cartilaginous', 'Secondary cartilaginous', 'Fibrous'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Plane synovial. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0378 The pelvic inlet is bounded by all the following EXCEPT:
['Upper part of the sacrum', 'Arcuate line', 'Ischial tuberosity', 'Upper border of symphysis pubis'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Ischial tuberosity. The remaining options describe the normal anatomy correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0379 The major part of the pelvic floor is formed by:
['Obturator internus', 'Piriformis', 'Levator ani', 'Coccygeus'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Levator ani. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0380 The uterine artery is a branch from which artery?
['Common iliac', 'External iliac', 'Anterior division of internal iliac', 'Posterior division of internal iliac'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Anterior division of internal iliac. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0381 The sacral plexus is formed in front of which muscle?
['Psoas major', 'Obturator internus', 'Piriformis', 'Iliacus'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Piriformis. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0382 The rectum starts at the level of which vertebra?
['S1', 'S2', 'S3', 'S4'] KEY 2
The correct answer is S3. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0383 The trigone lines the ______ of the urinary bladder.
['Apex', 'Base', 'Superior surface', 'Inferior surface'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Base. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0384 The ejaculatory duct opens in which part of the male urethra?
['Terminal', 'Penile', 'Membranous', 'Prostatic'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Prostatic. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0385 The superficial perineal pouch lies:
['Above the levator ani', 'Between levator ani and perineal membrane', 'Between perineal membrane and superficial fascia of the perineum', 'None of the above'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Between perineal membrane and superficial fascia of the perineum. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0386 The sphincter urethrae is present in:
['Ischiorectal fossa', 'Superficial perineal pouch', 'Deep perineal pouch', 'Anal triangle of the perineum'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Deep perineal pouch. The remaining options do not fit the same anatomical relationship or classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0387 An example of a girdle bone is:
['Hip bone.', 'Humerus.', 'Femur.', 'Metacarpal bone.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 0
The hip bone is part of the pelvic girdle.

IAE-1-SRC-0388 The..........is cartilaginous joint:
['Sagittal suture', 'Gomphosis', 'Symphysis pubis', 'Syndesmosis', 'Elbow joint'] KEY 2
The pubic symphysis is a secondary cartilaginous joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0389 The spermatic cord contains all the following structures, except:
['Epididymis.', 'Testicular artery.', 'Testicular veins.', 'Lymph vessels.', 'Vas deferens.'] KEY 0
The epididymis lies on the testis and is not a content of the spermatic cord.

IAE-1-SRC-0390 All the followings are parts of the uterus, except:
['The body.', 'The cervix.', 'The fundus.', 'The fallopian tubes.'] KEY 3
The fallopian tubes are related adnexal structures, not parts of the uterus itself.

IAE-1-SRC-0391 Regarding the pelvic cavity, one is incorrect:
['The male and female pelvis contains Urinary bladder and urethra anteriorly.', 'The male and female pelvis contains Ureters and large blood vessels laterally.', 'The female pelvis contains Uterus, Uterine tubes and vagina posteriorly.', 'The male pelvis contains Seminal vesicles and vas deferens in the middle.'] KEY 2
In the female pelvis, the uterus and vagina occupy the middle compartment; the posterior compartment contains the rectum.

IAE-1-SRC-0392 Regarding the male urethra, one is correct:
['The Prostatic urethra passes through the deep perineal pouch.', 'Penile Urethra is 15 cm in length.', 'The Prostatic urethra is 8 cm in length.', 'Membranous urethra passes through the prostate.'] KEY 1
The penile or spongy urethra is about 15 cm long.

IAE-1-SRC-0393 Internal anal sphincter is ___ while the external anal sphincter is ___.
 KEY
The internal sphincter is smooth muscle and involuntary, while the external sphincter is skeletal muscle and voluntary.

IAE-1-SRC-0394 Which one of the following shares in formation of shoulder girdle?
['Scapula.', 'Radius.', 'Ulna.', 'Humerus.', 'Scaphoid.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the shoulder girdle is formed of the clavicle and the scapula, which together attach the free upper limb to the axial skeleton. The humerus (d) is the bone of the arm, the radius and ulna (b, c) are forearm bones and the scaphoid (e) is a carpal bone, so none of them belongs to the girdle.

IAE-1-SRC-0395 Which of the following is a bone of distal row of carpal bone?
['Scaphoid.', 'Lunate.', 'Capitate.', 'Triquetrium.', 'Pisiform.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the capitate belongs to the distal carpal row, together with the trapezium, trapezoid and hamate. The scaphoid, lunate, triquetral and pisiform all belong to the proximal row, the pisiform lying in front of the triquetral as a sesamoid in the tendon of flexor carpi ulnaris.

IAE-1-SRC-0396 Match each joint in left column with its type in right column:
 KEY
Synovial joints are classified by the shape of their articular surfaces and the movements they allow. The acromioclavicular and intercarpal joints are plane joints permitting only gliding, the elbow is a uniaxial hinge, the superior radioulnar joint is a pivot allowing rotation of the radial head, and the shoulder is a multiaxial ball and socket joint, the most mobile joint in the body.

IAE-1-SRC-0397 Match each joint in left column with its type in right column:
 KEY
The wrist (radiocarpal) joint is ellipsoid, allowing flexion, extension and side to side movement. The carpometacarpal joint of the thumb is the classic saddle joint, which is what permits opposition, while the carpometacarpal joint of the index is a plane joint with almost no movement. The metacarpophalangeal joints are condylar and the interphalangeal joints are simple hinges.

IAE-1-SRC-0398 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['In elbow joint, humerus articulates with radius only.', 'Inferior radioulnar joint is ellipsoid.', 'Interphalangeal joints are condylar.', 'Lower end of radius articulates with scaphoid & lunate.', 'Lower end of ulna articulates with capitate.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the distal end of the radius carries the articular surface for the scaphoid and lunate at the wrist joint. Option (a) is wrong because the humerus articulates with both radius and ulna at the elbow, and (b) is wrong because the inferior radioulnar joint is a pivot. Option (c) is wrong because interphalangeal joints are hinge joints, and (e) is wrong because the lower end of the ulna does not articulate with the capitate.

IAE-1-SRC-0399 Match each structure in left column with its appropriate description in right column:
 KEY
The upper limb is described region by region. The axilla is the pyramidal space between the upper arm and the chest wall, the pectoral region contains the muscles running from the thoracic wall to the limb, and the shoulder region contains the scapulohumeral muscles including the rotator cuff. The back carries the muscles linking the limb to the vertebral column, and the cubital fossa is the triangular hollow in front of the elbow.

IAE-1-SRC-0400 Which of the following is inserted in the humerus?
['Trapezius.', 'Latissimus dorsi.', 'Levator scapulae.', 'Rhomboides minor.', 'Rhomboides major.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because latissimus dorsi passes from the trunk to the floor of the bicipital groove of the humerus, which is why it can extend, adduct and medially rotate the arm. Trapezius is inserted into the clavicle and scapula, and levator scapulae and both rhomboids are inserted into the scapula only.

IAE-1-SRC-0401 Which of the following is the most superficial?
['Pectoralis minor muscle.', 'Clavipectoral fascia.', 'Subclavius.', 'Ribs.', 'Pectoralis major muscle.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because pectoralis major is the most superficial structure of the pectoral region, lying immediately deep to the superficial fascia and breast. Deep to it lie the clavipectoral fascia and, enclosed by that fascia, subclavius and pectoralis minor, with the ribs deepest of all.

IAE-1-SRC-0402 Which of the following shares in the formation of the medial wall of the axilla?
['Subscapularis.', 'Latissimus dorsi.', 'Serratus anterior.', 'Pectoralis major.', 'Axillary fascia.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the medial wall of the axilla is formed by the upper ribs with their intercostal muscles covered by serratus anterior. Subscapularis and latissimus dorsi (a, b) form the posterior wall, pectoralis major (d) the anterior wall, and the axillary fascia (e) forms the base.

IAE-1-SRC-0403 Which of the following shares in formation of lateral wall of axilla?
['Muscles of arm.', 'Pectoralis minor.', 'Serratus anterior.', 'Pectoralis major.', 'Teres major.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the lateral wall of the axilla is very narrow, being formed by the bicipital groove of the humerus with the muscles attached there, that is the coracobrachialis and the two heads of biceps. Serratus anterior (c) forms the medial wall, the pectoral muscles (b, d) the anterior wall and teres major (e) the posterior wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0404 Axillary fascia shares in the formation of:
['Lateral wall of axilla.', 'Medial wall of axilla.', 'Anterior wall of axilla.', 'Posterior wall of axilla.', 'Base of axilla.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the base, or floor, of the axilla is formed by the skin of the armpit together with the superficial and axillary fasciae. The four walls are formed by muscles and bones instead: the pectoral muscles in front, subscapularis, teres major and latissimus dorsi behind, serratus anterior and ribs medially, and the humerus laterally.

IAE-1-SRC-0405 As regards the axillary artery, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is the continuation of the brachial artery.', 'It begins at inner border of 1st rib.', 'It ends by forming subclavian artery.', 'It ends at lower border of teres major.', 'It is divided into 3 parts by pectoralis major.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the axillary artery ends at the lower border of teres major, where it becomes the brachial artery. Option (a) is wrong because it is the continuation of the subclavian artery, and (b) is wrong because it begins at the outer border of the first rib. Option (c) reverses the sequence, and (e) is wrong because it is pectoralis minor that divides the artery into three parts.

IAE-1-SRC-0406 As regards the axillary vein, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is the continuation of the basilic vein.', 'It ends at outer border of 1st rib.', 'It ends by forming subclavian vein.', 'It begins at lower border of teres major.', 'It lies lateral to axillary artery.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because this statement is incorrect: the axillary vein lies medial to the axillary artery throughout its course. The other statements are true, since the vein is the upward continuation of the basilic vein beginning at the lower border of teres major (a, d) and ends at the outer border of the first rib by becoming the subclavian vein (b, c).

IAE-1-SRC-0407 Brachial plexus gives all the following branches EXCEPT:
['Radial nerve.', 'Ulnar nerve.', 'Accessory nerve.', 'Median nerve.', 'Musculocutaneous nerve.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the accessory nerve is the eleventh cranial nerve, which supplies trapezius and has nothing to do with the brachial plexus. The radial, median and musculocutaneous nerves and the ulnar nerve are all terminal branches of the plexus, arising from its cords.

IAE-1-SRC-0408 Axilla contains all the following EXCEPT:
['Radial nerve.', 'Axillary tail of breast.', 'Trapezius.', 'Axillary vein.', 'Median nerve.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because trapezius is a muscle of the back that does not enter the axilla at all. The axilla contains the axillary vessels, the cords and branches of the brachial plexus including the radial and median nerves, axillary lymph nodes and the axillary tail of the breast.

IAE-1-SRC-0410 Which one of the following can be seen from front of scapula?
['Teres major.', 'Teres minor.', 'Supraspinatus.', 'Infraspinatus.', 'Subscapularis.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because subscapularis arises from the costal, that is anterior, surface of the scapula and is therefore the only one of these muscles seen from the front. Supraspinatus, infraspinatus, teres minor and teres major all arise from the dorsal surface of the scapula.

IAE-1-SRC-0411 All the following share in the formation of the rotator cuff EXCEPT:
['Teres major.', 'Teres minor.', 'Supraspinatus.', 'Infraspinatus.', 'Subscapularis.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because teres major is not part of the rotator cuff; it is inserted into the humerus below the cuff and is a medial rotator and adductor. The cuff is formed by supraspinatus, infraspinatus, teres minor and subscapularis, whose tendons blend with the capsule of the shoulder joint and hold the head of the humerus in the glenoid.

IAE-1-SRC-0412 Dislocation of shoulder joint is common to occur in:
['Anterior direction.', 'Posterior direction.', 'Inferior direction.', 'Superior direction.', 'All directions.'] KEY 0
Most shoulder dislocations are anterior, usually anteroinferior. The inferior capsule is the weakest part of the joint, which helps explain the path of escape, but the usual clinical direction is anterior rather than simply inferior.

IAE-1-SRC-0413 Anterior compartment of arm contains all the following muscles EXCEPT:
['Short head of biceps brachii.', 'Long head of biceps brachii.', 'Coracobrachialis.', 'Brachialis.', 'Brachioradialis.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because brachioradialis, despite its name, belongs to the extensor compartment of the forearm and is supplied by the radial nerve. The anterior compartment of the arm contains the two heads of biceps brachii, coracobrachialis and brachialis, all supplied by the musculocutaneous nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0414 As regards the muscles of the anterior compartment of the arm, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Biceps lies deep to brachialis muscle.', 'Biceps brachii has 2 heads.', 'Lateral head of biceps passes through shoulder joint.', 'Corachobrachialis is the shortest muscle of anterior compartment of arm.', 'Corachobrachialis lies medial to short head of biceps.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because this statement is incorrect: brachialis is the deeper muscle and biceps lies superficial to it. The remaining statements are true, since biceps has two heads (b), the long or lateral head passes through the shoulder joint inside the capsule (c), and coracobrachialis is the shortest muscle of the compartment and lies medial to the short head of biceps (d, e).

IAE-1-SRC-0415 As regards the vessels in front of arm, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Brachial artery ends at level of neck of radius.', 'Brachial artery is formed by union of radial & ulnar arteries.', 'Basilic vein begins from dorsal venous arch.', 'Cephalic vein ends in axillary vein.', 'Cephalic vein ascends in superficial fascia.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the brachial artery is the continuation of the axillary artery and ends by dividing into the radial and ulnar arteries, not by being formed from them. The other statements are true, since the artery ends at the level of the neck of the radius (a), the basilic vein begins from the medial end of the dorsal venous arch (c), and the cephalic vein ascends in the superficial fascia to end in the axillary vein (d, e).

IAE-1-SRC-0416 As regards nerves in front of arm, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Median nerve supplies biceps muscle.', 'Musculocutaneous nerve pierces medial intermuscular septum.', 'Ulnar nerve passes behind medial epicondyle.', 'Ulnar nerve supplies brachialis muscle.', 'Median nerve passes to back of arm.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the ulnar nerve passes behind the medial epicondyle of the humerus to enter the forearm, where it can be rolled against the bone. Options (a) and (d) are wrong because biceps and brachialis are supplied by the musculocutaneous nerve, and (b) is wrong because it is the ulnar nerve that pierces the medial intermuscular septum. Option (e) is wrong because the median nerve stays in front of the arm and gives no branches there.

IAE-1-SRC-0417 As regards back of arm, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Radial nerve pierces lateral intermuscular septum.', 'Axillary nerve lies close to surgical neck of humerus.', 'Profunda brachii artery runs in spiral groove.', 'Radial nerve passes infront of lateral epicondyle.', 'Triceps brachii muscle has long, short & lateral heads.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because this statement is incorrect: triceps brachii has long, medial and lateral heads, there being no short head. The other statements are true, since the radial nerve pierces the lateral intermuscular septum and then passes in front of the lateral epicondyle (a, d), the axillary nerve winds round the surgical neck of the humerus (b), and the profunda brachii accompanies the radial nerve in the spiral groove (c).

IAE-1-SRC-0418 Lateral boundary of cubital fossa is formed by:
['Pronator teres.', 'Brachioradialis.', 'Supinator.', 'Brachialis.', 'Bicipital aponeurosis.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because brachioradialis forms the lateral boundary of the triangular cubital fossa. Pronator teres (a) forms the medial boundary, brachialis (d) with supinator (c) forms the floor, and the bicipital aponeurosis (e) reinforces the roof.

IAE-1-SRC-0419 Match each structure in the left column with its relation to cubital fossa in the right column:
 KEY
The cubital fossa is a triangular hollow in front of the elbow. Its base is an imaginary line joining the two humeral epicondyles, its lateral and medial boundaries are brachioradialis and pronator teres, its floor is brachialis with supinator, and its roof is the deep fascia strengthened by the bicipital aponeurosis, with the median cubital vein lying in the superficial fascia above.

IAE-1-SRC-0420 As regards contents of cubital fossa, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Brachial artery lies lateral to tendon of biceps.', 'Radial nerve is the most medial structure in the fossa.', 'Brachial artery bifurcates at level of neck of radius.', 'Brachial artery is the most lateral structure in the fossa.', 'Median nerve lies lateral to brachial artery.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the brachial artery divides into the radial and ulnar arteries at the level of the neck of the radius, within the fossa. Option (a) is wrong because the biceps tendon lies lateral to the artery, and (b) and (d) are wrong because the radial nerve is the most lateral structure while the artery lies in the middle of the fossa. Option (e) is wrong because the median nerve lies medial to the brachial artery.

IAE-1-SRC-0421 The artery used in measurement of blood pressure is the:
['Brachial artery.', 'Radial artery.', 'Ulnar artery.', 'Axillary artery.', 'Subclavian artery.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the brachial artery in the cubital fossa is compressed by the cuff and auscultated to measure arterial blood pressure, as it is large, superficial and lies against the humerus. The radial artery (b) is used for feeling the pulse rather than for cuff measurement, and the ulnar, axillary and subclavian arteries are less accessible for routine measurement.

IAE-1-SRC-0422 The vein commonly used for intravenous injection is the:
['Cephalic vein.', 'Basilic vein.', 'Axillary vein.', 'Median cubital vein.', 'Subclavian vein.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the median cubital vein lies superficially in the roof of the cubital fossa, is relatively fixed and is separated from the brachial artery by the bicipital aponeurosis, which makes it the standard site for venepuncture. The cephalic and basilic veins (a, b) are more mobile, while the axillary and subclavian veins (c, e) are deep.

IAE-1-SRC-0423 In the front of the forearm, which one of the following is the most lateral & superficial muscle?
['Flexor carpi ulnaris.', 'Flexor carpi radialis.', 'Pronator quadratus.', 'Pronator teres.', 'Flexor digitorum superficialis.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because pronator teres is the most lateral muscle of the superficial flexor group, passing obliquely from the medial epicondyle to the lateral surface of the radius. Flexor carpi ulnaris (a) is the most medial, flexor digitorum superficialis (e) lies deep to the other superficial muscles, and pronator quadratus (c) is the deepest muscle at the wrist.

IAE-1-SRC-0424 In the front of the forearm, which one of the following is the most Medial & deep muscle?
['Flexor digitorum profundus.', 'Flexor carpi radialis.', 'Flexor pollicis longus.', 'Palmaris longus.', 'Flexor digitorum superficialis.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because flexor digitorum profundus is a deep muscle occupying the medial side of the front of the forearm, arising from the ulna and interosseous membrane. Flexor pollicis longus (c) is the deep muscle on the lateral side, while flexor carpi radialis, palmaris longus and flexor digitorum superficialis belong to the superficial group.

IAE-1-SRC-0425 In the front of the forearm, which one of the following muscles lies over the lower ends of radius & ulna?
['Flexor carpi ulnaris.', 'Flexor carpi radialis.', 'Pronator quadratus.', 'Pronator teres.', 'Flexor digitorum superficialis.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because pronator quadratus is a flat quadrilateral muscle stretching across the lower quarter of the anterior surfaces of the ulna and radius, deep to all the flexor tendons. Pronator teres (d) lies at the upper end of the forearm, and the other muscles listed run longitudinally as flexors of the wrist and fingers.

IAE-1-SRC-0426 Which of the following lies medially in front of forearm?
['Anterior interosseous nerve.', 'Ulnar nerve.', 'Median nerve.', 'Radial nerve.', 'Anterior interosseous vessels.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the ulnar nerve descends on the medial side of the front of the forearm, under cover of flexor carpi ulnaris and with the ulnar artery on its lateral side. The median nerve (c) runs in the middle of the forearm, the radial nerve (d) is on the lateral side, and the anterior interosseous nerve and vessels (a, e) lie deeply on the interosseous membrane.

IAE-1-SRC-0427 Most muscles in front of forearm are supplied by:
['Anterior interosseous nerve.', 'Ulnar nerve.', 'Median nerve.', 'Radial nerve.', 'Musculocutaneous nerve.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the median nerve, directly and through its anterior interosseous branch, supplies all the flexor muscles of the front of the forearm except flexor carpi ulnaris and the medial half of flexor digitorum profundus, which are supplied by the ulnar nerve. The radial nerve supplies the extensor compartment, and the musculocutaneous nerve ends in the arm.

IAE-1-SRC-0428 All the following pass deep to flexor retinaculum EXCEPT:
['Median nerve.', 'Ulnar nerve.', 'Flexor pollicis longus.', 'Flexor digitorum superficialis.', 'Flexor digitorum profundus.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the ulnar nerve passes superficial to the flexor retinaculum, lateral to the pisiform, and so escapes compression in the carpal tunnel. The median nerve and the tendons of flexor pollicis longus, flexor digitorum superficialis and flexor digitorum profundus all pass deep to the retinaculum within the tunnel.

IAE-1-SRC-0429 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Carpal tunnel syndrome is due to compression of ulnar nerve.', 'In carpal tunnel syndrome, there is wasting of muscles of thenar eminence.', 'In carpal tunnel syndrome, there is pain in lateral 3½ fingers.', 'Apex of palmar aponeurosis gives attachment to palmaris longus.', 'Palmar aponeurosis improves gripping of objects.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because this statement is incorrect: carpal tunnel syndrome is caused by compression of the median nerve, not the ulnar nerve, which passes superficial to the retinaculum. The other statements are true, since median nerve compression causes thenar wasting and pain in the lateral three and a half digits (b, c), and the palmar aponeurosis receives palmaris longus at its apex and improves grip (d, e).

IAE-1-SRC-0430 Which group of muscles in palm is formed of less than 4 muscles:
['Lumbricals.', 'Thenar muscles.', 'Hypothenar muscles.', 'Palmar interosseii.', 'Dorsal interosseii.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the hypothenar group contains only three muscles, namely abductor, flexor and opponens digiti minimi, palmaris brevis being a small skin muscle in the superficial fascia. There are four lumbricals, four palmar and four dorsal interossei, and the thenar group also contains four muscles when adductor pollicis is included.

IAE-1-SRC-0431 Match each structure in the left column with its appropriate description in the right column:
 KEY
The short muscles of the hand are grouped by the digit they move. The thenar muscles form the ball of the thumb and act on it, the hypothenar muscles act on the little finger, and the four lumbricals arise from the tendons of flexor digitorum profundus and are inserted into the extensor expansions. Palmaris brevis is exceptional in lying within the superficial fascia over the hypothenar eminence.

IAE-1-SRC-0432 In the back of the forearm, which one of the following is superficial & lateral?
['Extensor digiti minimi.', 'Anconeus.', 'Extensor carpi ulnaris.', 'Brachioradialis.', 'Extensor digitorum.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because brachioradialis is the most lateral and superficial muscle of the extensor compartment, forming the lateral border of the forearm as it passes to the styloid process of the radius. Extensor carpi ulnaris and extensor digiti minimi lie medially and posteriorly, extensor digitorum lies in the middle, and anconeus is a small muscle at the back of the elbow.

IAE-1-SRC-0433 In the back of the forearm, which one of the following is superficial & posterior?
['Extensor carpi radialis longus.', 'Extensor carpi radialis brevis.', 'Extensor carpi ulnaris.', 'Brachioradialis.', 'Abductor pollicis longus.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because extensor carpi ulnaris lies superficially on the posterior aspect of the forearm along its ulnar side. The two extensores carpi radiales and brachioradialis lie laterally (a, b, d), while abductor pollicis longus (e) belongs to the deep group emerging distally between the superficial muscles.

IAE-1-SRC-0434 In the back of the forearm, which one of the following is deep?
['Extensor digiti minimi.', 'Anconeus.', 'Extensor carpi ulnaris.', 'Brachioradialis.', 'Extensor Indicis.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because extensor indicis belongs to the deep group of extensors, along with supinator, abductor pollicis longus and the extensores pollicis brevis and longus. Extensor digiti minimi, extensor carpi ulnaris, brachioradialis and anconeus all belong to the superficial group of the extensor compartment.

IAE-1-SRC-0435 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Dorsal venous arch lies in superficial fascia.', 'The only content in anatomical snuff box is ulnar artery.', 'Snuff box is bounded medially by tendon of extensor pollicis longus.', 'Cephalic vein begins from lateral end of dorsal venous arch.', 'Extensor retinaculum lies on back of wrist.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the only content of the anatomical snuff box is the radial artery, which crosses the floor of the box on its way to the palm. The remaining statements are true, since the dorsal venous arch lies in the superficial fascia and gives rise to the cephalic vein from its lateral end (a, d), the box is bounded medially by the tendon of extensor pollicis longus (c), and the extensor retinaculum lies on the back of the wrist (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0436 The shoulder joint liable to dislocate in which direction:
['Superior.', 'Anterior.', 'Inferior.', 'Posterior.'] KEY 1
The shoulder joint most commonly dislocates anteriorly, usually in an anteroinferior direction. Inferior capsular weakness explains why the humeral head escapes in that direction, but the standard clinical description is anterior dislocation.

IAE-1-SRC-0437 Flexors (ant group) of fore arm is mainly supplied by:
['Radial nerve.', 'Median nerve.', 'Ulnar nerve.', 'Musculo cutenous nerve.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the median nerve supplies all the muscles of the anterior (flexor) compartment of the forearm except flexor carpi ulnaris and the medial half of flexor digitorum profundus, which are ulnar. The radial nerve supplies the extensor compartment, and the musculocutaneous nerve ends in the arm as the lateral cutaneous nerve of the forearm.

IAE-1-SRC-0438 Upper limb is connected to vertebral column by which of the following muscles:
['Teres major, Teres minor.', 'Pectoralis major, Pectoralis minor.', 'Trapizeus, latismuss dorsi.', 'Supra spinatous, Infra spinatous.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because trapezius and latissimus dorsi both arise from the vertebral spines and insert into the pectoral girdle or humerus, so they link the upper limb to the vertebral column. Pectoralis major and minor connect the limb to the anterior chest wall, while the teres and spinati muscles run from the scapula to the humerus only.

IAE-1-SRC-0439 All are the following pass through cubital fossa except:
['Brachial artery.', 'Median nerve.', 'Radial nerve.', 'Ulnar nerve.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the ulnar nerve passes behind the medial epicondyle of the humerus and so lies outside the cubital fossa altogether. The contents of the fossa from medial to lateral are the median nerve, the brachial artery and the tendon of biceps, with the radial nerve under cover of brachioradialis at its lateral angle.

IAE-1-SRC-0440 The brachial artery ends at which level:
['Teres major.', '1st rib.', 'Neck of radius.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the brachial artery ends in the cubital fossa at the level of the neck of the radius by dividing into the radial and ulnar arteries. The lower border of teres major marks where the axillary artery becomes the brachial, and the outer border of the first rib marks the beginning of the axillary artery.

IAE-1-SRC-0441 Which of the following content of carpal tunnel:
['Ulnar nerve.', 'Radial nerve.', 'Median nerve.', 'Musculocutaneous nerve.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the median nerve passes through the carpal tunnel deep to the flexor retinaculum, together with the tendons of the long flexors of the fingers and thumb. The ulnar nerve and artery pass superficial to the retinaculum in the ulnar canal, and the radial and musculocutaneous nerves do not reach the tunnel.

IAE-1-SRC-0442 Wound in the most lateral part of cubital fossa could affect all the following except:
['Biceps tendon.', 'Supinator muscle.', 'Ulnar nerve.', 'Radial nerve.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the ulnar nerve lies far medially, behind the medial epicondyle, and cannot be injured by a wound in the lateral part of the fossa. The biceps tendon, the supinator forming part of the floor, and the radial nerve dividing under brachioradialis all lie in or near the lateral angle of the fossa.

IAE-1-SRC-0443 Pulsation of which artery could be felt at the snuff box:
['Brachial.', 'Radial.', 'Ulnar.', 'Ant. Interosseus.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the radial artery crosses the floor of the anatomical snuff box on the scaphoid and trapezium, where its pulsation can be felt. The ulnar and anterior interosseous arteries remain on the flexor side of the wrist, and the brachial artery ends in the cubital fossa.

IAE-1-SRC-0444 Carpal tunnel syndrome is due to compression on which of the following nerves:
['Musculocutaneous.', 'Ulnar.', 'Radial.', 'Median.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because carpal tunnel syndrome results from compression of the median nerve beneath the flexor retinaculum, producing tingling and sensory loss in the lateral three and a half digits and wasting of the thenar muscles. The ulnar nerve escapes because it passes superficial to the retinaculum.

IAE-1-SRC-0445 Nerves of the post compartment of the arm:
['Ulnar.', 'Radial.', 'Axillary.', 'Median.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the radial nerve is the nerve of the posterior compartment of the arm, running in the spiral groove and supplying the three heads of triceps. The median and ulnar nerves simply traverse the arm without supplying any muscle in it, and the axillary nerve supplies deltoid and teres minor around the shoulder.

IAE-1-SRC-0446 Nerve that pierce corachobrachialis muscle:
['Ulnar.', 'Radial.', 'Musculocutaneous.', 'Median.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the musculocutaneous nerve leaves the axilla by piercing coracobrachialis, then runs between biceps and brachialis supplying all three flexors of the arm. The median and ulnar nerves descend without piercing any muscle in the arm, and the radial nerve passes into the spiral groove.

IAE-1-SRC-0447 Nerve that pierce the lateral inter muscular septum of the arm:
['Ulner.', 'Radial.', 'Musculocutaneous.', 'Median.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the radial nerve leaves the spiral groove by piercing the lateral intermuscular septum to enter the anterior compartment above the elbow. It is the ulnar nerve that pierces the medial intermuscular septum to reach the back of the arm.

IAE-1-SRC-0448 Artery related to spiral groove of humerus:
['Ulnar.', 'Radial.', 'Profunda brachii.', 'Brachial.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the profunda brachii artery accompanies the radial nerve in the spiral groove on the back of the humerus. The brachial artery runs on the front of the arm, and the radial and ulnar arteries begin only in the cubital fossa.

IAE-1-SRC-0449 Muscles of hand is mainly supplied by:
['Median nerve.', 'Radial nerve.', 'Ulnar nerve.', 'Musculocutaneous.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the ulnar nerve supplies all the intrinsic muscles of the hand except the three thenar muscles and the lateral two lumbricals, which are median. This is why an ulnar lesion produces the characteristic claw hand with wasting of the interossei.

IAE-1-SRC-0450 The Axillary artery is divided by which muscle:
['Subscapularis.', 'Pectoralis major.', 'Scalenus Anterior.', 'Pectoralis minor.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because pectoralis minor crosses the axillary artery and divides it into three parts, the first above the muscle, the second behind it and the third below it. Scalenus anterior performs the same role for the subclavian artery, not the axillary.

IAE-1-SRC-0451 The axillary vein is continuation of which:
['Cephalic vein.', 'Basilic vein.', 'Brachial vein.', 'Subclavian vein.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the basilic vein pierces the deep fascia in the arm, joins the brachial veins and continues as the axillary vein at the lower border of teres major. The cephalic vein instead ends by joining the axillary vein near the clavicle, and the axillary vein itself continues as the subclavian vein at the first rib.

IAE-1-SRC-0452 The muscle that form the lateral boundary of cubital fossa is:
['Supinator.', 'Biceps.', 'Brachioradialis.', 'Pronator teres.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because brachioradialis forms the lateral boundary of the triangular cubital fossa, while pronator teres forms its medial boundary. Supinator and brachialis form part of the floor, and the biceps tendon is a content of the fossa rather than a boundary.

IAE-1-SRC-0453 All are plane synovial joints except:
['Inter carpal.', 'Acromioclavicular.', 'Carpometacarpal of little.', 'Wrist joint.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the wrist (radiocarpal) joint is an ellipsoid or condyloid synovial joint, not a plane one, and permits flexion, extension, abduction and adduction. The intercarpal, acromioclavicular and fifth carpometacarpal joints are all plane synovial joints allowing only gliding movements.

IAE-1-SRC-0454 Which of the following muscles enclosed within clavipectoral fascia:
['Pectoralis major.', 'Deltoid.', 'Pectoralis minor.', 'Sub scapularis.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the clavipectoral fascia extends from the clavicle to the axillary fascia and splits to enclose pectoralis minor and subclavius. Pectoralis major lies superficial to this fascia, and deltoid and subscapularis lie well outside it.

IAE-1-SRC-0455 The muscles of wrist extension (back of arm) is mainly supplied by:
['Median nerve.', 'Post interosseus nerve.', 'Musculocutaneous nerve.', 'Ulnar nerve.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the posterior interosseous nerve, the deep terminal branch of the radial nerve, supplies the extensor muscles of the forearm that extend the wrist and fingers. The median and ulnar nerves supply the flexor compartment, and the musculocutaneous nerve ends as a cutaneous nerve of the forearm.

IAE-1-SRC-0456 Which of the following muscle not related to rotator cuff:
['Teres minor.', 'Supra spinatous.', 'Teres major.', 'Infra spinatous.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because teres major is inserted into the medial lip of the bicipital groove and takes no part in the rotator cuff. The cuff is formed by supraspinatus, infraspinatus, teres minor and subscapularis, whose tendons blend with the capsule of the shoulder joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0457 Which of the following muscles pierces the capsule of shoulder joint:
['Long head of triceps.', 'Short head of biceps.', 'Long head of biceps.', 'Lateral head of triceps.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the long head of biceps arises from the supraglenoid tubercle inside the joint and pierces the capsule to descend in the bicipital groove, making it an intracapsular but extrasynovial structure. The long head of triceps arises from the infraglenoid tubercle outside the capsule, and the short head of biceps arises from the coracoid process.

IAE-1-SRC-0459 Which is wrong statement about cephalic vein:
['Begins at the lateral side of dorsal venous arch.', 'End in Basilic vein.', 'End in axillary vein.', 'Runs along lat side of upper limb.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the cephalic vein does not end in the basilic vein; it pierces the clavipectoral fascia in the deltopectoral groove and ends in the axillary vein. It is only connected to the basilic vein by the median cubital vein in front of the elbow. The remaining statements are correct, since it begins from the lateral end of the dorsal venous arch and ascends along the lateral side of the limb.

IAE-1-SRC-0460 Choose the incorrect statement about ulnar nerve:
['Runs superfacial to flexor Retinaculum.', 'Pierce the medial intermuscular septum.', 'The main nerve in hand.', 'Share in supplying muscle of arm.', 'Pass behind medial epicondyle.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the ulnar nerve gives no muscular branches in the arm at all; its first muscular branches are to flexor carpi ulnaris and the medial half of flexor digitorum profundus in the forearm. The other statements are true, since it crosses superficial to the flexor retinaculum, pierces the medial intermuscular septum to reach the back of the arm, is the principal motor nerve of the hand and passes behind the medial epicondyle.

IAE-1-SRC-0461 Regarding axilla all are true except:
['Pectoralis major, minor forms the ant wall.', 'Serratus ant shares in post wall.', 'Its apex bounded by clavicle.', 'Coracobrachialis share in lat wall.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because serratus anterior forms the medial wall of the axilla over the upper ribs, whereas the posterior wall is formed by subscapularis, teres major and latissimus dorsi. The remaining statements are true, since the two pectoral muscles form the anterior wall, the apex is bounded by the clavicle, the first rib and the upper border of the scapula, and the lateral wall is the bicipital groove with coracobrachialis and biceps.

IAE-1-SRC-0462 All the following is related to the snuff box except:
['Abductor pollicis longus.', 'Extensor pollicis brevis.', 'Abductor pollicis brevis.', 'Extonsor pollicis longus.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because abductor pollicis brevis is a thenar muscle in the palm and has no relation to the snuff box. The anterior boundary of the box is formed by the tendons of abductor pollicis longus and extensor pollicis brevis, and its posterior boundary by the tendon of extensor pollicis longus.

IAE-1-SRC-0463 Which of the following pass deep to flexor Retinaculum:
['Ulnar nerve.', 'Palmaris longus.', 'Ulnar artery.', 'Flexor pollicis longus.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the tendon of flexor pollicis longus passes deep to the flexor retinaculum through the carpal tunnel, in its own synovial sheath. The ulnar nerve and artery pass superficial to the retinaculum, and palmaris longus is inserted into its anterior surface and into the palmar aponeurosis.

IAE-1-SRC-0464 Wrist joint formed by all the following bones except:
['Radius.', 'Scaphoid.', 'Ulna.', 'Lunate.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the ulna takes no part in the wrist joint, being separated from the carpus by the triangular articular disc of the inferior radioulnar joint. The joint is formed above by the lower end of the radius and that disc, and below by the scaphoid, lunate and triquetral bones.

IAE-1-SRC-0465 Which of the following pairs pass through spiral groove of the humerus:
['Ulnar nerve and vessels.', 'Radial artery and nerve.', 'Radial nerve and profunda brachii.', 'Musculocutaneous and brachial.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the spiral (radial) groove on the back of the humerus transmits the radial nerve together with the profunda brachii artery and its accompanying veins. This close relationship explains why a mid-shaft fracture of the humerus may cause wrist drop from radial nerve injury.

IAE-1-SRC-0466 Which one of the following joints is ellipsoid?
['Superior radioulnar joint.', 'Ankle joint.', 'Wrist joint.', 'Interphalangeal joint.', 'Sternoclavicular joint.'] KEY 2
The radiocarpal joint is ellipsoid/condyloid.

IAE-1-SRC-0467 Which type of bone is the pisiform?
['Long.', 'Sesamoid.', 'Irregular.', 'Pneumatic.', 'Flat.'] KEY 1
Pisiform is a sesamoid carpal bone within the flexor carpi ulnaris tendon.

IAE-1-SRC-0468 The shoulder joint is:
['Hinge.', 'Ellipsoid.', 'Bicondylar.', 'Ball and socket.'] KEY 3
The glenohumeral joint is a multiaxial ball-and-socket joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0469 The elbow joint is:
['Hinge.', 'Ellipsoid.', 'Bicondylar.', 'Ball and socket.'] KEY 0
The humeroulnar component of the elbow is a hinge joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0470 An example of unipennate muscle:
['Sartorius.', 'Flexor pollicis longus.', 'Dorsal interosseous.', 'Rectus femoris.', 'Deltoid.'] KEY 1
Flexor pollicis longus is classically described as unipennate.

IAE-1-SRC-0471 What is the type of the lunate (carpal) bone?
['Long.', 'Sesamoid.', 'Pneumatic.', 'Flat.', 'Short.'] KEY 4
The lunate is classified as a short carpal bone because it is one of the small cuboidal bones of the wrist.

IAE-1-SRC-0472 Which of the following is a long bone?
['Clavicle.', 'Vertebra.', 'Sternum.', 'Maxilla.', 'Scapula.'] KEY 0
The clavicle is classified as a modified long bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0473 The retinacula:
['Surround the muscles of the upper and lower limbs.', 'Separate different groups of muscles which have different actions.', 'Are transverse thickened bands of deep fascia present at wrist joint.', 'Form tough sheaths around big blood vessels.'] KEY 2
Retinacula are localized transverse thickenings of deep fascia that hold tendons in place at joints such as the wrist.

IAE-1-SRC-0474 The scapula is considered:
['Long bone', 'Irregular bone', 'Flat bone', 'Short bone', 'Pneumatic bone'] KEY 2
The scapula is classified as a flat bone because it is thin, broad, and mainly provides a large surface for muscle attachment rather than a shaft like a long bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0475 The following bone is part of the appendicular skeleton:
['Skull', 'Ribs.', 'Vertebrae.', 'Scapula.', 'Sternum.'] KEY 3
The scapula belongs to the appendicular skeleton as part of the pectoral girdle.

IAE-1-SRC-0476 The humerus is considered:
['Long bone', 'Irregular bone', 'Flat bone', 'Short bone', 'Pneumatic bone.'] KEY 0
The humerus is a long bone because it has a shaft with expanded ends and serves as the single long skeletal element of the arm.

IAE-1-SRC-0477 Regarding the saddle joint, one of the following is true:
['It is a fibrous joint.', 'Allows supination and pronation movements only.', 'Articular surfaces are concave.', 'Hip joint is an example.', 'The carpometacarpal joint of the thumb is an example.'] KEY 4
The first carpometacarpal joint of the thumb is the classic example of a saddle joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0478 The elbow joint is an example of:
['Fibrous joint.', 'Plane synovial joint.', 'Uniaxial synovial joint.', 'Biaxial synovial joint.', 'Cartilaginous joint.'] KEY 2
The elbow is a hinge-type synovial joint with movement mainly around one axis.

IAE-1-SRC-0479 The superior radio-ulnar joint is an example of:
['Fibrous joint.', 'Plane synovial joint.', 'Uniaxial synovial joint.', 'Biaxial synovial joint.', 'Cartilaginous joint.'] KEY 2
The superior radioulnar joint is a pivot synovial joint and is functionally uniaxial.

IAE-1-SRC-0480 The wrist joint is an example of:
['Fibrous joint.', 'Plane synovial joint.', 'Uniaxial synovial joint.', 'Biaxial synovial joint.', 'Cartilaginous joint.'] KEY 3
The wrist joint is a condyloid synovial joint and allows biaxial movement.

IAE-1-SRC-0481 The intercarpal joint is an example of:
['Fibrous joint.', 'Plane synovial joint.', 'Uniaxial synovial joint.', 'Biaxial synovial joint.', 'Cartilaginous joint.'] KEY 1
Intercarpal joints are plane synovial joints that permit gliding.

IAE-1-SRC-0482 Shoulder girdle is formed of ___ and ___.
 KEY
The shoulder girdle consists of the scapula and clavicle.

IAE-1-SRC-0483 The skeleton of the arm is formed of ___.
 KEY
The arm contains a single bone, the humerus.

IAE-1-SRC-0484 Forearm consists of two bones ___ medially and ___ laterally.
 KEY
In anatomical position, the ulna is medial and the radius is lateral.

IAE-1-SRC-0485 As regards the hip bone, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['The ischium forms the lower & posterior part of the hip bone.', 'The inner surface of the ilium is the gluteal surface.', 'The upper border of the ilium forms the iliac crest.', 'Ischium has one body & one ramus.', 'Ischial spine lies between greater & lesser sciatic notches.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the inner surface of the ilium forms the iliac fossa, while the gluteal surface is its outer surface. The other statements are true, since the ischium forms the lower and posterior part of the hip bone and has a body and one ramus (a, d), the iliac crest is the upper border of the ilium (c), and the ischial spine separates the greater from the lesser sciatic notch (e).

IAE-1-SRC-0486 As regards the femur, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Lesser trochanter is anterior.', 'Anterior aspect of shaft shows linea aspera.', 'Intercondylar notch separates the 2 condyles posteriorly.', 'Its lower end articulates with tibia & fibula.', 'Head articulates with obturator foramen in hip joint.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the intercondylar notch or fossa separates the medial and lateral condyles of the femur posteriorly. Option (a) is wrong because the lesser trochanter projects posteriorly, and (b) is wrong because the linea aspera lies on the posterior aspect of the shaft. Option (d) is wrong because the lower end of the femur articulates with the tibia and patella, not the fibula, and (e) is wrong because the head articulates with the acetabulum.

IAE-1-SRC-0487 As regards the deep fascia of thigh, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It is called fascia lata.', 'It is thickened medially to form iliotibial tract.', 'Saphenous opening lies below lateral part of inguinal ligament.', 'Saphenous opening lies in lower part of thigh.', 'Saphenous opening is pierced by small saphenous vein.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the deep fascia of the thigh is named the fascia lata. Option (b) is wrong because the fascia lata is thickened laterally to form the iliotibial tract, and (c) and (d) are wrong because the saphenous opening lies in the upper part of the thigh below the medial part of the inguinal ligament. Option (e) is wrong because it is the great saphenous vein that pierces the saphenous opening.

IAE-1-SRC-0488 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['Anterior compartment of thigh is extensor & supplied by obturator N.', 'Anterior compartment of thigh is adductor & is supplied by femoral N.', 'Medial compartment of thigh is adductor & is supplied by femoral N.', 'Posterior compartment of thigh is flexor & is supplied by sciatic N.', 'Posterior compartment of thigh is flexor & is supplied by femoral N.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the posterior compartment of the thigh contains the flexors of the knee, the hamstrings, and is supplied by the sciatic nerve. The anterior compartment is extensor and supplied by the femoral nerve, which makes (a) and (b) wrong, and the medial compartment is adductor and supplied by the obturator nerve, which makes (c) and (e) wrong.

IAE-1-SRC-0489 Which of the following IS NOT a hip flexor?
['Sartorius.', 'Pectineus.', 'Iliacus.', 'Vastus medialis.', 'Psoas major.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because vastus medialis crosses only the knee joint and is therefore purely an extensor of the knee with no action on the hip. Sartorius, pectineus, iliacus and psoas major all cross the hip joint anteriorly and act as flexors of the hip.

IAE-1-SRC-0490 Which one of the following DOES NOT extend knee?
['Vastus lateralis.', 'Vastus intermedius.', 'Rectus femoris.', 'Vastus medialis.', 'Psoas major.'] KEY 4
