The correct answer is (E) because psoas major is inserted into the lesser trochanter of the femur and acts only on the hip, flexing it. The three vasti and rectus femoris together form the quadriceps femoris, the great extensor of the knee.

IAE-1-SRC-0491 Femoral nerve supplies all the following EXCEPT:
['Sartorius.', 'Iliacus.', 'Psoas major.', 'Vastus medialis.', 'Rectus femoris.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because psoas major is supplied directly by the ventral rami of the lumbar plexus and not by the femoral nerve. Sartorius, iliacus, vastus medialis and rectus femoris all belong to the anterior compartment territory of the femoral nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0492 Which one of the following is a bipennate muscle?
['Vastus lateralis.', 'Vastus intermedius.', 'Rectus femoris.', 'Vastus medialis.', 'Psoas major.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because rectus femoris is bipennate, its fibers running obliquely into both sides of a central tendon. The vasti are unipennate or multipennate in arrangement and psoas major is a long fusiform muscle, so none of them answers the question.

IAE-1-SRC-0493 Medial boundary of femoral triangle is formed by:
['Inguinal ligament.', 'Medial border of Sartorius.', 'Psoas major.', 'Medial border of adductor longus.', 'Iliacus.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the femoral triangle is bounded medially by the medial border of adductor longus. Its base above is the inguinal ligament (a) and its lateral boundary is the medial border of sartorius (b), while psoas major and iliacus (c, e) form part of its floor rather than its boundaries.

IAE-1-SRC-0494 The most lateral muscle in the floor of the femoral triangle is:
['Adductor longus.', 'Iliacus.', 'Psoas major.', 'Pectineus.', 'Rectus femoris.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the floor of the femoral triangle is formed, from lateral to medial, by iliacus, psoas major, pectineus and adductor longus, so iliacus is the most lateral. Rectus femoris (e) does not contribute to the floor of the triangle.

IAE-1-SRC-0495 The most medial content in femoral triangle is:
['Femoral canal.', 'Femoral artery.', 'Femoral vein.', 'Femoral nerve.', 'Femoral branch of genito-femoral nerve.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the contents of the femoral triangle are arranged from lateral to medial as femoral nerve, femoral artery, femoral vein and then the femoral canal, so the canal is the most medial. This is the reason a femoral hernia descends through the most medial compartment of the femoral sheath.

IAE-1-SRC-0496 Intermediate compartment of femoral sheath contains:
['Femoral artery.', 'Femoral nerve.', 'Femoral branch of genito-femoral nerve.', 'Femoral canal.', 'Femoral vein.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the femoral sheath has three compartments: the lateral one contains the femoral artery, the intermediate one the femoral vein, and the medial one is the femoral canal. The femoral nerve lies outside the sheath altogether.

IAE-1-SRC-0497 Femoral sheath contains all the following EXCEPT:
['Femoral artery.', 'Femoral nerve.', 'Femoral branch of genito-femoral nerve.', 'Deep inguinal lymph nodes.', 'Femoral vein.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the femoral nerve lies outside and lateral to the femoral sheath, on iliacus. The sheath encloses the femoral artery and vein, the femoral branch of the genitofemoral nerve and, in its medial compartment, a deep inguinal lymph node with loose areolar tissue.

IAE-1-SRC-0498 As regards the medial compartment of the femoral sheath, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is called femoral canal.', 'It contains superficial inguinal lymph nodes.', 'Its upper end shows femoral ring.', 'It may give passage to inguinal hernia.', 'It contains no vessels.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because this statement is incorrect: the femoral canal contains a deep inguinal lymph node, not superficial inguinal nodes, which lie in the superficial fascia. The remaining statements are true, since the medial compartment is the femoral canal, its upper end is the femoral ring, it contains no vessels and it is the route taken by a hernia descending into the thigh.

IAE-1-SRC-0499 As regards the femoral nerve, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It gives a motor branch called saphenous nerve.', 'It is a branch of lumbar plexus.', 'It descends between iliacus & psoas major.', 'It lies outside femoral sheath.', 'It supplies muscles of anterior compartment of thigh.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because this statement is incorrect: the saphenous nerve is the long cutaneous branch of the femoral nerve, not a motor branch. The remaining statements are true, since the femoral nerve arises from the lumbar plexus, descends in the groove between iliacus and psoas major, lies outside the femoral sheath and supplies the anterior compartment of the thigh.

IAE-1-SRC-0500 As regards the femoral artery, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is the continuation of external iliac artery.', 'It is a content of the adductor canal.', 'It ends by forming popliteal artery.', 'It lies outside femoral sheath.', 'It begins at midinguinal point.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the femoral artery lies inside the lateral compartment of the femoral sheath. The remaining statements are true, since the femoral artery begins at the midinguinal point as the continuation of the external iliac artery, traverses the adductor canal and becomes the popliteal artery at the opening in adductor magnus.

IAE-1-SRC-0501 As regards the femoral vein, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Femoral vein is the continuation of external iliac vein.', 'Femoral vein begins at opening in adductor magnus.', 'It ascends at first in femoral triangle followed by adductor canal.', 'It lies lateral to femoral artery.', 'It ends by forming internal iliac vein.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the femoral vein begins at the opening in adductor magnus as the continuation of the popliteal vein. Options (a) and (e) are wrong because it is the continuation of the popliteal vein and ends by becoming the external iliac vein, and (c) is wrong because it ascends first in the adductor canal and then in the femoral triangle. Option (d) is wrong because in the femoral triangle it lies medial to the femoral artery.

IAE-1-SRC-0502 The muscle which is formed of 2 parts belonging to 2 different compartments of thigh is:
['Gracilis.', 'Adductor longus.', 'Adductor brevis.', 'Adductor magnus.', 'Obturator externus.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because adductor magnus is formed of an adductor part, which belongs to the medial compartment and is supplied by the obturator nerve, and an ischial or hamstring part, which belongs to the posterior compartment and is supplied by the sciatic nerve. The other muscles listed lie wholly within one compartment.

IAE-1-SRC-0503 Adductor canal contains all the following EXCEPT:
['Femoral artery.', 'Femoral vein.', 'Femoral nerve.', 'Saphenous nerve.', 'Nerve to vastus medialis.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the femoral nerve itself ends in the femoral triangle by dividing into its branches and does not enter the adductor canal. The canal transmits the femoral artery and vein, the saphenous nerve and the nerve to vastus medialis.

IAE-1-SRC-0504 As regards subsartorial canal, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It is an intermuscular tunnel.', 'It lies in middle 1/3 of medial side of thigh.', 'It begins at apex of femoral triangle.', 'It communicates with popliteal fossa.', 'It lies deep to gracilis.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because this statement is incorrect: the subsartorial or adductor canal lies deep to sartorius, which is why it carries that name. The remaining statements are true, since it is an intermuscular tunnel in the middle third of the medial side of the thigh, begins at the apex of the femoral triangle and communicates below with the popliteal fossa through the opening in adductor magnus.

IAE-1-SRC-0505 As regards gluteal region, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Gluteus medius adducts hip.', 'Gluteus medius is intermediate in position.', 'Gluteus maximus is supplied by superior gluteal nerve.', 'Gluteii of both sides contract during walking to prevent tilt of pelvis to unsupported side.', 'Injury of superior gluteal nerve in one side causes waddling gait.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the glutei medius and minimus of the supported side contract during walking to steady the pelvis and prevent it tilting to the unsupported side. Option (a) is wrong because gluteus medius abducts the hip, and (b) is wrong because gluteus minimus is the deepest with gluteus medius between it and gluteus maximus. Option (c) is wrong because gluteus maximus is supplied by the inferior gluteal nerve, and (e) is wrong because a waddling gait results only when the lesion is bilateral, unilateral injury giving a lurching gait.

IAE-1-SRC-0506 Match each muscle in the left column with its nerve supply in the right column:
 KEY
The short lateral rotators take their nerve supply from the nerves that lie alongside them. Piriformis is supplied directly by branches of the sacral plexus, the superior gemellus travels with and is supplied by the nerve to obturator internus, and the inferior gemellus is supplied by the nerve to quadratus femoris. Obturator externus, being a muscle of the obturator group on the outer aspect of the membrane, is supplied by the obturator nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0507 Short lateral rotators of the hip include all the following muscles EXCEPT:
['Piriformis.', 'Obturator externus.', 'Obturator internus.', 'Quadratus femoris.', 'Gluteus medius.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because gluteus medius is an abductor and medial rotator of the hip, not one of the short lateral rotators. The short lateral rotators are piriformis, obturator internus and externus, the two gemelli and quadratus femoris.

IAE-1-SRC-0508 The structure that passes through greater sciatic foramen above piriformis is:
['Sciatic nerve.', 'Posterior cutaneous nerve of thigh.', 'Superior gluteal nerve.', 'Inferior gluteal nerve.', 'Pudendal nerve.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the superior gluteal nerve, with the superior gluteal vessels, is the only structure that leaves the pelvis above piriformis. The sciatic nerve, the posterior cutaneous nerve of the thigh, the inferior gluteal nerve and the pudendal nerve all emerge below piriformis.

IAE-1-SRC-0509 The nerve that passes from greater to lesser sciatic foramen is:
['Sciatic nerve.', 'Posterior cutaneous nerve of thigh.', 'Superior gluteal nerve.', 'Inferior gluteal nerve.', 'Nerve to obturator internus.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the nerve to obturator internus leaves the pelvis through the greater sciatic foramen and immediately re-enters the perineum through the lesser sciatic foramen to reach the muscle, exactly as the pudendal nerve does. The other nerves listed continue into the gluteal region and thigh and do not re-enter through the lesser foramen.

IAE-1-SRC-0510 All the following pass through lesser sciatic foramen EXCEPT:
['Obturator externus.', 'Obturator internus.', 'Nerve to obturator internus.', 'Internal pudendal vessels.', 'Pudendal nerve.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because obturator externus lies on the outer surface of the obturator membrane and passes behind the neck of the femur without any relation to the lesser sciatic foramen. The tendon of obturator internus, the nerve to obturator internus, the internal pudendal vessels and the pudendal nerve all traverse that foramen.

IAE-1-SRC-0511 As regards sciatic nerve, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It descends in middle of back of thigh.', 'It is the largest branch of sacral plexus.', 'It is the thickest nerve in the body.', 'It is commonly injured during intramuscular injection in upper outer quadrant of buttock.', 'It ends by dividing into tibial & common peroneal nerves.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the upper outer quadrant of the buttock is chosen precisely because it is safe, the sciatic nerve lying in the lower medial part of the gluteal region. The remaining statements are true, since the sciatic nerve is the thickest nerve in the body and the largest branch of the sacral plexus, descends in the midline of the back of the thigh and divides into the tibial and common peroneal nerves.

IAE-1-SRC-0512 As regards back of thigh, choose the CORRECT statement:
['It contains the hamstring muscles which flex the hip.', 'Hamstring muscles extend the knee.', 'Hamstring muscles are supplied by profunda artery.', 'Biceps femoris lies medially.', 'Semimembranosus is superficial to semitendinosus.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the hamstrings receive their blood supply from the perforating branches of the profunda femoris artery. Options (a) and (b) are wrong because the hamstrings extend the hip and flex the knee, and (d) is wrong because biceps femoris lies laterally. Option (e) is wrong because semitendinosus is superficial and semimembranosus deep.

IAE-1-SRC-0513 Match each muscle in the left column with its position in popliteal fossa in the right column:
 KEY
The popliteal fossa is a diamond bounded above by the hamstrings and below by the heads of gastrocnemius. Biceps femoris forms the superolateral boundary and semitendinosus, with semimembranosus, the superomedial boundary. Below, the medial head of gastrocnemius is inferomedial while plantaris, arising with the lateral head from the lateral supracondylar line, is inferolateral.

IAE-1-SRC-0514 Which of the following forms superomedial boundary of popliteal fossa?
['Biceps femoris.', 'Adductor longus.', 'Semimembranosus.', 'Popliteal fascia.', 'Popliteal surface of femur.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because semimembranosus, together with semitendinosus, forms the superomedial boundary of the popliteal fossa. Biceps femoris (a) is superolateral, while the popliteal fascia (d) is the roof and the popliteal surface of the femur (e) part of the floor. Adductor longus (b) does not reach the fossa.

IAE-1-SRC-0515 Which of the following shares in the formation of the roof of the popliteal fossa?
['Fascia covering Popliteus.', 'Back of capsule of knee joint.', 'Sartorius.', 'Popliteal surface of femur.', 'Popliteal fascia.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the roof of the popliteal fossa is formed by the popliteal fascia, the thickened deep fascia bridging the fossa, covered by superficial fascia and skin. The fascia covering popliteus, the back of the capsule of the knee joint and the popliteal surface of the femur (a, b, d) all belong to the floor, and sartorius (c) lies on the medial side of the thigh.

IAE-1-SRC-0516 Which of the following is the uppermost structure in the floor of popliteal fossa?
['Fascia covering Popliteus.', 'Back of capsule of knee joint.', 'Sartorius.', 'Popliteal surface of femur.', 'Popliteal fascia.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the floor of the popliteal fossa is formed from above downwards by the popliteal surface of the femur, then the back of the capsule of the knee joint, and finally the fascia covering popliteus. The popliteal fascia (e) is the roof, and sartorius (c) takes no part in the fossa.

IAE-1-SRC-0517 As regards popliteal vessels, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Popliteal artery begins at distal border of Popliteus.', 'Popliteal artery is the deepest structure in popliteal fossa.', 'Popliteal artery ends by dividing into anterior & posterior peroneal arteries.', 'Popliteal vein runs deep to popliteal artery.', 'Popliteal vein ends by forming anterior tibial vein.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the popliteal artery lies on the floor of the fossa and is therefore its deepest structure, which is why its pulse is difficult to feel. Option (a) is wrong because the artery begins at the opening in adductor magnus, and (c) is wrong because it ends by dividing into the anterior and posterior tibial arteries. Option (d) is wrong because the popliteal vein lies superficial to the artery, and (e) is wrong because the vein becomes the femoral vein.

IAE-1-SRC-0518 As regards common peroneal nerve, choose INCORRECT statement:
['It descends downwards & laterally in popliteal fossa.', 'It runs close to biceps femoris muscle.', 'It ends lateral to neck of fibula.', 'Its deep peroneal branch supplies posterior compartment of leg.', 'Its superficial peroneal branch supplies lateral compartment of leg.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the deep peroneal nerve supplies the anterior compartment of the leg, the posterior compartment being supplied by the tibial nerve. The remaining statements are true, since the common peroneal nerve descends laterally along the medial border of biceps femoris and divides lateral to the neck of the fibula, its superficial branch supplying the lateral compartment.

IAE-1-SRC-0519 As regards bones of leg, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Shaft of tibia forms the shin.', 'Lower end of tibia articulates with fibula & talus.', 'Head of fibula shares in formation of inferior tibiofibular joint.', 'Lower end of fibula forms lateral malleolus.', 'Condyles of tibia share in formation of knee joint.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because this statement is incorrect: the head of the fibula forms the superior tibiofibular joint, while the inferior tibiofibular joint is formed by the lower end of the fibula. The remaining statements are true, since the anterior border and medial surface of the tibial shaft form the shin, the lower end of the tibia articulates with the fibula and the talus, the lower end of the fibula forms the lateral malleolus and the tibial condyles take part in the knee joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0520 Deep peroneal nerve supplies all the following EXCEPT:
['Tibialis anterior.', 'Extensor hallucis longus.', 'Peroneus longus.', 'Extensor digitorum longus.', 'Peroneus tertius.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because peroneus longus belongs to the lateral compartment of the leg and is supplied by the superficial peroneal nerve. Tibialis anterior, extensor hallucis longus, extensor digitorum longus and peroneus tertius are all anterior compartment muscles supplied by the deep peroneal nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0521 The most medial structure deep to extensor retinaculae is:
['Peroneus tertius.', 'Anterior tibial nerve.', 'Anterior tibial vessels.', 'Extensor hallucis longus.', 'Tibialis anterior.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the structures deep to the extensor retinacula run, from medial to lateral, as tibialis anterior, extensor hallucis longus, the anterior tibial vessels with the deep peroneal nerve, extensor digitorum longus and peroneus tertius. Tibialis anterior is therefore the most medial and peroneus tertius the most lateral.

IAE-1-SRC-0522 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Anterior tibial artery descends infront of ankle joint.', 'Anterior tibial artery passes midway between the 2 malleoli.', 'Anterior tibial artery continues as dorsalis pedis artery.', 'Anterior tibial nerve begins at distal border of Popliteus.', 'Anterior tibial nerve supplies muscles of anterior compartment of leg.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because this statement is incorrect: the anterior tibial nerve, that is the deep peroneal nerve, begins on the lateral side of the neck of the fibula where the common peroneal nerve divides; it is the posterior tibial artery that begins at the distal border of popliteus. The remaining statements are true, since the anterior tibial artery crosses in front of the ankle midway between the malleoli and continues as the dorsalis pedis, and the nerve supplies the anterior compartment.

IAE-1-SRC-0523 Choose the CORRECT statement:
['Dorsal venous arch is formed by union of metatarsal veins.', 'Great saphenous vein begins from lateral end of dorsal venous arch.', 'Great saphenous vein ends in popliteal vein.', 'Short saphenous vein ascends infront of leg.', 'Varicose veins are due to increased valves in superficial veins.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because the dorsal venous arch of the foot is formed by the union of the dorsal metatarsal veins. Option (b) is wrong because the great saphenous vein begins from the medial end of the arch, and (c) is wrong because it ends in the femoral vein while the short saphenous vein ends in the popliteal vein. Option (d) is wrong because the short saphenous vein ascends in the back of the leg, and (e) is wrong because varicose veins result from defective, incompetent valves.

IAE-1-SRC-0524 The only muscle in dorsum of foot is:
['Extensor hallucis longus.', 'Extensor digitorum longus.', 'Extensor digitorum brevis.', 'Peroneus tertius.', 'Peroneus brevis.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because extensor digitorum brevis is the only muscle whose belly lies on the dorsum of the foot, arising from the calcaneus deep to the inferior extensor retinaculum. The other structures listed reach the dorsum only as tendons, their bellies lying in the leg.

IAE-1-SRC-0525 As regards lateral compartment of leg, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It contains peroneus longus & peroneus tertius.', 'Its muscles are supplied by superficial peroneal nerve.', 'Its nerve supplies skin of dorsum & toes.', 'Its muscles lie behind lateral malleolus.', 'Its muscles are fixed by peroneal retinaculae.'] KEY 0
The correct answer is (A) because this statement is incorrect: the lateral compartment contains peroneus longus and peroneus brevis, while peroneus tertius belongs to the anterior compartment. The remaining statements are true, since these muscles are supplied by the superficial peroneal nerve, which also supplies the skin of the dorsum and toes, and their tendons pass behind the lateral malleolus held by the peroneal retinacula.

IAE-1-SRC-0526 Which one of the following is a superficial muscle in back of leg?
['Popliteus.', 'Plantaris.', 'Flexor hallucis longus.', 'Flexor digitorum longus.', 'Tibialis posterior.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because plantaris belongs, with gastrocnemius and soleus, to the superficial group of the calf. Popliteus, flexor hallucis longus, flexor digitorum longus and tibialis posterior make up the deep group of the back of the leg.

IAE-1-SRC-0527 Which one of the following has 2 bellies?
['Popliteus.', 'Plantaris.', 'Soleus.', 'Gastrocnemius.', 'Tibialis posterior.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because gastrocnemius has two bellies, a medial and a lateral head, arising from the corresponding condyles of the femur and uniting to join the tendo calcaneus. The other muscles listed each have a single belly.

IAE-1-SRC-0528 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Posterior tibial nerve is the continuation of the tibial nerve.', 'Posterior tibial nerve gives medial & lateral plantar nerves.', 'Posterior tibial artery begins deep to flexor retinaculum.', 'Posterior tibial artery is one of terminal branches of popliteal artery.', 'Posterior tibial nerve supplies deep muscles of back of leg.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because this statement is incorrect: the posterior tibial artery begins at the distal border of popliteus, where the popliteal artery divides, and only later passes deep to the flexor retinaculum. The remaining statements are true, since the posterior tibial nerve continues the tibial nerve, divides into medial and lateral plantar nerves, supplies the deep calf muscles, and the artery is a terminal branch of the popliteal artery.

IAE-1-SRC-0529 Deep to flexor retinaculum, posterior tibial vessels lie between:
['Flexor digitorum longus & flexor hallucis longus.', 'Tibialis posterior & flexor digitorum longus.', 'Flexor digitorum longus & tibial nerve.', 'Tibial nerve & flexor hallucis longus.', 'Tibialis posterior & flexor hallucis longus.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the structures passing deep to the flexor retinaculum are arranged from medial to lateral as tibialis posterior, flexor digitorum longus, the posterior tibial vessels, the tibial nerve and flexor hallucis longus. The vessels therefore lie between flexor digitorum longus and the tibial nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0530 First layer of sole contains:
['Flexor hallucis longus.', 'Flexor digitorum longus.', 'Flexor digitorum accessorius.', 'Lumbrical muscles.', 'Flexor digitorum brevis.'] KEY 4
The correct answer is (E) because the first layer of the sole consists of abductor hallucis, flexor digitorum brevis and abductor digiti minimi. Flexor digitorum accessorius and the lumbricals belong to the second layer (c, d), while the long flexor tendons (a, b) reach the sole from the leg and lie in the second layer.

IAE-1-SRC-0531 Second layer of sole contains:
['Abductor hallucis.', 'Flexor digitorum accessories.', 'Flexor digitorum brevis.', 'Abductor digiti minimi.', 'Adductor hallucis.'] KEY 1
The correct answer is (B) because the second layer of the sole contains flexor digitorum accessorius, also called quadratus plantae, together with the lumbricals and the long flexor tendons. Abductor hallucis, flexor digitorum brevis and abductor digiti minimi form the first layer (a, c, d), while adductor hallucis (e) belongs to the third layer.

IAE-1-SRC-0532 Third layer of sole contains:
['Tendon of Tibialis posterior.', 'Plantar interosseii.', 'Flexor digitorum brevis.', 'Flexor hallucis brevis.', 'Tendon of peroneus longus.'] KEY 3
The correct answer is (D) because the third layer of the sole comprises flexor hallucis brevis, adductor hallucis and flexor digiti minimi brevis. The plantar interossei (b) lie in the fourth layer with the tendons of tibialis posterior and peroneus longus (a, e), and flexor digitorum brevis (c) is in the first layer.

IAE-1-SRC-0533 Match each joint in left column with its type in right column:
 KEY
The hip is the classical synovial ball and socket joint, the head of the femur fitting the acetabulum. The knee is a modified hinge, since rotation accompanies its flexion and extension, whereas the ankle is a pure hinge permitting only plantar flexion and dorsiflexion. The talocalcaneonavicular joint is also of the ball and socket variety, the head of the talus resting in a socket formed by the navicular, calcaneus and spring ligament.

IAE-1-SRC-0534 Match each joint in left column with its appropriate movement in right column:
 KEY
Because it is a ball and socket joint, the hip permits circumduction, the sequential combination of flexion, abduction, extension and adduction. The knee undergoes locking by medial rotation of the femur in full extension and unlocking by popliteus, while the hinge-like ankle allows only plantar flexion and dorsiflexion. Inversion and eversion of the foot take place at the talocalcaneonavicular and subtalar joints.

IAE-1-SRC-0535 Adduction of toes occurs in which direction?
['Towards the middle toe.', 'Away from big toe.', 'Towards big toe.', 'Towards the 2nd toe.', 'Towards midline of body.'] KEY 3
Toe adduction is toward the axis of the second toe.

IAE-1-SRC-0536 One of the following is biaxial joint:
['Shoulder.', 'Hip.', 'Elbow.', 'Knee.'] KEY 3
The source classifies the knee as biaxial because it permits flexion-extension with limited rotation.

IAE-1-SRC-0537 Inversion means:
['Bending foot downwards.', 'Bending foot upwards.', 'Moving foot so that sole faces medially.', 'Moving foot so that sole faces laterally.', 'Medial rotation of leg.'] KEY 2
Inversion is the movement that turns the sole medially toward the midline.

IAE-1-SRC-0538 What is the type of the ankle joint?
['Condyloid joint.', 'Plane joint.', 'Hinge joint.', 'Pivot joint.', 'Saddle joint.'] KEY 2
The talocrural ankle joint is a hinge synovial joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0539 The hip joint is:
['Hinge.', 'Ellipsoid.', 'Bicondylar.', 'Ball and socket.'] KEY 3
The hip is a multiaxial ball-and-socket joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0540 The sole of the foot is referred to as the ________ surface.
['Dorsal', 'Plantar', 'Medial', 'Lateral', 'Superior'] KEY 1
The sole of the foot is the plantar surface.

IAE-1-SRC-0541 The patella is considered:
['Long bone.', 'Irregular bone.', 'Sesamoid bone.', 'Short bone.', 'Pneumatic bone.'] KEY 2
The patella is a sesamoid bone embedded in the quadriceps tendon.

IAE-1-SRC-0542 The thigh consists of one bone the ___.
 KEY
The femur is the single bone of the thigh.

IAE-1-SRC-0543 The leg consists of two bones the ___ medially and the ___ laterally.
 KEY
The tibia is medial and the fibula is lateral in the leg.

IAE-1-SRC-0544 All of the following are 2ry cartilaginous joints except:
['Sterna angle.', 'Symphesis pupis.', 'Sarco iliac joint.', 'Inter vertebral disc.'] KEY 2
The correct answer is (C) because the sacroiliac joint is a synovial joint between the auricular surfaces of the sacrum and the ilium, even though it becomes progressively fibrosed with age. The sternal angle (manubriosternal joint), the symphysis pubis and the intervertebral discs are all secondary cartilaginous joints, in which the bone ends are covered by hyaline cartilage and united by fibrocartilage.

IAE-1-SRC-0545 Apex of the heart is formed by ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the left ventricle, because the apex of the heart is formed entirely by the left ventricle and lies in the left fifth intercostal space about 9 cm from the midline. The right ventricle forms most of the anterior surface but does not reach the apex.

IAE-1-SRC-0546 The subarachnoid space lies between ___ and ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the arachnoid mater and the pia mater, because the subarachnoid space is the interval between these two inner meninges and is the space that contains the cerebrospinal fluid. The subdural space, by contrast, lies between the dura and the arachnoid.

IAE-1-SRC-0547 Sternal angle opposite to which costal cartilage ___, at the level of disc between ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the second costal cartilage and the disc between the fourth and fifth thoracic vertebrae, because the sternal angle marks the manubriosternal joint at the level of the second costal cartilages. It is the key surface landmark for the plane that separates the superior from the inferior mediastinum.

IAE-1-SRC-0548 The vessels supplying the lung tissue are named ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the bronchial arteries, because these branches of the descending thoracic aorta supply the lung tissue itself, that is the bronchi and connective tissue framework. The pulmonary arteries carry deoxygenated blood for gas exchange and are functional rather than nutritive vessels.

IAE-1-SRC-0549 The posterior interventricular artery is branch from ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the right coronary artery, because in the great majority of hearts the posterior interventricular (posterior descending) artery is its terminal branch, which defines a right-dominant circulation. It descends in the posterior interventricular groove to supply the posterior third of the interventricular septum.

IAE-1-SRC-0550 Sensory nerve supply to the scalp behind the auricle by ___ while in front the auricle by ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the lesser occipital nerve and the auriculotemporal nerve, because the scalp behind the auricle is supplied by the lesser occipital nerve from the cervical plexus, while the scalp in front of and above the auricle is supplied by the auriculotemporal branch of the mandibular nerve. This is the classic boundary between cervical and trigeminal territory on the scalp.

IAE-1-SRC-0551 The peritoneal sac behind stomach is ___ while in front stomach is ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the lesser sac and the greater sac, because the lesser sac (omental bursa) lies behind the stomach and lesser omentum while the greater sac lies in front of the stomach and forms the main part of the peritoneal cavity. The two communicate only through the epiploic foramen.

IAE-1-SRC-0552 The hilum of the liver is named ___ and lies on which surface? ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the porta hepatis and the visceral surface, because the hilum of the liver is the transverse fissure on its visceral (inferior) surface through which the portal vein and hepatic artery enter and the hepatic ducts leave. It forms the horizontal bar of the H-shaped set of grooves on that surface.

IAE-1-SRC-0553 The peritoneal fold between the spleen and left kidney is named ___, while the fold that encloses the jejunum and ileum is named ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the lienorenal ligament and the mesentery, because the lienorenal ligament passes from the hilum of the spleen to the front of the left kidney and carries the splenic vessels and the tail of the pancreas. The jejunum and ileum are suspended by the mesentery, whose root runs obliquely across the posterior abdominal wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0554 The vena caval opening of the diaphragm is located at the level of ___ and transmits ___ and ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are T8, the inferior vena cava and the right phrenic nerve, because the caval opening lies in the central tendon at the level of the eighth thoracic vertebra and transmits these two structures. Because it lies in the tendon, the opening is widened rather than closed when the diaphragm contracts, which aids venous return.

IAE-1-SRC-0555 The cavity of the cerebral hemisphere is ___, while the cavity of the spinal cord is ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the lateral ventricle and the central canal, because each cerebral hemisphere contains a lateral ventricle while the cavity of the spinal cord is the narrow central canal. Both are lined by ependyma and contain cerebrospinal fluid, and they are continuous through the third and fourth ventricles.

IAE-1-SRC-0556 The esophagus passes through which parts of the mediastinum? ___ and ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the superior and the posterior mediastinum, because the oesophagus enters the thorax behind the trachea in the superior mediastinum and then descends behind the pericardium in the posterior mediastinum to pierce the diaphragm at T10. It does not pass through the middle mediastinum, which is occupied by the heart and pericardium.

IAE-1-SRC-0557 Urogenital triangle of perineum is divided into 2 pouches by ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the perineal membrane, because this triangular sheet of deep fascia divides the urogenital triangle into a superficial perineal pouch below it and a deep perineal pouch above it. The two pouches have quite different contents, which is why the distinction matters in urethral rupture.

IAE-1-SRC-0558 Ejaculatory duct ends at ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the prostatic urethra, because each ejaculatory duct, formed by the union of the vas deferens and the duct of the seminal vesicle, opens on the posterior wall of the prostatic urethra on the colliculus seminalis beside the prostatic sinus. This is where the seminal and prostatic secretions join the urinary tract.

IAE-1-SRC-0559 Ureter begins form kidney at level of ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the first lumbar vertebra, because the ureter begins at the renal pelvis in the hilum of the kidney, which lies at the level of L1 in the transpyloric plane. It then descends on psoas major to cross the pelvic brim at the bifurcation of the common iliac artery.

IAE-1-SRC-0560 Triceps is supplied by the ___ nerve.
 KEY
The correct answer is the radial nerve, because all three heads of triceps brachii are supplied by the radial nerve, the nerve of the posterior compartment of the arm. This is why a lesion of the nerve high in the axilla weakens elbow extension as well as causing wrist drop.

IAE-1-SRC-0561 The plane that divides the body into a large right part & a small left part is:
['Median', 'Right parasagittal', 'Left parasagittal', 'Coronal', 'Transverse'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Left parasagittal because a parasagittal plane placed to the left of the median plane leaves a large right portion and a small left portion. The median plane would give two equal halves, and a right parasagittal plane would do the reverse, giving a small right part. Coronal and transverse planes divide the body front/back and upper/lower respectively.

IAE-1-SRC-0562 Which of the following is responsible for the growth of the bone in length?
['Periosteum', 'Endosteum', 'Epiphysis', 'Epiphyseal plate', 'Diaphysis'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the epiphyseal plate because interstitial growth of its hyaline cartilage, followed by ossification on the diaphyseal side, lengthens the bone until the plate fuses. The periosteum adds bone appositionally and so increases diameter, not length. The endosteum lines the medullary cavity and remodels it rather than elongating the bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0563 The parasagittal plane divides the body into:
['Right & left equal halves', 'Right & left unequal halves', 'Anterior & posterior parts', 'Upper & lower equal parts', 'Upper & lower unequal parts'] KEY 1
The correct answer is right & left unequal halves because a parasagittal plane is any vertical antero-posterior plane parallel to, but not through, the midline. Only the median (mid-sagittal) plane gives equal right and left halves. Anterior/posterior division is the coronal plane and upper/lower division is the transverse plane.

IAE-1-SRC-0564 Which part of the bone is responsible for its growth in diameter (width)?
['Primary center of ossification', 'Periosteum', 'Endosteum', 'Epiphyseal plate', 'Metaphysis'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Periosteum because its deep osteogenic layer lays down successive circumferential lamellae on the outer surface, thickening the shaft. The epiphyseal plate is responsible for length only. The primary centre converts the cartilaginous shaft into bone but does not govern subsequent appositional widening.

IAE-1-SRC-0565 The epidermis of the skin is thick in:
['Eyelids', 'Front of arm', 'Dorsum of hand', 'Palm of hand', 'Skin of chest'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Palm of hand because the palms and soles carry thick, hairless skin with a well-developed stratum corneum and stratum lucidum adapted to friction and pressure. The eyelid has the thinnest epidermis in the body. The arm, dorsum of hand and chest all carry thin hairy skin.

IAE-1-SRC-0566 A bone that ossifies from a membrane:
['Scapula', 'Hip bone', 'Clavicle', 'Skull base', 'Patella'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Clavicle because it is the first bone in the body to ossify and does so mainly in membrane. The skull vault and facial bones are also membranous, but the skull base is cartilaginous. The scapula, hip bone and patella all ossify in cartilage.

IAE-1-SRC-0567 The epiphyseal plate of cartilage is:
['1ry cartilaginous joint', 'Fibrous joint', '2ry cartilaginous joint', 'Plane synovial joint', 'Hinge synovial joint'] KEY 0
The correct answer is a primary cartilaginous joint because the bones are united by hyaline cartilage, it is immobile and it is temporary, disappearing when the epiphysis fuses. Secondary cartilaginous joints unite bones by fibrocartilage, lie in the midline and are permanent. It is not synovial as there is no joint cavity or synovial membrane.

IAE-1-SRC-0568 Superficial fascia is devoid of fat at:
['Eyelids', 'Thigh', 'Arm', 'Back of neck', 'Palm of hand'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the Eyelids because their superficial fascia is loose and fat-free, which is why oedema collects there so readily. Fat is also absent from the external ear, penis and scrotum. The thigh, arm, back of neck and palm all contain fat in the superficial fascia, the palm in the form of protective fibro-fatty pads.

IAE-1-SRC-0569 Which of the following is a short bone?
['Radius', 'Sternum', 'Vertebra', 'Maxilla', 'Tarsal bone'] KEY 4
The correct answer is a Tarsal bone because tarsals, like carpals, are cuboidal with nearly equal length, breadth and thickness. The radius is a long bone, the sternum a flat bone, the vertebra an irregular bone and the maxilla a pneumatic bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0570 Thick skin is present in:
['Face', 'Dorsum of hand', 'Abdomen', 'Palm of hand', 'Back'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Palm of hand because palmar and plantar skin is thick, hairless, rich in sweat glands and firmly bound to the underlying aponeurosis. The face, dorsum of hand, abdomen and back all carry thin hairy skin, even though the skin of the back is tough and dermally thick, its epidermis is not of the thick glabrous type.

IAE-1-SRC-0571 Thick cleavage (Langer's) lines of skin represent:
['Skin creases over joints', 'Junctional lines between superficial & deep fascia', 'The direction of rows of elastic fibers in the dermis', 'The direction of collagen fibers in the dermis', 'Finger prints'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the direction of collagen fibers in the dermis because Langer's lines map the predominant orientation of dermal collagen bundles. Incisions made along these lines gape less and heal with a finer scar, which is their surgical importance. Skin creases over joints and finger prints are separate surface features unrelated to this fibre orientation.

IAE-1-SRC-0572 Regarding sesamoid bones, all the following statements are true EXCEPT:
['Usually appear as small nodules', 'They develop within tendons of muscles', 'Patella is one of its examples', 'They prevent obstruction of blood supply to joint', 'They help to reduce friction of tendon'] KEY 3
The correct answer is that they prevent obstruction of blood supply to a joint, because this is not a function of sesamoid bones. Their real roles are to reduce friction where a tendon crosses a bony surface, to alter the angle of pull and so increase the mechanical advantage of the muscle, and to resist pressure. The other four statements are all correct descriptions, the patella being the largest sesamoid bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0573 In which one of the following cavities is yellow bone marrow found?
['Cranial cavity', 'Joint cavity', 'Medullary cavity', 'Epiphyseal cavity', 'Thoracic cavity'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Medullary cavity because the marrow cavity of the shaft of an adult long bone contains fatty yellow marrow. Red haemopoietic marrow persists instead in the flat bones, vertebrae and the ends of long bones. A joint cavity contains synovial fluid, and there is no such structure as an epiphyseal cavity.

IAE-1-SRC-0574 The presence of epiphyseal plate indicates that the bone is:
['Increasing in length', 'Increasing in diameter', 'Decreasing in diameter', 'Dead', 'Stopped increasing in length'] KEY 0
The correct answer is increasing in length because an unfused epiphyseal plate means active interstitial cartilage growth is still elongating the bone. Once longitudinal growth ceases the plate ossifies and is replaced by the epiphyseal line. Growth in diameter depends on the periosteum and continues independently of the plate.

IAE-1-SRC-0575 Tarsal bones are classified as:
['Long bones', 'Short bones', 'Flat bones', 'Sesamoid bones', 'Irregular bones'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Short bones because the tarsals are cuboidal, with length, breadth and thickness nearly equal, and they are made of spongy bone with a thin compact shell. They are not developed in tendons, so they are not sesamoid. Their regular cuboidal outline excludes the irregular category.

IAE-1-SRC-0576 Primary center of ossification of a long bone:
['Lies at metaphysis', 'Appears after birth', 'Causes ossification of epiphysis', 'Increases breadth of bone', 'Changes cartilaginous shaft into bone'] KEY 4
The correct answer is that it changes the cartilaginous shaft into bone, because the primary centre appears in the middle of the cartilaginous diaphysis and ossifies it. It appears before birth, usually in the intrauterine period, so the option stating it appears after birth is wrong. Ossification of the epiphysis is the work of secondary centres, and growth in breadth is periosteal.

IAE-1-SRC-0577 What is the name of the vertical plane that divides the body into anterior & posterior parts?
['Median', 'Right parasagittal', 'Left parasagittal', 'Coronal', 'Transverse'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Coronal because the coronal (frontal) plane is a vertical plane running from side to side at right angles to the median plane, separating front from back. The median and parasagittal planes are also vertical but divide right from left. The transverse plane is horizontal and divides upper from lower.

IAE-1-SRC-0578 What is the structure that never exists in superficial fascia?
['Tendons', 'Fat', 'Blood vessels', 'Nerves', 'Muscles'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Tendons because tendons always lie deep to the deep fascia and never within the superficial fascia. The superficial fascia normally contains fat, cutaneous vessels, cutaneous nerves and lymphatics. Muscles can occur in it, the panniculus carnosus group such as platysma and the muscles of facial expression being the standard examples.

IAE-1-SRC-0579 Concerning the deep fascia, all the following are true EXCEPT:
['Forms retinaculae', 'Forms fibrous flexor sheaths', 'Is present in face', 'Covers the muscles', 'Divides limbs into compartments'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that it is present in the face, because deep fascia is absent from the face, allowing the muscles of facial expression to insert into skin. Deep fascia is well developed in the limbs, where it invests the muscles, thickens to form retinacula and fibrous flexor sheaths, and sends intermuscular septa that create osteofascial compartments. It is also absent over the anterior abdominal wall as a distinct layer.

IAE-1-SRC-0580 Which one of the following is not a part of the axial skeleton?
['Scapula', 'Sternum', 'Vertebrae', 'Ribs', 'Skull'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the Scapula because it belongs to the pectoral girdle and therefore to the appendicular skeleton. The axial skeleton consists of the skull, hyoid, vertebral column, ribs and sternum. The appendicular skeleton comprises the limb bones together with the pectoral and pelvic girdles.

IAE-1-SRC-0581 Which one of the following is a flat bone?
['Vertebra', 'Humerus', 'Carpal bone', 'Clavicle', 'Scapula'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the Scapula because flat bones consist of two plates of compact bone enclosing a layer of spongy bone, exactly the structure of the scapula, ribs, sternum and skull vault. The vertebra is irregular, the humerus and clavicle are long bones and a carpal bone is short.

IAE-1-SRC-0582 The metaphysis of the long bone is:
['The part of the epiphysis near the epiphyseal plate', 'The part of the epiphysis away from the epiphyseal plate', 'The part of the diaphysis near the epiphyseal plate', 'The part of the diaphysis near the center of the bone', 'The place of the epiphyseal plate'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the part of the diaphysis near the epiphyseal plate, since the metaphysis is the flared end of the shaft immediately adjoining the growth plate. It is the most vascular and most metabolically active part of the bone, which is why acute osteomyelitis characteristically starts there. It belongs to the diaphysis, not to the epiphysis, and it is not the plate itself.

IAE-1-SRC-0583 Which of the following is a characteristic of the long bone?
['Increases in length by periosteum', 'Its shaft has a medullary cavity', 'It is covered from outside by endosteum', 'Its metaphysis is a part of its epiphysis', 'Its primary center of ossification appears after birth'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that its shaft has a medullary cavity, which is a defining feature of a typical long bone and contains marrow. Length is gained from the epiphyseal plate, not the periosteum, and the outer covering is periosteum while endosteum lines the marrow cavity. The metaphysis belongs to the diaphysis, and the primary centre appears before birth.

IAE-1-SRC-0584 The skeleton of the hand includes all the following EXCEPT:
['Three phalanges in index finger', 'Five metacarpal bones', 'Seven carpal bones', 'Two phalanges in thumb', 'Three phalanges in little finger'] KEY 2
The correct answer is seven carpal bones, because there are eight carpal bones arranged in two rows of four. The remaining statements are correct: five metacarpals, two phalanges in the thumb and three in each of the other four fingers, giving fourteen phalanges in all.

IAE-1-SRC-0585 Which one of the following is an irregular bone?
['Scapula', 'Vertebra', 'Clavicle', 'Skull cap', 'Patella'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Vertebra because its complex shape fits none of the other categories, which is the definition of an irregular bone. The scapula and skull cap are flat bones, the clavicle is a long bone and the patella is a sesamoid bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0586 The primary center of ossification of the long bone:
['Changes the cartilaginous shaft into bone', 'Appears in the epiphysis', 'Appears immediately after birth', 'Appears at site of epiphyseal cartilage', 'Helps in growth of bone in width'] KEY 0
The correct answer is that it changes the cartilaginous shaft into bone, since the primary centre arises in the middle of the diaphyseal cartilage model and spreads towards both ends. Centres appearing in the epiphyses are secondary centres, and the primary centre appears during intrauterine life rather than after birth. Growth in width is a periosteal function.

IAE-1-SRC-0587 Which of the following is an example of hyaline cartilage?
['Intervertebral disc', 'Tip of nose', 'Symphysis pubis', 'Auricle of ear', 'Epiphyseal plate of long bone'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the epiphyseal plate of a long bone, which is composed of hyaline cartilage, as are the costal cartilages and the articular cartilages of synovial joints. The intervertebral disc and symphysis pubis are white fibrocartilage, forming secondary cartilaginous joints. The tip of the nose and the auricle of the ear are yellow elastic cartilage.

IAE-1-SRC-0588 An example of secondary cartilaginous joint:
['Symphysis pubis', 'Sternocostal', 'Costochondral', 'Sacroiliac', 'Sternoclavicular'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Symphysis pubis because secondary cartilaginous joints (symphyses) unite bones by fibrocartilage, lie in the median plane and are permanent. The costochondral junction is a primary cartilaginous joint of hyaline cartilage. The sternocostal, sacroiliac and sternoclavicular joints are synovial.

IAE-1-SRC-0589 An example of unipennate muscle:
['Sartorius', 'Palmar interosseous', 'Dorsal Interosseous', 'Rectus femoris', 'Deltoid'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Palmar interosseous because its fibres arise from one side of a single tendon, like one half of a feather, which defines a unipennate muscle. The dorsal interossei and rectus femoris are bipennate, with fibres on both sides of a central tendon. Sartorius has parallel (strap) fibres and deltoid is multipennate.

IAE-1-SRC-0590 Which of the following muscles is a bipennate muscle?
['Flexor pollicis longus', 'Subscapularis', '2nd dorsal interosseous', '3rd palmar interosseous', 'Deltoid'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the 2nd dorsal interosseous because the dorsal interossei are bipennate, their fibres converging on both sides of a central tendon like a complete feather. Flexor pollicis longus and the palmar interossei are unipennate. Deltoid is multipennate and subscapularis is a multipennate/spiral flat muscle.

IAE-1-SRC-0591 What is the type of the interphalangeal joints?
['Condyloid', 'Plane', 'Hinge', 'Pivot', 'Saddle'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Hinge because the interphalangeal joints are uniaxial synovial joints permitting only flexion and extension about a transverse axis. Condyloid (ellipsoid) joints such as the metacarpophalangeal joints are biaxial. Pivot joints allow rotation and saddle joints, like the carpometacarpal joint of the thumb, are biaxial with opposition.

IAE-1-SRC-0592 What is the joint that contains a cartilaginous disc?
['Superior radioulnar', 'Ankle', 'Metacarpophalangeal joints', 'Sternoclavicular', 'Carpometacarpal joint of thumb'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Sternoclavicular joint because it contains a complete intra-articular fibrocartilaginous disc that divides the joint into two compartments and prevents medial dislocation of the clavicle. The other listed joints have no intra-articular disc. Other disc-containing joints include the temporomandibular joint and the inferior radioulnar joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0593 Which one of the following joints is the most moveable?
['Suture', 'Syndesmosis', 'Hinge', 'Gomphosis', 'Primary cartilaginous'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Hinge because it is the only synovial joint in the list, and synovial joints are the freely movable (diarthrodial) class. Sutures and gomphoses are immobile fibrous joints and a syndesmosis permits only slight movement. Primary cartilaginous joints are immobile.

IAE-1-SRC-0594 The function of the tendon is to link:
['A muscle to a bone', 'A bone to a cartilage', 'A muscle to skin', 'A cartilage to skin', 'A bone to a bone'] KEY 0
The correct answer is a muscle to a bone because a tendon is a cord of dense regular collagen that transmits the pull of a muscle to the skeleton. Structures joining bone to bone are ligaments, which is the classic distractor here. Muscles that insert into skin do so directly by fleshy fibres, as in the muscles of facial expression, not by tendons.

IAE-1-SRC-0595 Which one of the following muscles is a multipennate muscle?
['Flexor pollicis longus', 'Rectus femoris', 'Dorsal interosseii', 'Temporalis', 'Deltoid'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the Deltoid because its acromial part is built of several intramuscular septa with fibres arranged like a series of feathers, the definition of a multipennate muscle. Flexor pollicis longus is unipennate, while rectus femoris and the dorsal interossei are bipennate. Temporalis is a fan-shaped triangular muscle.

IAE-1-SRC-0596 Match each joint in left column with its type in right column:
 KEY
The inferior tibiofibular joint is a fibrous joint of the syndesmosis type, held by the interosseous ligament. The intervertebral disc is a midline secondary cartilaginous joint of fibrocartilage. The interphalangeal joints are uniaxial hinges, the radioulnar joints are uniaxial pivots allowing pronation and supination, and the acromioclavicular joint is a plane synovial joint permitting gliding.

IAE-1-SRC-0597 The 2ry cartilaginous joint unites bones by:
['Synovial fluid', 'White fibrocartilage', 'Fibrous tissue', 'Yellow elastic cartilage', 'Hyaline cartilage'] KEY 1
The correct answer is White fibrocartilage because in a symphysis the bone ends are capped with hyaline cartilage but are united by an intervening plate of white fibrocartilage. Union by hyaline cartilage alone defines a primary cartilaginous joint. Fibrous tissue unites fibrous joints and synovial fluid belongs to synovial joints.

IAE-1-SRC-0598 The joints between the bones of the skull vault (cap) belong to:
['Synovial', 'Syndesmosis', 'Sutures', 'Symphysis', 'Gomphosis'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Sutures because the vault bones are joined edge to edge by a thin layer of fibrous tissue, forming immobile fibrous joints that later ossify. A syndesmosis is a fibrous joint with a longer intervening ligament, as between the tibia and fibula. A gomphosis is the peg-and-socket fibrous joint between a tooth and its alveolus.

IAE-1-SRC-0599 Which one of the following muscles is a unipennate muscle?
['Flexor pollicis longus', 'Tibialis anterior', 'Dorsal interosseous', 'Temporalis', 'Deltoid'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Flexor pollicis longus because its fleshy fibres arise from one side only of its long tendon. Tibialis anterior is described as circumpennate, the dorsal interossei are bipennate and deltoid is multipennate. Temporalis is a fan-shaped triangular muscle rather than a pennate one.

IAE-1-SRC-0600 Which one of the following joints is a hinge synovial?
['Superior radioulnar', 'Shoulder', 'Wrist', 'Interphalangeal', 'Acromioclavicular'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Interphalangeal joint, a uniaxial hinge allowing flexion and extension only. The superior radioulnar joint is a pivot, the shoulder is a ball and socket, the wrist is ellipsoid and the acromioclavicular joint is plane.

IAE-1-SRC-0601 Primary cartilaginous joints:
['Are usually temporary', 'Allow wide range of movement', 'Are present between teeth & jaw', 'Unite bones by fibrous tissue', 'Unite lower ends of tibia & fibula'] KEY 0
The correct answer is that they are usually temporary, because the epiphyseal plate, the commonest example, disappears when the epiphysis fuses with the shaft. They are immobile, not widely mobile, and they unite bones by hyaline cartilage rather than fibrous tissue. The tooth-and-jaw joint is a gomphosis and the lower tibiofibular union is a syndesmosis, both fibrous.

IAE-1-SRC-0602 Which one of the following joints is ellipsoid?
['Superior radioulnar', 'Ankle', 'Wrist', 'Interphalangeal', 'Sternoclavicular'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Wrist because the ellipsoid (condyloid) radiocarpal joint is biaxial, allowing flexion, extension, abduction and adduction, and therefore circumduction, but no true rotation. The superior radioulnar joint is a pivot, the ankle and interphalangeal joints are hinges and the sternoclavicular joint is a saddle-type synovial joint with a disc.

IAE-1-SRC-0603 In flexion of wrist, which muscle is a synergist?
['Flexor carpi ulnaris (flexor of wrist)', 'Extensor carpi ulnaris (extensor of wrist)', 'Triceps (extensor of elbow)', 'Flexor digitorum profundus (flexor of fingers)', 'Extensor digitorum (extensor of fingers)'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Flexor digitorum profundus because, although its main action is on the fingers, it crosses the wrist anteriorly and assists the prime mover in the same direction of movement, which is the definition of a synergist. Flexor carpi ulnaris is itself the agonist, and the extensors listed act in the opposite direction as antagonists. Triceps acts at the elbow and is unrelated to wrist flexion.

IAE-1-SRC-0604 Match each joint in left column with its type in right column:
 KEY
The inferior tibiofibular joint is a fibrous syndesmosis. The carpometacarpal joint of the thumb is the classic saddle joint, permitting opposition. The elbow is a uniaxial hinge, the epiphyseal plate is a temporary primary cartilaginous joint, and the metacarpophalangeal joints are biaxial ellipsoid joints.

IAE-1-SRC-0605 All the following are types of muscle attachments EXCEPT:
['Tendon', 'Raphe', 'Ligament', 'Aponeurosis', 'Fleshy fibers'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Ligament because ligaments connect bone to bone and are not a mode of muscle attachment. Muscles attach by fleshy fibres, tendons, flattened aponeuroses, or raphes where two muscles interdigitate in a tendinous seam. The pharyngeal and pterygomandibular raphes are standard examples.

IAE-1-SRC-0606 Match each muscle in left column with its appropriate definition in right column:
 KEY
The agonist (prime mover) initiates and carries out the movement, while the antagonist produces the opposite action and relaxes progressively to control it. A synergist assists the agonist in the same movement, and a fixator steadies a proximal joint or the origin of the prime mover so that its action is efficiently transmitted. These four roles are interchangeable depending on the movement being analysed.

IAE-1-SRC-0607 An example of parallel type of muscle is:
['Rectus femoris', 'Sartorius', 'Dorsal interosseous', 'Deltoid', 'Flexor pollicis longus'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Sartorius because its long strap-like fibres run parallel to the line of pull, which gives a wide range of shortening but relatively little power. Rectus femoris and the dorsal interossei are bipennate, flexor pollicis longus is unipennate and deltoid is multipennate. Pennate arrangements pack in more fibres and so favour power over range.

IAE-1-SRC-0608 Symphysis pubis is:
['Pivot synovial joint', 'Primary cartilaginous joint', 'Hinge synovial joint', 'Secondary cartilaginous joint', 'Fibrous joint'] KEY 3
The correct answer is a Secondary cartilaginous joint because the two pubic bones are capped with hyaline cartilage and united by an interpubic fibrocartilaginous disc in the median plane. It is permanent and allows only slight movement, increasing in late pregnancy. It has no joint cavity or synovial membrane, so it is not synovial.

IAE-1-SRC-0609 Match each muscle in left column with its example in right column:
 KEY
Flexor pollicis longus has fibres on one side of its tendon (unipennate) and the dorsal interossei have fibres on both sides (bipennate). Deltoid contains several tendinous septa and is multipennate, tibialis anterior has fibres converging around its tendon and is circumpennate, and sartorius has long parallel strap fibres. The printed premise 'Pcrollel fibers' is a scanning artefact of 'Parallel fibers' and has been normalised.

IAE-1-SRC-0610 Most of the joints that lie in the median plane of the body are:
['Syndesmosis', 'Primary cartilaginous joint', 'Gomphosis', 'Secondary cartilaginous joint', 'Plane synovial'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Secondary cartilaginous joint because symphyses are characteristically midline structures: the symphysis pubis, the intervertebral discs and the manubriosternal joint. Primary cartilaginous joints such as epiphyseal plates are not confined to the midline. Syndesmoses and gomphoses are fibrous joints found elsewhere.

IAE-1-SRC-0611 All are characters of synovial joint EXCEPT:
['It has a joint cavity', 'A capsule binds its articulating bones', 'May contain infra-articular disc', 'The articular surfaces are covered by synovial membrane', 'They are freely mobile joints'] KEY 3
The correct answer is that the articular surfaces are covered by synovial membrane, because the articular surfaces are covered by hyaline articular cartilage; the synovial membrane lines the rest of the capsule but stops at the cartilage margins. All the other features are true: a cavity, a fibrous capsule, sometimes an intra-articular disc, and free mobility. This distinction explains why articular cartilage is avascular and nourished by synovial fluid.

IAE-1-SRC-0612 Which of the following is a uniaxial joint?
['Metacarpophalangeal', 'Intervertebral disc', 'Interphalangeal', 'Hip joint', 'Wrist'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Interphalangeal joint because it is a hinge permitting movement about one axis only. The metacarpophalangeal joint and the wrist are biaxial ellipsoid joints and the hip is a multiaxial ball and socket. The intervertebral disc is a cartilaginous joint, not classified by axes in this way.

IAE-1-SRC-0613 As regards the cartilaginous joints, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Secondary cartilaginous joints allow limited movement', 'Syndesmosis is a type of primary cartilaginous joint', 'Primary cartilaginous joints are usually temporary', 'Secondary cartilaginous joints lie usually in median plane', 'In secondary cartilaginous joints, articular surfaces are covered by hyaline cartilage'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that a syndesmosis is a type of primary cartilaginous joint, which is false: a syndesmosis is a fibrous joint in which bones are united by an interosseous ligament. The other statements are all true, since symphyses allow limited movement, lie in the median plane and have hyaline-covered bone ends united by fibrocartilage, while primary cartilaginous joints such as the epiphyseal plate are temporary.

IAE-1-SRC-0614 Primary cartilaginous joints:
['Allow free movement', 'Usually ossifies with age', 'Unite two pubic bones', 'Unite lower end of tibia & fibula', 'Occur between the tooth & socket in the jaw'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that they usually ossify with age, since the epiphyseal plate and the costochondral and spheno-occipital junctions are temporary and eventually replaced by bone. They are immobile rather than freely movable. The pubic bones are united by a secondary cartilaginous joint, the lower tibiofibular union is a syndesmosis and the tooth-socket joint is a gomphosis.

IAE-1-SRC-0615 Match each joint in left column with its type in right column:
 KEY
The elbow is a uniaxial hinge and the carpometacarpal joint of the thumb is the classic saddle joint. The wrist is a biaxial ellipsoid joint and the shoulder is a multiaxial ball and socket. Gomphoses and sutures are both fibrous joints, so the same target is legitimately used twice in this set.

IAE-1-SRC-0616 As regards the muscles, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['A bipennate muscle looks like a complete feather', 'The most movable part of the muscle is its origin', 'A raphe is a mixture of fleshy & tendinous fibers', 'The muscle may be inserted into the skin', 'Most muscles have tendons attached to bones'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that the most movable part of the muscle is its origin, which is false: the origin is the relatively fixed attachment and the insertion is the more movable one. A bipennate muscle does resemble a complete feather, a raphe is an interdigitating fleshy-tendinous seam, and muscles of facial expression are indeed inserted into skin. Most muscles do attach to bone through tendons.

IAE-1-SRC-0617 As regards the skeletal muscles, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['The origin is the most moveable end of the muscle', 'Contraction means approximation of origin & insertion', 'Septa from deep fascia divide them into groups', 'They are mostly under voluntary control', 'They are innervated by motor, sensory & autonomic nerve fibers'] KEY 0
The correct answer is that the origin is the most moveable end, which is incorrect because the origin is the fixed end and the insertion moves towards it. Contraction does approximate origin and insertion, intermuscular septa derived from deep fascia do divide limb muscles into compartments, and skeletal muscle is largely voluntary. Its nerve supply carries motor, sensory (proprioceptive) and autonomic vasomotor fibres.

IAE-1-SRC-0618 Match each muscle in left column with its appropriate definition in right column:
 KEY
Trapezius has spiralling fibres and sternocleidomastoid is grouped with the cruciate muscles because the two muscles of opposite sides cross like a letter X. The palmar interossei and flexor pollicis longus are unipennate, the dorsal interossei and rectus femoris are bipennate, and deltoid is multipennate. Targets are legitimately reused because there are seven premises and only five categories.

IAE-1-SRC-0619 Which part of the spinal nerve that does not contain motor fibers? OR Which part of the spinal nerve is formed of purely sensory fibers?
['Ventral ramus', 'Dorsal root', 'Ventral root', 'Dorsal ramus', 'Nerve trunk'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Dorsal root because it carries only the central processes of the pseudounipolar sensory neurons whose cell bodies lie in the dorsal root ganglion. The ventral root is purely motor, and once the two roots unite the nerve trunk and both rami are mixed. This is the anatomical basis of the Bell-Magendie law.

IAE-1-SRC-0620 Which part of the spinal nerve is formed of purely motor fibers?
['Ventral ramus', 'Dorsal root', 'Ventral root', 'Dorsal ramus', 'Nerve trunk'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Ventral root because it contains the axons of the anterior horn cells, together with preganglionic autonomic fibres in the appropriate segments. The dorsal root is purely sensory, and the nerve trunk and both rami are mixed after the roots join.

IAE-1-SRC-0621 The sympathetic outflow is:
['Cervical', 'Cranio-sacral', 'Sacral', 'Lumbo-sacral', 'Thoraco-lumbar'] KEY 4
The correct answer is Thoraco-lumbar because preganglionic sympathetic neurons lie in the lateral horn from T1 to L2 only. Cranio-sacral is the parasympathetic outflow, carried by cranial nerves III, VII, IX and X and by S2, S3 and S4. The sympathetic system nevertheless reaches every spinal nerve through grey rami communicantes.

IAE-1-SRC-0622 Which part of the thoracic spinal nerve that contains preganglionic sympathetic fibers?
['Dorsal root', 'Dorsal root ganglion', 'Ventral root', 'Dorsal ramus', 'Grey ramus communicans'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Ventral root because preganglionic sympathetic axons leave the lateral horn with the somatic motor fibres and then pass to the sympathetic chain through the white ramus communicans. The grey ramus carries postganglionic, not preganglionic, fibres back to the spinal nerve. The dorsal root and its ganglion are sensory.

IAE-1-SRC-0623 The parasympathetic outflow is:
['Cervical', 'Cranio-sacral', 'Sacral', 'Lumbo-sacral', 'Thoraco-lumbar'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Cranio-sacral because parasympathetic preganglionic neurons lie in the brainstem nuclei of cranial nerves III, VII, IX and X and in the grey matter of sacral segments S2 to S4. Thoraco-lumbar describes the sympathetic outflow. Sacral alone is incomplete because it ignores the large cranial component, chiefly the vagus.

IAE-1-SRC-0624 Which ventral rami that do not form nerve plexuses?
['Cervical', 'Thoracic', 'Lumbar', 'Sacral', 'Coccygeal'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Thoracic rami because the ventral rami of T2 to T11 run independently as intercostal nerves and retain a strictly segmental distribution. The cervical, lumbar and sacral rami interlace to form the cervical, brachial, lumbar and sacral plexuses, and the coccygeal ramus joins the small coccygeal plexus.

IAE-1-SRC-0625 As regards the nervous system, choose the correct statement:
['Nerve plexuses are formed by dorsal rami of spinal nerves', 'Pia matter is the outermost one of the meninges', 'Sympathetic system has cranio-sacral outflow', 'Ganglia are groups of nerve cells outside CNS', 'Ventral rami of spinal nerves are purely motor'] KEY 3
The correct answer is that ganglia are groups of nerve cells outside the central nervous system, the standard definition, as opposed to nuclei which lie within the CNS. Plexuses are formed by ventral rami, dura mater is the outermost meninx and the sympathetic outflow is thoraco-lumbar. Ventral rami are mixed, not purely motor.

IAE-1-SRC-0626 Match each structure in the left column with its definition in the right column:
 KEY
Collections of neuronal cell bodies are called ganglia in the peripheral nervous system and nuclei in the central nervous system. Bundles of axons are called tracts centrally and nerves peripherally. The functional junction where one neuron communicates with another is the synapse.

IAE-1-SRC-0627 Cerebro-spinal fluid lies between:
['Dura & arachnoid matter', 'Pia & arachnoid matter', 'Dura & pia matter', 'White matter & grey matter', 'None of the above'] KEY 1
The correct answer is between the pia and the arachnoid mater, because cerebrospinal fluid fills the subarachnoid space. The space between dura and arachnoid is only a potential subdural space and contains no CSF normally. This is why a lumbar puncture needle must pass through the dura and arachnoid to reach the fluid.

IAE-1-SRC-0628 As regards the spinal nerves, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Nerve plexuses are formed by dorsal rami of spinal nerves', 'Its dorsal roots are entirely sensory', 'Are 31 pairs', 'Its dorsal roots have ganglia', 'Ventral rami of spinal nerves are mixed'] KEY 0
The correct answer is that nerve plexuses are formed by dorsal rami, which is false because all the great plexuses are formed by ventral rami; dorsal rami keep a segmental distribution to the back. The remaining statements are true: there are 31 pairs of spinal nerves, the dorsal roots are entirely sensory and bear ganglia, and the ventral rami are mixed.

IAE-1-SRC-0629 Sympathetic nervous system:
['Has no distribution to limbs', 'Inhibits secretion of sweat glands', 'Is connected to all spinal nerves by gray rami communicans', 'Has a cranio-sacral outflow', 'Causes constriction of the pupil'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that it is connected to all spinal nerves by grey rami communicantes, which is how sympathetic fibres reach the vessels, sweat glands and arrector pili of the whole body including the limbs. It stimulates rather than inhibits sweating, its outflow is thoraco-lumbar rather than cranio-sacral, and it dilates the pupil. Constriction of the pupil is parasympathetic.

IAE-1-SRC-0630 An example of an anatomical end artery:
['Coronary', 'Mesenteric', 'Cerebral', 'Central retinal', 'Renal'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Central retinal artery because it has no anastomosis whatsoever with neighbouring arteries, so its occlusion causes immediate and total blindness of that eye. The coronary and cerebral arteries are functional end arteries, having anastomoses that are too small to be effective. The mesenteric and renal arteries have richer connections.

IAE-1-SRC-0631 Which valve prevents the backward flow of the blood to the right atrium?
['Aortic', 'Pulmonary', 'Tricuspid', 'None of the above', 'Mitral'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Tricuspid valve because it guards the right atrioventricular orifice and closes during ventricular systole to stop regurgitation into the right atrium. The mitral valve performs the same duty on the left side. The pulmonary and aortic valves guard the outflow tracts, not the atrioventricular openings.

IAE-1-SRC-0632 An example of a functional end artery:
['Coronary', 'Mesenteric', 'Cerebral', 'Central retinal', 'Renal'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Coronary because the coronary arteries do anastomose, but only through channels too fine to maintain the myocardium if a branch is suddenly blocked, which is the definition of a functional end artery and the basis of myocardial infarction. The central retinal artery is an anatomical end artery with no anastomosis at all. The mesenteric arteries anastomose freely through their arcades.

IAE-1-SRC-0633 Lymph capillaries are absent from:
['Bones', 'Stomach', 'Lungs', 'Articular cartilage', 'Lips'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Articular cartilage because it is avascular and alymphatic, being nourished by diffusion from synovial fluid. Bone, stomach, lung and lip all contain lymphatic capillaries. Avascularity is the general rule that predicts absence of lymphatics.

IAE-1-SRC-0634 Blood sinusoids are numerous in:
['Epiphyseal cartilage', 'Liver', 'Cornea', 'Epidermis', 'Lips'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Liver because sinusoids are wide, thin-walled, discontinuous vessels found in the liver, spleen, bone marrow and some endocrine glands, allowing intimate exchange between blood and parenchymal cells. The cornea and epidermis are avascular. Epiphyseal cartilage and the lips have ordinary capillaries.

IAE-1-SRC-0635 The circulation that begins from capillaries & ends into capillaries is called:
['Coronary', 'Cerebral', 'Portal', 'Systemic', 'Pulmonary'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Portal because a portal system starts in one capillary bed and ends in a second capillary or sinusoidal bed without passing through the heart in between. The hepatic portal vein and the hypophyseal portal system are the two classic examples. The systemic and pulmonary circuits both begin and end at the heart.

IAE-1-SRC-0636 If the lymphatics of the upper limb become obstructed, the arm on that side is likely to:
['Become ischemic', 'Become hyperemic', 'Become oedematous', 'Lose tissue fluid', 'Remain unchanged'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that it becomes oedematous, because lymphatics return protein-rich interstitial fluid to the blood and their blockage causes it to accumulate as lymphoedema. Arterial supply and venous drainage are unaffected, so the limb is neither ischaemic nor hyperaemic. This is the mechanism of arm swelling after axillary lymph node clearance.

IAE-1-SRC-0637 Match each description in the left column with its appropriate vessel in the right column:
 KEY
Veins, especially in the limbs, contain numerous valves that ensure one-way flow against gravity. Sinusoids are the wide exchange channels of the liver, while ordinary capillaries perform exchange throughout the body. Large arteries near the heart are rich in elastic tissue to smooth the pulse, and arteriovenous anastomoses in the skin regulate heat loss.

IAE-1-SRC-0638 The circulation that connects two sets of capillaries:
['Systemic', 'Portal', 'Pulmonary', 'All of the above', 'None of the above'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Portal because by definition a portal vessel begins and ends in capillaries, linking two capillary beds in series. The systemic and pulmonary circulations each interpose the heart between their capillary beds. The hepatic portal and hypophyseal portal systems are the standard human examples.

IAE-1-SRC-0639 Communication between arteries & veins in the tissues can be by:
['Direct between arterioles & venules', 'Capillaries', 'Cavernous tissue', 'Sinusoids', 'All of the above'] KEY 4
The correct answer is All of the above because arterial blood may reach the veins through ordinary capillaries, through wide sinusoids, through erectile cavernous tissue, or directly through arteriovenous anastomoses between arterioles and venules. Each route is found in particular tissues. All four are legitimate arteriovenous communications.

IAE-1-SRC-0640 Choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Veins of limbs possess valves', 'Most veins have branches', 'Portal circulation possesses two sets of capillaries', 'Arteries carry the blood away from the heart', 'Some arteries are tortuous'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that most veins have branches, which is incorrect terminology: veins receive tributaries, whereas arteries give branches. The other statements are true, since limb veins are richly valved, the portal system links two capillary beds, arteries convey blood away from the heart, and vessels such as the facial, splenic and uterine arteries are characteristically tortuous.

IAE-1-SRC-0641 As regards anastomoses, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Arterio-venous anastomoses are numerous in skin of nose', 'Anatomical end artery has no anastomosis with other arteries', 'Arterio-venous anastomosis helps to control body temperature', 'Arterial anastomosis help to compensate a block in blood flow', 'Functional end arteries are sufficient to compensate a block of an artery'] KEY 4
The correct answer is that functional end arteries are sufficient to compensate a block, which is false: their anastomoses are too small to sustain the tissue, which is precisely why occlusion of a coronary or cerebral branch causes infarction. The remaining statements are all correct descriptions of anastomoses and end arteries.

IAE-1-SRC-0642 As regards blood vessels, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Arteries have branches', 'Veins have tributaries', 'Venae comitants accompany big veins', 'Valves are numerous in limb veins', 'Sinusoids slow down blood flow'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that venae comitantes accompany big veins, which is incorrect: venae comitantes are paired small veins that accompany a medium-sized or small artery within a common fascial sheath. All the other statements are true, since arteries branch, veins receive tributaries, limb veins are richly valved, and the wide irregular sinusoids slow the blood to allow exchange.

IAE-1-SRC-0643 Arterio-venous anastomosis is absent in:
['Lips', 'Heart', 'Skin of nose', 'Hand', 'Mucous membrane of intestine'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Heart because the myocardium is supplied by coronary arteries that behave as functional end arteries without effective arteriovenous shunts. Arteriovenous anastomoses are plentiful in the skin of the lips, nose and hand, where they regulate heat loss, and in the intestinal mucosa. Their absence in the heart contributes to the severity of coronary occlusion.

IAE-1-SRC-0644 Which of the following is a tortuous artery?
['Facial artery', 'Lingual artery', 'Uterine artery', 'Splenic artery', 'All of the above'] KEY 4
