The correct answer is All of the above because each of these arteries is characteristically tortuous, an adaptation that allows the organ it supplies to move or to enlarge without stretching the vessel. The facial and lingual arteries accommodate movements of the face and tongue, the uterine artery accommodates pregnancy and the splenic artery accommodates changes in splenic size.

IAE-1-SRC-0645 Lymph capillaries are absent in:
['Avascular structures', 'Articular cartilage', 'Nails', 'Central nervous system', 'All of the above'] KEY 4
The correct answer is All of the above because lymphatics are lacking wherever there are no blood vessels, as in cartilage, nails, cornea and epidermis, and also in the central nervous system, which drains instead through perivascular routes into the cerebrospinal fluid. Each option is therefore individually correct.

IAE-1-SRC-0646 Lymph capillaries:
['Start blindly', 'Are present in all tissues of body', 'Are permeable to small particles only', 'Are smaller in caliber than blood capillaries', 'All the above'] KEY 0
The correct answer is that they start blindly, because lymph capillaries begin as closed-ended endothelial tubes in the tissue spaces. They are not present in all tissues, they are wider and more permeable than blood capillaries, and their loose junctions admit large particles and even cells, which is how tumour cells and bacteria spread. Therefore the composite option is wrong.

IAE-1-SRC-0647 As regards lymph vessels, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['They contain numerous valves', 'They accompany veins', 'They drain into venous system', 'All of them end in thoracic duct', 'Are interrupted by one or more lymph nodes'] KEY 3
The correct answer is that all of them end in the thoracic duct, which is false because lymph from the right side of the head and neck, the right upper limb and the right thorax drains through the right lymphatic duct. The other statements are true: lymph vessels are richly valved, accompany veins, are interrupted by nodes and finally empty into the great veins at the root of the neck.

IAE-1-SRC-0648 As regards lymph nodes, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['They become enlarged when inflamed', 'Each node receives several afferent lymphatics', 'Efferent lymph vessels leave the node from its convex surface', 'Lie along the course of lymph vessels', 'They filter the circulating lymph'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that efferent vessels leave from the convex surface, which is incorrect because efferent lymphatics leave at the hilum, the concave surface, while numerous afferents enter over the convex surface. The other statements are true: nodes enlarge in inflammation, lie along lymph vessels and filter the lymph.

IAE-1-SRC-0649 Which of the following has rich lymphatics?
['Bone marrow', 'Spinal cord', 'Spleen', 'Eyeball', 'Dermis of skin'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the Dermis of the skin, which contains a dense superficial and deep lymphatic plexus draining to the regional nodes. The spinal cord, like the rest of the central nervous system, has no lymphatics, and the eyeball and bone marrow are likewise devoid of them. The splenic pulp also lacks true lymphatic capillaries.

IAE-1-SRC-0650 What are the anatomical positions you know?
 KEY
The correct answer is the anatomical position together with the supine, prone, lithotomy and lateral positions, because anatomical terminology is meaningless unless referred to a fixed standard posture. Note particularly that the palms face forwards, which is why the radius is lateral and the ulna medial.

IAE-1-SRC-0651 What are the anatomical planes?
 KEY
The correct answer is median/sagittal, coronal and transverse, because these three mutually perpendicular planes are the reference frame for description and for all cross-sectional imaging. Only the median plane gives equal halves; parasagittal planes give unequal parts.

IAE-1-SRC-0652 What are the Langer's lines?
 KEY
The correct answer is that Langer's lines represent the direction of the dermal collagen fibres, because this is what determines wound gaping and scarring. Incisions should therefore be planned parallel to them wherever possible.

IAE-1-SRC-0653 What are the skin creases?
 KEY
The correct answer is that skin creases are flexure lines produced by firm attachment of the skin to deep structures at sites of movement. They differ from Langer's lines, which are invisible and reflect dermal collagen orientation.

IAE-1-SRC-0654 Mention sites where the superficial fascia is firmly attached to the skin?
 KEY
The correct answer is the scalp, palm, sole, skin creases and external ear, because in these sites fibrous septa tie the dermis to the deeper layers. The clinical importance is free bleeding and gaping of wounds and limited mobility of the skin.

IAE-1-SRC-0655 Mention sites where the superficial fascia is loosely attached to the skin?
 KEY
The correct answer is the dorsum of the hand and foot, penis, scrotum and eyelids, because loose areolar superficial fascia there permits free skin mobility. The clinical consequence is that these are the first sites to show swelling in oedema.

IAE-1-SRC-0656 Mention sites where the superficial fascia is devoid of fatty tissue?
 KEY
The correct answer is the eyelids, auricle, penis and scrotum, because the superficial fascia in these regions is purely loose areolar tissue with no fatty layer. This is a constant examination point and contrasts with the thick fatty panniculus of the abdominal wall and buttock.

IAE-1-SRC-0657 Enumerate the functions of the superficial fascia?
 KEY
The correct answer covers fat storage, contour, insulation, protection, mobility, and the conveying of cutaneous vessels and nerves, because all of these depend on the areolar and adipose composition of the layer. Remember that the muscles of facial expression are the classic exception of muscle within superficial fascia.

IAE-1-SRC-0658 Mention sites without deep fascia?
 KEY
The correct answer is the face, the anterior abdominal wall and the surface of subcutaneous bones, because in each of these sites a tough inelastic sheet would be functionally harmful. This contrasts with the limbs, where the deep fascia is thick and forms compartments and retinacula.

IAE-1-SRC-0659 Enumerate the functions of the deep fascia?
 KEY
The correct answer includes retention of muscles, compartmentation, retinacula and fibrous sheaths, vascular sheaths and the venous pump, because all arise from the tough, inelastic, well-organised nature of this layer. The compartment concept also explains compartment syndrome clinically.

IAE-1-SRC-0660 What are the curves of the vertebral column?
 KEY
The correct answer is two primary curves (thoracic and sacral) and two secondary curves (cervical and lumbar), because the primary curves persist from the fetal C-shape while the secondary ones develop with head control and with walking. These curves give the column its resilience.

IAE-1-SRC-0661 Classify the bones according to their shapes with examples for each type?
 KEY
The correct answer is long, short, flat, irregular, pneumatic and sesamoid bones with their examples, because this shape-based classification is the standard one used throughout the course. Sesamoid bones are noteworthy because they reduce friction and alter the angle of pull of a tendon.

IAE-1-SRC-0662 Describe the structure of a growing long bone. (A labelled diagram is required in the printed paper.)
 KEY
The correct answer must include the diaphysis, epiphysis, metaphysis, epiphyseal plate, medullary cavity, periosteum and endosteum, because the examiner's diagram is marked on these labels. The key functional pair is the epiphyseal plate for length and the periosteum for width.

IAE-1-SRC-0663 Classify the fibrous joints with examples for each type?
 KEY
The correct answer is suture, syndesmosis and gomphosis with their examples, because these are the three recognised fibrous joints, all lacking a cavity. The inferior tibiofibular joint is the standard syndesmosis example and is frequently asked.

IAE-1-SRC-0664 Classify the cartilaginous joints with examples for each type?
 KEY
The correct answer distinguishes primary (hyaline, temporary, may be lateral) from secondary (fibrocartilage, permanent, median plane) cartilaginous joints. Note that the manubriosternal joint and the symphysis pubis are the classic secondary examples.

IAE-1-SRC-0665 Classify the synovial joints with examples for each type?
 KEY
The correct answer groups the six types by the number of axes: hinge and pivot (uniaxial), ellipsoid and saddle (biaxial), ball-and-socket and plane (multiaxial). The carpometacarpal joint of the thumb as the saddle joint and the wrist as the ellipsoid joint are the most frequently examined examples.

IAE-1-SRC-0666 What are the factors that maintain joint stability? Add a note on the ligaments.
 KEY
The correct answer is bony, ligamentous and muscular factors, with muscle tone the most important in mobile joints. The note on ligaments must mention that they are inelastic, poorly vascular, slow to heal and richly supplied with proprioceptive endings.

IAE-1-SRC-0667 Describe the structure of a typical synovial joint. (A labelled diagram is required in the printed paper.)
 KEY
The correct answer must name articular cartilage, fibrous capsule, synovial membrane, joint cavity with synovial fluid, and the accessory structures, since these are the marked labels. Remember that the synovial membrane never covers the articular cartilage, a common examination trap.

IAE-1-SRC-0668 Classify the skeletal muscles according to their shapes with examples for each type?
 KEY
The correct answer covers parallel, pennate (uni-, bi-, multi-, circum-), cruciate, spiral and sphincteric muscles with their standard examples, because the examiner's matching questions draw directly on this list. Pennate arrangement trades range of movement for power.

IAE-1-SRC-0669 Describe the structure of a typical spinal nerve. (A labelled diagram is required in the printed paper.)
 KEY
The correct answer must state that the dorsal root is sensory with a ganglion, the ventral root is motor, the trunk is mixed and divides into dorsal and ventral rami, and that white and grey rami communicantes connect it to the sympathetic chain. A common error is to attribute the plexuses to the dorsal rami; the plexuses are formed by ventral rami.

IAE-1-SRC-0670 Describe the systemic circulation?
 KEY
The correct answer traces left atrium to left ventricle to aorta to body capillaries and back by the venae cavae to the right atrium. The contrast with the pulmonary circulation, in which the artery carries deoxygenated and the veins oxygenated blood, must be stated.

IAE-1-SRC-0671 Mention types of arteries?
 KEY
The correct answer gives both the structural classification (elastic, muscular, arterioles) and the functional one (anatomical and functional end arteries), because both are asked in the MCQ paper. The central artery of the retina and the coronary arteries are the required examples.

IAE-1-SRC-0672 Mention types of arteriovenous connection?
 KEY
The correct answer is capillaries, sinusoids, cavernous tissue and arterio-venous anastomoses, because these are the four channels by which blood passes from the arterial to the venous side. The thermoregulatory role of the A-V anastomosis is the point most often examined.

IAE-1-SRC-0673 List areas without lymphatics?
 KEY
The correct answer is the avascular structures plus the CNS, eyeball, internal ear, bone marrow and splenic pulp, because these have alternative drainage arrangements. The clinical relevance is that tumours arising in them do not spread by the lymphatic route.

IAE-1-SRC-0674 List 3 lymphatic aggregates?
 KEY
The correct answer is the tonsils, the Peyer's patches of the ileum and the appendix, because these are the three aggregates named in the course; the thymus and spleen may be added. They are strategically placed at the entrances of the respiratory and alimentary tracts.

IAE-1-SRC-0675 List 2 sites rich in lymphatics?
 KEY
The correct answer is the dermis of the skin and the mucous (and serous) membranes, because these surfaces are the body's interfaces with the exterior and need efficient drainage and immune surveillance. It contrasts directly with the avascular structures and the CNS, which have none.

IAE-1-SRC-0676 Incisions must be made parallel to Langer's lines to ___. They run longitudinally in ___ and transversely in ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are that a parallel incision separates rather than cuts the dermal collagen bundles, so the wound does not gape and the scar is fine; the lines run longitudinally in the limbs and transversely in the neck and trunk. An incision across the lines gapes and heals with a wide scar.

IAE-1-SRC-0677 Factors maintaining stability of the joint are ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the bony, ligamentous and muscular factors, since these are the three classical groups; muscle tone is the most important in mobile joints such as the shoulder, while bony fit dominates in the hip.

IAE-1-SRC-0678 Torn ligament (sprain) heals slowly because the ligaments are ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is that ligaments are poorly vascular, so the repair process is slow; being dense inelastic fibrous tissue with few vessels, they also heal by fibrous scar rather than by regeneration.

IAE-1-SRC-0679 Impulse can pass from a neuron to the other by meeting of ___ of one neuron with ___ of the other neuron, and this is called ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the axon terminals of one neuron, the dendrites or cell body of the next, and the synapse. Transmission at the synapse is chemical and one-way, which determines the direction of conduction in a nerve pathway.

IAE-1-SRC-0680 The CNS is covered by 3 meninges which are ___, ___, ___ with the CSF in the ___ space.
 KEY
The correct answers are dura, arachnoid and pia mater from outside inwards, with the cerebrospinal fluid in the subarachnoid space between the arachnoid and the pia. The subdural and extradural spaces are only potential spaces and contain no CSF.

IAE-1-SRC-0681 The ventral rami of all the thoracic & upper 2 lumbar spinal nerves are ___ while the middle sacral segments (S2,3,4) of the spinal cord are ___.
 KEY
The correct answers are the sympathetic (thoraco-lumbar) outflow from T1-L2 and the parasympathetic (sacral) outflow from S2, S3 and S4. The preganglionic sympathetic fibres leave in the ventral roots and reach the sympathetic chain through the white rami communicantes, which exist only at these levels.

IAE-1-SRC-0682 The portal vein is the vein situated between ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is between two sets of capillaries, which is the defining feature of a portal circulation; the hepatic portal vein begins in the capillaries of the gut and ends in the sinusoids of the liver. The hypophyseal portal system is the other classical example.

IAE-1-SRC-0683 Anatomical end arteries are the arteries that have no ___ (e.g. ___).
 KEY
The correct answers are the complete absence of anastomosis and the central artery of the retina. Because no collateral channel exists, occlusion of such an artery causes infarction of the whole territory it supplies, in this case sudden blindness.

IAE-1-SRC-0684 Functional end arteries, the anastomosis are present but ___ (e.g. ___ and ___).
 KEY
The correct answers are that the anastomoses are inadequate, and the standard examples are the coronary and cerebral arteries. This is why coronary occlusion produces myocardial infarction and cerebral occlusion produces a stroke, even though small communications exist.

IAE-1-SRC-0685 Arterio-venous connection includes: ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the four channels by which arterial blood reaches the veins. Capillaries are the usual route, sinusoids occur in liver, spleen and bone marrow, cavernous tissue in the penis and nasal mucosa, and A-V anastomoses bypass the capillary bed to regulate temperature.

IAE-1-SRC-0686 Lymphatic aggregations are found in: ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the tonsils, the Peyer's patches and the appendix, which are unencapsulated masses of lymphoid tissue guarding the entrances of the respiratory and alimentary tracts. The thymus and spleen are the encapsulated lymphoid organs.

IAE-1-SRC-0687 Sites without lymphatics are: ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the avascular structures plus the CNS, eyeball, internal ear, bone marrow and splenic pulp, because these drain their tissue fluid by other routes such as the cerebrospinal fluid or wide venous sinusoids.

IAE-1-SRC-0688 Avascular structures include ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the epidermis, hair, nails, cornea, lens and articular cartilage, all of which are nourished by diffusion from neighbouring tissue fluid rather than by their own vessels. Being avascular they also have no lymphatics and heal poorly.

IAE-1-SRC-0689 Sites rich in lymphatics include ___.
 KEY
The correct answer is the dermis and the mucous and serous membranes, the surfaces at which the body meets the exterior and needs both drainage and immune surveillance. This richness explains the early lymphatic spread of skin and mucosal carcinomas.

IAE-1-SRC-0690 In anatomical position, all the following statements are true EXCEPT:
['The person is standing erect.', 'The arms are straight by the sides.', 'Palms are facing forward.', 'Legs are crossed.'] KEY 3
In anatomical position, the legs are straight and close to each other, not crossed.

IAE-1-SRC-0691 The sagittal plane:
['Is a horizontal plane.', 'Lies parallel to the median plane.', 'Divides the body into upper and lower parts.', 'Divides the body into anterior and posterior parts.'] KEY 1
A sagittal plane is vertical, lies parallel to the median plane, and divides the body into right and left parts.

IAE-1-SRC-0692 A structure present towards the front of the body is described as:
['Anterior.', 'Posterior.', 'Superior.', 'Inferior.'] KEY 0
Anterior describes a position toward the front of the body.

IAE-1-SRC-0693 A structure present in the sole of the foot is described as:
['Ventral.', 'Dorsal.', 'Palmar.', 'Plantar.'] KEY 3
Plantar refers to the sole of the foot.

IAE-1-SRC-0694 A point present away from the origin of a structure is described as:
['Superior.', 'Inferior.', 'Proximal.', 'Distal.'] KEY 3
Distal means farther from the origin or point of attachment of a structure.

IAE-1-SRC-0695 Approximation of two opposing surfaces is called:
['Flexion.', 'Extension.', 'Adduction.', 'Abduction.'] KEY 0
Flexion decreases the angle between articulating parts and approximates opposing surfaces.

IAE-1-SRC-0696 Abduction of the fingers is movement away from the...... finger.
['Little.', 'Ring.', 'Middle.', 'Index.', 'Thumb.'] KEY 2
The middle finger is the central axis of the hand; finger abduction is movement away from it.

IAE-1-SRC-0697 Which of the following is NOT a rotatory movement?
['Pronation.', 'Supination.', 'Retraction.', 'Eversion.'] KEY 2
Retraction is posterior movement of a structure rather than a rotatory movement.

IAE-1-SRC-0698 Rotation of the foot to make the sole face medially is called:
['Dorsiflexion.', 'Plantar flexion.', 'Inversion.', 'Eversion.'] KEY 2
Inversion turns the sole of the foot medially.

IAE-1-SRC-0699 Which of the following is a combined movement?
['Flexion.', 'Circumduction.', 'Supination.', 'Pronation.'] KEY 1
Circumduction combines flexion, abduction, extension, and adduction.

IAE-1-SRC-0700 One of the following is an axial bone:
['Clavicle.', 'Mandible.', 'Scapula.', 'Hip bone.'] KEY 1
The mandible belongs to the skull and therefore to the axial skeleton.

IAE-1-SRC-0701 The shoulder girdle is formed by:
['Clavicle.', 'Scapula.', 'Sternum.', 'A + B.'] KEY 3
The shoulder girdle consists of the clavicle and scapula.

IAE-1-SRC-0702 Regarding ossification of bones, choose the correct statement:
['Clavicle ossifies in a connective tissue membrane.', 'Skull vault bones ossify in cartilage models.', 'Primary centers of ossification appear in the epiphyses.', 'Secondary centers of ossification appear before birth.'] KEY 0
The clavicle ossifies predominantly in a connective-tissue membrane. Primary centers usually appear before birth in diaphyses; secondary centers usually appear after birth in epiphyses.

IAE-1-SRC-0703 A sesamoid bone is:
['Patella.', 'Vertebra.', 'Pisiform.', 'A + C.'] KEY 3
Both the patella and pisiform are sesamoid bones.

IAE-1-SRC-0704 Regarding the two ends of a growing bone, choose the incorrect statement:
['They are called epiphyses.', 'They enclose medullary cavities.', 'They are covered by hyaline cartilage.', 'They ossify by secondary centers of ossification.'] KEY 1
Epiphyses do not enclose medullary cavities; the principal medullary cavity is in the diaphysis.

IAE-1-SRC-0705 A bone gets its blood supply through:
['Nutrient artery.', 'Periosteal arteries.', 'Arteries of the attached muscles.', 'All of the above.'] KEY 3
Bone receives blood from nutrient, periosteal, and muscular arterial sources; the nutrient artery is the most important source.

IAE-1-SRC-0706 One of the following is NOT a cartilaginous joint:
['Intervertebral disc.', 'First costal cartilage.', 'Symphysis pubis.', 'Inferior tibiofibular joint.'] KEY 3
The inferior tibiofibular joint is a fibrous syndesmosis rather than a cartilaginous joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0707 A pivot synovial joint is a...... joint.
['Plane.', 'Uniaxial.', 'Biaxial.', 'Polyaxial.'] KEY 1
A pivot joint permits rotation around a single axis and is therefore uniaxial.

IAE-1-SRC-0708 An example of a hinge synovial joint is:
['Superior radioulnar.', 'Elbow.', 'Sternoclavicular.', 'Ankle.', 'B + D.'] KEY 4
The elbow and ankle are hinge joints. The superior radioulnar joint is pivot, while the sternoclavicular joint is saddle.

IAE-1-SRC-0709 All the following combinations are true EXCEPT:
['Wrist joint — ellipsoid.', 'Middle radioulnar joint — pivot.', 'Carpometacarpal joint of the thumb — saddle.', 'Intercarpal joints — plane.'] KEY 1
The middle radioulnar joint is a fibrous syndesmosis, not a pivot synovial joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0710 Cardiac muscles are described as:
['Striated voluntary.', 'Branched voluntary.', 'Branched involuntary.', 'Smooth involuntary.'] KEY 2
Cardiac muscle fibers are striated, branched, and involuntary.

IAE-1-SRC-0711 Regarding skeletal muscles, choose the correct statement:
['Their proximal attachment is the insertion.', 'They are striated involuntary.', 'Their fibers are not branched.', 'They are supplied by the autonomic nervous system.'] KEY 2
Skeletal muscle fibers are striated, voluntary, and normally unbranched.

IAE-1-SRC-0712 A bipennate muscle is:
['Rectus femoris.', 'Rectus abdominis.', 'Deltoid.', 'Tibialis anterior.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 0
Rectus femoris is bipennate; rectus abdominis is strap-like with intersections, deltoid is multipennate, and tibialis anterior is circumpennate in the source classification.

IAE-1-SRC-0713 One of the following is a parallel arrangement of skeletal muscle fibers:
['Triangular.', 'Fusiform.', 'Unipennate.', 'Bipennate.', 'Spiralized.'] KEY 1
Fusiform muscles are a form of parallel muscle-fiber arrangement.

IAE-1-SRC-0714 Regarding the deep fascia, choose the incorrect statement:
['It invests the deep structures.', 'It is a dense inelastic fibrous membrane.', 'It covers the whole body.', 'It gives attachment for some skeletal muscles.', 'It forms retinacula around the distal joints.'] KEY 2
Deep fascia does not cover the entire body; it is absent in areas including the face, scalp, and anterior abdominal wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0715 Regarding the nerve cell, all the following statements are true EXCEPT:
['The cell body contains the nucleus.', 'The axon is a single long process.', 'The junction between two cells is called a synapse.', 'The axon transmits impulses towards the cell body.'] KEY 3
An axon transmits impulses away from the cell body, whereas dendrites transmit impulses toward it.

IAE-1-SRC-0716 The number of cervical spinal cord segments is:
['1.', '5.', '7.', '8.', '12.'] KEY 3
The spinal cord has 31 segments: 8 cervical, 12 thoracic, 5 lumbar, 5 sacral, and 1 coccygeal.

IAE-1-SRC-0717 The cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) circulates in:
['Extradural space.', 'Subdural space.', 'Subarachnoid space.', 'Central canal of the spinal cord.', 'C + D.'] KEY 4
CSF circulates in the subarachnoid space and within the central canal of the spinal cord.

IAE-1-SRC-0718 One of the following is NOT a part of the brain stem:
['Midbrain.', 'Interbrain (diencephalon).', 'Pons.', 'Medulla oblongata.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 1
The brainstem consists of the midbrain, pons, and medulla oblongata; the diencephalon is not part of it.

IAE-1-SRC-0719 Regarding the spinal nerve, choose the incorrect statement:
['The dorsal root is sensory.', 'The ventral root is motor.', 'The trunk is mixed.', 'The dorsal ramus tends to form plexuses.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 3
Dorsal rami are relatively small and generally do not form plexuses; the larger ventral rami tend to form plexuses.

IAE-1-SRC-0720 In infants, the spinal cord ends at the level of the lower border of...... lumbar vertebra.
['First.', 'Second.', 'Third.', 'Fourth.'] KEY 2
In infants, the spinal cord terminates near the lower border of L3; in adults it ends near the lower border of L1 in the source explanation.

IAE-1-SRC-0721 The preganglionic parasympathetic fibers arise with the following cranial nerves EXCEPT:
['Oculomotor.', 'Trigeminal.', 'Facial.', 'Glossopharyngeal.', 'Vagus.'] KEY 1
Preganglionic parasympathetic fibers arise with cranial nerves III, VII, IX, and X, but not with the trigeminal nerve.

IAE-1-SRC-0722 One of the following is NOT a function of the parasympathetic system:
['Constriction of the pupil.', 'Constriction of the bronchi.', 'Stimulation of all glands.', 'Slowing of the heart rate.', 'Stimulation of intestinal motility.'] KEY 2
The parasympathetic system does not stimulate all glands; sweat glands, for example, receive sympathetic stimulation.

IAE-1-SRC-0723 All the following statements are true regarding the systemic circulation EXCEPT:
['Superior and inferior venae cavae open into the right atrium.', 'Tricuspid valve lies between right atrium and right ventricle.', 'Pulmonary artery carries oxygenated blood.', 'Left atrium receives blood from pulmonary veins.', 'Mitral valve lies between left atrium and left ventricle.'] KEY 2
The pulmonary artery carries deoxygenated blood, while the pulmonary veins carry oxygenated blood.

IAE-1-SRC-0724 The valve lying between the right ventricle and the artery leaving it is the...... valve.
['Mitral.', 'Tricuspid.', 'Pulmonary.', 'Aortic.'] KEY 2
The pulmonary valve lies between the right ventricle and the pulmonary trunk.

IAE-1-SRC-0725 Blood vessels that carry blood away from the heart are called:
['Arteries.', 'Veins.', 'Capillaries.', 'Lymphatics.'] KEY 0
Arteries are defined as vessels that carry blood away from the heart.

IAE-1-SRC-0726 Regarding the veins, choose the correct statement:
['They have elastic walls.', 'They may have valves.', 'They are pulsating.', 'They have narrow lumens.'] KEY 1
Veins may contain valves, particularly where blood must flow against gravity.

IAE-1-SRC-0727 The heart gets its arterial blood supply through:
['Bronchial arteries.', 'Coronary arteries.', 'Coronary sinus.', 'Pulmonary arteries.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 1
The myocardium receives arterial blood through the right and left coronary arteries; most venous drainage enters the coronary sinus.

IAE-1-SRC-0728 The circulation of the blood from the intestine to the liver is called:
['Pulmonary circulation.', 'Systemic circulation.', 'Portal circulation.', 'Coronary circulation.'] KEY 2
Blood from the intestine reaches the liver through the hepatic portal circulation.

IAE-1-SRC-0729 All the following statements are true about capillaries EXCEPT:
['They are minute channels.', 'They are numerous in cornea.', 'They have very thin walls.', 'They allow gas exchange.'] KEY 1
The cornea is avascular and contains no capillaries; capillaries are also absent from cartilage and the epidermis.

IAE-1-SRC-0730 Match each muscle form with its corresponding example.
 KEY
The printed key maps quadrilateral to quadratus lumborum, fusiform to biceps brachii, strap-like to sartorius, strap-like with tendinous intersections to rectus abdominis, and unipennate to flexor pollicis longus.

IAE-1-SRC-0731 Match each bone with its definition.
 KEY
The humerus is the arm bone, the scaphoid is a carpal bone, the radius is lateral in the forearm, the femur is the thigh bone, and the ulna is medial in anatomical position.

IAE-1-SRC-0732 The plane dividing the body vertically into a large right part and a small left part is:
['Right paramedian plane', 'Left parasagittal plane', 'Coronal plane', 'Horizontal plane'] KEY 1
A left parasagittal plane is a vertical plane parallel to and left of the median plane, so it divides the body into a larger right part and a smaller left part. A right paramedian plane produces the opposite proportions. Coronal and horizontal planes divide the body into anterior-posterior and superior-inferior parts, respectively.

IAE-1-SRC-0733 Which of the following bones does not belong to the axial skeleton?
['Maxilla', 'Sacrum', 'Patella', 'Floating ribs'] KEY 2
The patella is part of the lower limb and therefore belongs to the appendicular skeleton. The maxilla is a skull bone, the sacrum is part of the vertebral column, and the floating ribs are part of the thoracic cage; all three belong to the axial skeleton.

IAE-1-SRC-0734 Regarding the clavicle, which statement is incorrect?
['It forms the pectoral girdle with the scapula', 'It is an example of a long bone', 'It does not participate significantly in blood-cell production', 'It ossifies completely from membrane'] KEY 3
The clavicle is predominantly ossified by intramembranous ossification, but it does not ossify completely from membrane because its ends have cartilaginous contributions. It forms the pectoral girdle with the scapula and is classified as a modified long bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0735 Which term can be used to describe a structure on the sole of the foot?
['Dorsal', 'Distal', 'Palmar', 'Plantar'] KEY 3
Plantar refers to the sole of the foot. Dorsal refers to the upper surface of the foot, distal describes position farther from the trunk or point of origin, and palmar refers to the palm of the hand.

IAE-1-SRC-0736 The nutrient artery is directed downward in all the following bones EXCEPT:
['Humerus', 'Tibia', 'Fibula', 'Ulna'] KEY 3
The nutrient canal of the ulna is directed toward the elbow and therefore upward. In the humerus it is directed downward toward the elbow, while in the tibia and fibula it is directed downward away from the knee. This follows the rule that nutrient canals are directed away from the more actively growing end.

IAE-1-SRC-0737 Which of the following sites lacks conventional superficial fascia?
['Back of the neck', 'Scalp', 'Scrotum', 'Face'] KEY 2
The scrotum lacks the usual fat-containing superficial fascia. Its subcutaneous tissue is modified to form the dartos layer, which contains smooth muscle and no fat. The scalp, face, and back of the neck possess superficial fascial tissue.

IAE-1-SRC-0738 Which of the following is the best example of a sesamoid bone?
['Maxilla', 'Clavicle', 'Pisiform', 'Scapula'] KEY 2
The pisiform is a sesamoid bone embedded in the tendon of flexor carpi ulnaris. The maxilla is an irregular bone, the clavicle is a modified long bone, and the scapula is a flat bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0739 Choose the incorrect statement:
['False ribs have costal cartilages that do not reach the sternum directly', 'The pelvic girdle is formed by the hip bones', 'In the anatomical position, the hand is pronated', 'The big toe has two phalanges'] KEY 2
In the anatomical position, the forearm is supinated and the palms face anteriorly; the hand is not pronated. False ribs do not attach directly to the sternum, the paired hip bones form the pelvic girdle, and the big toe has proximal and distal phalanges only.

IAE-1-SRC-0740 Appositional growth of a long bone is a function of the:
['Metaphysis', 'Epiphyseal cartilage', 'Diaphysis', 'Periosteum'] KEY 3
Appositional growth increases the thickness and diameter of a bone through osteogenic activity in the inner layer of the periosteum. Epiphyseal cartilage is responsible for longitudinal growth, while metaphysis and diaphysis are regions of the bone rather than the principal membrane responsible for appositional growth.

IAE-1-SRC-0741 The most metabolically active part of a growing long bone is the:
['Periosteum', 'Diaphysis', 'Metaphysis', 'Epiphysis'] KEY 2
The metaphysis is highly vascular and undergoes active growth and remodeling adjacent to the epiphyseal plate. The diaphysis forms the shaft, the epiphysis forms the end of the bone, and the periosteum is important for appositional growth but is not the most active region in longitudinal growth.

IAE-1-SRC-0742 Regarding the skin, choose the incorrect statement:
['The skin has a role in the activation of vitamin D3', 'Skin creases indicate strong collagen attachment to underlying structures', 'An incision in the forearm is made horizontally', 'The skin is loosely attached to the superficial fascia on the dorsum of the hand'] KEY 2
Surgical incisions in the limbs generally follow the predominantly longitudinal cleavage lines rather than being made horizontally across the forearm. The skin participates in vitamin D3 synthesis, creases occur where the skin is firmly attached to deeper structures, and the skin over the dorsum of the hand is relatively mobile.

IAE-1-SRC-0743 Regarding the foot, choose the incorrect statement:
['There are seven tarsal bones', 'The middle toe is the axis of abduction of the toes', 'A structure in the sole is described as plantar', 'The big toe has two phalanges'] KEY 1
The axis for abduction and adduction of the toes passes through the second toe, not the middle or third toe. The foot has seven tarsal bones, plantar refers to the sole, and the big toe has two phalanges.

IAE-1-SRC-0744 The movement in which the radius and ulna become parallel is:
['Inversion', 'Eversion', 'Pronation', 'Supination'] KEY 3
During supination, the radius and ulna lie parallel. During pronation, the radius crosses anteriorly over the ulna. Inversion and eversion are movements of the foot rather than the forearm.

IAE-1-SRC-0745 Which of the following joints does not belong to the cartilaginous type?
['Symphysis pubis', 'Third costochondral joint', 'Third sternocostal joint', 'Epiphyseal cartilage'] KEY 2
The third sternocostal joint is a plane synovial joint. The symphysis pubis is a secondary cartilaginous joint, while the costochondral joints and epiphyseal cartilage are primary cartilaginous joints.

IAE-1-SRC-0746 The movement of a limb away from the trunk is:
['Circumduction', 'Flexion', 'Adduction', 'Abduction'] KEY 3
Abduction moves a limb away from the median plane of the body. Adduction moves it toward the median plane, flexion decreases the angle at a joint, and circumduction combines flexion, extension, abduction, and adduction.

IAE-1-SRC-0747 Regarding the fifth rib, choose the incorrect statement:
['It articulates with its costal cartilage by a primary cartilaginous joint', 'It is a false rib', 'Its costal cartilage reaches the sternum', 'It articulates with the vertebral column posteriorly'] KEY 1
The fifth rib is a true rib because its costal cartilage attaches directly to the sternum. Its costochondral joint is primary cartilaginous, and the rib articulates posteriorly with the thoracic vertebral column.

IAE-1-SRC-0748 Which of the following joints does not belong to the synovial variety?
['Intercarpal joints', 'Carpometacarpal joint of the thumb', 'Inferior tibiofibular joint', 'Inferior radioulnar joint'] KEY 2
The inferior tibiofibular joint is a fibrous syndesmosis. Intercarpal joints are plane synovial joints, the first carpometacarpal joint is a saddle synovial joint, and the inferior radioulnar joint is a pivot synovial joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0749 Regarding sutures, select the incorrect statement:
['They belong to fibrous joints', 'They may ossify completely', 'They have a wide range of movement', 'They separate skull bones'] KEY 2
Sutures are essentially immobile fibrous joints and do not permit a wide range of movement. They occur between skull bones and may later ossify to form synostoses.

IAE-1-SRC-0750 All the following are examples of pinnate muscles EXCEPT:
['Tibialis anterior', 'Flexor pollicis longus', 'Deltoid', 'Rectus abdominis'] KEY 3
Rectus abdominis is a parallel, strap-like muscle interrupted by tendinous intersections rather than a pinnate muscle. Tibialis anterior and flexor pollicis longus have unipennate architecture, while the deltoid is multipennate.

IAE-1-SRC-0751 During a surgical operation, a longitudinal incision could be made at which of the following sites?
['Neck', 'Thoracic wall', 'Forearm', 'Abdominal wall'] KEY 2
Skin cleavage lines in the limbs generally run longitudinally, so a longitudinal incision is suitable in the forearm. Cleavage lines over the neck and trunk are predominantly transverse, making transverse incisions more favorable at those sites.

IAE-1-SRC-0752 Regarding sesamoid bones, all the following statements are true EXCEPT:
['Usually appear as small nodules.', 'They develop within tendons of muscles.', 'Patella is one of its examples.', 'They prevent obstruction of the blood supply to joints.', 'They help to reduce friction of tendon.'] KEY 3
Sesamoid bones reduce tendon friction, resist pressure and improve muscular leverage; they do not prevent obstruction of a joint's blood supply.

IAE-1-SRC-0753 As regards the neurons, choose the CORRECT statement:
['Its axon contains its nucleus.', 'Axons are short multiple processes.', 'Axons carry nerve impulses towards cell bodies.', 'Dendrites carry nerve impulses away from cell bodies.', 'Bundles of axons outside CNS form nerves.'] KEY 4
Bundles of axons in the peripheral nervous system are called nerves. A neuron's nucleus is in its cell body, dendrites generally conduct toward the cell body and the usually single axon conducts away.

IAE-1-SRC-0754 Menisci are present in:
['Shoulder.', 'Elbow.', 'Wrist.', 'Ankle.', 'Knee.'] KEY 4
The knee contains medial and lateral fibrocartilaginous menisci that improve congruity, distribute load and absorb shock.

IAE-1-SRC-0755 All the following lie within the grey matter of the spinal cord EXCEPT:
['Anterior horns.', 'Lateral horns.', 'Ascending tracts.', 'Grey commissure.', 'Central canal.'] KEY 2
Ascending tracts are bundles of axons in the spinal cord's white matter. The horns and grey commissure are grey matter, which surrounds the central canal.

IAE-1-SRC-0756 Example of an anatomical end artery:
['Coronary artery.', 'Mesenteric artery.', 'Cerebral artery.', 'Central retinal artery.', 'Renal artery.'] KEY 3
The central retinal artery is an anatomical end artery because it has no effective arterial anastomosis with neighbouring vessels.

IAE-1-SRC-0757 A parasagittal plane divides the body into:
['Right & left equal halves.', 'Right & left unequal halves.', 'Anterior & posterior parts.', 'Upper & lower equal parts.', 'Upper & lower unequal parts.'] KEY 1
A parasagittal plane runs parallel to the median plane but away from the midline, producing unequal right and left portions.

IAE-1-SRC-0758 One of the following joints is uniaxial:
['Shoulder.', 'Wrist.', 'Elbow.', 'Knee.'] KEY 2
The elbow is a uniaxial hinge joint whose principal movements are flexion and extension around a transverse axis.

IAE-1-SRC-0759 The long fibrous cord for attachment of muscles is called:
['Aponeurosis.', 'Tendon.', 'Bipennate.', 'Raphe.', 'Cruciate.'] KEY 1
A tendon is a cord of dense collagen that attaches a muscle to bone. An aponeurosis is a broad, flat tendinous sheet.

IAE-1-SRC-0760 An example of secondary cartilaginous joint:
['Symphysis pubis.', 'Sternocostal joint.', 'Costochondral joint.', 'Sacroiliac joint.', 'Sternoclavicular joint.'] KEY 0
The symphysis pubis is a permanent secondary cartilaginous joint in which hyaline-covered bone ends are united by fibrocartilage.

IAE-1-SRC-0761 Which ventral rami do not form nerve plexuses?
['Cervical', 'Thoracic', 'Lumbar', 'Sacral', 'Coccygeal'] KEY 1
Thoracic ventral rami remain segmental as intercostal and subcostal nerves rather than interlacing to form a plexus.

IAE-1-SRC-0762 The joints between the bones of the skull vault (cap) belong to:
['Synovial joint.', 'Syndesmosis.', 'Sutures.', 'Symphysis.', 'Gomphosis.'] KEY 2
The bones of the skull vault are united by sutures, which are immobile fibrous joints.

IAE-1-SRC-0763 The medial structure is the structure that lies:
['Closer to the upper end of the body.', 'Closer to the lower end of the body.', 'Closer to the front of the body.', 'Closer to the back of the body.', 'Closer to the median plane of the body.'] KEY 4
Medial describes a structure positioned closer to the body's median plane.

IAE-1-SRC-0764 All the following contain mixed nerve fibers EXCEPT:
['Dorsal rami.', 'Ventral rami.', 'Spinal nerve trunk.', 'Dorsal & ventral rami.', 'Dorsal & ventral roots.'] KEY 4
The dorsal and ventral roots are not mixed: the dorsal root is sensory and the ventral root is motor. The spinal nerve trunk and both rami are mixed.

IAE-1-SRC-0765 What is the type of joint that connects the roots of the teeth to their sockets?
['Gomphosis.', 'Sutures.', 'Cartilagenous.', 'Synovial.'] KEY 0
A gomphosis is a peg-and-socket fibrous joint connecting a tooth root to its alveolus through the periodontal ligament.

IAE-1-SRC-0766 The wrist joint is:
['Hinge.', 'Ellipsoid.', 'Bicondylar.', 'Ball and socket.'] KEY 1
The radiocarpal joint is a biaxial ellipsoid joint permitting flexion, extension, abduction, adduction and circumduction.

IAE-1-SRC-0767 The intermuscular septa and interosseous membranes:
['Surround the muscles of the upper and lower limbs.', 'Separate different groups of muscles which have different actions.', 'Are transverse thickened bands of deep fascia present at wrist joint.', 'Form tough sheaths around big blood vessels.'] KEY 1
Intermuscular septa and interosseous membranes help divide the limbs into osteofascial compartments containing muscle groups with related actions.

IAE-1-SRC-0768 All are characters of synovial joint EXCEPT:
['It has a joint cavity.', 'A capsule binds its articulating bones.', 'May contain intra-articular disc', 'The articular surfaces are covered by synovial membrane.', 'They are freely mobile joints.'] KEY 3
Synovial membrane lines the capsule but does not cover the articular surfaces, which are covered by articular cartilage.

IAE-1-SRC-0769 What is the type of joint of the intervertebral discs?
['Fibrous.', 'Primary cartilaginous.', 'Secondary cartilaginous.', 'Synovial.'] KEY 2
Adjacent vertebral bodies are united through intervertebral discs as secondary cartilaginous joints, or symphyses.

IAE-1-SRC-0770 Which one of the following joints is a hinge synovial?
['Superior radioulnar joint.', 'Shoulder joint.', 'Wrist joint.', 'Elbow joint.', 'Acromioclavicular joint.'] KEY 3
The elbow is a hinge synovial joint. The superior radioulnar joint is pivot, the shoulder is ball-and-socket, the wrist is ellipsoid and the acromioclavicular joint is plane.

IAE-1-SRC-0771 Primary cartilaginous joints:
['Are formed by fibrocartilage.', 'Do not ossify with age.', 'Show no movements.', 'Include intervertebral disc.', 'Appear between parts of sternum.'] KEY 2
Primary cartilaginous joints are immobile unions of hyaline cartilage. They commonly ossify with age; intervertebral discs and the manubriosternal joint are secondary cartilaginous joints.

IAE-1-SRC-0772 Choose the correct arrangement from outside inwards:
['Pia matter, subarachnoid space, arachnoid matter, subdural space & dura matter.', 'Dura matter, subdural space, pia matter, subarachnoid space & arachnoid matter.', 'Arachnoid matter, subdural space, pia matter, subarachnoid space & dura matter.', 'Dura matter, subdural space, arachnoid matter, subarachnoid space & pia matter.', 'Dura matter, subdural space, subarachnoid space, pia matter & arachnoid matter.'] KEY 3
From outside inward, the sequence is dura, potential subdural space, arachnoid, CSF-filled subarachnoid space and pia.

IAE-1-SRC-0773 Communication between arteries & veins in the tissues can be by:
['Direct connection between arterioles & venules.', 'Capillaries.', 'Cavernous tissue.', 'Sinusoids.', 'All of the above.'] KEY 4
Arterial blood can reach veins through ordinary capillaries, sinusoids, cavernous tissue or direct arteriovenous anastomoses.

IAE-1-SRC-0774 As regards the cartilaginous joints, choose INCORRECT statement:
['Secondary cartilaginous joints allow limited movement.', 'Syndesmosis is a type of primary cartilaginous joint.', 'Primary cartilaginous joints are usually temporary.', 'Secondary cartilaginous joints lie usually in median plane.', 'In secondary cartilaginous joints, articular surfaces are connected by fibrocartilage.'] KEY 1
A syndesmosis is a fibrous joint, not a primary cartilaginous joint. The remaining statements describe primary or secondary cartilaginous joints correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0775 What is the type of joint present at the ends of the growing long bones?
['Fibrous.', 'Primary cartilaginous.', 'Secondary cartilaginous.', 'Synovial.'] KEY 1
The epiphyseal plate is a temporary primary cartilaginous joint (synchondrosis).

IAE-1-SRC-0776 The smooth muscles are:
['Voluntary.', 'Present in the heart.', 'Present in the wall of blood vessels.', 'Attached to the bones.'] KEY 2
Smooth muscle is involuntary and forms part of vessel walls.

IAE-1-SRC-0777 Cervical segments of spinal cord are:
['Five.', 'Six.', 'Seven.', 'Eight.', 'Twelve.'] KEY 3
There are eight cervical spinal cord segments.

IAE-1-SRC-0778 As regards anastomoses, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Arterio-venous anastomoses are numerous in skin of nose.', 'An anatomical end artery has no anastomosis with other arteries.', 'Arterio-venous anastomosis helps to control body temperature.', 'Arterial anastomosis helps to compensate a block in blood flow.', 'Functional end arteries are sufficient to compensate a block of an artery.'] KEY 4
Functional end-artery anastomoses are inadequate to prevent ischemia after occlusion.

IAE-1-SRC-0779 Which one of the following is an ellipsoid joint?
['Shoulder.', 'Elbow.', 'Wrist.', 'Hip.', 'Knee.'] KEY 2
The radiocarpal (wrist) joint is an ellipsoid/condyloid joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0780 Which one of the following structures never lies inside synovial cavity?
['Articular cartilage.', 'Menisci.', 'Synovial fluid.', 'Fibrous capsule.', 'Ligaments.'] KEY 3
The fibrous capsule encloses the joint rather than occupying its cavity.

IAE-1-SRC-0781 Intra-membranous ossification occurs in:
['Clavicle.', 'Vertebrae.', 'Ribs.', 'Base of the skull.'] KEY 0
The clavicle ossifies predominantly by intramembranous ossification.

IAE-1-SRC-0782 The example of the muscle that is inserted in a raphe is:
['Digastric.', 'Biceps.', 'Popliteus.', 'Pharyngeal muscles.', 'Facial muscles.'] KEY 3
Pharyngeal constrictors insert posteriorly into the pharyngeal raphe.

IAE-1-SRC-0783 As regards the muscles, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['A bipennate muscle looks like a complete feather.', 'The most moveable part of the muscle is its origin.', 'A raphe is a mixture of fleshy and tendinous fibers.', 'The muscle may be inserted into the skin.', 'Most muscles have tendons attached to bones.'] KEY 1
The origin is generally the less movable attachment; insertion is usually more movable.

IAE-1-SRC-0784 An example of a triangular muscle is:
['Sartorius.', 'External abdominal oblique muscle.', 'Temporalis.', 'Rectus femoris.', 'Dorsal interosseous.'] KEY 2
Temporalis is a convergent, triangular muscle.

IAE-1-SRC-0785 Intra-cartilaginous ossification occurs in:
['Roof of the skull.', 'Clavicle.', 'Skull cap.', 'Vertebrae.'] KEY 3
Vertebrae develop mainly by endochondral (intracartilaginous) ossification.

IAE-1-SRC-0786 As regards the skeletal muscle, choose the INCORRECT statement.
['The origin is the most movable end of the muscle.', 'Contraction means approximation of origin and insertion.', 'Septa from deep fascia divide them into groups.', 'They are mostly under voluntary control.', 'They are innervated by motor, sensory and autonomic nerve fibers.'] KEY 0
The origin is usually the less movable attachment.

IAE-1-SRC-0787 Appendicular skeleton is formed of:
['Skull and mandible.', 'Bones of the upper and lower limbs.', 'Ribs and sternum.', 'Vertebral column.'] KEY 1
The appendicular skeleton includes limbs and their girdles.

IAE-1-SRC-0788 One of the functions of superficial fascia is:
['Presence of skin muscles.', 'Formation of aponeurosis.', 'Formation of broad sheets.', 'Formation of interosseous membranes.'] KEY 0
Superficial fascia contains or supports cutaneous muscles in certain regions.

IAE-1-SRC-0789 Primary cartilaginous joints:
['Are usually temporary.', 'Allow wide range of movement.', 'Are present between teeth and jaw.', 'Unite bones by fibrous tissue.', 'Unite lower ends of tibia and fibula.'] KEY 0
Synchondroses are commonly temporary and later ossify.

IAE-1-SRC-0790 Which one of the following muscles is a multipennate muscle?
['Flexor pollicis longus.', 'Rectus femoris.', 'Dorsal interossei.', 'Temporalis.', 'Deltoid.'] KEY 4
Deltoid is a classic multipennate muscle.

IAE-1-SRC-0791 The skeletal muscles are:
['Involuntary.', 'Present in the heart.', 'Present in the wall of blood vessels.', 'Attached to the bones.'] KEY 3
Most skeletal muscles attach to bones and produce voluntary movement.

IAE-1-SRC-0792 As regards blood vessels, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['Arteries have branches.', 'Veins have tributaries.', 'Venae comitantes accompany big veins.', 'Valves are numerous in limb veins.', 'Sinusoids slow down blood flow.'] KEY 2
Venae comitantes typically accompany medium-sized arteries, not big veins.

IAE-1-SRC-0793 The articular surface of the synovial joint is covered by:
['Synovial membrane.', 'Fibrous capsule.', 'Hyaline cartilage.', 'Capsular ligament.', 'Extracapsular ligament.'] KEY 2
Synovial joint surfaces are usually covered by hyaline articular cartilage.

IAE-1-SRC-0794 One of the functions of deep fascia is that it:
['Facilitates the movement of the skin.', 'Prevents heat loss from the body.', 'Contains many types of glands.', 'Forms the interosseous membranes.'] KEY 3
Deep fascia contributes to strong interosseous membranes.

IAE-1-SRC-0795 Which part of the spinal nerve does not contain motor fibers?
['Ventral ramus.', 'Dorsal root.', 'Ventral root.', 'Dorsal ramus.', 'Nerve trunk.'] KEY 1
The dorsal root carries sensory afferent fibers only.

IAE-1-SRC-0796 Secondary cartilaginous joints:
['Are formed partly by fibrocartilage.', 'Ossify with age.', 'Show no movements.', 'Include epiphyseal plate of long bones.'] KEY 0
Symphyses unite bones partly by fibrocartilage and permit limited movement.

IAE-1-SRC-0797 Which of the following is a rotary movement?
['Protraction.', 'Inversion.', 'Abduction.', 'Flexion.', 'Pronation.'] KEY 4
Pronation is rotation of the forearm that turns the palm posteriorly/downward.

IAE-1-SRC-0798 In which of the following cavities is yellow bone marrow found?
['Cranial cavity.', 'Joint cavity.', 'Medullary cavity.', 'Epiphyseal cavity.'] KEY 2
Yellow marrow occupies the medullary cavity of adult long bones.

IAE-1-SRC-0799 Which of the following is (are) not striated?
['Skeletal muscle and cardiac muscle.', 'Skeletal muscle and smooth muscle.', 'Smooth muscle and cardiac muscle.', 'Smooth muscle only.', 'Cardiac muscle only.'] KEY 3
Smooth muscle is non-striated; skeletal and cardiac muscle are striated.

IAE-1-SRC-0800 Which of the following positions describes anatomical position?
['Standing erect.', 'Curled into a ball, as a baby in the uterus.', 'Sitting comfortably with your hands on your knees.', "Lying on one's side.", 'The eyes looking to the side.'] KEY 0
The anatomical position is the standard erect standing posture used for anatomical description.

IAE-1-SRC-0801 The following is a feature of anatomical position:
['Palms of the hands directed medially.', 'Facing laterally.', 'Ankles are apart.', 'Trunk flexed.', 'Has upper limbs by side, and the palms facing forward.'] KEY 4
In anatomical position the upper limbs lie by the sides and the palms face forward.

IAE-1-SRC-0802 The vertical plane that divided the body into anterior and posterior parts is:
['Sagittal', 'Horizontal', 'Parasagittal', 'Median', 'Coronal'] KEY 4
A coronal plane divides the body into anterior and posterior parts.

IAE-1-SRC-0803 Regarding the anatomical planes, choose the correct answer:
['Sagittal plane divides body into anterior and posterior halves.', 'Coronal plane is a horizontal plane.', 'Parasagittal plane is perpendicular to median plane.', 'Coronal plane divides body into anterior and posterior parts.', 'Transverse plane divides the body into right and left parts.'] KEY 3
The coronal plane separates anterior from posterior parts of the body.

IAE-1-SRC-0804 Regarding the anatomical planes, one of the following is true:
['The median plane cuts the anterior surface of the body along the anterior median line.', 'Coronal plane is a horizontal plane at right angle to the sagittal plane.', 'The terms proximal and distal are used in limbs in place of anterior and posterior.', 'Horizontal planes are drawn parallel to the coronal plane.'] KEY 0
The median plane intersects the anterior surface along the anterior median line.

IAE-1-SRC-0805 ________ plane passes through the midline of the body, dividing it into right and left halves.
['Coronal', 'Midsagittal', 'Parasagittal', 'Transverse', 'Horizontal'] KEY 1
The midsagittal plane passes through the midline and divides the body into equal right and left halves.

IAE-1-SRC-0806 The following term is correct:
['Peripheral means near the center of the body.', 'Proximal means away from the trunk.', 'Inferior means nearer to the head.', 'Distal means away from the root of the limb.', 'Lateral means nearer to the median plane.'] KEY 3
Distal means farther from the root or point of attachment of a limb.

IAE-1-SRC-0807 Which directional term best describes the position of the phalanges with respect to carpal bones:
['Lateral.', 'Distal.', 'Anterior.', 'Proximal.', 'Posterior.'] KEY 1
Phalanges are farther from the limb root than the carpal bones, so they are distal to them.

IAE-1-SRC-0808 Which directional term best describes the position of the radius bone to phalanges:
['Lateral.', 'Distal.', 'Anterior.', 'Proximal.', 'Posterior.'] KEY 3
The radius is nearer the root of the upper limb than the phalanges, so it is proximal to them.

IAE-1-SRC-0809 Regarding the movements, the correct statement is:
['Adduction: Movement away from the midline of the body', 'Flexion: Increase the angle between two bones', 'Pronation: Upward or palm upward.', 'Eversion: Turning of the sole of the foot outward.', 'Rotation: Turning upward or downward.'] KEY 3
Eversion means turning the sole of the foot outward.

IAE-1-SRC-0810 The following statement regarding the fascia is true:
['Superficial fascia not contains muscles.', 'Deep fascia acts as a heat insulator.', 'Deep fascia is a transparent weak membrane.', 'Superficial fascia gives the body its round contour.'] KEY 3
The fat in superficial fascia contributes to the rounded contour of the body.

IAE-1-SRC-0811 Regarding the retinacula, they:
['Are tight sheets of deep fascia around the muscles of limbs and neck.', 'Are partitions of deep fascia separating groups of muscles.', 'Are transverse thickenings of deep fascia around the wrist and ankle.', 'Are strong sheaths of deep fascia investing large vessels and nerves.', 'Are strong bands of superficial fascia giving attachments to muscles.'] KEY 2
Retinacula are transverse thickenings of deep fascia at the wrist and ankle that hold tendons in place.

IAE-1-SRC-0812 Regarding the skeletal muscles, choose the correct:
['They are smooth muscles', 'They are voluntary muscles.', 'They are supplied by sensory nerve fibers.', 'The mobile attachment is called the origin.'] KEY 1
Skeletal muscles are voluntary muscles.

IAE-1-SRC-0813 Regarding the skeletal muscles, one of the following is true:
['Tibialis anterior is a bipennate muscle.', 'Extensor digitorum longus is multipennate muscle.', 'Deltoid is a multipennate muscle.', 'Rectus femoris is a parallel muscle.', 'Sartorius is a unipennate muscle.'] KEY 2
The deltoid is classically described as a multipennate muscle.

IAE-1-SRC-0814 Regarding the metaphysis of long bone, it is:
['The part of the diaphysis near the center of the bone.', 'The part of the diaphysis close to the epiphyseal cartilage.', 'Part of the epiphysis.', 'The cartilaginous plate separating the epiphysis and diaphysis of a long bone.', 'None of the above.'] KEY 1
The metaphysis is the expanded part of the shaft adjacent to the epiphyseal cartilage.

IAE-1-SRC-0815 The site of growth of long bone in length is due to the activity of:
['Epiphysis.', 'Metaphysis.', 'Periosteum.', 'Diaphysis.', 'Epiphyseal cartilage.'] KEY 4
Growth in length of a long bone occurs at the epiphyseal cartilage.

IAE-1-SRC-0816 Besides support and protection, the skeleton serves other important functions, including
['Reproduction', 'Locomotion', 'Respiration', 'Circulation', 'Hemostasis'] KEY 1
The skeleton acts as a lever system for muscles and therefore contributes to locomotion.

IAE-1-SRC-0817 A vertebra is considered:
['Long bone', 'Irregular bone', 'Flat bone', 'Short bone', 'Pneumatic bone'] KEY 1
A vertebra is an irregular bone because its shape is complex and specialized for protection of the spinal cord and articulation with adjacent vertebrae.

IAE-1-SRC-0818 The epiphyseal plate is an example of:
['Fibrous joint.', 'Plane synovial joint.', 'Uniaxial synovial joint.', 'Biaxial synovial joint.', 'Cartilaginous joint.'] KEY 4
The epiphyseal plate is a primary cartilaginous joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0819 A polyaxial synovial joint having a medial rotation movement its variety is:
['Hinge.', 'Condyloid.', 'Ball and socket.', 'Pivot.', 'plane joint'] KEY 2
Ball-and-socket joints are polyaxial and allow medial rotation.

IAE-1-SRC-0820 An example of secondary cartilaginous joints is:
['Sagittal suture of the skull', 'Intervertebral joints.', 'Inferior tibio-fibular joint.', 'Knee joint.', 'Elbow joint'] KEY 1
Intervertebral joints between vertebral bodies are secondary cartilaginous joints.

IAE-1-SRC-0821 The stability of joints depends upon:
['Shape of the articulating bones.', 'Type of joint.', 'Axis of movement', 'Position in the body.', 'Number of bone articulating'] KEY 0
The shape and congruence of the articulating surfaces are major determinants of joint stability.

IAE-1-SRC-0822 Regarding the synovial joints, choose the correct answer:
['They are joints of limited movements.', 'Their never be intracapsular tendon of a muscle.', 'Synovial membrane connects the articulating bones.', 'The articulating bones are separated by a joint cavity.', 'The articulating bones are covered with a synovial membrane.'] KEY 3
Synovial joints are defined by the presence of a joint cavity separating the articulating bones.

IAE-1-SRC-0823 Regarding the veins, one is correct:
['Veins are vessels that carry blood out of the heart.', 'Systemic veins return deoxygenated blood from the body to the right atrium.', 'Pulmonary veins return oxygenated blood from the lungs to the right atrium.', 'All systemic veins have no valves.', 'Venules are the smallest veins which arise from the arteries.'] KEY 1
Systemic veins return deoxygenated blood from the body to the right atrium, whereas pulmonary veins are the main exception because they carry oxygenated blood from the lungs to the left atrium.

IAE-1-SRC-0824 Regarding the arteries, one is correct:
['Arteries are vessels that carry blood back to the heart.', 'Systemic arteries carry deoxygenated blood.', 'Pulmonary arteries carry oxygenated blood.', 'Arterioles are the largest arteries, which open in the capillaries.', 'Anastomosis is the joining of branches of arteries.'] KEY 4
An anastomosis is a joining or communication between branches of arteries.

IAE-1-SRC-0825 Which organ systems function to control other organ systems?
['Reproductive and urinary', 'Cardiovascular and muscular', 'Nervous and endocrine', 'Integumentary and skeletal'] KEY 2
The nervous and endocrine systems regulate and coordinate the activities of other organ systems.

IAE-1-SRC-0826 Regarding number of spinal nerves groups, one of the following is true;
['8 Cervical nerves.', '11 Thoracic nerves.', '7 Lumbar nerves.', '4 Sacral nerves.', '2 Coccygeal nerves.'] KEY 0
Only the cervical group has 8 pairs; thoracic, lumbar, sacral, and coccygeal counts make the other options incorrect.

IAE-1-SRC-0827 Regarding the spinal cord, one is correct:
['Length is 35 cm in the male and 30 cm in female', 'Location is in the upper third of the vertebral canal', 'It begins as the continuation of the pons.', 'It ends in the newborn at the level of the third lumbar vertebra.', 'Its Segments are 40 segments.'] KEY 3
In the newborn, the spinal cord ends around the level of L3; the other statements are anatomically incorrect.

IAE-1-SRC-0828 Regarding the autonomic nervous system, one is correct:
['It is concerned with innervation of voluntary structures.', 'Sympathetic system Prepare the body for an emergency.', 'Sympathetic system increases the peristalsis and glandular activity and open sphincters.', 'Parasympathetic system it increases the heart rate, raises the blood pressure.', 'Sympathetic system Responsible for conserving and restoring energy.'] KEY 1
The sympathetic system mediates the emergency or fight-or-flight response.

IAE-1-SRC-0829 Regarding the skin appendages, one is correct:
['The sebaceous gland opens into the hair follicles.', 'The sweat glands present over the surface of the lips.', 'The root of nail is its distal edge.', 'The hair follicles are projections from the dermis.', 'The erector pilli muscle connects the hair follicles to each others.'] KEY 0
Sebaceous glands usually discharge into hair follicles.

IAE-1-SRC-0830 Trunk of the human body formed of thorax, ___ and pelvis.
 KEY
The trunk is classically divided into thorax, abdomen, and pelvis.

IAE-1-SRC-0831 Coronal plane divides the body into ___ and ___ parts.
 KEY
A coronal plane separates the body into anterior and posterior parts.

IAE-1-SRC-0832 ___ is toward the head while ___ is toward the feet.
 KEY
Superior means toward the head and inferior means toward the feet.

IAE-1-SRC-0833 ___ is toward the front while ___ is toward the back of the body.
 KEY
Anterior refers to the front and posterior to the back of the body.

IAE-1-SRC-0834 ___ is toward the midline while ___ is toward the side of the body.
 KEY
Medial means toward the midline and lateral means away from it.

IAE-1-SRC-0835 Palmar is ___ of the hand while planter is ___ of the foot.
 KEY
Palmar refers to the anterior surface of the hand, while plantar refers to the sole of the foot.

IAE-1-SRC-0836 Vertebral column is formed of ___ vertebrae.
 KEY
The vertebral column is formed from 33 vertebrae.

IAE-1-SRC-0837 Vertebrae are classified into 7 ___ vertebrae, 12 ___ vertebrae, 5 ___ vertebrae, 5 ___ vertebrae and 4 ___ vertebrae.
 KEY
The vertebral column is divided into cervical, thoracic, lumbar, sacral, and coccygeal regions.

IAE-1-SRC-0838 According to shape femur is ___ bone and scapula is ___ bone.
 KEY
The femur is a long bone and the scapula is a flat bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0839 According to shape ___ is sesamoid bone and ___ is pneumatic bone.
 KEY
The patella is a sesamoid bone and the maxilla is a pneumatic bone.

IAE-1-SRC-0840 Blood transported by the pulmonary veins returns to the:
['Left ventricle', 'Right atrium', 'Aorta', 'Right ventricle', 'Left atrium'] KEY 4
The four pulmonary veins carry oxygenated blood from the lungs to the left atrium. Blood then passes through the mitral valve into the left ventricle.

IAE-1-SRC-0841 The joint between the manubrium and body of the sternum is:
['Suture', 'Secondary cartilaginous', 'Primary cartilaginous', 'Plane synovial', 'Syndesmosis'] KEY 1
The manubriosternal joint is a secondary cartilaginous joint (symphysis) united by fibrocartilage. It forms the palpable sternal angle.

IAE-1-SRC-0842 The number of spinal cord segments is:
['44', '31', '30', '40', '33'] KEY 1
The spinal cord has 31 segments: 8 cervical, 12 thoracic, 5 lumbar, 5 sacral, and 1 coccygeal. Each segment gives rise to a pair of spinal nerves.

IAE-1-SRC-0843 For proper healing of a wound in the skin of the leg, the incision should be made:
['Longitudinally', 'Obliquely', 'In a T shape', 'Transversely', 'Circularly'] KEY 0
Langer's lines generally run longitudinally in the limbs. An incision parallel to these collagen lines gapes less and heals with a finer scar.

IAE-1-SRC-0844 An example of a strap-like muscle is:
['Sartorius', 'Temporalis', 'Deltoid', 'Trapezius', 'Biceps'] KEY 0
Sartorius is a long, narrow muscle with parallel fibers and is the classic strap muscle. Temporalis is fan-shaped, deltoid is multipennate, and biceps is fusiform.

IAE-1-SRC-0845 The epidermis of the skin is thick in:
['Palms', 'Legs', 'Eyelids', 'Dorsum of hands', 'Skin of chest'] KEY 0
Thick, glabrous skin is found on the palms and soles and has a prominent stratum corneum and stratum lucidum. Eyelid skin is among the thinnest in the body.

IAE-1-SRC-0846 Which structure never exists in superficial fascia?
['Nerves', 'Vessels', 'Tendon', 'Fat', 'Muscle'] KEY 2
Superficial fascia commonly contains fat, cutaneous nerves, vessels, and sometimes muscles such as platysma. Tendons lie deep to the deep fascia and are not found in superficial fascia.

IAE-1-SRC-0847 Which part of a spinal nerve is formed of purely motor fibers?
['Ventral ramus', 'Dorsal root', 'Ventral root', 'Dorsal ramus', 'Nerve trunk'] KEY 2
The ventral root carries motor axons away from the spinal cord. The dorsal root is sensory, while the spinal nerve trunk and both rami are mixed.

IAE-1-SRC-0848 An example of an anatomical end artery is:
['Coronary', 'Mesenteric', 'Cerebral', 'Renal', 'Central retinal'] KEY 4
The central retinal artery has no effective anastomosis and is a true anatomical end artery. Coronary and cerebral arteries are generally described as functional end arteries because their anastomoses are inadequate.

IAE-1-SRC-0849 Which one of the following structures has no lymphatics?
['Liver', 'Skin', 'Intestine', 'Brain', 'Pleura'] KEY 3
Brain parenchyma has no conventional lymphatic vessels; interstitial fluid drains through perivascular and cerebrospinal-fluid pathways. The liver, skin, intestine, and pleura all have lymphatic vessels.

IAE-1-SRC-0850 If the lymphatics of the upper limb become obstructed, the arm on that side is likely to:
['Become ischemic', 'Become oedematous', 'Become hyperemic', 'Lose tissue fluid', 'Remain unchanged'] KEY 1
Lymphatic obstruction prevents return of protein-rich interstitial fluid to the circulation, producing lymphoedema. It does not directly obstruct arterial inflow or cause tissue-fluid loss.

IAE-1-SRC-0851 Cerebrospinal fluid lies between:
['White matter and grey matter', 'Dura and pia mater', 'Dura and arachnoid mater', 'Pia and arachnoid mater', 'Pia and brain'] KEY 3
Cerebrospinal fluid fills the subarachnoid space between the arachnoid and pia mater. The subdural space between dura and arachnoid is only a potential space.

IAE-1-SRC-0852 Which one of the following joints is the most movable?
['Syndesmosis', 'Primary cartilaginous joint', 'Suture', 'Gomphosis', 'Hinge'] KEY 4
A hinge joint is synovial and therefore freely movable. Syndesmoses, sutures, and gomphoses are fibrous joints, while primary cartilaginous joints are essentially immobile.

IAE-1-SRC-0853 Most joints that lie in the median plane of the body are:
['Syndesmosis', 'Primary cartilaginous joint', 'Plane synovial', 'Gomphosis', 'Secondary cartilaginous joint'] KEY 4
Secondary cartilaginous joints, or symphyses, characteristically lie in the midline, as at the symphysis pubis, intervertebral discs, and manubriosternal joint.

IAE-1-SRC-0854 During an operation to remove a swelling from the back under general anesthesia, the patient should be placed in which position?
['Supine', 'Erect', 'Anatomical', 'Prone', 'Flexion'] KEY 3
The prone position places the patient face down and exposes the posterior surface of the trunk for surgery. Supine would place the back against the operating table.

IAE-1-SRC-0855 A piece of glass lacerated the wrist of a teenager. The tendons crossing the wrist were no longer held in place during movement. Which structure was most likely cut?
['Radius and ulna', 'Intermuscular septum', 'Wrist joint capsule', 'Retinaculum', 'Palmar aponeurosis'] KEY 3
Retinacula are localized thickenings of deep fascia that hold tendons close to the skeleton at the wrist and prevent bowstringing. Cutting one allows the tendons to displace during movement.

IAE-1-SRC-0856 A patient sustained a fracture of the lateral bone of the leg. The affected bone is the:
['Radius', 'Tibia', 'Fibula', 'Patella', 'Talus'] KEY 2
The fibula is the slender lateral bone of the leg, while the tibia is the larger medial weight-bearing bone. Radius is in the forearm, patella at the knee, and talus in the foot.

IAE-1-SRC-0857 A patient has severe forearm pain with inability to pronate and supinate the radius over the ulna. The affected type of joint is:
['Hinge', 'Ellipsoid', 'Pivot', 'Plane synovial', 'Condyloid'] KEY 2
Pronation and supination occur at the proximal and distal radioulnar joints, both pivot synovial joints. Pivot joints permit rotation around a longitudinal axis.

IAE-1-SRC-0858 Which type of bone makes the skull lighter and more resonant?
['Pneumatic bones', 'Sesamoid bones', 'Squamous bones', 'Short bones', 'Irregular bones'] KEY 0
Pneumatic bones contain air-filled sinuses, reducing skull weight and adding resonance to the voice. Examples include the frontal, ethmoid, sphenoid, and maxilla.

IAE-1-SRC-0859 During elbow flexion, which term describes the muscles that initiate the movement?
['Antagonists', 'Fixators', 'Prime movers', 'Proprioceptors', 'Synergists'] KEY 2
The muscle chiefly responsible for initiating and producing a movement is the prime mover, or agonist. Synergists assist it, antagonists oppose it, and fixators stabilize proximal parts.

IAE-1-SRC-0860 The sperms remain viable in the female genital tract for:
['2 days', '3 days', '4 days', '5 days', '6 days'] KEY 0
The correct answer is 2 days because sperms retain their fertilising capacity in the female genital tract for roughly 48 hours, even though they may remain motile a little longer. The secondary oocyte by contrast survives only about 24 hours. Together these give the short fertile window around ovulation.

IAE-1-SRC-0861 Ovulation is the process of liberation of:
['Mature ovum', 'Secondary oocyte', 'Second polar body', 'Primary oocyte', 'Oogonium'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Secondary oocyte because at ovulation the cell released is arrested in metaphase of the second meiotic division. It becomes a mature ovum only if a sperm penetrates it and the second meiotic division is completed. The primary oocyte and oogonium are earlier stages that remain in the ovary.

IAE-1-SRC-0862 Which of the following cells contains 23 chromosomes?
['Daughter spermatogonium', 'Secondary spermatocyte', 'Primary spermatocyte', 'Oogonium', 'Daughter oogonium'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Secondary spermatocyte because it is produced by the first meiotic (reduction) division and therefore carries the haploid number of 23 chromosomes, although each still has two chromatids. Spermatogonia, oogonia and primary spermatocytes are all diploid with 46 chromosomes.

IAE-1-SRC-0863 Corpus luteum of pregnancy secretes progesterone for:
['9 days', '15 days', '20 days', '2-3 months', '3-4 months'] KEY 4
The correct answer is 3-4 months because the corpus luteum of pregnancy is maintained by chorionic gonadotrophin and continues to secrete progesterone until the placenta takes over steroid production at about the fourth month. The corpus luteum of menstruation, by contrast, degenerates after some 14 days.

IAE-1-SRC-0864 Which of the following cells is diploid?
['Mature sperm', 'Secondary oocyte', 'Primary spermatocyte', 'Immature sperm', 'Spermatid'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Primary spermatocyte because it is formed from a spermatogonium by mitosis and has not yet undergone the reduction division, so it retains 46 chromosomes. The secondary oocyte, spermatid and sperm are all haploid, having completed the first meiotic division.

IAE-1-SRC-0865 Which of the following is a diploid cell?
['Mature sperm', 'Secondary spermatocyte', 'Spermatid', 'Primary oocyte', '2nd polar body'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Primary oocyte because it arises from an oogonium by mitosis and remains arrested in prophase of the first meiotic division with 46 chromosomes until puberty. Secondary spermatocytes, spermatids, sperms and polar bodies are all haploid products of meiosis.

IAE-1-SRC-0866 Necrospermia means:
['Absence of sperms', 'Low sperm count', 'Dead sperms', 'Abnormal sperms', 'High sperm count'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Dead sperms because necrospermia denotes semen in which the spermatozoa present are non-viable. Complete absence of sperms is azoospermia, a low count is oligospermia and an excess of abnormal forms is teratospermia. Distinguishing these terms matters because each points to a different cause of infertility.

IAE-1-SRC-0867 The corpus luteum of pregnancy is maintained by a hormone secreted from:
['Pituitary', 'Ovary', 'Chorion', 'Epiblast', 'Hypoblast'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Chorion because human chorionic gonadotrophin, produced by the syncytiotrophoblast of the chorion, rescues and maintains the corpus luteum during early pregnancy. Pituitary luteinising hormone maintains the corpus luteum only in the ordinary menstrual cycle. This is also the hormone detected by pregnancy tests.

IAE-1-SRC-0868 The acrosome of the sperm is derived from:
['Mitochondria', 'Chromatin network', 'Centriole', 'Golgi apparatus', 'Smooth endoplasmic reticulum'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Golgi apparatus because during spermiogenesis the Golgi complex of the spermatid forms the acrosomal vesicle that spreads as a cap over the nucleus. The mitochondria instead form the helical sheath of the middle piece and the centriole gives rise to the axoneme of the tail.

IAE-1-SRC-0869 The acrosome is formed from which part of the spermatid?
['Nucleus', 'Golgi complex', 'Mitochondria', 'Centriole', 'Cytoplasm'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Golgi complex, whose vesicles coalesce into the acrosomal granule and then flatten over the anterior two thirds of the nucleus. The acrosome is effectively a large lysosome, containing hyaluronidase and acrosin used to penetrate the corona radiata and zona pellucida. The nucleus itself merely condenses.

IAE-1-SRC-0870 The oocyte completes the first meiotic division at:
['Fertilization', 'Gastrulation', 'Implantation', 'Puberty', 'Ovulation'] KEY 4
The correct answer is Ovulation because the primary oocyte, arrested since fetal life in prophase I, completes the first meiotic division just before ovulation under the influence of the luteinising hormone surge, extruding the first polar body. The second meiotic division is completed only at fertilization.

IAE-1-SRC-0871 The largest cell of the spermatogenic series is:
['Oogonium', 'Primary spermatocyte', 'Secondary spermatocyte', 'Spermatid', 'Sperm'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Primary spermatocyte because it is the largest cell of the series, having grown considerably during the growth phase before entering the long prophase of the first meiotic division. Each subsequent stage is smaller, culminating in the spermatid and the highly condensed mature sperm.

IAE-1-SRC-0872 The oocyte remains arrested until puberty in:
['Meiosis II metaphase', 'Meiosis II prophase', 'Meiosis I metaphase', 'Meiosis I prophase', 'G-phase'] KEY 3
The correct answer is prophase of the first meiotic division, specifically the diplotene or dictyotene stage, in which all primary oocytes are held from fetal life until puberty. The second meiotic arrest, in metaphase II, occurs later and is broken only by fertilization. This prolonged arrest is thought to underlie the increased risk of non-disjunction in older mothers.

IAE-1-SRC-0873 Complete absence of sperms in semen is called:
['Teratospermia', 'Necrospermia', 'Oligospermia', 'Azospermia', 'Hypospermia'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Azospermia because the prefix a- denotes complete absence of spermatozoa from the ejaculate. Oligospermia is a reduced count, necrospermia means the sperms present are dead and teratospermia means an excess of abnormal forms. Azoospermia may be obstructive or non-obstructive in origin.

IAE-1-SRC-0874 The normal diploid number of chromosomes in the human somatic cell is:
['46 autosomes + 2 sex chromosomes (identical)', '46 autosomes + 2 sex chromosomes (same or different)', '44 autosomes + 2 sex chromosomes (identical)', '44 autosomes + 2 sex chromosomes (may be same or different)', '45 autosomes + 1 sex chromosome'] KEY 3
The correct answer is 44 autosomes plus 2 sex chromosomes that may be the same or different, giving the total of 46. The two sex chromosomes are identical (XX) in the female but different (XY) in the male, so any option insisting they are identical is wrong. Options quoting 46 autosomes ignore that the sex chromosomes are included in the 46.

IAE-1-SRC-0875 Match each cell in the left column with its chromosomal formula in the right column:
 KEY
The oogonium and the primary spermatocyte are diploid, so they carry 44 autosomes with XX and XY respectively. After the reduction division the secondary spermatocyte and the sperm are haploid and may carry either an X or a Y, which is why the father determines the sex of the child. The secondary oocyte can only carry an X.

IAE-1-SRC-0876 The chromosomal formula of the first polar body is:
['22+Y', '22+X', '44+XY', '44+XX', 'None of the above'] KEY 1
The correct answer is 22+X because the first polar body is the small haploid cell extruded when the primary oocyte completes the first meiotic division, and every female gamete carries an X chromosome. It can never carry a Y, and it is not diploid because the reduction division has already occurred.

IAE-1-SRC-0877 The time taken for a spermatogonium to become a mature sperm is:
['Two days', 'Ten days', '60-64 days', '50 days', '73-75 days'] KEY 2
The correct answer is 60-64 days, the figure given in this course for the whole cycle of spermatogenesis from spermatogonium to released spermatozoon. This is why an insult to the testis shows its effect on the semen only about two months later. The sperm then needs further time in the epididymis to acquire motility.

IAE-1-SRC-0878 Which of the following cells does not undergo cell division?
['Primary spermatocyte', 'Secondary spermatocyte', 'Primary oocyte', 'Secondary oocyte', 'Spermatid'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the Spermatid because it is the end product of meiosis and merely transforms into a spermatozoon during spermiogenesis without dividing again. Primary and secondary spermatocytes divide in meiosis I and II respectively, and both oocyte stages retain the capacity to complete their meiotic divisions.
