IAE-1-SRC-0879 Spermiogenesis includes all the following EXCEPT:
['Formation of the acrosome', 'Golgi material forms the tail', 'Condensation of the nucleus', 'Formation of a mitochondrial helical sheath', 'Shedding of most of cytoplasm'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that the Golgi material forms the tail, which is false because the Golgi complex forms the acrosome while the tail develops from the centriole. The other four events are genuine features of spermiogenesis: acrosome formation, nuclear condensation, the mitochondrial helix of the middle piece and shedding of surplus cytoplasm as the residual body.

IAE-1-SRC-0880 Before fertilization the sperm must undergo:
['Completing second meiotic division', 'Spermiogenesis', 'Capacitation', 'Acrosome formation', 'Losing its tail'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Capacitation because the sperm must spend several hours in the female genital tract, during which a glycoprotein coat and seminal proteins are removed from the acrosomal membrane, before it can undergo the acrosome reaction and fertilise. Spermiogenesis and acrosome formation are completed earlier, in the testis. The sperm keeps its tail until it enters the oocyte.

IAE-1-SRC-0881 The chromosomal formula of the secondary oocyte is:
['44 autosomes + 2 X chromosomes', '45 autosomes + 1 X chromosome', '22 autosomes + 1 Y chromosome', '22 autosomes + 1 X chromosome', '22 autosomes + 2 X chromosomes'] KEY 3
The correct answer is 22 autosomes plus one X chromosome because the secondary oocyte is haploid, having completed the first meiotic division, and every oocyte carries an X. A Y chromosome can only be carried by a sperm, and 44XX would be the diploid formula of the oogonium or primary oocyte.

IAE-1-SRC-0882 The non-cellular layer that surrounds the ovum is:
['Corona radiate', 'Cummulusoophorus', 'Zona pellucida', 'Theca folliculi', 'Stratum granulosum'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Zona pellucida because it is an acellular glycoprotein coat secreted around the oocyte, unlike the corona radiata, cumulus oophorus and stratum granulosum, which are all layers of follicular cells. The theca folliculi is a cellular condensation of ovarian stroma outside the follicle. The zona carries the sperm receptors and mediates the zona reaction that blocks polyspermy.

IAE-1-SRC-0883 Zona pellucida is:
['A membrane that surrounds the Graafian follicle', 'A membrane that surrounds the ovum', 'The surface covering of the ovary', 'A clear area on the head of the sperm', 'The surface covering of the testis'] KEY 1
The correct answer is a membrane that surrounds the ovum, since the zona pellucida is deposited immediately around the oocyte between it and the follicular cells. The surface covering of the ovary is the germinal epithelium and that of the testis is the tunica albuginea. The clear area on the head of the sperm is the acrosome.

IAE-1-SRC-0884 Ovulation:
['Is facilitated by progesterone', 'Occurs 3 weeks after start of menstruation', 'Is accompanied by completion of the 2nd meiotic division', 'Is accompanied by pain in some women', 'Is accompanied by a fall in basal temperature'] KEY 3
The correct answer is that it is accompanied by pain in some women, the mid-cycle pain known as mittelschmerz. Ovulation is triggered by the luteinising hormone surge rather than progesterone, occurs about 14 days after the onset of menstruation, and is followed by a rise rather than a fall in basal temperature. The second meiotic division is completed only at fertilization.

IAE-1-SRC-0885 The second meiotic division of the ovum occurs:
['Before ovulation', 'At ovulation', '24 hours before ovulation', '48 hours after ovulation', 'On fertilization'] KEY 4
The correct answer is on fertilization because the secondary oocyte is arrested in metaphase II and completes that division only when a sperm penetrates it, extruding the second polar body. If fertilization does not occur the oocyte degenerates still arrested. The first meiotic division, by contrast, is completed just before ovulation.

IAE-1-SRC-0886 On ovulation, the ovary discharges:
['Primary oocyte + corona radiata', 'Primary oocyte + stratum granulosum', 'Secondary oocyte + corona radiata', 'Secondary oocyte + stratum granulosum', 'Mature Graafian follicle'] KEY 2
The correct answer is a secondary oocyte with its corona radiata, because the first meiotic division is completed just before ovulation and the oocyte is shed still surrounded by the innermost layer of follicular cells. A primary oocyte is never released, and the Graafian follicle itself remains in the ovary to become the corpus luteum.

IAE-1-SRC-0887 If fertilization does not occur, the corpus luteum starts to regress after:
['6 days', '12-14 days', '20 days', '21-24 days', '25-30 days'] KEY 1
The correct answer is 12-14 days because in the absence of chorionic gonadotrophin the corpus luteum of menstruation degenerates about two weeks after ovulation, and the resulting fall in progesterone precipitates menstruation. Only if implantation occurs is it rescued and maintained for months as the corpus luteum of pregnancy.

IAE-1-SRC-0888 Corpus luteum of pregnancy remains active till:
['14 days after fetilization', 'End of implantation', 'End of pregnancy', '3rd week', '4th month'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the 4th month because the corpus luteum of pregnancy continues to secrete progesterone until the placenta is able to take over steroid production, after which it regresses. It does not persist to the end of pregnancy. The 14-day figure applies to the corpus luteum of menstruation.

IAE-1-SRC-0889 The sources of progesterone secreted during pregnancy include:
['Corpus luteum', 'Pituitary gland', 'Placenta', 'Corpus luteum & placenta', 'All of the above'] KEY 3
The correct answer is corpus luteum & placenta because progesterone comes from the corpus luteum during the first months and from the placenta thereafter. The pituitary secretes gonadotrophins, not progesterone, so the option including it is wrong. This luteo-placental shift occurs at about the fourth month.

IAE-1-SRC-0890 Contraceptive pills act by:
['Killing the fertilized ovum', 'Killing the sperms', 'Having a testosterone-like action', 'Suppressing follicle maturation', 'Stopping menstrual flow'] KEY 3
The correct answer is suppressing follicle maturation, because the oestrogen and progestogen in the pill inhibit pituitary gonadotrophin release, so no follicle ripens and ovulation does not occur. The pill is neither spermicidal nor abortifacient, and withdrawal bleeding still takes place each month.

IAE-1-SRC-0891 The follicular phase of the ovarian cycle is under the control of:
['Progesterone', 'Corpus luteum', 'Luteinizing hormone', 'Pituitary FSH', 'None of the above'] KEY 3
The correct answer is pituitary FSH because follicle stimulating hormone drives the growth and maturation of the ovarian follicles in the first half of the cycle. Luteinising hormone triggers ovulation and then maintains the corpus luteum, whose progesterone governs the luteal phase.

IAE-1-SRC-0892 A newly-married wife noticed a delay in her expected menstrual cycle for two weeks. A pregnancy test confirmed her pregnancy.
 KEY
The correct answers follow from the timing of ovulation and from the endocrinology of early pregnancy: fertilization occurs about 14 days before the missed period, chorionic gonadotrophin from the syncytiotrophoblast is the hormone detected by the test, and it is this hormone that rescues the corpus luteum until placental take-over at about the fourth month.

IAE-1-SRC-0893 A married couple suffered from infertility. Semen analysis of the husband showed a sperm count of 20 million/ml with abnormal forms up to 35%.
 KEY
The correct answers rest on the standards of semen analysis and on sperm structure: a count of 20 million/ml is at the lower limit and counts as oligospermia in this course, abnormal forms should not exceed 10%, motility depends on the tail and its mitochondrial middle piece, and penetration depends on the acrosomal enzymes.

IAE-1-SRC-0894 A married 20 year-old female noticed a delay in her menstrual cycle for one month.
 KEY
The correct answers are pregnancy, hCG, day 14 and the secondary oocyte, because in a married woman of reproductive age amenorrhoea is due to pregnancy until proved otherwise, hCG is the hormone that rises, ovulation occurs at mid-cycle, and the cell shed at ovulation is the secondary oocyte rather than a mature ovum.

IAE-1-SRC-0895 Placenta praevia results from implantation of the blastocyst in the:
['Pelvic peritoneum', 'Medial part of uterine tube', 'Lateral part of uterine tube', 'Upper part of the uterus', 'Lower part of the uterus'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the lower part of the uterus because a placenta that develops in the lower uterine segment lies in front of the presenting part and is called placenta praevia. Implantation in the tube or peritoneum gives an ectopic pregnancy, not placenta praevia, and implantation in the upper uterus is the normal situation.

IAE-1-SRC-0896 Implantation of blastocyst is completed in which post-fertilization day?
['5th', '7th', '9th', '11th', '13th'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the 9th day because implantation begins about the 6th day and is completed around the 9th to 10th day, when the blastocyst is entirely embedded and the surface defect is closed by a fibrin plug. The 5th and 7th days are too early and the 11th and 13th are after burial is complete.

IAE-1-SRC-0897 The commonest site of ectopic pregnancy is:
['Internal os', 'Ovary', 'Peritoneal sac', 'Uterine tube', 'Cervix'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the uterine tube because the great majority of ectopic pregnancies implant in the tube, most often in its ampulla, where fertilization itself takes place. Ovarian, peritoneal and cervical implantations are all rare. The tube cannot expand, so tubal pregnancy usually ruptures with severe haemorrhage.

IAE-1-SRC-0898 The cell that is fertilized by the sperm is the:
['Primary oocyte', 'Secondary oocyte', 'Mature ovum', 'First polar body', 'Second polar body'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the secondary oocyte because the cell released at ovulation and penetrated by the sperm is arrested in metaphase II; it becomes a mature ovum only after the sperm triggers completion of that division. The primary oocyte never leaves the ovary and the polar bodies are non-functional.

IAE-1-SRC-0899 Implantation in the lower uterine segment is called placenta:
['Previa', 'Acreta', 'Membranacea', 'Succenturiata', 'Percreta'] KEY 0
The correct answer is praevia because that term denotes a placenta implanted in the lower uterine segment. Accreta and percreta describe abnormal depth of attachment to or through the myometrium, membranacea is a thin placenta covering the whole chorion and succenturiata is an accessory lobe.

IAE-1-SRC-0900 Enlargement of the cleaving zygote is prevented by:
['Corona radiata', 'Zona pellucida', 'Vitelline membrane', 'Fallopian tube', 'Follicular cells'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the zona pellucida because it persists during cleavage and confines the dividing cells, so that the blastomeres become smaller with each division and the whole conceptus does not grow. The corona radiata and follicular cells are shed soon after fertilization. The zona is lost only just before implantation.

IAE-1-SRC-0901 Prevents enlargement of the dividing zygote:
['Vitelline membrane', 'Zona pellucida', 'Fallopian tube diameter', 'Gonadotrophins', 'Decidua reaction'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the zona pellucida, the tough glycoprotein coat that surrounds the conceptus throughout cleavage and restricts its overall size. The vitelline membrane is the oocyte's own plasma membrane and does not constrain growth, and the tubal lumen plays no part in limiting the size of the conceptus.

IAE-1-SRC-0902 The process of sperm capacitation takes place within the:
['Testes', 'Male urethra', 'Female genital tract', 'Ovum', 'Embryo'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the female genital tract because capacitation is the removal of a glycoprotein coat and seminal plasma proteins from the sperm surface, and it requires several hours of exposure to the secretions of the uterus and uterine tube. It cannot occur in the testis or male urethra, and it must be completed before the acrosome reaction.

IAE-1-SRC-0903 No sperm can penetrate the oocyte after fertilization due to:
['Hyaluronidase action', 'Corona radiata resistance', 'Negative chemotaxis', 'Zona reaction', 'Antibodies against remaining sperms'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the zona reaction because release of the cortical granules changes the zona pellucida so that its sperm receptors are inactivated and it becomes impenetrable, thus blocking polyspermy. Hyaluronidase in fact helps sperms to penetrate, and there is no antibody or chemotactic mechanism involved.

IAE-1-SRC-0904 Sperm capacitation occurs in the:
['Seminiferous tubules', 'Rate testes', 'Epididymis', 'Vas deferens', 'Uterus'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the uterus because it is the only part of the female genital tract offered, and capacitation takes place in the uterus and uterine tube. The seminiferous tubules, rete testis, epididymis and vas deferens are all male passages, where the sperm matures but is not yet capacitated.

IAE-1-SRC-0905 The site of acrosomal reaction is:
['In the epididymis', 'In the vas deferens', 'In the vagina after ejaculation', 'In the Fallopian tube', 'In the seminiferous tubules of the testes'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Fallopian tube because the acrosome reaction occurs when a capacitated sperm contacts the zona pellucida of the oocyte, and that meeting takes place in the ampulla of the tube. It cannot occur in the male passages, where the sperm has not yet been capacitated, nor in the vagina.

IAE-1-SRC-0906 Where does the process of sperm capacitation take place?
['Within the testes', 'Within the male urethra', 'Within the female genital tract', 'Within the ovum', 'Within the embryo'] KEY 2
The correct answer is within the female genital tract, since capacitation requires several hours of contact with uterine and tubal secretions before the sperm can undergo the acrosome reaction. Sperms leaving the testis and male urethra are motile but not yet capable of fertilizing.

IAE-1-SRC-0907 What is the result of the zone reaction (cortical reaction)?
['The corona radiata is formed', 'The zone pellucida is formed', 'Sperms can now enter the egg', 'Acrosomes are removed', 'Sperms can no longer enter the egg'] KEY 4
The correct answer is that sperms can no longer enter the egg, because the cortical granules discharge enzymes that inactivate the sperm receptors of the zona and harden it. The zona pellucida and corona radiata are formed long before, in the ovary, and the reaction does not remove acrosomes.

IAE-1-SRC-0908 Where does the process of sperm capacitation take place?
['Within the testis', 'Within the urethra', 'Within the ovum', 'Within the embryo', 'Within the female genital tract'] KEY 4
The correct answer is within the female genital tract, the constant answer to this question however the options are arranged. Capacitation is a functional maturation occurring in the uterus and uterine tube, and it is a prerequisite for the acrosome reaction and fertilization.

IAE-1-SRC-0909 The stage that implants into the uterus is:
['Morula', 'Blastocyst', '2 cell stage', '4 cell stage', 'Zygote'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the blastocyst because implantation is carried out by its trophoblast after the zona pellucida has been shed, on about the sixth day. The zygote and the 2-cell and 4-cell stages are still in the uterine tube, and the morula only reaches the uterine cavity, where it cavitates into a blastocyst before implanting.

IAE-1-SRC-0910 Results of fertilization include all the following EXCEPT:
['Restoration of diploid number of chromosomes', 'Initiation of cleavage', 'Completion of 2nd meiotic division', 'Extrusion of 2nd polar body', 'Beginning of implantation'] KEY 4
The correct answer is beginning of implantation because that occurs about six days later, after cleavage and blastocyst formation, and is not an immediate result of fertilization. Fertilization itself restores the diploid number, determines sex, triggers completion of meiosis II with extrusion of the second polar body, and initiates cleavage.

IAE-1-SRC-0911 The first stage that reaches the uterine lumen after fertilization is:
['The zygote', 'The 2-cell stage', 'The 4-cell stage', 'The morula', 'The blastocyst'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the morula because cleavage takes place as the conceptus travels along the uterine tube, and it enters the uterine cavity about the fourth day as a solid ball of cells. Fluid then accumulates within it and it becomes a blastocyst inside the uterus, so the blastocyst is not the first stage to arrive.

IAE-1-SRC-0912 The sex of the embryo is determined at the time of:
['Fertilization', 'Spermatogenesis', 'Ovulation', 'Development of gonads', 'Development of external genitalia'] KEY 0
The correct answer is fertilization because the genetic sex is settled at the moment the sperm, carrying either an X or a Y chromosome, unites with the always-X oocyte. Gonadal and phenotypic differentiation occur weeks later and merely express that already-determined chromosomal sex.

IAE-1-SRC-0913 Only one sperm can penetrate the oocyte membrane due to:
['The corona radiate', 'The Zona reaction', 'Hyaluronidase action', 'Capacitation', 'Formation of 2nd polar body'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the zona reaction because cortical granule enzymes render the zona pellucida impenetrable once the first sperm has entered. Hyaluronidase and capacitation assist penetration rather than prevent it, and extrusion of the second polar body is a consequence of fertilization, not a barrier to polyspermy.

IAE-1-SRC-0914 Normal site of fertilization is:
['Fundus of uterus', 'Medial part of uterine tube', 'Lateral part of uterine tube', 'On surface of ovary', 'Body of uterus'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the lateral part of the uterine tube, that is the ampulla, where the sperm meets the oocyte within about a day of ovulation. Fertilization in the uterus itself is too late, because the oocyte survives only some 24 hours, and fertilization on the ovarian surface would give an ovarian ectopic pregnancy.

IAE-1-SRC-0915 Implantation of blastocyst begins in which post-fertilization day?
['5th', '6th', '9th', '11th', '13th'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the 6th day because the blastocyst sheds its zona pellucida and its trophoblast begins to invade the endometrium at that time, the process being completed by about the 9th or 10th day. Days 11 and 13 are too late and day 5 is before hatching.

IAE-1-SRC-0916 The normal site for implantation is:
['Cervix of uterus', 'Anterior wall of body of uterus', 'Posterior wall of body of uterus', 'Lower part of uterus', 'Surface of ovary'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the posterior wall of the body of the uterus, the usual site of normal implantation in the upper part of the uterine body. Implantation in the lower uterus gives placenta praevia, and implantation in the cervix or on the ovary is ectopic.

IAE-1-SRC-0917 Which of the following could not be a place for ectopic pregnancy?
['Uterine tube', 'Lower part of body of uterus', 'Ovary', 'Peritoneum', 'Abdominal cavity'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the lower part of the body of the uterus, because implantation there is still inside the uterine cavity and is therefore not ectopic; it produces placenta praevia instead. The tube, ovary, peritoneum and abdominal cavity are all recognised ectopic sites.

IAE-1-SRC-0918 Which part of the decidua lies between the embryonic pole of the blastocyst & the endometrium?
['Decidua basalis', 'Decidua parietalis', 'Decidua capsularis', 'All of the above', 'None of the above'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the decidua basalis because it is the part deep to the embryonic pole, between the conceptus and the myometrium, and it forms the maternal part of the placenta. The decidua capsularis covers the conceptus superficially and the decidua parietalis lines the rest of the uterine cavity.

IAE-1-SRC-0919 A newly-married woman had a delayed menstrual period & her pregnancy test was positive. Two weeks later, she got bleeding & abdominal pain. In the hospital, she was diagnosed to have ectopic pregnancy.
 KEY
The correct answers follow from the anatomy of the uterine tube and the behaviour of the trophoblast: the narrow, non-distensible tube is eroded and stretched, giving pain and bleeding and often rupture, while normal implantation occurs high in the uterine body where the endometrium can undergo the decidual reaction.

IAE-1-SRC-0920 A 27-old wife discovered that she had a positive pregnancy test but the level of the hormones was lower than expected. Later she got unilateral abdominal pain suggestive of appendicitis with mild vaginal bleeding. In the hospital, she knew that she had disturbed tubal pregnancy.
 KEY
The correct answers rest on the requirements for successful implantation: the zona must be shed, the implanting stage is the blastocyst, burial is complete by day 9-10 and the normal site is the upper uterine body. A tubal site fails because the tube cannot accommodate the growing conceptus, whereas a low uterine site is intrauterine and therefore not ectopic.

IAE-1-SRC-0921 Define spermatogenesis and mention its steps?
 KEY
The correct answer must define the process and name the four phases of proliferation, growth, maturation and transformation. Note that only the maturation phase is meiotic and that the growth and transformation phases involve no cell division at all.

IAE-1-SRC-0922 Define spermiogenesis and describe the changes that occur to the spermatid during this process?
 KEY
The correct answer is that the Golgi forms the acrosome, the nucleus condenses, the centriole forms the tail, the mitochondria form the middle-piece helix and the excess cytoplasm is shed. The trap is to attribute the tail to the Golgi; the Golgi makes only the acrosome.

IAE-1-SRC-0923 Mention 2 cells that have the diploid number of chromosomes in spermatogenesis?
 KEY
The correct answer is the spermatogonium and the primary spermatocyte, since the first meiotic division that halves the chromosome number takes place only when the primary spermatocyte divides. Their formula is 44XY.

IAE-1-SRC-0924 Mention 3 cells that have the haploid number of chromosomes in spermatogenesis?
 KEY
The correct answer is the secondary spermatocyte, the spermatid and the sperm, all produced after the reduction division. Their formula 22X or 22Y is the reason the father determines the sex of the child.

IAE-1-SRC-0925 As regards this semen analysis: sperm count 40,000,000; motility less than normal; abnormal forms more than 20%. Is this male fertile? Why?
 KEY
The correct answers rest on the three parameters of a semen analysis: count, motility and morphology. Here the count is adequate but the motility is reduced and more than 20% of forms are abnormal, twice the accepted 10% limit, so fertilization is unlikely.

IAE-1-SRC-0926 Define oogenesis and mention its steps?
 KEY
The correct answer must contrast oogenesis with spermatogenesis: it starts in fetal life, has two long arrests, has no transformation phase, and produces one ovum instead of four gametes. The two arrests (prophase I until puberty, metaphase II until fertilization) are the most examined points.

IAE-1-SRC-0927 Mention 2 cells that have the diploid number of chromosomes in oogenesis?
 KEY
The correct answer is the oogonium and the primary oocyte, with the formula 44XX, since the reduction division is only completed just before ovulation. Every female cell of the series carries two X chromosomes at this stage.

IAE-1-SRC-0928 Mention 3 cells that have the haploid number of chromosomes in oogenesis?
 KEY
The correct answer is the secondary oocyte, the polar bodies and the mature ovum, all with the formula 22X. The polar bodies are haploid but non-functional and eventually degenerate.

IAE-1-SRC-0929 Mention the 2 uterine phases and the 2 ovarian phases of the female cycle?
 KEY
The correct answer pairs the ovarian follicular and luteal phases with the uterine proliferative and secretory phases, since each uterine change is the endometrial response to the ovarian hormone of that phase. Ovulation on day 14 is the dividing line.

IAE-1-SRC-0930 What is the expected day of ovulation? What are its results?
 KEY
The correct answer is day 14, with discharge of a secondary oocyte with its corona radiata, formation of the corpus luteum, a rise in basal temperature and possible mittelschmerz. The 24-hour survival of the oocyte, against 48 hours for the sperm, defines the fertile window.

IAE-1-SRC-0931 What is the pregnancy hormone?
 KEY
The correct answer is human chorionic gonadotrophin, produced by the syncytiotrophoblast, because it is both the hormone detected by pregnancy tests and the hormone that maintains the corpus luteum. Progesterone is the hormone of pregnancy maintenance, but hCG is the hormone that signals pregnancy.

IAE-1-SRC-0932 The normal haploid chromosome number of the human is:
['22 autosomes + 2 identical sex chromosomes', '22 autosomes + 2 different sex chromosomes', '23 autosomes + 2 different sex chromosomes', '23 autosomes + a male or female sex chromosome', '22 autosomes + a male or female sex chromosome'] KEY 4
The correct answer is 22 autosomes + a male or female sex chromosome because the haploid gamete carries exactly half of the 46 somatic chromosomes, that is 22 autosomes plus a single sex chromosome (X or Y). Options containing two sex chromosomes describe the diploid somatic pattern, not a gamete. The options quoting 23 autosomes are wrong because only 22 of the 23 haploid chromosomes are autosomes.

IAE-1-SRC-0933 Differentiation of the primordial germ cells in the male begins at:
['3rd month of intrauterine life', '9th month of intrauterine life', 'Birth', 'Puberty', 'None of the above'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Puberty because in the male the primordial germ cells remain dormant as spermatogonia in the seminiferous tubules throughout intrauterine life and childhood, and only begin to differentiate when spermatogenesis is switched on at puberty. This contrasts sharply with the female, in whom the germ cells enter meiosis during intrauterine life. The intrauterine and birth options therefore describe the female timetable, not the male one.

IAE-1-SRC-0934 Which of the following cells are not capable of cell division and increasing the number of male germ cells?
['Spermatogonia', '1ry spermatocytes', '2ry spermatocytes', 'Spermatid', 'All of the above'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Spermatid because it is the end product of the maturation divisions and undergoes only spermiogenesis, a process of transformation without any further cell division. Spermatogonia divide mitotically, while primary and secondary spermatocytes divide by the first and second meiotic divisions respectively, so all three of them still increase cell number.

IAE-1-SRC-0935 As regards spermiogenesis, all are true EXCEPT:
['The nucleus contains half the number of chromosomes', 'Golgi zone becomes the acrosome', 'Mitochondria form the bulk of the middle piece', 'The centrioles form the axial filament', 'Cytoplasm forms the head of the sperm'] KEY 4
The correct answer is that the cytoplasm forms the head of the sperm, because during spermiogenesis most of the cytoplasm is actually shed as a residual body; it is the condensed nucleus capped by the acrosome that forms the head. The other statements are all correct features of spermiogenesis: the spermatid nucleus is haploid, the Golgi zone becomes the acrosome, the mitochondria form the helical sheath of the middle piece and the centrioles give the axial filament of the tail.

IAE-1-SRC-0936 When the 2ry oocyte is ovulated, it is surrounded by:
['Theca interna', 'Zona pellucida', 'Corona radiata', 'All of the above', 'B & C only'] KEY 4
The correct answer is B and C only because the ovulated secondary oocyte carries with it the non-cellular zona pellucida immediately around it and the innermost layer of follicular cells, the corona radiata, outside that. The theca interna is a vascular endocrine layer of the wall of the Graafian follicle that stays behind in the ovary and contributes to the corpus luteum, so it is not shed with the oocyte.

IAE-1-SRC-0937 During the progestational (secretory) phase of the menstrual cycle:
['The follicle matures', 'Glands constrict', 'Arteries become tortuous', 'Level of progesterone drops', 'Endometrium is thin'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that the arteries become tortuous because under the influence of progesterone from the corpus luteum the spiral arteries of the endometrium elongate and coil. Follicle maturation belongs to the preceding follicular phase, the glands dilate and become tortuous rather than constrict, progesterone is at its peak rather than dropping, and the endometrium is thick and oedematous rather than thin.

IAE-1-SRC-0938 The spermatid contains:
['46 Chromosomes', '44 Chromosomes', '23 Chromosomes', 'None of the above'] KEY 2
The correct answer is 23 chromosomes because the spermatid is produced by the second meiotic division of the secondary spermatocyte and is therefore haploid, carrying 22 autosomes plus one sex chromosome. Forty-six is the diploid somatic number found in spermatogonia and primary spermatocytes, and 44 counts only the autosomes while ignoring the sex chromosomes.

IAE-1-SRC-0939 Concerning the spermatogenesis, all of the following are true except:
['It occurs in the seminephrous tubules of the testis', 'The cycle lapses for about 64 days', 'It starts from puberty until menopause', 'One spermatogonium gives 4 sperms'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that it starts from puberty until menopause, because menopause is a female event; in the male spermatogenesis begins at puberty and continues, with gradually declining efficiency, into old age. The other statements are true: spermatogenesis takes place in the seminiferous tubules, the whole cycle takes about 60-64 days, and each spermatogonium ultimately yields four spermatozoa.

IAE-1-SRC-0940 The first meiotic division occurs in the:
['Primary spermatocyte', 'Secondary spermatocyte', 'Spermatid', 'Sperm'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the Primary spermatocyte because it is the diploid cell that undergoes the first (reduction) meiotic division to give two haploid secondary spermatocytes. The secondary spermatocyte performs the second meiotic division, while the spermatid and the mature sperm never divide at all.

IAE-1-SRC-0941 The second meiotic division occurs in the:
['Primary spermatocyte', 'Secondary spermatocyte', 'Spermatid', 'Sperm'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Secondary spermatocyte because it divides by the second (equational) meiotic division to give two spermatids, so that each primary spermatocyte finally yields four spermatids. The primary spermatocyte performs the first meiotic division, and neither the spermatid nor the sperm divides.

IAE-1-SRC-0942 Oligospermia is:
['Decreased number of sperms in semen', 'Decreased amount of semen', 'Dead sperms in semen', 'No sperms are found in the semen'] KEY 0
The correct answer is a decreased number of sperms in the semen, since the prefix oligo- means few and refers to the sperm count, not to the volume of the ejaculate. Dead sperms in the semen is necrospermia and complete absence of sperms is azoospermia.

IAE-1-SRC-0943 The male is considered infertile if the total number of sperms in the semen is less than:
['10,000,000', '20,000,000', '30,000,000'] KEY 1
The correct answer is 20,000,000 because a count below about 20 million per millilitre is the classical threshold below which fertilisation becomes unlikely and the male is regarded as infertile. Ten million is well below the threshold and would already be clearly abnormal, while 30 million still lies within the fertile range.

IAE-1-SRC-0944 All are true about the sperm except:
['Motile', 'Diploid cell', 'Can penetrate into the ovum', 'Formed of head, middle piece and tail'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that the sperm is a diploid cell, because it is in fact haploid, carrying only 23 chromosomes after the two meiotic divisions. The remaining statements are true: the sperm is actively motile by its tail, it penetrates the oocyte at fertilisation, and it is structurally divided into a head, a middle piece and a tail.

IAE-1-SRC-0945 Oogenesis:
['Starts at the puberty until the menopause', 'Starts at the intrauterine life of the female', 'Occurs in the fallopian tube', 'None of the above'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that it starts in the intrauterine life of the female, because the oogonia multiply and enter the first meiotic division as primary oocytes before birth, then arrest in prophase until puberty. Only the completion of oogenesis, not its beginning, occurs between puberty and menopause, and it takes place in the ovary rather than in the fallopian tube.

IAE-1-SRC-0946 The corpus luteum is the remaining part of the graffian follicle after ovulation, it secrets mainly:
['Progesterone', 'Estrogen', 'Lactogen', 'Human chorionic gonadotrophin hormone'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Progesterone because the luteinised granulosa cells of the corpus luteum are the main source of progesterone, which prepares and maintains the secretory endometrium. Oestrogen is secreted in smaller amounts and is mainly a product of the maturing follicle, while human chorionic gonadotrophin comes from the syncytiotrophoblast of the chorion, not from the corpus luteum.

IAE-1-SRC-0947 Sperm capacitation includes all of the following except:
['Removal of the glycoprotein coat of the sperm', 'Acrosomal reaction', 'Fusion of the sperm head with the corona radiate cells', 'Releasing of the acrosomal enzymes', 'Transportation of the sperm'] KEY 4
The correct answer is transportation of the sperm because transport through the female genital tract is a mechanical event and not part of capacitation itself. Capacitation is the conditioning of the sperm in the female tract, comprising removal of the glycoprotein coat and seminal plasma proteins from the acrosome and the changes that permit the acrosomal reaction with release of its enzymes.

IAE-1-SRC-0948 The uni-ovular twins results from:
['Fertilization of one ovum with 2 sperms', 'Fertilization of two ovum with 2 sperms', 'Fertilization of one ovum with one sperm and the resulting zygote is divided into 2 embryos', 'None of the above'] KEY 2
The correct answer is fertilisation of one ovum by one sperm with subsequent division of the zygote into two embryos, because uni-ovular (identical, monozygotic) twins arise from a single zygote and are therefore genetically identical and always of the same sex. Fertilisation of two ova by two sperms produces bi-ovular (fraternal) twins, and fertilisation of one ovum by two sperms is an abnormal, non-viable event.

IAE-1-SRC-0949 All about the bi-ovular twins are true except:
['They are formed from fertilization of 2 ova with 2 sperms', 'They are different in shape or sex', 'They have double placenta, amnion & umbilical cords', 'Incidence is 30%'] KEY 3
The correct answer is that the incidence is 30%, which is far too high; bi-ovular twinning is an uncommon event and nothing approaching a third of pregnancies. The other statements are correct, since bi-ovular twins arise from two ova fertilised by two sperms, may differ in appearance and sex, and each has its own placenta, amnion and umbilical cord.

IAE-1-SRC-0950 The zona pellucida disappears at the:
['4th Day', '5th Day', '6th Day', '7th Day'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the 5th day because the zona pellucida must be shed before the blastocyst can attach to the endometrium, and this hatching occurs on about day five, shortly before implantation begins on day six. Persistence of the zona to day six or seven would prevent implantation, while day four is still the morula stage when the zona is intact.

IAE-1-SRC-0951 The main cause of tubal implantation is:
['Fertilization occurs at the fallopian tube', 'Early disappearance of the zona pellucida', 'The tubal epithelium is similar to the dicidua', 'None of the above'] KEY 1
The correct answer is early disappearance of the zona pellucida because premature hatching allows the trophoblast to become sticky and implant while the conceptus is still inside the tube. Normal fertilisation always occurs in the tube without causing tubal implantation, so that alone is not the cause, and the tubal mucosa does not in fact undergo a true decidual reaction.

IAE-1-SRC-0952 The dicidua in which implantation occurs is:
['Dicidua basalis', 'Dicidua parietals', 'Dicidua capsularis'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the decidua basalis because it is the part of the endometrium lying deep to the implanted blastocyst, between the embryonic pole and the uterine wall, and it later forms the maternal part of the placenta. The decidua capsularis is the thin layer that covers the conceptus superficially, and the decidua parietalis lines the rest of the uterine cavity.

IAE-1-SRC-0953 The dicidua that covers the blastocyst after implantation is:
['Dicidua basalis', 'Dicidua parietals', 'Dicidua capsularis'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the decidua capsularis because after interstitial implantation the blastocyst becomes buried in the endometrium and the overlying superficial layer closes over it as a thin capsule. The decidua basalis lies deep to the conceptus and the decidua parietalis lines the remainder of the uterine cavity.

IAE-1-SRC-0954 The rest of the uterine endometrium after implantation is now called:
['Dicidua basalis', 'Dicidua parietals', 'Dicidua capsularis'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the decidua parietalis because this is the name given to the decidualised endometrium lining the whole of the rest of the uterine cavity away from the implantation site. The basalis lies deep to the conceptus and the capsularis covers it; as pregnancy advances the capsularis fuses with the parietalis and the uterine cavity is obliterated.

IAE-1-SRC-0955 The layer of the chorion that forms the fetal blood vessels is:
['Cytotrophoblast', 'Syncytiotrophoblast', 'Extra-embryonic mesoderm'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the extra-embryonic mesoderm because the mesodermal core that grows into the villus differentiates into the fetal capillaries, converting a secondary villus into a tertiary villus. The cytotrophoblast and syncytiotrophoblast are the two epithelial trophoblastic layers of the villus and are not angiogenic.

IAE-1-SRC-0956 The embryonic disc becomes bilaminar at the:
['1st Week', '2nd Week', '3rd Week', '4th Week'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the 2nd week because during the second week the inner cell mass differentiates into the epiblast and the hypoblast, forming the bilaminar germ disc; this is why the second week is called the week of twos. The first week is occupied by fertilisation and cleavage, while the third week is the week of gastrulation when the disc becomes trilaminar.

IAE-1-SRC-0957 The mass of cells that faces the amniotic cavity is:
['Hypoblast', 'Amniogenic epithelium', "Heusser's Membrane", 'Epiblast'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the Epiblast because it is the dorsal layer of the bilaminar disc and its upper surface forms the floor of the amniotic cavity. The hypoblast is the ventral layer facing the yolk sac, Heusser's membrane lines the primary yolk sac, and the amniogenic epithelium forms the roof and walls of the amniotic cavity rather than the disc itself.

IAE-1-SRC-0958 The primary yolk sac is roofed by:
['Hypoblast', 'Amniogenic epithelium', "Heusser's Membrane", 'Epiblast'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the Hypoblast because the primary yolk sac lies immediately beneath the disc and its roof is formed by the hypoblast, while its walls and floor are lined by the exocoelomic (Heusser's) membrane. The epiblast forms the floor of the amniotic cavity above, and the amniogenic epithelium belongs to the amnion.

IAE-1-SRC-0959 The cavity between the 2 layers of the primary mesoderm is:
['Amion', 'Yolk sac', 'Extra-embryonic coelom', 'Intra-embrtonic coelom'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the extra-embryonic coelom because cavitation within the extra-embryonic (primary) mesoderm splits it into a somatopleuric layer lining the trophoblast and amnion and a splanchnopleuric layer covering the yolk sac, and the cleft between them is the extra-embryonic coelom or chorionic cavity. The intra-embryonic coelom appears later within the lateral plate mesoderm of the embryo itself.

IAE-1-SRC-0960 The neuro-enteric canal is a temporary canal that connects the:
['Amnion with the extra-embryonic coelom', 'Amnion with the intra-embryonic coelom', 'Yolk sac with the amnion', 'Yolk sac with the extra-embryonic coelom'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the yolk sac with the amnion because the neurenteric canal is formed when the notochordal canal opens into the yolk sac while its cranial end still communicates with the amniotic cavity through the primitive pit, thereby transiently joining the two cavities. It has no connection with the extra-embryonic coelom and disappears once the notochordal plate folds off as the definitive notochord.

IAE-1-SRC-0961 The posterior (Caudal) neuropore is closed at:
['24th day', '26th day', '27th day', '28th day'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the 27th day because closure of the neural tube proceeds in both directions from the cervical region, the cranial neuropore closing about day 25 and the caudal neuropore about two days later on day 27. Failure of closure of this caudal neuropore produces spina bifida, just as failure of the cranial neuropore produces anencephaly.

IAE-1-SRC-0962 The embryonic disc becomes trilaminar at the:
['1st Week', '2nd Week', '3rd Week', '4th Week'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the 3rd week because gastrulation, the process by which epiblast cells migrate through the primitive streak to form the three definitive germ layers, takes place during the third week. The disc is bilaminar in the second week, and by the fourth week folding and organogenesis are already under way.

IAE-1-SRC-0963 The secondary mesoderm develops between the ectoderm and the endoderm except in:
['Oral membrane', 'Coloacal membrane', 'Across the notochord', 'All of the above'] KEY 3
The correct answer is all of the above because the intraembryonic mesoderm spreads everywhere between the ectoderm and endoderm apart from three sites: the oropharyngeal (oral) membrane and the cloacal membrane, where ectoderm and endoderm remain in direct contact, and the midline occupied by the notochord. These are precisely the regions where the two outer germ layers stay apposed.

IAE-1-SRC-0964 The total number of the segmented paraxial mesoderm (Somites) is:
['40 somites', '42 somites', '44 somites', '46 somites'] KEY 1
The correct answer is 42 somites because the paraxial mesoderm becomes segmented craniocaudally into about 42 pairs, comprising 4 occipital, 8 cervical, 12 thoracic, 5 lumbar, 5 sacral and about 8 coccygeal somites. Several of the most caudal coccygeal somites subsequently disappear, so the definitive contributing number is smaller.

IAE-1-SRC-0965 The intra-embryonic coelom is developed within the:
['Para-axial mesoderm', 'Intermediate plate mesoderm', 'Lateral plate mesoderm', 'Cardiogenic mesoderm'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the lateral plate mesoderm because clefts appear within it and coalesce to split it into a somatic (parietal) and a splanchnic (visceral) layer, the cavity between them being the intra-embryonic coelom that becomes the pericardial, pleural and peritoneal cavities. The paraxial mesoderm segments into somites and the intermediate mesoderm forms the urogenital system, neither of which cavitates in this way.

IAE-1-SRC-0966 The diaphragm is derived from:
['Septum transversum', 'Cardiogenic plate', 'Dermomyotomes', 'Sclerotomes'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the septum transversum because it forms the central tendon of the diaphragm, to which the pleuroperitoneal membranes, the dorsal mesentery of the oesophagus and the body wall musculature are subsequently added. The cardiogenic plate forms the heart, while sclerotomes form vertebrae and dermomyotomes form dermis and trunk muscles.

IAE-1-SRC-0967 The urogenital system is derived from:
['Para-axial mesoderm', 'Intermediate plate mesoderm', 'Lateral plate mesoderm', 'Cardiogenic mesoderm'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the intermediate plate mesoderm because this column, lying between the paraxial mesoderm and the lateral plate, forms the nephrogenic cord from which the kidneys, ureters and the genital ridges develop. The paraxial mesoderm gives somites, the lateral plate gives the body wall and serous membranes, and the cardiogenic mesoderm gives the heart.

IAE-1-SRC-0968 The parietal layer of the lateral plate mesoderm forms which of the following:
['Muscles of the limbs', 'Anterolateral body wall muscles', 'Smooth muscles of the viscera (GIT)', 'A + B', 'B + C'] KEY 3
The correct answer is A and B because the parietal (somatic) layer of the lateral plate mesoderm, together with the overlying ectoderm, forms the somatopleure that gives rise to the body wall including the limb buds and the anterolateral body wall. The smooth muscle of the gut belongs to the visceral (splanchnic) layer, so any option including it is incorrect.

IAE-1-SRC-0969 The visceral layer of the lateral plate mesoderm forms which of the following:
['Muscles of the limbs', 'Anterolateral body wall muscles', 'Smooth muscles of the viscera (GIT)', 'A + B', 'B + C'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the smooth muscles of the viscera because the visceral (splanchnic) layer of the lateral plate mesoderm covers the endodermal gut tube as the splanchnopleure and supplies the smooth muscle and connective tissue of the gut wall, together with the visceral layers of the serous membranes. Limb and body wall musculature belong to the somatic layer and the somites.

IAE-1-SRC-0970 The vertebral column is derived from which of the following:
['Sclerotomes only', 'Notochord only', 'Dermomyotomes', 'A + B', 'B + C'] KEY 3
The correct answer is A and B because the bodies and arches of the vertebrae are formed by the sclerotomes of the somites, while the notochord persists between them as the nucleus pulposus of each intervertebral disc. Dermomyotomes give the dermis and the segmental muscles, not the vertebrae themselves.

IAE-1-SRC-0971 As a result of the head fold, the most cranial structure of the embryo will be:
['The oral membrane', 'The septum transversum', 'The forebrain', 'The cardiogenic plate of mesoderm'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the forebrain because the head fold rotates the cranial end of the disc through nearly 180 degrees, so that the rapidly growing forebrain overhangs everything else and comes to lie most cranially. In doing so it carries the oral membrane, the cardiogenic plate and the septum transversum backwards beneath it, reversing their original order.

IAE-1-SRC-0972 As a result of the tail fold, the connecting stalk becomes:
['Caudal to the embryo', 'Cranial to the embryo', 'Ventral to the embryo', 'Dorsal to the embryo'] KEY 2
The correct answer is ventral to the embryo because the tail fold swings the caudal end of the disc forwards and downwards, carrying the connecting stalk from its original caudal position onto the ventral aspect of the embryo where it joins the yolk sac to form the umbilical cord. It never comes to lie dorsally or cranially.

IAE-1-SRC-0973 As a result of the 2 lateral folds, the yolk sac becomes:
['Expanded to surround the embryo', 'Compressed inside the endoderm to form the gut tube', 'Completely absorbed', 'None of the above'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that it is compressed inside the endoderm to form the gut tube, because the two lateral folds meet ventrally and pinch the wide communication between the embryo and the yolk sac into a narrow vitello-intestinal duct, incorporating the dorsal part of the yolk sac as the primitive gut. It is the amnion, not the yolk sac, that expands to surround the embryo.

IAE-1-SRC-0974 All of the following are derivatives of the endoderm except:
['Lining of the GIT & its glands', 'Lining of the respiratory passages', 'Endocrine glands (Except for the pituitary gland)', 'Salivary glands'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the salivary glands because they develop as ectodermal ingrowths from the lining of the stomatodeum, which is itself ectodermal, rather than from the endodermal foregut. The epithelial lining of the gut and its glands, the lining of the respiratory tract, and the endocrine glands such as the thyroid and parathyroids are all endodermal.

IAE-1-SRC-0975 All of the following are derivatives of the mesoderm except:
['Musculoskeletal system', 'Teeth except for the enamel', 'Cardiovascular system & lymphatic system', 'Lining of the body cavities', 'Arachnoid & pia matter'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the arachnoid and pia mater because these two leptomeninges are derived from neural crest ectoderm, whereas the tough dura mater is mesodermal. The musculoskeletal system, the dentine and pulp of the teeth, the whole cardiovascular and lymphatic system and the mesothelial lining of the body cavities are all mesodermal derivatives.

IAE-1-SRC-0976 The chorion frondosum is facing the:
['Dicidua basalis', 'Dicidua capsularis', 'Dicidua parietalis'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the decidua basalis because the villi related to the deep, well-vascularised decidua basalis continue to proliferate and form the bushy chorion frondosum, which becomes the fetal part of the placenta. The villi facing the poorly supplied decidua capsularis atrophy instead to form the smooth chorion laeve.

IAE-1-SRC-0977 The chorion laeve is facing the:
['Dicidua basalis', 'Dicidua capsularis', 'Dicidua parietalis'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the decidua capsularis because the villi on the superficial pole of the conceptus are compressed and poorly supplied as the capsularis stretches, so they degenerate leaving the smooth chorion laeve. The chorion frondosum, in contrast, faces the richly vascular decidua basalis.

IAE-1-SRC-0978 The primary chorionic villus is formed of:
['Cytotrophoblast + Syncytiotrophoblast', 'Cytotrophoblast, Syncytiotrophoblast + Mesodermal core', 'Cytotrophoblast, Syncytiotrophoblast, Mesodermal core + fetal blood vessels', 'All of the above'] KEY 0
The correct answer is cytotrophoblast plus syncytiotrophoblast because the primary villus consists only of a cytotrophoblastic core covered by syncytiotrophoblast. When extra-embryonic mesoderm invades that core it becomes a secondary villus, and when blood vessels differentiate within the mesoderm it becomes a tertiary villus.

IAE-1-SRC-0979 The sources of the amniotic fluid is:
['Fetal urine', 'Secretions of the GIT of the fetus', 'Amniogenic epithelium', 'All of the above'] KEY 3
The correct answer is all of the above because amniotic fluid is contributed to by the amniogenic epithelium and maternal tissue fluid early on, and increasingly by fetal urine and by secretions of the fetal respiratory and gastrointestinal tracts as pregnancy advances. This is why obstruction of the fetal gut causes hydramnios and renal agenesis causes oligohydramnios.

IAE-1-SRC-0980 All are the contents of the umbilical cord at birth except:
['2 Umbilical arteries', '2 Umbilical veins', "Wharton's jelly", 'Allantois & vitellointestinal duct'] KEY 1
The correct answer is two umbilical veins because only one umbilical vein, the left, persists to term while the right umbilical vein disappears early in development. The cord at birth normally contains two umbilical arteries, one umbilical vein and Wharton's jelly, together with the vestigial remnants of the allantois and vitello-intestinal duct.

IAE-1-SRC-0981 The lateral umbilical ligament is the remnant of:
['Umbilical arteries', 'Left umbilical vein', 'Allantois', 'Vitellointestinal duct'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the umbilical arteries because after birth their distal obliterated parts persist on either side of the bladder as the medial (lateral) umbilical ligaments, while their proximal parts remain patent as the superior vesical arteries. The left umbilical vein becomes the ligamentum teres and the allantois becomes the urachus or median umbilical ligament.

IAE-1-SRC-0982 The ligamentum teres of the liver is the remnant of:
['Umbilical arteries', 'Left umbilical vein', 'Allantois', 'Vitellointestinal duct'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the left umbilical vein because after birth it is obliterated and persists in the free margin of the falciform ligament as the ligamentum teres of the liver. The ductus venosus that bypassed the liver becomes the ligamentum venosum, while the umbilical arteries and allantois form the umbilical ligaments of the anterior abdominal wall.

IAE-1-SRC-0983 The median umbilical ligament id the remnant of the:
['Umbilical arteries', 'Left umbilical vein', 'Allantois', 'Vitellointestinal duct'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the allantois because its intra-embryonic part becomes the urachus, which after birth is obliterated to form the single midline median umbilical ligament running from the apex of the bladder to the umbilicus. The paired lateral umbilical ligaments are the remnants of the umbilical arteries.

IAE-1-SRC-0984 All of the following anomalies might be fatal to the embryo except:
['Too long umbilical cord', 'True knot of the umbilical cord', 'False knots of the umbilical cord', 'All of the above'] KEY 2
The correct answer is false knots because they are merely local accumulations of Wharton's jelly or tortuosities of the umbilical vessels and do not obstruct the circulation. A true knot can tighten and occlude the vessels, and an excessively long cord may prolapse or coil around the fetal neck, both of which can be fatal.

IAE-1-SRC-0985 When the villi are invading deep into the endometrium the placenta will be:
['Membrancea', 'Acreta', 'Bipartite', 'Accessory'] KEY 1
The correct answer is placenta accreta because in this condition the chorionic villi invade abnormally deeply and adhere directly to the myometrium owing to a deficient decidua basalis, so the placenta fails to separate after delivery and causes severe haemorrhage. Placenta membranacea, bipartite and accessory placenta are anomalies of placental shape rather than of depth of invasion.

IAE-1-SRC-0986 One of the following veins carries oxygenated blood:
['Axillary vein', 'Left umbilical vein', 'Vitelline vein', 'Inferior vena cava'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the left umbilical vein because it returns freshly oxygenated blood from the placenta to the fetus, making it the only truly oxygenated vein in the body. The umbilical arteries, by contrast, carry deoxygenated blood to the placenta; the axillary and vitelline veins and the inferior vena cava all carry venous blood.

IAE-1-SRC-0987 Down's syndrome is due to:
['Triasomy of the chromosome 14', 'Triasomy of the chromosome 18', 'Triasomy of the chromosome 21', 'Triasomy of the chromosome 23'] KEY 2
The correct answer is trisomy of chromosome 21 because Down syndrome results from an extra copy of chromosome 21, usually through non-disjunction during maternal meiosis, giving 47 chromosomes. Trisomy 18 is Edward syndrome and trisomy 13 is Patau syndrome, while there is no chromosome 23 in the numbered autosomal series.

IAE-1-SRC-0988 Kleinfelter's syndrome is due to:
['Triasomy of the X chromosome', 'Triasomy of the chromosome 18', 'Triasomy of the Y chromosome', 'Triasomy of the chromosome 21'] KEY 0
The correct answer is trisomy involving the X chromosome because Klinefelter syndrome has the karyotype 47,XXY, that is an extra X chromosome in a male, giving 44 autosomes plus XXY. Trisomy 18 is Edward syndrome and trisomy 21 is Down syndrome, and an extra Y alone produces the 47,XYY supermale rather than Klinefelter.

IAE-1-SRC-0989 The zona pellucida is produced by the activity of:
['The ovum', 'The follicular cells', 'The corpus luteum', 'A + B', 'All of the above'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the ovum and the follicular cells together because the glycoprotein layer of the zona pellucida is secreted jointly by the oocyte and by the surrounding granulosa (follicular) cells as the primary follicle grows. The corpus luteum is an endocrine structure that only forms after ovulation and plays no part in secreting the zona.

IAE-1-SRC-0990 Extra-embryonic coelom will form:
['Pericardial cavity', 'Pelural cavity', 'Peritoneal cavity', 'All of the above', 'None of the above'] KEY 4
The correct answer is none of the above because the pericardial, pleural and peritoneal cavities are all derived from the intra-embryonic coelom formed within the lateral plate mesoderm. The extra-embryonic coelom is the chorionic cavity outside the embryo, and it is progressively obliterated as the amnion expands.

IAE-1-SRC-0991 At the caudal end of the primitive streak, the ectoderm & the endoderm meet at:
['Notochord', 'Oral membrane', 'Coloacal membrane', 'None of the above'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the cloacal membrane because it is the bilaminar area at the caudal end of the disc where ectoderm and endoderm remain in direct contact with no intervening mesoderm. The corresponding site at the cranial end is the oropharyngeal or oral membrane, and the notochord is a midline mesodermal rod rather than a membrane.

IAE-1-SRC-0992 The notochord:
['Is endodermal in origin', 'Forms the nucleus pulposus of the intervertebral disc', 'Form the muscles of the back', 'All of the above'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that it forms the nucleus pulposus of the intervertebral disc, which is the only adult remnant of the notochord. The notochord is derived from epiblast cells migrating through the primitive node and so is ectodermal rather than endodermal in origin, and the muscles of the back come from the myotomes of the somites.

IAE-1-SRC-0993 Regarding the amnion, all of the following are true except:
['Amniotic cavity contains watery fluid', 'Hydroamnios may be due to esophageal atresia', 'Oligohydraminos may be due to renal agenesis (Failure of formation of the kidneys)', 'It prevents fetal movements'] KEY 3
The correct answer is that it prevents fetal movements, because the amniotic fluid does exactly the opposite and permits free symmetrical movement of the fetus, which is essential for normal musculoskeletal development. The other statements are true: the cavity contains watery fluid, oesophageal atresia prevents swallowing and causes hydramnios, and renal agenesis abolishes fetal urine and causes oligohydramnios.

IAE-1-SRC-0994 Drugs will produce a great teratogenic effect during which period of pregnancy:
['1st week of pregnancy', 'During the first 10-12 weeks of pregnancy', 'Between the 20 & 30 weeks of pregnancy', 'At the end of pregnancy'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the first 10-12 weeks because this covers the embryonic period of organogenesis, when the primordia of all the organ systems are being laid down and are maximally susceptible to teratogens. In the first week the conceptus tends to respond in an all-or-none fashion, while after the first trimester the fetus is growing and maturing rather than forming organs, so defects are functional rather than gross structural.

IAE-1-SRC-0995 A woman who believes she is pregnant complains of edema and vaginal bleeding. Examination reveals high plasma hCG concentrations and placental tissue, but no evidence of an embryo. How would you explain this condition?
 KEY
The correct answer is a hydatidiform mole because trophoblastic tissue with very high hCG and no embryo is the defining combination. The paternal-only genome permits trophoblastic overgrowth while the embryo proper never forms.

IAE-1-SRC-0996 A young woman who has missed two menstrual periods complains of intense abdominal pain. What might an initial diagnosis be, and how would you confirm it?
 KEY
The correct answer is ectopic (tubal) pregnancy because amenorrhoea plus acute abdominal pain in a woman of reproductive age is ectopic until proven otherwise. A positive hCG with an empty uterus on ultrasound confirms it.

IAE-1-SRC-0997 On ultrasound examination, it was determined that a fetus had well-developed facial and thoracic regions, but caudal structures were abnormal. Kidneys were absent, lumbar and sacral vertebrae were missing. What process may have been disturbed to cause such defects?
 KEY
The correct answer is a disturbance of gastrulation in the caudal region because the absent kidneys and missing lumbar and sacral vertebrae are all derivatives of caudal mesoderm, while the normally formed cranial structures show that early gastrulation proceeded correctly.

IAE-1-SRC-0998 A 22-year-old woman consumes large quantities of alcohol at a party and loses consciousness; 3 weeks later, she misses her second consecutive period. A pregnancy test is positive. Should she be concerned about the effects of her binge-drinking episode on her baby?
 KEY
The correct answer is yes because the exposure coincided with the third week of development, a highly sensitive stage, and alcohol is a well-established teratogen causing fetal alcohol spectrum disorder.

IAE-1-SRC-0999 The primitive streak develops in which of the following layers?
['Epiblast', 'Hypoblast', 'Amnion', 'Yolk sac', 'Trophoblast'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the Epiblast because the primitive streak appears as a thickening in the caudal midline of the epiblast, and it is epiblast cells that invaginate through it to form all three definitive germ layers. The hypoblast is displaced by these migrating cells rather than giving rise to the streak, and the amnion, yolk sac and trophoblast lie outside the embryonic disc altogether.

IAE-1-SRC-1000 Which of the following is derived from somites:
['Dermis', 'Ganglia', 'Pleura', 'Lungs', 'Kidneys'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the Dermis because each somite differentiates into a sclerotome, a myotome and a dermatome, and it is the dermatome that forms the dermis of the skin. Ganglia are neural crest derivatives, the pleura comes from the lateral plate mesoderm, the lining of the lungs is endodermal and the kidneys arise from the intermediate cell mass.

IAE-1-SRC-1001 Which syndrome has 44 XXY chromosomes?
["Down's syndrome", "Turner's syndrome", "Kleinfelter's syndrome", "Edward's syndrome", 'Cri du chat syndrome'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Klinefelter's syndrome because its karyotype is 44 autosomes plus XXY, an extra X chromosome in a phenotypic male, producing tall stature, small testes, gynaecomastia and infertility. Turner's syndrome is 44+XO, Down's is trisomy 21, Edward's is trisomy 18 and cri du chat is a deletion of the short arm of chromosome 5.

IAE-1-SRC-1002 An umbilical cord attached to the margin of the placenta is called:
['Velamentous insertion of cord', 'Placenta accreta', 'Battledore placenta', 'Succenturiata placenta', 'False cord knot'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Battledore placenta because that is the term for marginal insertion of the umbilical cord at the edge of the placental disc rather than centrally. Velamentous insertion is attachment to the membranes outside the placenta, placenta accreta is abnormally deep villous invasion, and succenturiate placenta is an accessory lobe.

IAE-1-SRC-1003 The major source of the intraembryonic mesoderm is:
['Primitive streak', 'Ectoderm', 'Endoderm', 'Prechordal plate', 'Neurenteric canal'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the primitive streak because the bulk of the intraembryonic mesoderm is formed by epiblast cells invaginating through it, with smaller contributions from the primitive node and the notochord. The endoderm is itself a product of this migration rather than a source of mesoderm, and the neurenteric canal is only a transient communication between the amniotic cavity and the yolk sac.

IAE-1-SRC-1004 The normal volume of the amniotic fluid at birth is:
['100 ml', '200 ml', '500 ml', '1000 ml', '1500 ml'] KEY 3
The correct answer is 1000 ml, that is about one litre, which is the normal volume of amniotic fluid at term. Volumes appreciably above this constitute hydramnios, classically caused by oesophageal atresia or anencephaly, and volumes appreciably below constitute oligohydramnios, classically caused by renal agenesis.

IAE-1-SRC-1005 Which of the following syndromes is caused by chromosomal monosomy?
["Down's syndrome", "Turner's syndrome", "Kleinfelter's syndrome", "Edward's syndrome", 'Cri du chat syndrome'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Turner's syndrome because its karyotype is 44+XO, a monosomy in which only a single X chromosome is present and the total count is 45. Down's and Edward's syndromes are trisomies of chromosomes 21 and 18, Klinefelter's has an extra X, and cri du chat is a partial deletion rather than a whole-chromosome monosomy.

IAE-1-SRC-1006 Which of the following syndromes has the genotype 44 + XXY?
["Down's syndrome", "Turner's syndrome", "Klinefelter's syndrome", 'Superfemale', "Edward's syndrome"] KEY 2
The correct answer is Klinefelter's syndrome, defined by the presence of an extra X chromosome in a male giving 44 autosomes plus XXY. The superfemale carries 44+XXX, Turner's carries 44+XO, and Down's and Edward's syndromes are autosomal trisomies of chromosomes 21 and 18 respectively.

IAE-1-SRC-1007 The kidneys develop from which part of the intraembryonic mesoderm?
['Cranial somites', 'Caudal somites', 'Intermediate cell mass', 'Visceral layer of lateral plate mesoderm', 'Somatic layer of lateral plate mesoderm'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the intermediate cell mass because this nephrogenic column between the paraxial mesoderm and the lateral plate gives rise to the pronephros, mesonephros and metanephros as well as to the gonads. The somites form the axial skeleton, muscles and dermis, while the two layers of the lateral plate form the body wall and the viscera.

IAE-1-SRC-1008 Which of the following is not found in the umbilical cord at full term?
['Right umbilical artery', "Wharton's jelly", 'Left umbilical vein', 'Allantois', 'Right umbilical vein'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the right umbilical vein because it degenerates early in development, leaving only the left umbilical vein to carry oxygenated blood from the placenta to the fetus. At term the cord contains two umbilical arteries, the single left umbilical vein, Wharton's jelly and the vestigial remnants of the allantois and vitello-intestinal duct.

IAE-1-SRC-1009 Which part of the skin is mesodermal in origin?
['Epidermis', 'Dermis', 'Nails', 'Pigment cells', 'Hairs'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the dermis because it is derived from the dermatome of the somites and from lateral plate mesoderm, whereas the epidermis and all of its appendages are ectodermal. Nails and hairs are epidermal appendages and the pigment cells are melanocytes derived from the neural crest, which is also ectodermal.

IAE-1-SRC-1010 The lining of the respiratory passages is derived from:
['Surface ectoderm', 'Neural crest', 'Mesoderm', 'Endoderm', 'Neuroectoderm'] KEY 3
The correct answer is Endoderm because the respiratory diverticulum buds from the ventral wall of the foregut, so the entire epithelial lining of the larynx, trachea, bronchi and alveoli is endodermal. The surrounding cartilage, smooth muscle and connective tissue come from splanchnic mesoderm, but the lining itself does not.

IAE-1-SRC-1011 Oligohydramnios in a pregnant female suggests which of the following anomalies?
['Renal agenesis', 'Esophageal atresia', 'Maternal diabetes', 'Intestinal obstruction', 'Pyloric stenosis'] KEY 0
The correct answer is renal agenesis because fetal urine is the major contributor to amniotic fluid in the later part of pregnancy, so failure of kidney formation results in a severely reduced fluid volume. The other options obstruct swallowing or increase fluid production and therefore cause hydramnios, the opposite abnormality.

IAE-1-SRC-1012 Which of the following syndromes has the genotype 44 + XO?
["Down's syndrome", "Turner's syndrome", "Klinefelter's syndrome", 'Superfemale', 'Supermale'] KEY 1
The correct answer is Turner's syndrome, in which a single X chromosome is present with 44 autosomes, giving a total of 45 and a phenotypic female with short stature, webbed neck, streak gonads and coarctation of the aorta. Klinefelter's is 44+XXY, the superfemale 44+XXX and the supermale 44+XYY.

IAE-1-SRC-1013 Which of the following is derived from the paraxial mesoderm?
['Spinal cord', 'Cardiac muscle', 'Kidneys', 'Pleura', 'Dermis of the skin'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the dermis of the skin because it arises from the dermatome portion of the somites, which are the segmented derivatives of the paraxial mesoderm. The spinal cord is neuroectodermal, cardiac muscle comes from splanchnic mesoderm, the kidneys from intermediate mesoderm and the pleura from the lateral plate.

IAE-1-SRC-1014 The number of somites present during the 22nd day of development is:
['Two', 'Four', 'Seven', 'Ten', 'Thirteen'] KEY 2
The correct answer is seven because the first somite appears on day 20 and roughly three new pairs are added each day thereafter, giving one pair on day 20, four pairs on day 21 and seven pairs on day 22. This regular rate makes the somite count a reliable means of estimating the age of a young embryo.

IAE-1-SRC-1015 The anterior neuropore closes on day:
['15', '18', '21', '25', '28'] KEY 3
The correct answer is day 25 because closure of the neural tube begins in the cervical region and proceeds in both directions, with the cranial (anterior) neuropore closing on about the twenty-fifth day and the caudal neuropore about two days later. Failure of the anterior neuropore to close results in anencephaly.

IAE-1-SRC-1016 Klinefelter's syndrome has a genotype of:
['44 + XY', '44 + XXX', '44 + XXY', '44 + X', '44 + XYY'] KEY 2
The correct answer is 44 + XXY because Klinefelter's syndrome results from non-disjunction of the sex chromosomes producing an extra X in a male. The genotype 44+XY is normal male, 44+XXX is the superfemale, 44+X is Turner's syndrome and 44+XYY is the supermale.

IAE-1-SRC-1017 The axial skeleton develops from:
['Paraxial mesoderm', 'Intermediate mesoderm', 'Lateral plate mesoderm', 'Surface ectoderm', 'Neural crest cells'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the paraxial mesoderm because it segments into somites whose sclerotomes migrate around the notochord and neural tube to form the vertebrae and ribs, that is the axial skeleton. The intermediate mesoderm forms the urogenital system, and the appendicular skeleton comes from lateral plate mesoderm in the limb buds.

IAE-1-SRC-1018 The genotype of Klinefelter's syndrome is:
['44 + X', '44 + XYY', '44 + XY', '44 + XXX', '44 + XXY'] KEY 4
The correct answer is 44 + XXY, the karyotype of Klinefelter's syndrome, in which an affected male has an additional X chromosome and a total of 47 chromosomes. Option 44+X is Turner's, 44+XYY is the supermale, 44+XY is a normal male and 44+XXX is the superfemale.

IAE-1-SRC-1019 The allantois is present in the:
['Septum transversum', 'Chorionic villi', 'Connecting stalk', 'Amniotic cavity', 'Placenta'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the connecting stalk because the allantois is a small endodermal diverticulum from the caudal part of the yolk sac that grows into the extra-embryonic mesoderm of the connecting stalk. Its blood vessels become the umbilical vessels, and its intra-embryonic part becomes the urachus.

IAE-1-SRC-1020 Which of the following is NOT derived from the ectoderm?
['Spinal cord', 'Epidermis and skin appendages', 'Neural crest cells', 'Sensory epithelium', 'Adrenal cortex'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the adrenal cortex because it develops from coelomic (mesodermal) epithelium, in contrast to the adrenal medulla which is a neural crest derivative and therefore ectodermal. The spinal cord and sensory epithelia come from neuroectoderm, the epidermis and its appendages from surface ectoderm, and the neural crest is itself ectodermal.

IAE-1-SRC-1021 The axial skeleton is derived from:
['Somites', 'Intermediate cell mass of mesoderm', 'Intraembryonic somatopleure', 'Neural groove', 'Intraembryonic splanchnopleure'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the somites because their sclerotomic portions form the vertebral column, the ribs and the base of the skull. The intermediate cell mass gives the urogenital system, the somatopleure and splanchnopleure give the body wall and the gut wall respectively, and the neural groove gives the central nervous system.

IAE-1-SRC-1022 Which of the following is endodermal in origin?
['Brain', 'Heart', 'Bronchial tree', 'Eye', 'Dermis of the skin'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the bronchial tree because its epithelial lining develops as an outgrowth of the endodermal foregut. The brain and the eye are neuroectodermal, the heart is derived from splanchnic mesoderm and the dermis from the somites and lateral plate mesoderm.

IAE-1-SRC-1023 Oligohydramnios could indicate:
['Diabetes in the mother', 'Knots in the umbilical cord', 'Renal agenesis', 'Large fetus', 'Esophageal atresia'] KEY 2
The correct answer is renal agenesis because the fetal kidneys produce most of the amniotic fluid in later pregnancy, so their absence gives a markedly reduced volume, a combination known as Potter sequence with pulmonary hypoplasia and limb deformities. Maternal diabetes and oesophageal atresia produce hydramnios instead, and cord knots do not alter the fluid volume.

IAE-1-SRC-1024 Which of the following drugs is NOT teratogenic?
['Anticonvulsants', 'Tetracycline', 'Heparin', 'Antithyroid drugs', 'Valium'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Heparin because its large, highly charged molecule cannot cross the placenta, which makes it the anticoagulant of choice in pregnancy. Anticonvulsants such as phenytoin, tetracycline which stains developing teeth and bone, antithyroid drugs which cause fetal goitre, and Valium are all recognised teratogens.

IAE-1-SRC-1025 At which day the embryo shows 10 pairs of somites?
['20', '21', '22', '23', '24'] KEY 3
The correct answer is day 23 because the first pair of somites appears on day 20 and about three further pairs are added daily, giving four pairs on day 21, seven on day 22 and ten on day 23. The somite count is therefore a standard criterion for estimating the age of an embryo in the somite period.

IAE-1-SRC-1026 Which of the following is ectodermal in origin:
['Brain', 'Heart', 'Lung', 'Liver', 'Kidney'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the Brain because the entire central nervous system develops from the neuroectoderm of the neural plate and neural tube. The heart arises from splanchnic mesoderm, the lung and liver are endodermal outgrowths of the foregut, and the kidney develops from the intermediate mesoderm.

IAE-1-SRC-1027 A boy having Down's syndrome is characterized by a chromosomal formula:
['44 + XO', '44 + XXY', '45 + XX', '43 + XY', '45 + XY'] KEY 4
The correct answer is 45 + XY because Down syndrome is trisomy 21, so an affected boy has 45 autosomes instead of 44 together with the normal male XY sex chromosomes, giving 47 in total. Option 44+XO is Turner's syndrome, 44+XXY is Klinefelter's, and 45+XX would be a girl with Down syndrome rather than a boy.

IAE-1-SRC-1028 Hydramnios could indicate which abnormality in the fetus?
['Esophageal atresia', 'Agenesis of the kidneys', 'A large liver', 'A large spleen', 'An anomaly in the heart'] KEY 0
The correct answer is oesophageal atresia because the fetus normally swallows amniotic fluid, and an atretic oesophagus blocks this route of removal so that fluid accumulates. Agenesis of the kidneys abolishes fetal urine and therefore causes the opposite condition, oligohydramnios, while organomegaly and cardiac anomalies are not classical causes.

IAE-1-SRC-1029 Which of the following is derived from the intermediate cell mass of intraembryonic mesoderm?
['Kidneys', 'Heart', 'Dermis', 'Trunk muscles', 'Blood vessels'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the Kidneys because the intermediate cell mass forms the nephrogenic cord and the urogenital ridge, from which the urinary system and the gonads develop. The heart and blood vessels come from splanchnic mesoderm, while the dermis and the trunk muscles come from the dermatomes and myotomes of the somites.

IAE-1-SRC-1030 The earliest somites begin to appear on day:
['15', '18', '20', '25', '28'] KEY 2
The correct answer is day 20 because the first pair of somites forms in the occipital region on the twentieth day, after which new pairs appear craniocaudally at a rate of about three per day. Days 15 and 18 fall in the period of gastrulation before segmentation begins, and by day 25 to 28 many pairs are already present.

IAE-1-SRC-1031 The neural crest cells give rise to the following EXCEPT:
['Melanocytes', 'Dorsal root ganglia', 'Autonomic ganglia', 'Adrenal medulla', 'Retina of the eye'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the retina of the eye because it develops from the optic vesicle, an outgrowth of the neural tube itself, and not from the neural crest. Melanocytes, the dorsal root and autonomic ganglia and the chromaffin cells of the adrenal medulla are all classical neural crest derivatives.

IAE-1-SRC-1032 The vessels of the umbilical cord are:
['1 artery, 1 vein', '2 arteries, 2 veins', '1 artery, 2 veins', '2 arteries, 1 vein', '2 arteries, no veins'] KEY 3
The correct answer is two arteries and one vein because the right umbilical vein degenerates, leaving the paired umbilical arteries carrying deoxygenated blood to the placenta and the single left umbilical vein returning oxygenated blood to the fetus. A single umbilical artery is an important anomaly associated with other congenital defects.

IAE-1-SRC-1033 Which of the following fetal anomalies may cause hydramnios (polyhydramnios)?
['Cardiac', 'Gastrointestinal', 'Musculoskeletal', 'Urinary', 'Nervous'] KEY 1
The correct answer is gastrointestinal anomalies because atresias of the oesophagus or duodenum prevent the fetus from swallowing and absorbing amniotic fluid, so it accumulates. Urinary anomalies such as renal agenesis reduce fluid production and cause oligohydramnios instead, and cardiac and musculoskeletal defects do not classically alter the volume in this way.

IAE-1-SRC-1034 Nucleus pulposus of intervertebral disc develops from the:
['Neural crest', 'Notochord', 'Sclerotome', 'Somite', 'Ectoderm'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Notochord because it is the sole adult remnant of the notochord, persisting as the gelatinous nucleus pulposus at the centre of each intervertebral disc. The sclerotomes of the somites form the vertebral bodies and the surrounding annulus fibrosus, but not the nucleus itself.

IAE-1-SRC-1035 Which germ layer forms the gastrointestinal tract?
['Endoderm', 'Ectoderm', 'Paraxial mesoderm', 'Intermediate mesoderm', 'Lateral plate mesoderm'] KEY 0
The correct answer is Endoderm because the epithelial lining of the gut tube and of its derived glands, including the liver and pancreas, is endodermal. The muscular and connective tissue coats of the gut are supplied by the splanchnic layer of the lateral plate mesoderm, but the question asks for the layer that forms the tract itself.

IAE-1-SRC-1036 Pigment cells of the skin (melanocytes) are derived from the:
['Notochord', 'Surface ectoderm', 'Neural crest', 'Lateral plate mesoderm', 'Dermomyotome'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Neural crest because melanoblasts arise from neural crest cells that migrate extensively to reach the basal layer of the epidermis, where they differentiate into melanocytes.
