The surrounding keratinocytes come from surface ectoderm and the dermis from the dermomyotome, but neither produces the pigment cells.

IAE-1-SRC-1037 Induces embryonic development of the nervous system:
['Connecting stalk', 'Notochord', 'Hypoblast', 'Primitive streak', 'Prochordal plate'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Notochord because it induces the overlying ectoderm to thicken into the neural plate, which is the first step of neurulation. The primitive streak generates the mesoderm and endoderm, the hypoblast is displaced during gastrulation and the connecting stalk is an extra-embryonic structure.

IAE-1-SRC-1038 The following structures are of neural crest origin EXCEPT:
['Melanocytes', 'Dorsal root ganglion', 'Schwann cells', 'Dura matter', 'Arachnoid matter'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the dura mater because it is mesodermal in origin, in contrast to the arachnoid and pia mater which are derived from the neural crest. Melanocytes, the dorsal root ganglion and the Schwann cells of peripheral nerves are all standard neural crest derivatives.

IAE-1-SRC-1039 Turner's syndrome has the following genotype:
['44 + XY', '44 + X', '44 + XXY', '44 + XXX', '44 + XYY'] KEY 1
The correct answer is 44 + X because Turner's syndrome is a monosomy in which only one sex chromosome, an X, is present, giving a total of 45 chromosomes. The genotype 44+XXY is Klinefelter's, 44+XXX is the superfemale, 44+XYY is the supermale and 44+XY is a normal male.

IAE-1-SRC-1040 On what week of intrauterine life does gastrulation occur?
['2nd', '4th', '5th', '3rd', '1st'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the 3rd week because gastrulation, the formation of the trilaminar germ disc through the primitive streak, is the defining event of the third week of development. The first week is cleavage and implantation, the second week produces the bilaminar disc, and by the fourth week folding and organogenesis are already in progress.

IAE-1-SRC-1041 The notochord is derived from:
['Endoderm', 'Hypoblast', 'Mesoderm', 'Ectoderm', 'Epiblast'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the Epiblast because notochordal cells are epiblast cells that migrate through the primitive node to form the notochordal process and then the definitive notochord. Since the epiblast is the source of all three germ layers and is itself ectodermal, the notochord is described as ectodermal in origin rather than endodermal.

IAE-1-SRC-1042 Lateral folding leads to the formation of an elongated:
['Yolk sac', 'Gut tube', 'Allantois', 'Spinal tube', 'Heart tube'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the Gut tube because the two lateral folds meet in the ventral midline and convert the flat endodermal sheet into an elongated tube, the primitive gut, while narrowing its communication with the yolk sac. The heart tube forms by fusion of the endocardial tubes and the neural tube by closure of the neural folds, both independently of lateral folding.

IAE-1-SRC-1043 Somites may differentiate into all of the following except:
['Bone', 'Epidermis of skin', 'Muscles', 'Cartilage', 'Connective tissue'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the epidermis of the skin because it is a surface ectodermal derivative, whereas the somite forms only mesodermal tissues. The sclerotome gives bone and cartilage, the myotome gives skeletal muscle and the dermatome gives the dermis and its connective tissue.

IAE-1-SRC-1044 One of the following is endodermal in origin:
['Gut epithelium', 'Enamel of teeth', 'Kidneys', 'Skeletal muscles', 'Bones'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the gut epithelium because the entire epithelial lining of the digestive tube is derived from the endoderm of the primitive gut. Enamel is ectodermal, while the kidneys, skeletal muscles and bones are all mesodermal derivatives.

IAE-1-SRC-1045 The arteries, veins and lymphatic channels develop from:
['Villi', 'Ectoderm', 'Mesoderm', 'Amnion', 'Endoderm'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Mesoderm because all blood vessels and lymphatics arise by vasculogenesis and angiogenesis from mesodermal blood islands and from angioblasts of mesodermal origin. Neither the ectoderm nor the endoderm forms vascular tissue, and the villi and amnion are structures rather than germ layers.

IAE-1-SRC-1046 Amniotic cavity is temporarily connected to yolk sac cavity via:
['Breakdown of oral membrane', 'Allantois', 'Neurenteric canal', 'Vitello-intestinal duct', 'Breakdown of cloacal membrane'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Neurenteric canal because it is the transient passage formed when the notochordal canal opens into the yolk sac while still communicating with the amniotic cavity through the primitive pit. The vitello-intestinal duct connects the gut to the yolk sac rather than the amnion, and the allantois is a diverticulum of the hindgut.

IAE-1-SRC-1047 All the following are parts of the intraembryonic mesoderm EXCEPT:
['Sclerotome', 'Intermediate cell mass', 'Dermatome', 'Lateral plate mesoderm', 'Connecting stalk'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the connecting stalk because it is composed of extra-embryonic mesoderm and lies outside the embryonic disc, later becoming part of the umbilical cord. The sclerotome and dermatome are subdivisions of the paraxial mesoderm, and the intermediate cell mass and lateral plate are the other two columns of intraembryonic mesoderm.

IAE-1-SRC-1048 All are true regarding the normal umbilical cord at birth EXCEPT:
['Contains one umbilical vein', 'Is covered by amnion', 'Contains the ductus venosus', 'Contains two umbilical arteries', 'Is approximately 50 cm long'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that it contains the ductus venosus, because that vessel is an intra-embryonic shunt lying within the liver which connects the umbilical vein to the inferior vena cava, and it is never a content of the cord. The cord is genuinely covered by amnion, contains one umbilical vein and two umbilical arteries, and measures about 50 cm at term.

IAE-1-SRC-1049 At which day in development does the somites start to appear?
['10th', '15th', '20th', '28th', '35th'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the 20th day because the first pair of somites appears in the occipital region on day 20, marking the start of the somite period. Days 10 and 15 precede the formation of the paraxial mesoderm columns, while by days 28 to 35 the full complement of about 42 pairs is already established.

IAE-1-SRC-1050 A case of hydramnios would make you suspect the baby may have:
['Renal agensis', 'Oesophageal atresia', 'Immature lungs', 'Absent spleen', 'Cardiac anomaly'] KEY 1
The correct answer is oesophageal atresia because an obstructed oesophagus prevents the fetus from swallowing amniotic fluid, so the volume rises and hydramnios results. Renal agenesis reduces fetal urine and therefore produces oligohydramnios, the opposite picture, while immature lungs, an absent spleen and cardiac anomalies do not classically alter the fluid volume.

IAE-1-SRC-1051 Match the syndrome with the correct chromosomal formula.
 KEY
Klinefelter's syndrome is an extra X in a male giving 44 + XXY, while Turner's syndrome is monosomy X giving 44 + XO. Down's syndrome is the autosomal trisomy 21, the superfemale carries an extra X as 44 + XXX, and the supermale carries an extra Y as 44 + XYY.

IAE-1-SRC-1052 Match the syndrome in the column "I" with its chromosomal alteration in column "II".
 KEY
The supermale has an extra Y chromosome (44 + XYY) and Klinefelter's an extra X in a male (44 + XXY), while Turner's is the monosomy 44 + XO. Among the autosomal trisomies, Patau's syndrome is trisomy 13 and Down's syndrome is trisomy 21.

IAE-1-SRC-1053 Mesoderm is absent in:
['Somites', 'Oral membrane', 'Nephrogenic cord', 'Sclerotomes', 'Dermomyotomes'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the oral membrane because at this site, like the cloacal membrane and the midline notochord, the ectoderm and endoderm remain in direct contact with no mesoderm intervening. The somites, sclerotomes, dermomyotomes and nephrogenic cord are all themselves mesodermal structures.

IAE-1-SRC-1054 By the end of 26 days, number of somites is:
['14', '16', '18', '19', '22'] KEY 3
The correct answer is 19 pairs because somites appear from day 20 at a rate of about three pairs per day, so by the end of day 26 approximately nineteen pairs are present. This orderly progression is why the somite count is used as an index of embryonic age during the fourth week.

IAE-1-SRC-1055 Skin develops from:
['Ectoderm', 'Endoderm', 'Mesoderm', 'Ectoderm & mesoderm', 'Ectoderm & Endoderm'] KEY 3
The correct answer is ectoderm and mesoderm because the skin is a dual-origin organ: the epidermis with its hairs, nails and glands comes from surface ectoderm, while the dermis comes from mesoderm, chiefly the dermatomes of the somites. The endoderm makes no contribution to the skin at all.

IAE-1-SRC-1056 Which of the following develops from neural crest?
['Dura mater', 'Suprarenal cortex', 'Dorsal root ganglion', 'Spinal cord', 'Cerebral hemisphere'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the dorsal root ganglion because the sensory neurons of the spinal ganglia are formed by neural crest cells that migrate laterally from the closing neural tube. The dura mater and suprarenal cortex are mesodermal, while the spinal cord and cerebral hemispheres develop from the neural tube itself rather than from the crest.

IAE-1-SRC-1057 Primitive streak:
['Appears at the cranial end of the embryonic disc', 'Its formation indicates the beginning of gastrulation', 'Is formed by hypoblast cells', 'Appears at the end of the 3rd week', 'Forms the primitive node at its caudal end'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that its formation indicates the beginning of gastrulation, since the appearance of the streak is the event that initiates the migration of epiblast cells to form the three germ layers. It appears at the caudal end of the disc, is formed by epiblast rather than hypoblast cells, appears at the beginning rather than the end of the third week, and its primitive node lies at its cranial end.

IAE-1-SRC-1058 Neurenteric canal is a temporary connection between:
['Amniotic cavity & extraembryonic ceolem', 'Yolk sac & extraembryonic coelem', 'Amniotic cavity & neural tube', 'Amniotic cavity & yolk sac', 'Yolk sac & intraembyonic coelom'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the amniotic cavity and the yolk sac, which the neurenteric canal joins transiently when the floor of the notochordal canal breaks down while the primitive pit still opens into the amnion. It disappears when the notochordal plate folds off to form the definitive notochord, and it never communicates with the coelomic cavities.

IAE-1-SRC-1059 Visceral pericardium originates from:
['Splanchnic layer of lateral plate mesoderm', 'Somatic layer of lateral plate mesoderm', 'Paraxial mesoderm', 'Intermediate mesoderm', 'Splanchnic layer of extraembryonic mesoderm'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the splanchnic layer of the lateral plate mesoderm because it clothes the developing viscera and therefore forms the visceral layer of every serous membrane, that is the visceral pericardium, the visceral pleura and the visceral peritoneum. The somatic layer lines the body wall and forms the corresponding parietal layers.

IAE-1-SRC-1060 Which one of the following is a derivative of the neural tube?
['Autonomic nervous system', 'Spinal cord', 'Suprarenal medulla', 'Pia matter', 'Schwann cells'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the spinal cord because the neural tube gives rise to the entire central nervous system, that is the brain and the spinal cord. The autonomic ganglia, the suprarenal medulla, the pia mater and the Schwann cells are all derived from the neural crest, which separates from the tube but is a distinct population.

IAE-1-SRC-1061 As regards the primitive node, choose the INCORRECT statement:
['It appears during 3rd gestational week', 'It forms the notochordal process', 'It is formed of ectoderm & endoderm', 'It has a small pit in its middle', 'It is formed at the cephalic end of the primitive streak'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that it is formed of ectoderm and endoderm, because the primitive node is in fact a heaping-up of epiblast cells at the cranial end of the streak. The other statements are correct: it appears in the third week, it contains the primitive pit, and the cells migrating through it form the notochordal process.

IAE-1-SRC-1062 At caudal end of the primitive streak, ectoderm & endoderm meet at:
['Notochordal canal', 'Cloacal membrane', 'Buccopharyngeal membrane', 'Intraembryonic coelon', 'None of the above'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the cloacal membrane because it is the caudal bilaminar area where ectoderm and endoderm are directly apposed without intervening mesoderm. The buccopharyngeal membrane is the equivalent structure at the cranial end of the disc, so it is the wrong end for this question.

IAE-1-SRC-1063 The intraembryonic mesoderm develops in:
['1st week', '2nd week', '3rd week', '4th week', '8th week'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the 3rd week because the intraembryonic mesoderm is formed during gastrulation, when epiblast cells migrate through the primitive streak between the epiblast and hypoblast. In the second week only the two layers of the bilaminar disc exist, and by the fourth week the mesoderm has already differentiated into paraxial, intermediate and lateral plate columns.

IAE-1-SRC-1064 The neural crest appears along the prominent margins of:
['Neural fold', 'Notochord', 'Neurenteric canal', 'Anterior neuropore', 'Posterior neuropore'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the neural fold because the neural crest cells arise at the crests of the elevated neural folds and detach as the folds fuse to form the neural tube. The neuropores are merely the temporary openings at each end of the closing tube, and the notochord lies ventral to the tube.

IAE-1-SRC-1065 Which of the following is derived from endoderm?
['Spleen', 'Kidneys', 'Blood vessels', 'Lymphatics', 'Lining epithelium of alveoli'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the lining epithelium of the alveoli because the whole respiratory epithelium, down to the alveolar lining, develops from the endodermal respiratory diverticulum of the foregut. The spleen, kidneys, blood vessels and lymphatics are all mesodermal derivatives.

IAE-1-SRC-1066 All the following are results of folding EXCEPT:
['Forebrain is at cranial end of embryo', 'Hindgut is enclosed within the tail fold', 'Foregut is enclosed within the head fold', 'Connecting stalk becomes dorsal', 'Oral membrane becomes ventral'] KEY 3
The correct answer is that the connecting stalk becomes dorsal, because folding in fact carries it to the ventral aspect of the embryo where it contributes to the umbilical cord. The other statements are true results of folding: the forebrain comes to lie most cranially, the head and tail folds enclose the foregut and hindgut respectively, and the oral membrane is rotated to a ventral position.

IAE-1-SRC-1067 As regards the somites, all the following statements are true EXCEPT:
['They develop from lateral plate mesoderm', 'Its ventromedial parts form sclerotomes', 'Its dorsolateral parts form dermomyotomes', 'Its dermatomes form dermis of skin', 'Its sclerotomes form vertebral column'] KEY 0
The correct answer is that they develop from lateral plate mesoderm, because somites in fact arise by segmentation of the paraxial mesoderm alongside the neural tube. The remaining statements are true: the ventromedial part becomes the sclerotome which forms the vertebral column, and the dorsolateral part becomes the dermomyotome whose dermatome forms the dermis.

IAE-1-SRC-1068 Segmentation is observed in the......... mesoderm:
['Paraxial', 'Intermediate', 'Lateral plate', 'Splanchnic', 'Branchial'] KEY 0
The correct answer is the paraxial mesoderm because it is the only column that breaks up into the repeating blocks called somites, the basis of the segmental plan of the body. The intermediate and lateral plate mesoderm remain unsegmented, the lateral plate splitting instead into somatic and splanchnic layers around the coelom.

IAE-1-SRC-1069 Sources of intraembryonic mesoderm:
['Primitive streak', 'Primitive node', 'Notochord', 'All of the above', 'None of the above'] KEY 3
The correct answer is all of the above because the intraembryonic mesoderm is contributed by the primitive streak, the primitive node and the notochord together. The streak supplies the bulk of it, while the node and the notochordal process contribute the axial component.

IAE-1-SRC-1070 Intraembryonic mesoderm is absent in:
['Notochord', 'Oropharyngeal membrane', 'Prochordal plate', 'Cloacal membrane', 'All of the above'] KEY 4
The correct answer is all of the above because at each of these sites the migrating mesoderm fails to intervene: the notochord occupies the midline axis, the prochordal plate lies cranial to it, and at the oropharyngeal and cloacal membranes the ectoderm and endoderm remain in direct contact. These bilaminar areas later break down to form the mouth and the anal and urogenital openings.

IAE-1-SRC-1071 Neural plate is derived from:
['Notochord', 'Mesoderm', 'Endoderm', 'Ectoderm dorsal to notochord', 'Ectoderm on whole dorsal aspect of embryo'] KEY 3
The correct answer is the ectoderm dorsal to the notochord, because only that midline strip of ectoderm receives the inducing signal from the underlying notochord and thickens into the neural plate. The remaining dorsal ectoderm stays as surface ectoderm and becomes the epidermis, so the answer specifying the whole dorsal aspect is too broad.

IAE-1-SRC-1072 At the cranial end of the notochord, ectoderm & endoderm meet at:
['Primitive node', 'Cloacal membrane', 'Oropharyngeal membrane', 'Heart forming mesoderm', 'Neural plate'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the oropharyngeal membrane because this bilaminar area at the cranial end of the notochord, also called the buccopharyngeal membrane, is where ectoderm and endoderm are directly apposed. It later ruptures to open the mouth into the foregut, while the corresponding caudal structure is the cloacal membrane.

IAE-1-SRC-1073 As regards the notochord, all the following statements are true EXCEPT:
['Is endodermal in origin', 'Develops between ectoderm & endoderm', 'Shares in formation of intervertebral disc', 'Forms the axis of the embryo', 'Is caudal to the prochordal plate'] KEY 0
The correct answer is that it is endodermal in origin, because the notochord arises from epiblast cells migrating through the primitive node and is therefore regarded as ectodermal. All the other statements are true: it lies between ectoderm and endoderm, forms the primitive axis of the embryo, lies caudal to the prochordal plate, and persists as the nucleus pulposus of the intervertebral disc.

IAE-1-SRC-1074 The notochord:
['Is derived from roof of yolk sac', 'Forms the nucleus pulposus of intervertebral disc', 'Is found caudal to cloacal membrane', 'Forms the spinal cord', 'Forms bodies of vertebrae'] KEY 1
The correct answer is that it forms the nucleus pulposus of the intervertebral disc, its only persisting adult derivative. It is derived from epiblast rather than from the roof of the yolk sac, it lies cranial to the cloacal membrane, and the spinal cord and vertebral bodies are formed by the neural tube and the sclerotomes respectively.

IAE-1-SRC-1075 All the following are derivatives of ectoderm EXCEPT:
['Pituitary gland', 'Nervous system', 'Pancreas', 'Adrenal medulla', 'Enamel of teeth'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the Pancreas because it develops as dorsal and ventral endodermal buds from the foregut. The pituitary is ectodermal from both Rathke's pouch and the neurohypophyseal bud, the nervous system from neuroectoderm, the adrenal medulla from neural crest, and enamel from the ectodermal enamel organ.

IAE-1-SRC-1076 All the following are derivatives of ectoderm EXCEPT:
['Epidermis of skin', 'Dermis of skin', 'Hairs', 'Nails', 'Sweat glands'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the dermis of the skin because it is mesodermal, formed from the dermatomes of the somites and from lateral plate mesoderm. The epidermis and all its appendages, that is the hairs, nails and sweat glands, are derivatives of the surface ectoderm.

IAE-1-SRC-1077 All the following are derivatives of ectoderm EXCEPT:
['Sensory epithelium of ear, nose & eye', 'Epithelium of skin', 'Epithelium of gastrointestinal tract', 'Brain & spinal cord', 'Peripheral nervous system'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the epithelium of the gastrointestinal tract because the lining of the gut tube is endodermal throughout. The special sensory epithelia, the epidermis, the brain and spinal cord and the peripheral nervous system are all derived from surface ectoderm, neuroectoderm or neural crest.

IAE-1-SRC-1078 All the following are fetal membranes EXCEPT:
['Umbilical cord', 'Amnion', 'Placenta', 'Yolk sac', 'Notochord'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the Notochord because it is an intra-embryonic axial structure, not a fetal membrane. The fetal membranes are the extra-embryonic structures that support the embryo, namely the amnion, chorion and placenta, the yolk sac, the allantois and the umbilical cord.

IAE-1-SRC-1079 Definitive yolk sac:
['Is lined by mesoderm', 'Has a wall made of ectoderm', 'Is found in umbilical cord', 'May be found in amniotic cavity', 'Is derived from trophoblast'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that it is found in the umbilical cord, because after folding the shrinking definitive (secondary) yolk sac is carried into the cord along with the vitelline duct. Its cavity is lined by endoderm covered externally by extra-embryonic mesoderm, and it is not a trophoblastic derivative.

IAE-1-SRC-1080 Secondary yolk sac cavity is lined by:
['Ectoderm', 'Mesoderm', 'Endoderm', 'All of the above', 'None of the above'] KEY 2
The correct answer is Endoderm because the cavity of the secondary yolk sac is lined by extra-embryonic endodermal cells derived from the hypoblast. Extra-embryonic mesoderm covers this endoderm on the outside, but it does not line the cavity itself.

IAE-1-SRC-1081 As regards amnion, all the following are true EXCEPT:
['Amniotic cavity contains watery fluid', 'It acts as a protective water jacket for the body', 'It prevents fetal movements', 'It is a medium for excretion of urine', 'It forms the bag of forewater during labor'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that it prevents fetal movements, because the amniotic fluid does the opposite and permits free symmetrical movement of the fetus, which is essential for normal musculoskeletal development. Its other functions are correctly stated: it cushions the fetus as a water jacket, receives fetal urine and forms the bag of forewaters that helps dilate the cervix in labour.

IAE-1-SRC-1082 The primitive umbilical cord contains all the following EXCEPT:
['Vitelline duct', 'One umbilical vein', 'Allantois', 'One umbilical artery', 'Extraembryonic coelom'] KEY 3
The correct answer is one umbilical artery because the cord contains two umbilical arteries, and a single artery is an anomaly associated with other congenital defects. The primitive cord correctly contains the vitelline duct, the allantois, the single persisting left umbilical vein and a portion of the extra-embryonic coelom holding the physiological midgut hernia.

IAE-1-SRC-1083 As regards anomalies of umbilical cord, the following are true EXCEPT:
['A long cord may coil around the neck of the fetus', 'A short cord may cause early placental separation', "A true knot is due to local accumulation of Wharton's jelly", 'A false knot does not affect the fetus', 'In battledore placenta, the umbilical cord is attached to margin of placenta'] KEY 2
The correct answer is that a true knot is due to accumulation of Wharton's jelly, because it is a local heaping of Wharton's jelly that produces a harmless FALSE knot. A true knot results from the fetus actually passing through a loop of a long cord and can obstruct the circulation, while the other statements are correct.

IAE-1-SRC-1084 Drugs will produce the greater teratogenic effect during the following period of pregnancy:
['On day of conception', 'During the first 10-12 weeks of pregnancy', 'Between 20th to 25th weeks of pregnancy', 'Between 25th to 30th weeks of pregnancy', 'At the terminal stage of pregnancy'] KEY 1
The correct answer is during the first 10 to 12 weeks because organogenesis takes place in the embryonic period, making this the phase of maximum susceptibility to teratogens. Exposure at conception tends to follow an all-or-none pattern, and later exposure in the fetal period usually causes growth or functional disturbance rather than gross structural malformation.

IAE-1-SRC-1085 Which one of the following structures is formed by the somatic layer of the lateral plate of intraembryonic mesoderm?
['Epidermis of skin', 'Smooth muscles of the wall of the esophagus', 'Supportive elements of the body wall', 'Muscles of the trunk', 'Dermis of the skin'] KEY 2
The correct answer is the supportive elements of the body wall because the somatic layer, together with the overlying ectoderm, forms the body wall and its connective tissue, bones and the parietal serous layers. The smooth muscle of the oesophagus comes from the splanchnic layer, the trunk muscles from the myotomes and the epidermis from surface ectoderm.

IAE-1-SRC-1086 Down syndrome is due to:
['Monosomy of autosomes', 'Monosomy of sex chromosomes', 'Trisomy of autosomes', 'Trisomy of sex chromosomes', 'None of the above'] KEY 2
The correct answer is trisomy of autosomes because Down syndrome is caused by three copies of chromosome 21, an autosome, usually from meiotic non-disjunction. Monosomy of a sex chromosome describes Turner's syndrome and trisomy of the sex chromosomes describes Klinefelter's and the super sex syndromes.

IAE-1-SRC-1087 As regards the allantois, choose the correct statement:
['Is a diverticulum of the hindgut', 'Completely degenerates postnatally', 'Is an outpouching of the coelom into the connecting stalk', 'Is not found in the umbilical cord at any time', 'All the above'] KEY 0
The correct answer is that it is a diverticulum of the hindgut, an endodermal outpouching that extends into the connecting stalk. It does not disappear completely, since its intra-embryonic part persists as the urachus, later the median umbilical ligament, and it is indeed present within the umbilical cord.

IAE-1-SRC-1088 All the following are mesodermal derivatives EXCEPT:
['Bones', 'Kidneys', 'Enamel of teeth', 'Skeletal muscles', 'Dermis of skin'] KEY 2
Enamel is ectodermal because it is produced by ameloblasts derived from the enamel organ. Bone, kidney, skeletal muscle, and dermis are mesodermal, whereas dentine, pulp, and cementum arise from neural crest-derived ectomesenchyme rather than mesoderm proper.

IAE-1-SRC-1089 Neural crest ectoderm gives rise to all the following EXCEPT:
['Sensory ganglia', 'Dura matter', 'Adrenal (suprarenal) medulla', 'Melanocytes of the skin', 'Autonomic ganglia'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the dura mater because it is mesodermal in origin, unlike the pia and arachnoid which come from the neural crest. The sensory and autonomic ganglia, the chromaffin cells of the adrenal medulla and the melanocytes of the skin are all classical neural crest derivatives.

IAE-1-SRC-1090 All the following are derivatives of ectoderm EXCEPT:
['Spinal cord', 'Cortex of suprarenal gland', 'Sensory ganglia of spinal nerves', 'Anterior pituitary gland', 'Retina of eye'] KEY 1
The correct answer is the cortex of the suprarenal gland because it develops from the coelomic mesothelium and is therefore mesodermal, whereas the medulla is neural crest. The spinal cord and retina come from the neural tube, the spinal sensory ganglia from the neural crest, and the anterior pituitary from Rathke's pouch of the stomodeal ectoderm.

IAE-1-SRC-1091 All the following are derivatives of mesoderm EXCEPT:
['Skeletal musculature', 'Musculature of blood vessels', 'Cardiac musculature', 'Suprarenal cortex', 'Suprarenal medulla'] KEY 4
The correct answer is the suprarenal medulla because its chromaffin cells are modified postganglionic sympathetic neurons derived from the neural crest, and so are ectodermal. Skeletal, smooth vascular and cardiac muscle are all mesodermal, as is the suprarenal cortex which arises from coelomic epithelium.

IAE-1-SRC-1092 As regards the somites, choose the correct statement:
['Differentiate into myotomes which give rise to muscles of trunk', 'Differentiate into sclerotomes which give rise to vertebrae', 'Arise by segmentation of paraxial mesoderm', 'Differentiate into dermatomes which give rise to dermis of skin', 'All of the above'] KEY 4
The correct answer is all of the above because every statement describes the somites accurately. They arise by segmentation of the paraxial mesoderm and each one differentiates into a myotome forming trunk muscle, a sclerotome forming the vertebrae, and a dermatome forming the dermis of the skin.

IAE-1-SRC-1093 The placental barrier does not include:
['The syncytiotrophoblast', 'The cytotrophoblast', 'The primary mesoderm', 'The endothelium of maternal blood vessels', 'The endothelium of fetal blood vessels'] KEY 3
Maternal blood enters the intervillous space directly and is not separated from the chorionic villi by maternal vascular endothelium. The early placental barrier includes syncytiotrophoblast, cytotrophoblast, villous connective tissue (extraembryonic mesoderm), and fetal capillary endothelium.

IAE-1-SRC-1094 The fetal part of the placenta is derived from:
['Decidua capsularis', 'Decidua basalis', 'Decidua parietalis', 'Chorion frondosum', 'Chorion laeve'] KEY 3
The chorion frondosum is the villous, fetal component of the placenta. The decidua basalis forms the maternal component, while the chorion laeve is the smooth, non-villous chorion.

IAE-1-SRC-1095 The wall of the chorionic vesicle is made of:
['Cytotrophoblast and syncytiotrophoblast', 'Cytotrophoblast and exocoelomic membrane', 'Syncytiotrophoblast and exocoelomic membrane', 'Trophoblast and extraembryonic somatic mesoderm', 'Trophoblast and extraembryonic splanchnic mesoderm'] KEY 3
The chorion is formed by trophoblast—cytotrophoblast plus syncytiotrophoblast—together with the extraembryonic somatic mesoderm lining it. Extraembryonic splanchnic mesoderm instead covers the yolk sac.

IAE-1-SRC-1096 The placenta is derived from:
['Chorion frondosum and decidua capsularis', 'Chorion laeve and decidua capsularis', 'Chorion frondosum and decidua basalis', 'Chorion laeve and decidua parietalis', 'Chorion frondosum and chorion laeve'] KEY 2
The placenta has a fetal component, the chorion frondosum, and a maternal component, the decidua basalis. The capsularis covers the conceptus and the parietalis lines the remainder of the uterus.

IAE-1-SRC-1097 A secondary chorionic villus is made of:
['Cytotrophoblast and syncytiotrophoblast', 'Trophoblast and secondary mesoderm', 'Trophoblast, primary mesoderm, and fetal capillaries', 'Syncytiotrophoblast and fetal capillaries', 'Trophoblast and primary mesoderm'] KEY 4
A secondary villus forms when extraembryonic mesoderm grows into the trophoblastic core of a primary villus. Fetal capillaries appear later, converting it into a tertiary villus.

IAE-1-SRC-1098 Attachment of the umbilical cord to the margin of the placenta is called:
['Placenta membranacea', 'Placenta praevia marginalis', 'Placenta succenturiata', 'Battledore placenta', 'Velamentous insertion of the cord'] KEY 3
A battledore placenta has marginal insertion of the umbilical cord. In velamentous insertion, the vessels enter the membranes before reaching the placental disc.

IAE-1-SRC-1099 When a chorionic villus becomes invaded by mesoderm, it is called:
['Secondary villus', 'Tertiary villus', 'Branching villus', 'Stem villus', 'Anchoring villus'] KEY 0
Mesodermal invasion converts a primary chorionic villus into a secondary villus. It becomes tertiary only after fetal capillaries differentiate within the mesodermal core.

IAE-1-SRC-1100 Pygopagus means:
['Monozygotic twins fused at the thoracic region', 'Dizygotic twins fused at the thoracic region', 'Monozygotic twins fused at the sacral region', 'Dizygotic twins fused at the sacral region', 'Monozygotic twins fused at the head region'] KEY 2
Pygopagus twins are conjoined monozygotic twins joined posteriorly at the sacral or buttock region. Thoracic fusion is thoracopagus, and fusion at the head is craniopagus.

IAE-1-SRC-1101 The following layers of the placental barrier disappear in the second half of pregnancy:
['Cytotrophoblast and syncytiotrophoblast', 'Primary mesoderm and fetal endothelium', 'Cytotrophoblast and primary mesoderm', 'Primary mesoderm and syncytiotrophoblast', 'Syncytiotrophoblast and fetal endothelium'] KEY 2
During pregnancy the placental barrier becomes much thinner: the cytotrophoblastic layer becomes discontinuous and largely disappears, and the villous connective tissue between trophoblast and fetal capillaries is markedly reduced. Syncytiotrophoblast and fetal capillary endothelium persist.

IAE-1-SRC-1102 Which of the following hormones is secreted by the placenta?
['Testosterone', 'Luteinizing hormone', 'Chorionic gonadotrophin', 'Follicle-stimulating hormone', 'Oocyte maturation inhibitor'] KEY 2
The syncytiotrophoblast secretes human chorionic gonadotrophin (hCG), which maintains the corpus luteum in early pregnancy. LH and FSH are pituitary hormones.

IAE-1-SRC-1103 On what day after fertilization does the morula reach the uterine cavity?
['4th day', '7th day', '9th day', '8th day', '10th day'] KEY 0
The conceptus undergoes cleavage while moving through the uterine tube and enters the uterine cavity as a morula at about day 4. It then becomes a blastocyst before implantation begins around day 6.

IAE-1-SRC-1104 As regards the extraembryonic mesoderm, all the following are true EXCEPT:
['It is the secondary mesoderm', 'It forms the connecting stalk', 'It shares in formation of the chorion', 'It forms the extraembryonic coelom', 'It surrounds the yolk sac'] KEY 0
In the terminology used by this course, extraembryonic mesoderm is the primary mesoderm, not secondary mesoderm. It contributes to the connecting stalk and chorion, surrounds the yolk sac, and splits to create the extraembryonic coelom.

IAE-1-SRC-1105 The intervillous spaces contain:
['Fetal blood', 'Maternal blood', 'A mixture of fetal and maternal blood', 'Blood with high carbon dioxide content', 'Blood with low oxygen content'] KEY 1
Maternal blood from the spiral arteries fills the intervillous spaces and bathes the chorionic villi. Fetal blood remains within capillaries inside the villi, so the two circulations normally do not mix.

IAE-1-SRC-1106 Which one of the following hormones is not secreted by the placenta?
['Progesterone', 'Estrogen', 'Human placental lactogen', 'Luteinizing hormone', 'Chorionic gonadotrophin'] KEY 3
The placenta secretes progesterone, estrogens, human placental lactogen, and hCG. Luteinizing hormone is secreted by the anterior pituitary, not by the placenta.

IAE-1-SRC-1107 Which of the following abnormalities of the placenta is due to an abnormality in implantation?
['Battledore placenta', 'Accessory placenta', 'Bipartite placenta', 'Placenta praevia', 'Tripartite placenta'] KEY 3
Placenta praevia results from implantation in the lower uterine segment, where the placenta approaches or covers the internal cervical os. The other choices describe abnormalities of placental shape, lobulation, or cord insertion.
